# Manifold

A browser where each tab can run on a different rendering engine — Blink (via CEF), Servo, WebKit (via WebKitGTK), or Blink again (via Microsoft Edge WebView2) — and the engine backing a tab can be swapped live without closing it.

Status: M0–M5 complete — single-engine tabs on Servo/CEF/WebKit, live engine swap, a real chrome, crash recovery, and multiple tabs shown as a force-directed graph (peek to preview, click to focus), all verified end-to-end. Full milestone plan: `C:\Users\Winston Li\.claude\plans\stateless-napping-sunset.md`. **M6 (WebView2, off-plan):** a fourth engine, native Windows API rather than a same-machine child of a separate SDK/runtime checkout — frame capture (`Windows.Graphics.Capture`), navigation, favicon, zoom, click/scroll/drag-selection input (real OS-level `SendMouseInput` via Composition hosting; keyboard/text entry stays CDP/script-driven — see Known limitations), taskbar-hiding for its off-screen host window, and popup interception (`target=_blank`/`window.open()`/ctrl+click) are all implemented and verified live.

## Architecture

```
manifold                  winit + wgpu compositor, process manager, swap logic
manifold-view-servo       Servo, same-machine child process (named pipe + shared memory)
manifold-view-cef         CEF (Blink), same-machine child process (named pipe + shared memory)
manifold-view-webkitgtk      WebKitGTK: native on Linux, hosted inside WSL2 on Windows (TCP transport either way: no shared memory across the VM boundary)
manifold-view-webview2    Microsoft Edge WebView2 (Blink), same-machine child process (named pipe + shared memory)
manifold-view-pdfium      one-shot PDF renderer: `<exe> <path>` writes one RGBA frame to stdout (`manifold_view_protocol::render`)
manifold-view-protocol    shared message types + both transports
manifold-pages            the `manifold://` pages (static HTML) and their router; every engine host serves them through its own scheme hook
```

Only `manifold-view-webkitgtk` is Linux-only (no Windows WebKit embedding exists); everything else builds natively on Windows. `manifold` itself (and `manifold-view-protocol`) also compiles and passes its unit tests on Linux (checked under WSL Ubuntu, 2026-10-02: `cargo check -p manifold`, `cargo test -p manifold`), and runs there under WSLg in `--headless` mode, including CEF tabs (a visible window there can take down WSLg's own compositor, see the on-demand CEF entry); `manifold-view-cef` builds there too (see the on-demand CEF entry below for `CEF_PATH`). Windows-only crates are `[target.'cfg(windows)'.dependencies]`, Windows-only calls are `cfg`-gated, and what has no Linux counterpart yet says so instead of failing silently (WebView2 is Unavailable, "needs Windows"; a WebKit tab starts the host directly; Open Containing Folder toasts the same; engine hosts are not killed with a force-killed manifold, said once on stderr). `manifold` and `ipc-protocol` need no GTK on Linux: `rfd` 0.15 resolves to the XDG portal there, and only `manifold-view-webkitgtk` pulls `gtk-sys`. Building on Linux keeps `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` off `/mnt/c`, e.g. `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=$HOME/mf-target cargo check -p manifold`.

**Engines by OS** (2026-10-23; the app-side switch is `ViewDesc::host_os` read through `process_manager/runtime.rs`, the pick-up rules are in `process_manager/availability.rs`). The principle is each OS's own system webview plus CEF and Servo; only Windows is built, released and tested today.

| Engine | Windows | Linux | macOS |
|---|---|---|---|
| WebView2 (Blink) | native; frames by `Windows.Graphics.Capture`; released | n/a | n/a |
| CEF (Blink) | released; hash-pinned download | layout checked against the linux64 archive, install never run; no release job, no pin | no `CefPlatform` entry (needs an app-bundle layout), no pin |
| Servo | released; hash-pinned download | not restricted by the app; not built or run here | not restricted by the app; not built or run here |
| WebKit | WSL2 + Ubuntu + `xvfb-run`, TCP frames; the host is downloaded on demand (built in `release.yml` on `ubuntu-22.04`, pinned under `linux-x64`; built and pinned in a dry run, 2026-10-23, see Releases below) | the host builds (CI runs only the app's tests); the app starts it directly over loopback TCP (written 2026-10-23, run only as far as the pure launch tests) | none; the native engine there is WKWebView, a new host that needs off-screen frame capture (unspiked) |

Why WebKitGTK stays on Linux: the host crate is mostly portable GTK3 (a `GtkOffscreenWindow` rendered to a pixbuf, input as synthesized `gdk::Event`s, a plain TCP or pipe transport), but the WebKitGTK library has no maintained Windows or macOS build (WinCairo and the Cocoa port are different ports); its web process, sandbox and compositing lean on Unix sockets, bubblewrap, EGL and X11 or Wayland. That is upstream knowledge, not checked in this repo. The offscreen window is a GTK3 widget: see PARKED "WebKit host on GTK4".

**CI** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) runs on every push and pull request: on Windows, `cargo fmt --all --check`, the pre-commit hook's clippy, and `cargo test` for the hook's crates; on Linux, `cargo test -p manifold -p manifold-view-protocol`. CEF (a download, CMake, ninja), Servo (a 33-minute build), WebKit (WSL) and the probes (a desktop and a GPU) stay local.

**A graph level's own source, behind one seam (`level.rs`):** shared graph chrome (fit/Reset View, the minimap, the LOD card count, the settle key, right-click hit-testing, `EXPORTSTATE`'s `cards`, edges and layout) reads the current level through `App::level_items`/`level_edges`/`layout_current_level` instead of walking `self.nodes`/`current_group` directly. The stored-node graph is one source; a JSON level (`json_level.rs`) is a second, read-only one built from a Json node's parsed value instead of `self.nodes`. A third level kind needs its own `Origin` arm and painter, its own state on `App`, and a branch in each of `level_items`, `level_edges`, `layout_current_level` and `insertion_group` (those branches are `if let` chains, so the compiler won't list them for you); the chrome that reads those methods (fit, minimap, LOD count) needs no changes. Moving to an `enum LevelSource` would make the compiler enforce that.

**A card is its type (since 2b-6):** a card's identity is `CardKind { ty, tags }` (`App::card_kind`, `card_kind.rs`), read from the document node's `Mirror::ty` plus a `Loaded` tag for a live tab; draw order, restorability and containment come from the `caps/` tables and edge table, not from an enum; click action, the Del verb, card style, the body painter, menus and parts of `drop_action` still `match` on the type constant (`TYPE_*`), and card size and filter text match on `Runtime`. Live per-card data (webview, edit buffer, decoded image) is `GraphNode.runtime`, an `enum Runtime` in `node.rs` (a group has `Runtime::None`). `NodePayload`, `ItemNodeKind`, `GroupPayload` and the caps bridge no longer exist, and a unit test (`deleted_names.rs`) fails if a source names them again. Plan and Outcome: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-node-model-2b-6-enums-to-type.md`.

**Module layout of `crates/manifold/src` (since the 2026-10-09/10 main.rs split, `main.rs` 3,895 to 1,108 lines):** `main.rs` keeps `struct App` (about 150 fields), its `Default`, `ContentSource`/`WebBody`, the settings-save constants and `fn main`. `App`'s methods live in child modules as `impl App` blocks, which can read its private fields, so a moved method is `pub(crate)` only if another file calls it. By area: *views* `graph_ui` (Graph view, camera), `card` (card frame and painters), `tab_view`, `image_view`, `json_level_ui`, `popups`, `settings_page`, `node_menu`, `chrome_ui` (toasts, `build_ui`, toolbar buttons), `theme`; *behaviour* `tabs` (spawn, focus, split, destroy), `groups`, `node_store` (id allocation, store open, node removal), `node_ops` (close, mute, pin, bookmark, print), `graph_nav`, `view_modes`, `shortcuts`, `fetched`, `closed`, `viewport` (geometry, zoom, navigation), `session_files` (settings, named sessions, demo), `reveal`, `event_loop` (winit loop, engine messages), `omnibar`, `debug_control`; *model and data* `search/` (page text, BM25 index, topic clustering and arrangement), `node`, `mirror`, `caps/`, `card_kind`, `level`, `links`, `jar`, `jar_defs`, `names`, `store/`, `session_sync`, `local_state`; *plumbing* `gpu`, `net`, `process_manager`, `view_install`, and the fetchers. `App`'s state is being grouped into sub-structs with their own `reset()`/`forget(id)` (`link: LinkGesture`, `interaction: GraphInteraction`, `spawns: SpawnTracker`, plus `camera`, `address_bar`, `find`). Tests live in `<module>/tests.rs` (the parent ends with `#[cfg(test)] mod tests;`; `main.rs` uses `#[path = "main/tests.rs"]`). Source-scanning tests (`names`, `every_id_keyed_field_is_forgotten_or_excused`) read specific files, so a method moved between files can fail one: fix the test's file list, not the move. Each move was proven by an identical sorted `cargo test -- --list` before and after, plus fmt, clippy, tests and the light gate. **Parked (spike 2026-10-10): grouping files into layer directories (`view/`, `app/`, `model/`, `network/`, `engine/`, `io/`) with `#[path = "model/jar.rs"] mod jar;`.** It works and keeps every `crate::x` path: with `profile.rs` and `jar.rs` moved, check, clippy, fmt and the full test list (1095) were fine. It needs three fixes per moved module that has a test directory: its `mod tests;` gets `#[path = "jar/tests.rs"]` (rustc otherwise looks for `model/tests.rs`, which would collide), `include_str!("../x.rs")` paths in `main/tests.rs` and the other source-scanning tests are updated, and `names::tests` (which lists source folders) and `gate tier` (crates/gate/src/tier.rs, which reads `io/profile.rs` as "a module directory on no list" and advises `light`) learn the new directories. Parked because it adds a non-idiomatic attribute per module for a layout the module paragraph above already documents; revisit if the flat list gets in the way.

## Prerequisites

- **Rust**: the toolchain `rust-toolchain.toml` pins (rustup installs it on first use). Nothing needs nightly features; it is pinned so a clippy update can't fail the `-D warnings` hook with no code change.
- **Visual Studio 2022, 17.14 or newer.** Older toolsets are missing STL symbols that Servo's build needs — this is a hard requirement, not a suggestion.
- **A real `ninja.exe`** on PATH (`scoop install ninja`), for CEF's build script.
  - If a Chromium `depot_tools` checkout is also on your PATH, **exclude it** for this project's builds — its extensionless `ninja` shell script shadows the real `ninja.exe` and breaks the CEF build. See the "Building" commands below, which strip it automatically.
- **WebView2 Runtime** — preinstalled on Windows 10 1803+/Windows 11; if missing, `manifold-view-webview2` exits before connecting with one `manifold_view_protocol::HOST_FATAL` line, "WebView2 runtime not found (0x80070002): install the WebView2 runtime (Microsoft Edge WebView2, Evergreen) from ...", which the tab's failed card shows (before 0.2.0 it panicked creating its environment and respawned in a crash loop). Simulate it with `WEBVIEW2_BROWSER_EXECUTABLE_FOLDER` set to an empty folder. A runtime that is found but fails when the host, already connected, creates its environment or controller (damaged, or an unusable user-data folder) ends the same way: the host sends `Crashed` with the `HOST_FATAL` line and exits, and the tab is left as a failed card naming it with one toast, not restarted (`tabs::after_crash`; it used to panic and respawn three times, then say only "engine-host connection lost"). Simulate it with `WEBVIEW2_USER_DATA_FOLDER` set to a file: "WebView2 couldn't create its controller (0x80080005: Server execution failed); ..." (2026-10-04). Check via: `reg query "HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\EdgeUpdate\Clients\{F3017226-FE2A-4295-8BDF-00C3A9A7E4C5}" /v pv`.
- **WSL2, distro `Ubuntu`**, with:
  ```bash
  sudo apt-get install -y libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libgtk-3-dev xvfb build-essential
  ```
  (Rust/cargo must also be installed inside that WSL2 distro, separately from the Windows-side toolchain.)

## Building

**Windows-side crates** (`manifold`, `manifold-view-servo`, `manifold-view-cef`, `manifold-view-webview2`) — from a regular shell at the repo root:

```bash
PATH=$(echo "$PATH" | tr ':' '\n' | grep -v -F "depot_tools" | tr '\n' ':') cargo build -p manifold -p manifold-view-servo -p manifold-view-cef -p manifold-view-webview2 -p manifold-view-pdfium
```

The `PATH` filtering is only needed if `depot_tools` is on your PATH; harmless otherwise.

**WebKit engine-host** — built *inside* WSL2, into its own target directory (it can't share `target/` with the Windows build — different OS, different toolchain):

```bash
wsl.exe -d Ubuntu -- bash -c 'cd "/mnt/c/Users/<you>/Documents/GitHub/manifold" && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-linux cargo build -p manifold-view-webkitgtk'
```

Adjust the path for wherever this repo actually lives on your machine (WSL2 sees your Windows drives under `/mnt/c/...`). `rust-toolchain.toml` applies there too: the first build makes WSL's rustup install the Linux build of the same nightly, and `target-linux` rebuilds once if WSL was on another toolchain.

### Runtime-file placement (required after every build)

Cargo doesn't do this itself — see the `TODO` in `crates/manifold/src/process_manager.rs`'s `engine_binary_path`. Both CEF and Servo need extra files placed next to their binaries after each build, because they'd otherwise collide (both ship an ANGLE `libEGL.dll`/`libGLESv2.dll` under the same filenames, and Windows resolves an implicit DLL dependency from the loading EXE's own directory first — whichever copy landed there last silently wins for *both* engines).

`manifold-view-webview2` doesn't need this: WebView2Loader is a single small loader, statically linked by the `webview2-com` crate (or at worst one lone DLL) — none of the ANGLE-style same-filename collision the other two have.

Run this after every build that touches `manifold-view-servo` or `manifold-view-cef`:
```bash
target/debug/gate.exe sync-runtime-deps
```
It finds Servo's ANGLE DLLs and CEF's runtime under `target/debug/build/{mozangle,cef-dll-sys}-*/` (or `…/{mozangle,cef-dll-sys}/*/`, where newer cargo nightlies put build-script output; it takes the newest of either), `cmp`s each against the copy already in place, and only recopies what's actually stale (safe to run any time — a no-op if nothing changed). Not a build.rs: a build script only sees its own crate's `OUT_DIR`, not a sibling crate's, and there's no reliable cross-crate coordination here without those upstream crates cooperating (they don't) — a plain script is simpler and more honest than fighting Cargo's model.

Why this needs a script at all rather than a one-time manual copy: Cargo has regenerated the `mozangle-<hash>`/`cef-dll-sys-<hash>` fingerprint directory these live under even on a rebuild that touched neither `Cargo.toml` nor `Cargo.lock`, leaving a stale copy in place that keeps working right up until it silently doesn't (Servo: panics with `egl function was not loaded`; CEF: silently runs stale code). The script picks the candidate whose own output *file* has the newest mtime, not the containing directory's — a build touches every candidate directory's own fingerprint bookkeeping on every invocation regardless of whether it actually recompiled that crate, which can leave several near-identical timestamps and make directory-mtime-based "newest" ambiguous.

A rebuild also tends to leave the *previous* hash's directory behind alongside the new one (Cargo doesn't clean up an old fingerprint just because it's no longer used) — these accumulate across sessions and are a repeat cause of this project filling the disk. Safe to prune anything under `target/debug/build/{mozangle,cef-dll-sys}-*` except whichever one `scripts/sync-runtime-deps.sh` just used — re-run the script afterward to confirm nothing needed recopying. Getting this wrong (deleting the wrong one) just means the next build regenerates whatever's missing, not silent breakage — but it's wasted rebuild time.

## Reading the entries below (staleness audit, 2026-10-01)

Everything from "Running" to the end is a dated log: each entry describes the code as it was when written, and names in backticks are not kept up to date. A search of every backticked identifier against the source found 156 that no longer exist. The big renames and removals, checked against the code, so an old entry can be read against today's:

| Old name in an entry | Now |
|---|---|
| `closed_tabs`, `closed_groups`, `ClosedTab`, `ClosedNode`, `MAX_CLOSED_TABS`/`MAX_CLOSED_GROUPS`, `group_export::GroupExport`, `import_group_export` | the `closed` field (`field::CLOSED`, the closing replica's id) on the closed root; Recently Closed is `Doc::closed_roots()`, a query over the document, capped at `MAX_CLOSED` = 20 (`evict_closed` deletes the oldest); `close_node`/`reopen_node` in `closed.rs`; Duplicate keeps a pure snapshot in `duplicate.rs` |
| `session.json`, `SessionJson`, `SessionTab`/`SessionNote`/..., `build_session_snapshot`, `import_session_from`, `next_group_id`, `SessionPlace` | the session store `session.mnf` (op log, spec `2026-09-25-session-op-log-design.md`); `session_sync::save_session`/`restore_session`; group files go through `import_group_json` |
| `alloc_tab_id`, `alloc_group_id`, `alloc_note_id`, `alloc_image_id`, `alloc_pdf_id`, `alloc_json_id`, `bump_past_id` | `App::alloc_node_id` (store node ids) |
| `build_json_viewer`, `json_viewer_tab`/`json_viewer_node`, "View as JSON", "Save as Node", `save_json_body_as_node`, `TabPayload.json_body` | removed: a tab whose page is JSON becomes a JSON node itself (content dispatch), shown as a level in the graph (`json_level*`, spec `2026-09-27-json-node-rework-design.md`) |
| `build_tab_context_menu`, `build_group_context_menu`, `build_note_context_menu`, `build_download_context_menu`, `NodeMenuAction`, `apply_node_menu_action` | `node_menu::build_node_context_menu`, `NodeMenuItem`, `NodeMenuConfig` |
| `tabs_at_level`, `groups_at_current_level`, `downloads_at_level`, ... | `App::level_scan` (`level.rs`, one pass bucketed by `Caps::draw_layer`) and `tabs_at_current_level` in `graph_nav.rs` |
| `build_tab_ui`, `start_transition_to_tab` | `build_tab_view_ui` (`tab_view.rs`), `start_transition_to_item` (`view_modes.rs`) |
| `bookmarks_open`, `history_open`, `downloads_open`, `*_engine_picker_open`, `show_anchored_popup`, ... | one `Option<AnchoredPopup>` with `toggle_popup`/`close_popup` (`popups/menus.rs`) |
| `advance_graph_camera`, `graph_camera_tween`, `pan_drag_history` | `GraphCamera::advance` and its `tween`, `momentum` and `drag_history` fields (`graph_ui.rs`) |
| `draw_card_glow`, `GROUP_OVERLOAD_COLOR` | `card_glow_shapes` (`card.rs`); `theme::DANGER_COLOR` and `GROUP_OVERLOAD_THRESHOLD` |
| `FailedTab`, `FailedTabPayload`, `retry_failed_tab` | `UnloadedState::Failed` on an unloaded tab card; `retry_missing_file` is for fetched files |
| `ImageRedirectFetcher`, `redirect_tab_to_image` | `RedirectFetcher` and the content-dispatch path (`redirect_fetch.rs`, `fetched.rs`) |

Treat an entry as history unless a newer entry or a spec says otherwise; the code and `docs/superpowers/specs` are the current truth.

## Running

**Superseded below -- kept for the debugging trail, not as current advice.** This section originally said to always use `cargo build --release`, because a debug build of a single idle tab ran at ~13-16fps (~60-80ms/frame) against release's smooth ~60fps (~16.8ms) -- a ~4-5x gap traced to `GpuState::upload_frame`: a brand-new GPU texture allocated from scratch on every uploaded frame, plus a CPU-side pixel format-conversion/downscale feeding the thumbnail, both of which a debug build pays full, unoptimized price for. Investigated (not assumed) via temporary frame-timing instrumentation, and confirmed *not* pan-specific along the way -- a control test holding a background-pan drag with zero movement measured the same frame time as sitting fully idle. Both root causes are now fixed directly in the app's own code (see this file's own changelog: the texture-reuse fix and the GPU-downscale fix, both below) rather than left as a "just build differently" workaround. Re-running the identical idle test after both fixes landed: debug dropped to ~16.7ms, matching release (~16.7ms, unchanged) almost exactly -- the specific gap this note used to warn about no longer exists.

```bash
target/debug/manifold.exe
```

That said, this doesn't make debug and release *generally* equivalent -- Rust's own optimization gap is real for any other CPU-bound path this investigation didn't specifically target, just no longer the dramatic, workload-defining one `upload_frame` used to be. `cargo build --release` remains worth reaching for if something *else* feels slow and profiling points at unoptimized CPU work, same as in any Rust project:

```bash
target/release/manifold.exe
```

Chrome (toolbars, buttons, popups, the graph's own cards) uses a small custom dark theme (`apply_style` in `manifold/src/theme.rs`) rather than egui's stock look — a three-step depth ladder (window background darker than a panel, darker than a raised button) plus one shared accent color for selection/hyperlinks/focus/active-widget fills, so the whole app reads as one designed surface instead of egui's flat gray-on-gray defaults where a button and the panel it sits on are nearly the same shade. Verified live via direct pixel sampling of a screenshot (toolbar background vs. a resting button's fill differ by a real, visible margin, not just a technically-nonzero one). Text uses IBM Plex Serif (`apply_fonts`) instead of egui's bundled Ubuntu-Light, embedded from `assets/fonts/IBMPlexSerif-Regular.ttf` (Google's static Regular-weight source, OFL-licensed) and inserted ahead of the bundled default in the proportional family rather than replacing it, so anything outside IBM Plex Serif's own glyph coverage still falls back instead of tofu-boxing. Picked from a side-by-side comparison of ten-odd candidates spanning plain-professional to serif — a deliberate "tinge of something else" rather than another neutral sans, not a final verdict. Verified live: toolbar button labels and address bar text render with visible serifs, clearly distinct from the prior sans-serif look. **Revisited:** corner rounding across buttons/inputs was originally bumped to 6px (window/menu popups 8px) specifically to read as "app chrome" rather than egui's tighter stock default — in practice that read closer to a soft, mobile-app pill-button look than the denser toolbar feel a desktop browser's chrome usually has, so it's been pulled back down to 3px for buttons/inputs and 4px for popups/menus (the context menu and the save toast, smaller transient elements, sit at 3px too). Same depth ladder and accent color, just noticeably tighter corners. Verified live: address bar and toolbar buttons render with visibly sharper, less pill-like corners in a Tab view screenshot.

**Revisited again — full editorial retheme.** The above got the chrome internally consistent, but side-by-side comparison against fresh mockups (built with the `superpowers:brainstorming` visual-companion tool) showed the *palette itself* (blue-gray/blue-accent) still read as generic dev-tool chrome rather than a deliberate identity, and the "IBM Plex Serif at Regular weight everywhere" choice above read soft for UI text once compared against real alternatives. Retheme, iterated through several rounds of mockups: `BG`/`TOOLBAR_FILL`/`SURFACE_RAISED`/`SURFACE_HOVER` shifted from neutral blue-black to warm near-black (`#17140F` base); `ACCENT` from blue (`#58A6FF`) to a muted gold (`#D7A24A`); `DANGER_COLOR` (renamed from the narrower `GROUP_OVERLOAD_COLOR` once its actual 9-site reuse was noticed — see this file's own commit history) from pure red to a warm rust (`#C4573A`) so it sits with the rest of the palette instead of against it; `TEXT_PRIMARY`/`TEXT_WEAK` warmed to cream/tan. Corner radius normalized to a uniform 2px everywhere it appears (was a mix of 3/4/6px) — every `rect_filled`/`rect_stroke`/`draw_card_glow` corner-radius call site checked individually, not blanket-replaced, since some nearby numbers at the identical literal (`4.0 * inv_zoom`, `6.0 * inv_zoom`) were actually circle radii, spinner sizes, or shrink margins that needed to stay untouched. Fonts split in two: **Literata** (Google's own "built for reading apps" text face, tuned for small-size legibility) replaces IBM Plex Serif for all body text, and a separate **Newsreader** Bold family (same reading-app design effort, so the pairing is deliberate) is used only for dialog titles/section headers via a new `heading_text()` helper, gathered from the eight `.strong()`-labeled popup titles that existed. Both are static single-weight instances generated locally via `fonttools varLib.instancer` from Google's variable-only upstream source files (`Literata[opsz,wght].ttf` wght=400/opsz=9, `Newsreader[opsz,wght].ttf` wght=700/opsz=20) — egui/ab_glyph has no variable-font-axis support, so the upstream variable files alone would have rasterized at whatever the font's default `fvar` instance happens to be, ignoring the weight/opsz this app actually wants. `egui_phosphor` switched from its default `regular` Cargo feature to `light` (47 call sites renamed from `egui_phosphor::regular::*` to `egui_phosphor::light::*`) to sit better against the less-heavy serif typography. Spacing/padding bumped up (`item_spacing`/`button_padding` roughly 1.6-1.7x) for the airier feel the editorial direction wants, except the graph view's own toolbar, which pushes the old tighter numbers back onto its local `Ui` — it's already packed with icon buttons and genuinely screen-space-constrained, unlike dialogs/menus/settings. Text-selection stroke changed from pure white to the new `TEXT_PRIMARY` cream (white read cold against a gold fill). The "audio playing" waveform indicator warmed from a minty `rgb(80,200,120)` to a sage `rgb(150,190,90)`, staying unambiguously "active/live" without clashing with the rest of the palette. Engine-identity badge colors (Servo orange/Chromium blue/WebKit teal/WebView2 blue) and the group/tag color swatches (the literal Okabe-Ito colorblind-safe palette, a past accessibility fix) were both deliberately left untouched — orthogonal to house theme in the first case, a confirmed-regression risk to touch at all in the second. Also added: `apply_dark_titlebar` forces the native Windows titlebar dark via `DwmSetWindowAttribute`/`DWMWA_USE_IMMERSIVE_DARK_MODE` regardless of OS theme, so a light-mode system doesn't draw a bright titlebar directly above this now-dark chrome — a full custom-drawn titlebar (matching the palette exactly, not just light/dark) was considered and deliberately deferred as a real feature addition, not a theme tweak. **Verified live**, not just compiled: ran `manifold.exe --headless`, drove it via the stdin debug-command channel (`SCREENSHOT path` / `CLICK x y left` / `QUIT`) against the real restored session, and inspected the resulting PNGs directly — confirmed the warm palette, gold accent border/dot-grid, sharp corners, and Light-weight icons on the Graph view, and confirmed both the Newsreader heading font (visibly distinct letterforms from body text) and the airy dialog spacing on the Settings panel. The real `session.json`/`settings.json` were backed up before this and diffed after (only a harmless tab-array reordering, no actual data change) — restored from backup anyway rather than leaving the reorder in place. Deliberately **not** touched this round: a primary-filled/secondary-outlined button convention (shown in the mockups but never confirmed as its own decision, and auditing which of the dozens of existing buttons across every dialog counts as "primary" is real per-dialog judgment, not a mechanical sweep) — buttons keep today's uniform single-style treatment.

**Contrast pass (2026-10-19).** `DANGER_COLOR` (now `#E07054`) and `TEXT_WEAK` (now `#B2A482`) were lightened so text in them keeps WCAG AA (4.5:1) on the page and raised surfaces (`DANGER_COLOR` was 3.5:1 on raised ones; the test `text_colors_keep_aa_contrast_on_their_surfaces` pins the ratios). The close button's hover fill uses `DANGER_COLOR` too, so its icon on that fill lost some contrast (3.4 to 2.5). Card menus now share one order: the kind's own actions, a rule, Rename / Duplicate / Pin / Color / Links, Move and Remove from Group, then delete (Export Group moved out of the delete block). The Settings page is up to 720 px wide with Help first. Confirm dialogs (session delete, jar, engine files, import) fill their main button: rust (`danger_button`) when it destroys something, gold (`primary_button`) when it does not (engine Download/Update); only the gold one opens focused, so Enter confirms it; a rust one opens with nothing focused, so a stray Enter does nothing. **One palette (2026-10-19):** the colors live in `manifold_pages::palette`; `theme.rs` builds its egui constants from it and the `manifold://` pages (which were still on the old blue palette) get them as CSS variables (`--bg`, `--raised`, `--text`, `--weak`, `--accent`, `--danger`), so the two cannot drift. The graph close button's hover uses a darker `DANGER_FILL` (light text on it is 4.5:1, up from 2.5:1); the recommended-tier and waveform green is one `SUCCESS_COLOR`. The JSON syntax palette, engine brand colors, note yellow and scrims stay local on purpose. **Roles (same day):** a hovered control is a surface step plus a neutral `OUTLINE` stroke (3:1 on the hover fill); gold (`ACCENT`) marks what is selected, focused, open or primary, and active-widget text is `BG`, not black. One table test (`colour_roles_keep_their_contrast_on_their_surfaces`) checks every foreground role on the surfaces it is drawn on. The pages share one `base.css` (palette variables, body, headings, key caps) and use the app's serif for body text; corner radii are `RADIUS`, `RADIUS_ITEM` and `RADIUS_CHIP`. Spacing and type sizes got no scale: the repeated values are too few (at most 5 uses each) for a scale to replace anything.

**Soft theme (2026-10-22, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-22-soft-theme-design.md`).** The chrome is Heptabase-style in dark and light, chosen by Settings "Theme" (`System`, `Light`, `Dark`; default System). Colors come from `manifold_pages::palette::{DARK, LIGHT}` (the one source for the app and the `manifold://` pages); `apply_style` fills egui's dark and light style slots and `theme::apply_theme_choice` picks one, so code reads colors from the `Visuals` it holds (`theme::accent`, `theme::danger`) or `theme::palette(&Visuals)` for the few app-only ones. Cards are the raised surface over a canvas (two-rect soft shadow, 12 px corners). Tag colors are stored as the Okabe-Ito entry; `theme::tag_shown` paints the light table's deeper variants on the light card. `App::choose_theme` sets the native window theme (winit owns the title bar), and `sync_theme` sends `HostToView::SetTheme` (protocol 5) to every engine host and reloads open `manifold://` tabs when the theme changes; hosts also read `VIEW_DARK` at spawn. `gate` seeds `"theme": "dark"` (or `GATE_THEME=light`) so shots do not follow the machine. The serif and gold earlier in this section are superseded by this.

**Look-alike rows (2026-10-19).** `disambiguate::qualifiers` is the one rule for rows that read the same: rows sharing a label get what differs, never more (host, else path, then the query items and fragment others lack, and `#n` for twins), and a label of its own gets nothing. The quick switcher and the address dropdown append it in italics after the label (`row_label`), computed over all listed tabs so it does not change as the query narrows; the trail strip leads with it (`q=cats page=2 · Results`) so a clipped label keeps it, and does not number a page seen twice. History and bookmarks are not wired in: their lists are windowed or per-url, and a title shared there is rare.

**What a drag will do (2026-10-19).** A dragged card shows its drop effect beside the pointer (`DropEffect::hint`: "Move into X", "Merge into X", "Group with X", "Make child of X", "Move out of group"), the cursor is the grabbing hand for the drag, "Dissolve Group" says it keeps the members, and Dissolve Group, Ungroup All and a group merge are undoable (`App::ungroup`, `merge_groups`, both through `fold_group`): the members move up a level (a merge: into the target) and the emptied group is *closed*, a Recently Closed entry holding where they went and their ids in the group field `held` (field 24, `<destination or -> <id>...`; a member moved elsewhere since stays; a merge's links stay on the target), and its Recently Closed row reads "(emptied)" with a hover saying reopening puts the members back, so reopening it, from the toast's Undo or from that list, puts them back (`regroup_held`; a member moved elsewhere meanwhile stays). The group keeps its id, so links, jar and color survive. Past the 20-entry cap the emptied groups are evicted before real closes. The toast is the one interactive toast, its Area takes clicks only then, and Ungroup All's tooltip counts the groups. Edges `recheck_edge_scope` cleared when the members moved up are not restored (PARKED.md). Every drop with an effect (Join, GroupWith, Eject, Reparent) and every Dissolve, Ungroup All and merge is on one session-local undo history (`undo.rs`, `UndoHistory`; depth 10, not saved or synced, a plain move is not on it). Its toast offers Undo, and Ctrl+Z undoes the newest step, Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y redoes it (Graph view only, not while a text field, popup or menu has the keyboard; debug command `UNDOKEY [shift]`); an "Undone: ..." toast carries a Redo button, and only a fresh step clears what can be redone. A card moved again, closed or changed remotely since is skipped ("Undone 2 of 3"), and a redo of a fold folds the group as it is now. A document-layer undo stack was decided against (PARKED.md). Gate view: `graph-drop-hint`; gate `closed` stage section `undo-redo`.

Starts on **Graph view**, with one root tab (Servo, loading `https://example.com`) already spawned and focused — click its node to enter Tab view. The graph is the primary view for managing multiple tabs:

- **Graph view**: every tab is a node (its last frame as a downscaled thumbnail, favicon, title, current engine) connected by an edge to the tab it was opened from. Hover a node to peek an enlarged preview without changing focus; click a node to focus it (switches to Tab view); drag a node to reposition it — edges settle to a rest length of 320 points, up from 180, so sibling nodes don't crowd/overlap each other at equilibrium; the small "x" at a node's corner closes that tab, reparenting its children to its own parent; a small circle at a node's bottom-left corner pins/unpins it (filled = pinned) — a pinned node is excluded from the layout entirely (the same exclusion a node currently being dragged already gets in `graph_layout::step`), so a future settle never moves it, though it still exerts its own spring/repulsion force on its unpinned neighbors when one runs. Saved in `session.json` (`SessionTab::pinned`).

**`Escape` dismisses a hover-peek preview early**: previously the only way to close a peek was moving the pointer away; `Escape` now does it too, without needing the pointer to move at all. Can't just clear `hovered` directly -- Graph view's Escape check runs before the per-node loop that re-detects `response.hovered()` each frame, so clearing it there would just get silently overwritten later the same frame by the pointer still sitting over the node. Instead a new `peek_suppressed` field is set to the currently-hovered id, checked by the peek-drawing code to skip rendering for that specific id; it's cleared automatically the moment `hovered` changes to anything else, so hovering away and back (or hovering a different node) always shows a fresh peek. New headless `DISMISSPEEK` debug command, since Graph view's own `Escape` handling reads real key state the existing `ESCAPE` debug command doesn't reach (that one's Tab-view-only). Verified live: hovered a node (peek visible), sent `DISMISSPEEK` with the pointer never moving and confirmed the peek disappeared while the node's own hover state (its "x" close button) stayed visible, then moved away and back and confirmed the peek reappeared.

**Fixed (layout model changed):** the force-directed layout used to run continuously, every frame, for as long as any node at the current level existed — which meant every unpinned node, *including whatever a drag was currently aimed at*, kept reacting to repulsion in real time. Confirmed live while building the drag-a-tab-onto-another-tab feature below: a slow, multi-step drag toward a target let that target's own repulsion push it away from the incoming drag, so the two visibly chased each other rather than meeting, and only a single fast cursor jump (no intermediate positions for the target to react to) reliably landed a drop. The layout now runs only as a one-shot settle burst (90 iterations at a fixed 1/60s step, comfortably past the convergence a full continuous run needed) whenever the visible node count at the current level actually changes (a spawn, a close, a group forming) — not every frame — and is skipped entirely while any drag is in progress, so nothing moves on its own while the user is trying to point at it. New/remaining nodes still auto-spread apart from each other via the settle burst; a node repositioned by hand simply stays exactly there afterward, with no ongoing correction if it now overlaps a sibling — the accepted tradeoff for a drop target that never runs away. Verified live: a slow, multi-step drag that previously required a single fast jump to land now completes reliably, with the target visibly staying in place as the drag approaches; a fresh two-node graph still auto-separates to the same equilibrium spacing as before. "+ New Tab" opens a child of whichever tab is currently focused (or a new root if none is). Drag empty background to pan, scroll to zoom (centered on the cursor); the zoom chip's Fit button (formerly "Reset View") fits every node currently visible at the current level (tabs/downloads, plus group cards at the top level) into frame — each node's own card bounds (not just its center point) plus a flat margin, rather than always the same fixed world-space rect regardless of how many nodes exist or how far one's drifted — falling back to a fixed default-sized rect only when there's nothing to fit (an empty group, or before any tab has spawned). The minimap (bottom right) shows only while some card at the level is out of view; with everything on screen it has nothing to navigate to and would only cover the cards in its corner. The chip also has −/+ buttons (×1.25 per click, clamped to the 15%–300% zoom range) and a clickable percentage readout that resets to 100% keeping the current pan. Entering/leaving a group reuses the same fit instead of the old fixed framing too, so a group's own members are framed on arrival rather than assuming they happen to sit near world center. Verified live: dragged a child node far from its parent, clicked Reset View, and the resulting frame centered on and fully contained both nodes with even margin on every side, re-run with a third tab present to confirm it scales to more than two nodes.

Graph view's default zoom is closer-in than a raw 1:1 scene (the scene's default world-space extent shrunk from 1600×1100 to 900×620) so nodes read larger on first open instead of needing an immediate manual zoom-in. **Fixed:** plain scroll used to pan (egui's `Scene` container hardcodes ctrl+scroll as its zoom gesture and plain scroll as pan, not configurable via its own builder), leaving zoom stuck behind a modifier and pan doubled up with the drag gesture above. Every `MouseWheel` event's modifiers are now forced to ctrl+command right before `ctx.run_ui` while in Graph view specifically (Tab view's page-content scroll is a separate `InputEvent::Scroll` path, untouched), so unmodified scroll always registers as `Scene`'s zoom gesture instead. Verified live: an unmodified scroll visibly zoomed the view around the cursor with no panning. **Fixed:** node decorations (favicon, engine/audio dots, pin toggle, close button, title-strip text, card borders) used to be painted in the same world-space units as the card itself, so `Scene`'s own zoom transform shrank them to illegible specks at `GRAPH_ZOOM_MIN` and ballooned them at `GRAPH_ZOOM_MAX` right along with the card. Every decoration's world-space size is now that literal times `inv_zoom` (`1.0 / GraphCamera::transform(...)`'s scale, the same scale factor the Tab↔Graph transition animation already derives) so `Scene`'s scale-up cancels back out to a constant on-screen size — deliberately excluding the card itself (`GRAPH_NODE_SIZE`) and the hover peek preview (`GRAPH_PEEK_SIZE`), which are content and meant to zoom. Verified live: zoomed out until a card's thumbnail shrank to a fraction of its original size while its favicon, pin dot, close button, and title text stayed visibly the same pixel size as at 100% zoom. **Fixed (properly this time):** that first pass sized text the same `* inv_zoom` way as the vector shapes, which fixed the on-screen *size* but not *sharpness* — egui rasterizes each glyph into its font atlas at the requested world-space size, so a small `inv_zoom` (high zoom-in) asked for a tiny atlas glyph that `Scene`'s transform then visibly blurred back up to size. All graph-view text (tab/download/group labels, the "x" close glyphs) now paints on a separate, untransformed `egui::Painter` targeting a dedicated foreground layer, at real fixed screen coordinates (each label's world-space anchor run through the same `translation + zoom_scale * world` projection `GraphCamera::transform` uses) and a real fixed font size — never rasterized at anything but its true on-screen size, so there's nothing for `Scene`'s transform to blur. Vector shapes (dots, borders, buttons) keep the original `inv_zoom`-sizing from the first pass, since re-tessellated geometry doesn't blur at any scale. Verified live: zoomed in until the card was several times its original size — the title-strip label and close button's "x" stayed crisp, correctly positioned, with no blur at the zoom level that showed it most before. **Fixed:** fixed-size text has the opposite problem at very low zoom — the card can shrink well below the label's natural width, and unlike a normal egui `Label` (which truncates itself during layout), text painted directly via `Painter::text` had no width limit and just overflowed past the shrunk card into open space. Tab/download/group labels are now truncated to the card's own current on-screen width (`card_width_pts * zoom_scale`, minus the label's fixed offset/padding) via a new `truncate_to_width` — a binary search over char-boundary prefixes for the longest one that, plus "…", still lays out inside that width, so the more the card shrinks the more of the label gets cut, same as an ordinary browser tab title ellipsizing in a narrow tab strip. Verified live: zoomed out until a card was a few dozen screen pixels wide — its label showed a truncated prefix plus "…" clipped exactly to the card's shrunk width, not spilling past it. Below `MIN_LABEL_CARD_SCREEN_WIDTH` (40pt on screen) a label is hidden entirely rather than truncated to a near-illegible one-or-two-letter sliver — a tab node keeps its engine-color dot and a download node its download-icon glyph either way, so there's still *some* identifying decoration at extreme zoom-out; a group card (no other decoration) just becomes a small colored card with no text. Verified live: at `GRAPH_ZOOM_MIN` a tab node's title strip showed no text or ellipsis at all, only the favicon and engine dot.

Arrow keys move a keyboard focus highlight between tab nodes at the current level without touching the mouse — reuses the same `focused` field the ACCENT border already highlights and every other "act on the focused tab" path already reads (`Ctrl+W`, "+ New Tab"'s parent, Enter-to-open), so a keyboard-selected node behaves exactly like a clicked one. Direction picks the nearest node in roughly that heading (`along + perpendicular * 2.0`, minimized — the standard CSS-spatial-navigation heuristic, so a same-row neighbor wins over a closer diagonal one) rather than jumping straight to the geometrically closest node regardless of direction; with nothing focused at the current level yet, any arrow press just picks a starting node instead. Enter opens whatever it's currently on, the same as clicking its card. Gated off while the settings popup or a group rename field is open, so arrow keys still do their normal job there. Verified live: two `GRAPHLEFT`s correctly walked the highlight two nodes leftward (not straight to the far one), `GRAPHRIGHT` correctly stepped back to the nearer neighbor rather than the original node, and `GRAPHOPEN` opened the highlighted node into a real, stable Tab view. That last check surfaced a real, pre-existing bug in the headless protocol itself: `ESCAPE`/`ENTER`/the address-bar dropdown's `ARROWUP`/`ARROWDOWN` commands latched a `pending_debug_*` flag that only `build_tab_ui` ever consumes (`std::mem::take`) — sent while already in Graph view (a common test-setup step, "make sure we're in Graph view first"), the flag had nothing to consume it that frame and stayed stuck `true` indefinitely, silently firing the *next* time any command opened a tab and bouncing straight back out to Graph before a screenshot could ever show the real Tab view (a genuine key press doesn't have this problem — an unconsumed `key_pressed` just evaporates at end of frame, it isn't a sticky latch). Fixed by only setting those four flags when `view_mode == Tab && transition.is_none()` — exactly the condition under which `build_tab_ui` will actually run to consume them next frame — confirmed by re-running the same `GRAPHOPEN` repro and getting a stable Tab view on the following screenshot instead of an unexplained bounce back to Graph.

Right-button drag over the graph panel draws a rectangle-select marquee, selecting every tab node its screen-space rectangle overlaps (shown as a translucent accent wash on each selected card, distinct from the keyboard-focus border so both can apply to the same node at once without being confused for each other). Deliberately the secondary button rather than plain drag: the primary button is already Scene's own pan gesture, so `Scene::drag_pan_buttons` is restricted to `PRIMARY` and the marquee reads raw pointer state directly off `ui.input` instead, leaving the two gestures with no way to compete over the same drag. Once something's selected, the toolbar grows two extra buttons — "Group Selected (N)" (top level only, matching "+ New Group"'s own no-nesting restriction) creates a new group at the centroid of the selected cards' positions and moves them all into it in one step, same end state as the existing drag-one-tab-onto-another flow but for any number at once; "Close Selected (N)" closes them all. Escape, a plain left-click on empty canvas (not a pan drag, and not a click that deletes a link line, ends pick-target mode, hits the minimap or dismisses a menu or popup), or picking a different node by click/keyboard, clears the selection; Ctrl-click on a tab card toggles it in or out of the selection without opening it (a plain click still opens it, as before), and Space on a focused tab card toggles it too. Verified live: a right-drag over two of four tab nodes correctly showed "Group Selected (2)"/"Close Selected (2)" and highlighted exactly those two cards (re-run with a fresh layout to rule out a coincidental overlap); clicking "Group Selected" produced a real "Group 1 / 2 tabs" card positioned where the two selected nodes actually were, with the other two nodes untouched; re-run clicking "Close Selected" instead correctly removed just the two selected nodes and left the other two in place. Dragging any one member of an active selection onto an *existing* group's card joins the whole selection to it in one drop, not just the node the pointer happened to grab — the same "the selection acts as one unit" idea "Group Selected"/"Close Selected" already apply, extended to the pre-existing single-tab drag-onto-group gesture. Verified live: selected two tabs, dragged one of them onto an empty group's card, and the group's count went straight from "0 tabs" to "2 tabs" with both nodes correctly gone from the top level. **Superseded 2026-10-23:** the toolbar buttons are gone; Pin, Group and Close are the selection bar floating above the selected cards (`graph_ui/selection_bar.rs`), shown from one selected card.

Switching between Graph and Tab view (clicking a node, or the "Graph"/Escape button) animates as a 220ms shrink/expand: the transitioning tab's live content morphs between full-window size (Tab view) and its graph node's thumbnail-sized rect (Graph view), smoothstep-eased, rather than cutting instantly. This reuses the exact same world→screen transform the graph's own `Scene` container uses to place nodes (replicated by hand in `GraphCamera::transform`, matching egui 0.36's internal `fit_to_rect_in_scene` formula — not exposed publicly, so not a dependency, just the same math) so the animated rect lands exactly on the real node position, not an approximation. During the animation the app stays logically in Graph view (chrome, physics tick, node layout all keep running underneath) with only the one transitioning tab's content quad drawn at an interpolated viewport rect, layered *on top of* the graph (not underneath it) so the shrinking/expanding content is never hidden behind other nodes/edges as it animates. **Fixed:** because Graph view's own node-click handling keeps running as that backdrop for the whole 220ms, clicking a node again (the same one, or a different one) before the first click's expand animation finished used to retarget `App::transition` mid-flight — the animation would jump partway through to the new node, and `focus_tab` would hide the first tab's still-animating content out from under it. `start_transition_to_tab` now ignores the click entirely while a `Transition` is already running, so extra clicks during the animation are just dropped rather than corrupting it.
**Card gestures (2026-10-19):** a click on a card selects it alone (Ctrl-click toggles it in the selection, a click on empty canvas clears it); a double-click, or Enter on the focused card, opens it: a Tab or Unloaded card into Tab view, an Image into Item view, a Download or PDF reveals its file, a Note starts editing, a Group or Json card is entered. A card whose file is missing still retries on a single click. Tests that open a card drive `DOUBLECLICK`, not `CLICK`.

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Tab groups**: "+ New Group" (top level only) creates an empty container node; dragging a tab card onto a group's card joins it, hiding it from the top level. Dragging one ungrouped tab onto *another* ungrouped tab (not a group card) creates a new group containing both, named the same "Group N" way "+ New Group" names one — the two tabs are the only kind of node an ungrouped tab can ever land on at the top level (a group's own members only render one level deep, inside that group's own subgraph, so an ungrouped tab and an already-grouped one never coexist on screen as drop targets for each other). Verified live with a normal slow, multi-step drag: a real "Group 1" / "2 tabs" card appeared containing both, the target visibly staying put as the drag approached rather than fleeing — see the layout-model fix noted above, prompted by exactly this feature's own live testing first exposing the problem. Double-clicking a group drills into its own subgraph in place of the top level's — the toolbar swaps to "◀ Groups" plus the group's name, and "+ New Tab" there joins the group automatically — the same "swap which node set this view renders" idea `ViewMode::Tab`/`Graph` already is, one level deeper, reusing the identical flat `graph_layout::step` simulation rather than a second nested one. No nesting in this first cut (a group can't contain another group). **Fixed (now animated):** entering/leaving a group used to reset pan/zoom to the new level's framing (same target "Reset View" jumps to) instantly; it now eases there over the same 220ms `TRANSITION_DURATION`/smoothstep curve as the Tab↔Graph switch, via a new, separate `GraphCameraTween` rather than reusing `Transition` itself — `Transition` morphs *one tab's* content quad between two rects, but entering a group swaps the entire visible node set, not one tab's content, so there's nothing to morph, only the camera to move. Because a group's members already live in the same shared world-space coordinates the group's own card does, easing the camera toward them doubles as a "zoom into the subgraph" reveal for free: the new level's real nodes render (tiny, correctly positioned) from the very first frame, and the camera animating in is what grows them into view — no separate rendered thumbnail of the subgraph needed. Verified live: entered a group containing one tab and the screenshot immediately after showed that tab's card filling almost the whole screen (the tight single-node framing the tween was headed toward), leaving the group brought back the top-level framing with the group's card and the other tab both visible again. A group's own card shows its name and a live member count; its "x" dissolves it (members return to the top level, never closed) rather than closing its tabs. Groups, their membership and nesting are saved in `session.json` (`groups`, each node's `group`), except the groups "Expand as Graph" makes. Verified live end-to-end: created a group, double-clicked into it, added a tab there via "+ New Tab" (correctly joining the group), went back and confirmed the group's card showed "1 tab", then dissolved it and confirmed the tab reappeared at the top level with its original parent edge intact. The drag-a-tab-onto-a-group interaction itself is confirmed correct by code inspection (the drop-target check runs against the real captured positions of both nodes every time); now that the layout only settles once per change instead of continuously (see above), the same reproducible-coordinates problem that made this hard to pin down live no longer applies going forward. A group defaults to "Group N"; while inside it, clicking its name in the toolbar swaps the plain button for a real, focused `egui::TextEdit` pre-filled with the current name — Enter commits (blank names are ignored, keeping the old name), Escape cancels. Verified live that clicking the name correctly enters rename mode with a focused, pre-filled field; the actual type-then-Enter commit wasn't independently live-tested, since this project's headless debug protocol has no command that synthesizes keystrokes into a browser-chrome `egui` widget the way `ADDRESSBAR`/`TYPE` do for the address bar and page content specifically — the commit/cancel logic mirrors the address bar's own established Enter/Escape handling in the same file. A member card also gets its own hover-revealed "eject" button (a "^" in the corner opposite the close "x") while viewing a group's own subgraph — previously the group's own "x" (dissolving the whole group, returning every member) was the *only* way to get a tab back out, with no way to remove just one. Clicking it clears that tab's `group` back to `None`, popping it back to the top level without touching any other member. Verified live: grouped two tabs, entered the group, ejected one — it disappeared from the subgraph immediately and reappeared at the top level (with its original parent edge intact) once the group's own count dropped to "1 tab" and the view was left. A group card also has its own always-visible color swatch (bottom-right corner, where every card kind has it now) — clicking it cycles through a fixed 6-color palette and back to no color, tinting the card's border (and the swatch itself) instead of the plain default `ACCENT` blue every group had before. Verified live: cycled a group's swatch through all 6 colors and confirmed the wrap back to the default blue border landed exactly on the 7th click. Dragging one group's card onto *another* group's card merges them — every member (tabs and downloads) moves to the target group and the dragged-from group is removed, the same "drop onto a card" language a tab already uses to join a group, just group-onto-group. Verified live: two one-tab groups, dragged the first onto the second — the first vanished entirely and the second's count went straight to "2 tabs", confirmed by entering it and seeing both real tabs present. Right-clicking a group's card (top level only) opens a context menu -- Rename, the same 6-color swatch row plus a "no color" option, and Dissolve -- consolidating what was otherwise the click-name/always-visible-swatch/hover-"x" trio spread across the card itself. Right-click is otherwise the rectangle-select marquee's own button; a press landing specifically on a group's card opens this menu instead of starting a marquee, since groups were never part of that selection in the first place. "Rename" swaps the menu's own contents to the same `TextEdit`-with-Enter/Escape the toolbar's in-subgraph rename already uses, rather than a second, separate text field. Verified live: right-clicked a group and got the full menu; picked a color swatch and the group's border tinted immediately; reopened the menu and confirmed that swatch showed selected; picked "Dissolve" and the group was gone.

**"Fit Subtree"**: a new entry in the tab context menu -- zooms/pans the graph to frame just that tab and its descendants (`App::subtree_ids`, a `parent`-link BFS restricted to nodes sharing the tab's own `group` so an off-screen descendant in a different group can't skew the fit), instead of "Reset View"'s whole-level frame. Same margin/fallback shape as `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect`, just scoped by `fit_subtree_scene_rect`. Sets `graph_scene_rect` directly, no animation, matching "Reset View"'s own directness rather than the separate `GraphCameraTween` group-enter/leave uses. Verified live: built a four-tab tree (root R, child A, A's own child B, and a second child C of R -- a sibling branch), right-clicked A and picked "Fit Subtree", and confirmed via the graph minimap's own viewport-outline indicator that the new framing covered only A and B's region, excluding R and C entirely.

**`Ctrl+Shift+W` close all at current level**: closes every tab `tabs_at_current_level()` returns (live or asleep) -- at the top level, every ungrouped tab (groups and their members untouched); inside a group's own subgraph, every tab member (the tabs that group's "Close Group and Contents" context-menu entry would close, though that entry also closes its other members and the group -- an emptied group itself is left behind rather than auto-dissolved, same as `Close Selected` never auto-dissolving anything either). Checked ahead of plain `Ctrl+W` in `handle_global_shortcuts`, same ordering `Ctrl+Shift+T`/`Ctrl+Shift+G` already need. New `CLOSEALLLEVEL` debug command for headless testing. Verified live both ways: three ungrouped top-level tabs all closed ("Recently Closed (3)") in one shot, and separately, two tabs grouped then closed from inside that group's own subgraph ("Recently Closed (2)") while "Group 1" itself stayed in the toolbar, now empty.

**Virtual cards** (`virtual_source.rs`, `level.rs`; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-virtual-cards-design.md`): a card with no document node, derived from other state and drawn on the current level beside the stored ones. `App.virtual_sources` holds `Rc<dyn VirtualSource>`s; a source supplies its `name`, its `entries` (`CardEntry`: id, `CardKind`, `Origin::Virtual(name)`, world rect) and `edges`, `paint_body`, `on_click`, `on_menu`, `on_drop` (what a release does: its target, or `None` over empty canvas, and `at`, the world point of the release; a release on the back button is an `Eject`), and optional `searchable_text` (default none, so search does not dim it). What a virtual card may do comes from the same `Caps` registry as stored cards, keyed by its kind's `application/vnd.manifold.virtual.*` row (plus the `*/*` row for every type): `selectable`, `drag_source`, `drop_target`, `menu`, `stored_controls` (a JSON level's cards have their own `application/vnd.manifold.json-level` row granting none, so the chrome needs no JSON special case; a JSON level lists no virtual cards). Handlers run with `&mut App` inside the card loop: return a `GraphAction` for anything that changes nodes. Shared chrome (draw and cull, Tab order, rectangle and click select, fit and Reset View, the minimap, search, `EXPORTSTATE`, where each card lists `"virtual": "<source>"` and its abilities) reads the level through `CardEntry`, never `self.nodes`. Persistence rule: a virtual card has no `NodeId` (its `CardId` is `virt(source, key)`, `is_virtual()`, `node()` is `None`), so it never reaches `edit_pos`, `edit_field`, `edit_group`, `edit_parent`, `apply_drop`, the store, sync, sessions or undo; node actions skip such ids in a mixed selection. Dragging a virtual card never moves its rect or emits `GraphAction::Drop`: the release calls the provider's `on_drop` directly, and the drag ghost is a translucent frame. The first production source is the trail strip (`trail_strip.rs`; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-trail-strip-design.md`): in Graph view the focused tab's page tree (`NavHistory::tree`, laid out by the same `tree_layout` as a JSON level) hangs under its card as `CardId::virt("trail", ..)` cards (key: the tab's slot and the entry's serial) of kind `application/vnd.manifold.virtual.trail-page` (drag-out only). The strip hangs wherever its tab's card is, and the camera moves only on a request, so `App::frame_new_strip` (called each frame from `apply_camera_requests`) eases the camera to the card and the strip's root when a strip appears (Graph view entered, another tab focused, a first branch) and they are not already in view; not on every frame, so it never fights a pan, and a strip that grows while you browse does not move the view (`gate trail` checks the strip is on screen right after the toggle). A click is `trail_pick`; dragging a page onto a card, empty canvas or the back button opens it as a card at the release point (`open_branch_as_card`), opened from the tab whose trail it came from; it has no menu. The Back and Forward popups draw the same tree in a scroll area. The test-only `fake` source covers the seam.

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Reopen closed group**: "Close Group" (the group card's right-click context-menu entry) is the one action that closes a whole group *and* dissolves it -- until now, undoing it meant reopening each member individually from "Recently Closed" and losing the group's own name/tag color/layout entirely, since `dissolve_group` deletes the `GroupNode` itself. `close_group` now snapshots the group first via the same `export_group` "Export Group..." already uses (a full `group_export::GroupExport` -- name, tag color, every member's url/engine/position), pushed onto a new bounded `closed_groups` stack (`MAX_CLOSED_GROUPS`, 5), before closing anything. The "Recently Closed" dropdown lists closed groups (a `FOLDER_SIMPLE` icon, name, and member count) above closed tabs, its own toolbar count now `closed_tabs.len() + closed_groups.len()`; picking one calls `import_group_export`, the same path a real file import uses, recreating the group with fresh tab ids at the top level. The individual `ClosedTab` entries `close_group`'s own per-member `close_tab` calls still produce (needed for their other bookkeeping -- parent-relinking, thumbnail cleanup, focus/hover reset) are thrown away immediately after (`closed_tabs` truncated back to its pre-loop length), so a closed group doesn't also leave N redundant single-tab undo entries a user could reopen from and get duplicates. Not reachable from `Ctrl+Shift+T` (that shortcut only ever looks at `closed_tabs`) -- only the dropdown reopens a closed group.

**Drag tab onto tab to reparent (inside a group)**: dropping one member of a group's own subgraph onto a sibling's card now re-parents it there. Deliberately scoped to *inside* a group's own subgraph only -- the equivalent top-level gesture (dropping one ungrouped tab onto another) already means "create a new group with both," so reusing it for reparenting there would be ambiguous; inside a group's own subgraph, that meaning doesn't apply and no drop gesture existed there at all yet, so it was a clean gap to fill rather than an overload. Guarded by a new `would_create_cycle(node_id, new_parent_id)` (walks the candidate new parent's own parent chain upward, checking it never reaches back to `node_id`) so dropping a tab onto its own descendant is a no-op rather than creating a parent-chain loop.

**Fixed: duplicate default group names**: all three "create a group" call sites derived a new group's default "Group N" name from `self.groups.len() + 1` -- shrinks when a group dissolves/closes, so a later new group's default name could collide with an existing one's (three groups "Group 1/2/3", dissolve "Group 2", create a new one: `len()` is back to 2, so the new group is also named "Group 3"). New `default_group_name(id)` derives the number from the group's own id instead (`alloc_group_id`'s monotonic, never-reused counter), so a default name is never reused regardless of what's been dissolved in between. Manually renaming two groups to the identical string is left alone -- that's a deliberate user choice, not an accidental default-naming collision.

**Window title reflects the focused tab**: the OS window title was hardcoded to `"manifold"` at window creation and never touched again, regardless of what tab was open -- confirmed while scoping this, no `set_title` call existed anywhere. New `App::update_window_title`, called once a frame, sets it to `"{title} — manifold"` for `self.focused`'s own tab (falling back to its url if the title hasn't arrived yet, and to the plain app name if nothing's focused) -- reflects `self.focused` regardless of `view_mode`, so it's still "the last tab you were looking at" from Graph view or the Settings page, same reasoning `self.focused` already serves as the default parent for a new tab there. Only actually calls `Window::set_title` (a real OS call) when the computed title changed from `last_window_title`, not once a frame regardless -- this app's winit loop runs `ControlFlow::Poll`, so it's already redrawing continuously.

**Fixed: dragging one selected tab left the rest of the selection behind**: rectangle-selecting several tabs, then dragging one of them to reposition, only moved that one card -- the rest of the selection stayed put, breaking its visual cohesion mid-drag. The drop-onto-a-group-card handling right below this already treated an active selection as one unit for *joining a group*; plain repositioning didn't have the same treatment. Fixed by applying the same `drag_delta()` to every tab in `self.selected_tabs` when the dragged card is part of one, instead of only the card the pointer happened to grab.

**Fixed: a crashed/respawned tab was invisible from the app itself**: `eprintln!("engine-host crashed ({reason}) for {tab_id}, restarting at {url}")` was the *only* trace of a crash -- console-only, so a user with no terminal open had no way to know a tab had crashed and quietly come back (e.g. mid-navigation, page state lost). Generalized `self.save_toast` to carry the caller's own full message text instead of always prepending "Saved " at render time -- every original "Saved <path>" call site (session save/export, bookmarks export, group export, screenshot, print-to-PDF, download-completed) now builds that text itself, freeing the toast up for unrelated short-lived messages. A crash now also shows "Tab crashed, restarted: {url}" through the exact same toast, rather than a second, separate notification mechanism.

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Group overload border tint**: a group card's border turns red (`GROUP_OVERLOAD_COLOR`) once its member count passes `GROUP_OVERLOAD_THRESHOLD` (8) -- a cheap glanceable "this one's gotten big" cue, distinct from every other color already in this app's chrome so it reads unambiguously as a warning. Only applies while the group has no explicit tag color of its own -- a deliberate user color choice still wins, same precedent tag colors already have over the plain default `ACCENT` border. The member-count query that already ran once per group card (previously just for the thumbnail grid/"N tabs" label) is now hoisted above the border draw and reused there too, rather than computed twice. Verified live: grouped 9 tabs at once (over the threshold) and confirmed the resulting card's border rendered visibly red instead of the default blue.

**Middle-click a tab node to close it**: same real-browser tab-strip convention, alongside the existing small "x" rather than replacing it -- `response.clicked_by(egui::PointerButton::Middle)` on the card's own interact response reuses `close_requested` directly, going through the exact same `close_tab` path as the "x". Verified live: middle-clicked a tab card and confirmed it closed with "Recently Closed (1)" appearing, same as clicking its own "x" would. Every card kind now closes on middle-click (a group dissolves, as its "x" does), since all of them share `draw_graph_card`.

**Export/Import bookmarks to an arbitrary file**: a new "Bookmarks" section in the settings popup, below the existing Sessions one -- Export writes `self.bookmarks` to wherever a real native file dialog (`rfd`, already a dependency for session export) picks; Import reads a file back and replaces the running list, saving it to the normal `bookmarks.json` too so the import sticks past restart. `bookmarks.rs` gained `save_to`/`load_from` (arbitrary-path variants of `save`/`load`), the same factoring `session.rs`'s own `save_to`/`load_from` already have. Hidden in headless mode like Export/Import Session -- a real modal file dialog blocks on OS/human input neither exists there; new `EXPORTBOOKMARKS`/`IMPORTBOOKMARKS` debug commands call the same underlying save/import directly instead. Verified live: bookmarked a tab ("Bookmarks (1)"), exported it, confirmed the written JSON's `title`/`url` matched the real page, then started fresh with no bookmarks, imported that same file, and confirmed "Bookmarks (1)" reappeared with the "Bookmark" toggle correctly showing pressed for the still-open matching tab.

**"Pin All"/"Unpin All"**: its own label swaps between the two depending on whether every open tab is already pinned (`all_pinned = !self.tabs.is_empty() && self.tabs.values().all(|n| n.pinned)`, the empty-set guard so a closed-down session doesn't show "Unpin All" with nothing open to unpin), same "one button/row, label swaps with state" shape "Collapse Groups"/"Expand Groups" already established -- simplified from two separate buttons into one after a later session pass flagged the inconsistency. Sets every open tab's pinned state in one click via `App::set_all_pinned` (`true`/`false`), same "just run the single-tab effect once per tab" shape `mute_all_except`/`close_all_except` already have, minus an "except" -- pinning has no natural tab to exclude the way muting excludes the one presumably still being listened to. Verified live: two tabs, confirmed the button read "Pin All", clicked it and confirmed both graph cards' pin dots turned filled blue and the button relabeled to "Unpin All", clicked again and confirmed both reverted to a plain outline with the label back to "Pin All".

**"Tab actions" dropdown**: folds "Mute Others", "Close Others", and the "Pin All"/"Unpin All" toggle above into one Tab-view toolbar dropdown, replacing what had grown to three always-visible buttons -- a later session pass flagged the toolbar as cramped (confirmed live: button text touching the window's right edge even at 1300pt wide). Same anchored `egui::Area`/`Frame` popup pattern "Bookmarks (N)"/"History (N)"/"Downloads (N)" already use; each row calls the exact same underlying method (`mute_all_except`/`close_all_except`/`set_all_pinned`) the old standalone buttons did, and the popup closes itself after any row is clicked. Verified live: opened the dropdown and confirmed all three rows, clicked "Pin All" and confirmed both tabs' pin dots turned filled blue with the dropdown closing itself, reopened it and confirmed the row now read "Unpin All", and separately confirmed "Close Others" closed the other two of three open tabs ("Recently Closed (2)") with the dropdown closing itself the same way.

**Drag a group member onto "◀ Groups" to eject it**: a second way to pop a tab back to the top level while viewing a group's own subgraph, alongside the existing hover-revealed eject button -- drop a dragged member card onto the toolbar's own "◀ Groups" back button and it's ejected instead of just repositioned. The back button's rect is screen-space (a toolbar widget), unlike the world-space `pos` the top-level tab/group drop targets check, so this reads the raw pointer position directly rather than reusing that same-space comparison. Reuses `remove_from_group_requested` directly -- the exact same effect the eject button itself already requests, just a different gesture reaching it. Verified live: grouped two tabs, entered the group, dragged one member card onto "◀ Groups" and confirmed it vanished from the subgraph immediately (matching the eject button's own already-verified behavior), then left the group and confirmed the ejected tab was a real top-level card while "Group 1" correctly showed just the one remaining member.

**Export/Import a single group**: a new "Export Group..." entry in the group context menu, and a top-level-only "Import Group..." Graph toolbar button, using the same `rfd` file-dialog pattern Export/Import Session and Bookmarks already have -- for sharing or backing up one group's own subtree without the whole graph. New `group_export.rs` module: `GroupExport { name, color, tabs }`, each tab keeping its `parent` link only when that parent is *also* a member of the same group (a member whose real parent was outside the group becomes a root in the imported copy, since that outside tab was never part of the export). Import allocates a fresh id for every tab via `alloc_tab_id` rather than reusing the exported ones -- reusing them would collide with an already-running session's own ids -- remapping `parent` links through the same old-id -> new-id map built while allocating them, and spawns via `SpawnPurpose::Restore` so imported tabs never steal focus. `EXPORTGROUP`/`IMPORTGROUP <path>` debug commands bypass the dialog for headless testing, same reasoning every other Export/Import feature's own debug commands already have -- `EXPORTGROUP` acts on whichever group's context menu is currently open. Verified live: grouped two tabs (parent-child), exported the group, confirmed the written JSON preserved the parent link between the two members, then started a fresh session with just its own root tab and imported that file -- confirmed a new "Group 1" appeared with both tabs as separate new nodes, the original root tab and its focus completely untouched. Since 2026-09-25 the export also holds the groups nested inside, at any depth (`GroupExport::groups`, each with name, color, position and parent group, and each tab's `group`); import gives them fresh ids too (`alloc_group_id`). "Expand as Graph" groups are left out, as in a session, with their tabs in the nearest exported group. Older files load unchanged (`a_file_without_nested_groups_loads`). Checked headless (`target/followups/gexp.txt` with `gexpseed`): a group holding a tab, a nested group and a group nested in that, each with a tab and one tab parented across levels, exported and re-imported as the same three-level tree.

**"Close Group"**: a new entry in the group context menu, next to Dissolve -- closes every tab member through the normal `close_tab` path (teardown, reparenting, the `closed_tabs` stack all still apply, same as `close_all_except`/`close_selected`), then dissolves the now-empty group. A download member is never closed (downloads have no "closed" state), just returned to the top level exactly as a plain Dissolve already would -- `App::close_group` closes tabs first, then reuses `dissolve_group` itself rather than duplicating its cleanup. Verified live: grouped two tabs, right-clicked the group's card, clicked "Close Group", and confirmed an empty top level (as of 2026-10-06 the canvas with its hint; it was a "No tabs. Click..." label then) with "Recently Closed (2)" reflecting both tabs closing through the real close path, and no leftover group card or "Ungroup All" button.

**Failed spawns and the empty graph (2026-10-06)**: a New or Root tab that fails to start leaves a failed card (a stored tab node, so it syncs and persists), deduplicated by url, engine and group: a repeat failure for the same intent refreshes the one card. A New or Root tab that lands clears the failed cards for its url and group (each is deleted outright, not closed). Failure toasts that share a cause (engine and error text) merge into one, counted by distinct intent. A failed retry beside a second card for its intent keeps the retried card and drops the other. An empty level (the graph, or any group) keeps its canvas, with the hint "Ctrl+T for a new tab · drop a file or URL · F1 for shortcuts" centred in it; the "No tabs." screen and `tab_spawn_error` are gone. `gate failed-spawn` forces the failure (a copy of the exe with no engine host beside it) and checks that the failed Root card exists at startup, that a New spawn of the same intent refreshes it through the twin path (same id), and that Retry keeps it.

**Inline Json cards (read side 2026-10-25, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-25-properties-4a-inline-json.md`; editing 2026-10-25, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-25-properties-4b-inline-json-editing.md`)**: a Json card is inline when the document holds a `BODY` tree for it (or it is a Json card with neither `PATH` nor URL), file-backed when it has a `PATH`. The tree's `v` key holds the document: objects are `Key` children, scalars leaves, and an array is an id-keyed map whose elements each carry `o` (an order string; `between` mints one between two neighbours) and `v`; `store/body.rs` converts both ways (`tree_to_value_refs` also returns a pointer-to-`NodeId` map for `Ref` leaves), and a document past 10,000 leaves (`INLINE_MAX_LEAVES`) or 64 levels is refused. `JsonPayload` has a `source` (`File{path}` or `Inline{version}`) and `refs`, so an inline card has no path: no file items in its menu, no refetch, no "Open Containing Folder", and its title and crumb use the card's name. A remote `BODY` change rebuilds the payload and swaps the open level's value in place (`App::swap_json_value`). The graph menu's **New JSON** and the headless `NEWJSON` make an empty-object card; a file card's menu has **Detach to inline** (`DETACHJSON <id>`), which copies the value into `BODY` and clears the path and url (the file stays on disk).
