Editing: in an inline level a double-click (or F2) edits a value or key in place, Delete removes the selected row, and a right-click menu adds keys and elements, inserts above or below, moves up or down, deletes, and changes the type; every edit goes through one writer, `App::edit_json(JsonOp)`, and each is one undo step. Detach is one undo step too (undo restores the file card and its path; redo detaches again; either is skipped when the body changed since). A `Ref` leaf shows as a chip with the target card's title ("missing" when dangling); a click opens the target; there is no UI to create a Ref. **Export JSON...** in an inline card's menu (`EXPORTJSON <id> <path>`) writes the document to a file. Export and import carry `BODY` as a real JSON value under `"body"`, duplicate copies it with fresh element ids, and `set_body` writes only the leaves that changed. A card's scalar `PROPS` values (not the `labels` subtree) join its index text, cut at 4 KiB (`App::props_text`). Debug: `JSONEDIT`. `gate smoke`'s `inline-json` and `inline-json-edit` marks drive it.

**Links (2026-10-07, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-links-design.md`)**: any two cards at the same level can be linked, and a card's opener can be set by hand. A card's context menu has a "Links" section: "Opened from..." and "Link to..." (each shown only when a valid target exists at this level), then one "Disconnect from X (link | opened from it | opened from here)" per live edge of the card. Choosing a verb enters pick-target mode: valid targets get an accent ring, a click on one connects, and Esc, a left click anywhere else in the graph (empty canvas, a card that is not a target, the minimap), a graph or card menu action, a level change or leaving Graph view cancels; keyboard shortcuts leave it on. Not offered in a JSON level. A group can be linked and can be the card another was opened from, but has no opener itself (it is made, never opened, and stores no `parent`). A download still in progress is no end for either edge until it completes (it is not stored before then). A link is a stored node of type `application/vnd.manifold.link` with `from` and `to` (the card that started it is `from`), undirected in meaning, any number per card; it is never a card itself, syncs like any other node, and goes when either end is deleted. Links draw as dashed accent curves, bowed like opened-from lines (solid), between the facing side midpoints of the two cards (the side picked by the dominant axis of the centre-to-centre delta, horizontal on a tie), and never move cards (only `opened-from` feeds the layout's springs). Both edges go through one path, `App::connect` / `App::disconnect` (`links.rs`), checked against the edge table (`caps/edges.rs`: `storage`, `acyclic`, `layout` and the menu verb per row); the drag reparent uses it too. Lifecycle: closing a card deletes its links to cards outside the closed subtree (Recently Closed restores only links with both ends inside); duplicate, Import Group and Export Group keep a link only when both ends are inside; an exported session drops links whose end is not a live card. A link is shown only while both ends are at one level: moving one end into or out of a group (Move into, a Join drop, Group Selected, Remove from Group) keeps the link stored but hides it, and its Disconnect row, until both ends are at the same level again. Merging a group into another moves the merged-away group's links onto the group it joins, keeping their direction, as a tab that turns into content hands its links to the new node (`App::retarget_links`); a link that would join the group to itself, or to a card it is already linked to, is dropped. Gestures (2026-10-07, polished 2026-10-08, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-07-link-gestures-design.md`): a click on a link curve (within 6 screen points of it) deletes that link. The line under the pointer draws thicker, with a pointing-hand cursor. A card on top takes the click, a drag from empty canvas pans as before, and it is off in pick-target mode, during a card drag, with a menu or popup open and in a JSON level. Of two links on one pair, the greater id goes first, and a double- or triple-click deletes only one (its later clicks take nothing). Hovering a card shows a 28 by 16 point pill handle on each edge's midpoint, whether or not it has a valid link target (a drag from one with none just cancels). A handle is hidden on an edge under 60 screen points and on a simplified level. Dragging from a handle shows the handles on every card, draws a dashed bowed rubber band (ending on the target's facing handle when one is in reach) and rings the valid targets. Releasing on a card, or within 24 screen points of one (the nearest, never the source), links the two, releasing on a card that refuses the pair shows the usual refusal toast, releasing on empty canvas or the source card does nothing, and Esc cancels. A right-press during it (or during a card drag) opens no menu and starts no marquee. Dragging a link curve (2026-10-09) retargets one end: a press on the curve that moves past egui's drag threshold takes the end nearer where it was grabbed off its card, and that end follows the pointer as a connect-drag from the other end (same rings, snap, rubber band, Esc and release rules; the old curve is not drawn meanwhile, the camera does not pan). Released on a valid card, the end moves there in place (`App::retarget_link`: one field of the same Link node, so its id, other end and direction stay); on empty canvas, or on a card already linked to the card at the other end, the link is deleted (as a click on it would); on its old card, after Esc, or on the window losing focus, nothing changes; any other refused card (another level) shows the usual toast and leaves the link. While it runs the dragged band previews the release (`App::retarget_preview`, the same resolution the release uses): the usual accent for a retarget, a thin red band ending in an "x" where the link would be deleted, blue with a slashed circle where the drop is refused, and the card in reach is ringed in the same colour (a weak grey-gold on its own old card). A plain click still deletes. Neither gesture applies to opened-from lines. Not yet: directed or named links, links across levels. Debug: `LINK`, `UNLINK`, `OPENEDFROM`, and `EXPORTSTATE`'s `links`, `connecting`, `edges` (with `near_from` and `near_to`, the curve at t = 0.2 and 0.8), `link_handles`, `connect_drag`, `retarget` and `retarget_preview` (Headless mode); `scripts/link-probe.sh` checks a link end to end.

**Labels (2026-10-24, specs `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-properties-design.md`, `docs/decisions/2026-10-24-properties-tags-json.md`)**: a label is a stored node of type `application/vnd.manifold.label` with a name and a colour (`Kind::Label`, `labels.rs`); like a link it is mirror and store state and never a graph card (the mirror's stored-only registry: `add_stored`, `forget_stored`, `stored_ids`). A card carries a label as one entry in its `PROPS` tree, `labels/@<label id>` = `Int(added_at_ms)`, so labels order by when they were added (`Mirror::card_labels`), a second replica adding the same label converges to one entry, and an entry whose label was deleted shows nothing. Names are unique by `names::name_key`. A card's border takes its first label's colour (`CardView.color`) and its bottom edge shows up to two chips plus "+N" (`paint_label_chips`; no chips in a dense view, none on jars). The selection bar's Label button opens a menu of every label with a check for all, some or none of the selection (`graph_ui/label_menu.rs`); a row toggles it on the whole selection as one undo step (`GraphAction::ToggleLabel`), the name field makes a new one, and a row's pencil renames or recolours it; deleting a label is not undoable. Find: a label's name matches the Graph filter and the page-text index, and a `#word` in the omnibar selects the cards whose label starts with it. A suggestion can be accepted as a label (the tag button, `ACCEPTSUGGESTIONLABEL`) as well as a group. The old per-card `color` field is gone from every kind but jars and labels; a stored one is ignored. A whole-document export keeps them, and a group export carries the labels its cards use: Import Group reuses a live label with the same name (its colour wins) and creates the rest. Labels with the same name (two replicas each making one) read as one label, the lowest id, and edits, unlabel and delete apply to all of them. `gate labels` drives it end to end.

**`F2` keyboard-only group rename**: while viewing a group's own subgraph, `F2` starts renaming it -- the exact same effect as clicking its name button in the toolbar (`App::rename_current_group_shortcut` mirrors that click handler exactly: same `renaming_group`/`pending_focus_rename` fields, same focused pre-filled `TextEdit`, same Enter-commits/Escape-cancels logic already in place). A no-op at the top level (nothing to rename there) or while already renaming. New headless `RENAMEGROUP` debug command. Verified live: created an empty group, entered its subgraph, sent `RENAMEGROUP`, and confirmed the toolbar's "Group 1" button switched to a real focused `TextEdit` pre-filled with "Group 1" -- same verification depth the original click-to-rename feature used (the actual type-then-Enter commit isn't independently testable here either, since this project's headless protocol still has no way to synthesize keystrokes into a chrome `egui` widget).

**Filter box in History and Bookmarks popups**: both dropdowns get a `TextEdit` at the top, same case-insensitive substring match against title and url that the Graph view's own live filter already uses -- unlike that one, a non-matching entry is skipped entirely (hidden, not dimmed), since the alternative here would just be a shorter dropdown showing everything unfiltered underneath. Cleared each time the popup is opened (`bookmarks_filter`/`history_filter`, new `App` fields), so a stale filter from a previous session never silently hides entries the button's own count still promises. Bookmarks also gained the `ScrollArea` History already had, now that a filter makes a long list worth scrolling too. New `HISTORYFILTERTEXT`/`BOOKMARKSFILTERTEXT` debug commands direct-set the text, same reason `GraphFilterText` already exists (headless mode can't type into a chrome `TextEdit` via a real key event). Verified live: navigated to three different domains (three history entries) and bookmarked two of them, confirmed both popups listed everything unfiltered, then filtered history to "org" (3 entries down to 1, the matching url) and bookmarks to "net" (2 down to 1) -- the bookmarks case in particular confirmed the match runs against the url, not just the visible title, since both bookmarked pages happened to share the identical "Example Domain" title. **Simplified (later session pass):** the three near-identical `to_lowercase().contains()` checks this paragraph, the Graph filter, and the Bookmarks folder grouping each grew independently were factored into one shared `filter_matches(filter, haystacks)`; `graph_filter_dims` is now just its negation. Pure dedup, no behavior change -- re-verified live afterward (history filtered from 3 entries to 1 on "org", identical to before the refactor).

**Simplified (later session pass):** every dropdown/popup (History, Bookmarks, Downloads, Recently Closed, Tab actions, and the tab/group right-click context menus) reimplemented its own "close on click outside the popup" check as an identical `ui.input(|i| ...)` block -- nine copies across the file. Factored into one shared `clicked_outside_popup(ui, area_rect, anchor_rect)`; `anchor_rect` is an `Option` since the toolbar-button-opened popups also exclude their own anchor button from the "outside" check (so re-clicking the button doesn't both close and reopen it in the same click) while the right-click context menus, with no anchor button, pass `None`. Pure dedup, no behavior change.

**Simplified (later session pass):** Recently Closed, Bookmarks, History, and Downloads each hand-rolled the same `Area`/`Frame` shell around their own list -- pinned below the anchor button, identical fill/stroke/corner-radius/margin, identical 220-320pt width range. Factored into a free function `build_list_popup_frame(ctx, id, anchor_rect, min_width, max_width, add_contents)` (not a method -- an `&mut self` method here would conflict with `add_contents` closures that themselves need to capture `&mut self`, e.g. Bookmarks' own filter box and folder-rename draft); returns the drawn area's rect for the caller's own `clicked_outside_popup` check, same as before. Each of the four popups is now just its own list-rendering closure passed in. Left Tab Actions and the two right-click context menus alone -- different width policy (no max width) or position source (a stored click point, not an anchor rect), so folding them in would've added parameters rather than removed code. Pure dedup, no behavior change.

**"Save Now" button + last-saved timestamp**: a new row in the settings popup's Sessions section, above the existing named-save controls -- the auto-restore `session.json` is already written on every meaningful mutation (`App::save_session`, called from a dozen call sites), so this is a manual trigger plus visibility into that otherwise-invisible continuous save, not a new save mechanism. `save_session` changed from `&self` to `&mut self` to record `last_session_save: Option<Instant>`, shown as "Last saved Ns ago" ("Not saved yet this run" before the first). **Found and fixed live**: the button and label were first laid out in one `ui.horizontal`, which overflowed the settings popup's fixed 200pt width (`ui.set_max_width(200.0)`) -- `ui.horizontal` doesn't wrap, so "Save Now" plus "Last saved Ns ago" together were wider than the popup's own painted background, leaving the label's tail rendered directly over the graph card behind it with no frame fill under it. Fixed by stacking the button and label on separate lines instead (the same vertical layout every other row in this popup already uses). Verified live: screenshot after the fix showed both fully inside the popup's frame with no bleed-through, and clicking "Save Now" reset the label from "Last saved 5s ago" to "Last saved 1s ago".

**`Ctrl+Shift+G` new group shortcut**: same effect as "+ New Group" (an empty group at the top level), now also reachable without the mouse -- factored the button's own creation logic into `App::new_group_shortcut`, shared by the button, the shortcut, and a new headless `NEWGROUP` debug command. Checked ahead of plain `Ctrl+G` (the Tab/Graph view toggle) in `handle_global_shortcuts`, same ordering `Ctrl+Shift+T` already needs since `matches_logically` ignores an extra Shift. No-ops while viewing a group's own subgraph, matching the button's own top-level-only restriction. Verified live via `NEWGROUP`: a real "Group 1 / 0 tabs" card appeared at the top level.

**Ungroup All**: a new Graph-view toolbar button, top level only, shown whenever at least one group exists -- dissolves every group in one click, each through the same `dissolve_group` a single group's own "x"/context-menu Dissolve already uses (members return to the top level, never closed), rather than a bulk shortcut around it. Verified live: right-drag-selected two tabs, "Group Selected" to form "Group 1", confirmed "Ungroup All" appeared in the toolbar, clicked it, and confirmed both tabs reappeared as separate top-level cards with the button itself gone (no groups left).

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Tab color tagging**: individual tab cards now get the same swatch groups already had (`TAG_COLOR_PALETTE`/`next_tag_color` are shared between the two, renamed from the group-only names they started as) -- bottom-right corner, the one spot not already claimed by the pin dot, close "x", or (inside a group) the eject button. Clicking cycles through the same 6 colors and back to none. Unlike a group card (whose border had no other state to protect), a tab card's border is also how focus/hover show, so the tag color only shows through in the resting state -- a focused tab keeps its `ACCENT` border and a hovered one keeps `text_color()` regardless of any tag, so "this is the open tab" and "this is what you're pointing at" stay the two strongest, most unambiguous signals. The color also survives an engine swap or a crash-triggered respawn (threaded through `TabSpawnOutcome`/`respawn_tab` the same way `pinned` already was), and is saved in `session.json` (each node's `color`). Verified live: colored an unfocused tab's swatch red, confirmed the border stayed its plain default while the cursor was still resting on the swatch (hover taking precedence, as designed), then moved the pointer away and confirmed the border actually turned red.

**Mute from Graph view**: the audio-indicator dot (top-right corner) is now interactive and doubles as a mute toggle, sharing the real `SetMuted` send with Tab view's own "Muted" toolbar button (factored into `App::toggle_muted`). Previously it was a plain, non-interactive dot shown only while `audio_playing` was true (WebView2/WebKit only); it now also shows -- and stays clickable -- whenever the tab is muted, even after audio stops, turning red instead of green so mute state reads clearly without needing to switch to Tab view first. Verified live: muted a tab via Tab view's own button, confirmed the red dot appeared on its Graph view card, clicked the dot and confirmed it disappeared (mute cleared, since Servo/CEF tabs never set `audio_playing` either way).

**Download context menu**: right-clicking a download's card opens Open Containing Folder / Copy Path / Remove from List, consolidating what a plain click and the card's own close "x" already did with one new action (Copy Path). Right-click over the graph panel is otherwise the rectangle-select marquee's own button; a press landing on a download's card now opens this menu instead, same reasoning `group_context_menu` already has for group cards. Copy Path and Open Containing Folder share a new `windows_path_for_download` helper (factored out of what `reveal_download` already did) so a WebKit-sourced WSL2 path gets the same `\\wsl.localhost\...` translation either way. Verified live via a temporary `TESTADDDOWNLOAD` debug command (removed after testing -- no engine actually completes downloads in a way headless-testable here): right-click opened the menu, Copy Path closed it without error, and reopening + Remove from List deleted the node (confirmed via the minimap's own dot count dropping).

**Omnibar (`Ctrl+K`, and the bar in the Graph, Item and Settings views; `omnibar.rs`)**: one field and dropdown. Typed text lists, in order, open tabs across *every* level (title, url or page text, see Search), the other cards (notes, groups, downloads, images, PDFs and Json, named as a screen reader reads them: `Row::Other`), history, settings pages, commands ("Suggest groups", "Arrange by topic"), the typed text itself and the engine's search suggestions; the Item view's bar is the address bar and offers no commands. Arrow keys move the highlight, Enter acts on it, Escape closes. Jumping to a tab calls `focus_tab` directly (no node to animate from when the card is in another group); choosing any other card shows its level in the Graph, from every host, and reveals it (`reveal_in_graph`). The Graph's bar also dims, rather than hides, every card the text misses, keeping edges and layout.

**Named session save/load**: the settings popup gets a "Sessions" section alongside the existing auto-restore `session.json` -- a name field + "Save As" writes the current tab graph to `sessions/<name>.json` (same app-data folder, `session::Session::save_as`/`save_to` factored out of the existing `save`/`load`), and the list below it (one row per saved name, click to open, "x" to delete) replays a save the same way startup replays `session.json`. Opening one tears down every currently-open tab/download/group first -- each `TabNode`'s own `Drop` already handles process shutdown+kill, so clearing the maps is enough -- then calls the same `apply_session` startup's `restore_session` was refactored to share, so the two never drift into replaying a session differently. A name is sanitized to alphanumeric/space/hyphen/underscore before touching the filesystem, so typed input can't path-traverse or collide with the `.json` extension. Verified live: saved a 2-tab session as "twotabs", opened a 3rd tab, opened "twotabs" back and confirmed the graph returned to exactly 2 tabs (matching the minimap's dot count too); separately confirmed "x" removes a save from the list and its file.

**Mute Others**: mutes every other open tab (`App::mute_all_except`) without touching the focused tab's own mute state, and without unmuting anything already muted. A one-shot action, not a toggle. Originally its own always-visible Tab-view toolbar button; now a row in the "Tab actions" dropdown (see below) after a later session pass folded it in. Verified live: focused the middle of three tabs, clicked Mute Others, confirmed both other tabs' Graph-view audio dots turned red while the focused one stayed clear.

**Group thumbnails**: a non-empty group's card now shows a 2x2 grid of up to 4 members' real thumbnails (the same `GpuState::thumbnail_id` lookup a tab's own card already uses, an unloaded cell falling back to a plain tinted square rather than a hole) above a bottom name strip, instead of just plain centered "Group N"/"N tabs" text -- more than 4 members adds a small "+N" badge in the grid's corner. An empty group keeps the original plain centered layout, since there's nothing to preview. Verified live: grouped two tabs, confirmed the card showed a 2x2 grid with one real captured thumbnail in its cell (the other cell dark, since that tab had never been individually visible in Tab view to capture one) and "Group 1" in the bottom strip.

**"Collapse Groups"/"Expand Groups"**: a new Graph toolbar button, top level only, shown whenever at least one group exists -- toggles `groups_collapsed`, a purely visual flag that makes every non-empty group card fall back to the plain centered name+count layout an empty group already used, skipping the thumbnail grid above. A denser overview for a graph with many or large groups; doesn't touch `groups`/membership at all, and the empty-group branch's count label is now computed from `member_count` instead of a hardcoded `"0 tabs"`, since a collapsed non-empty group now reaches that same branch too. Session-only, resets on restart. Verified live: grouped two tabs (2x2 grid shown), clicked "Collapse Groups" and confirmed the card switched to plain "Group 1" / "2 tabs" text with the button now reading "Expand Groups", clicked it again and confirmed the grid came back.

**Icon buttons for the Graph toolbar's group actions (later session pass)**: "+ New Group", "Import Group...", "Ungroup All", and the "Collapse Groups"/"Expand Groups" toggle above all switched from text labels to `egui_phosphor` icons (`FOLDER_PLUS`, `UPLOAD_SIMPLE`, `FOLDER_MINUS`, `CORNERS_IN`/`CORNERS_OUT`) with the old label kept as an `on_hover_text` tooltip -- an alternative to folding them into a dropdown (the fix already applied to Tab view's "Mute Others"/"Close Others"/Pin toggle) after the Graph toolbar was flagged as cramped again following this session's own additions. `egui_phosphor` (new dependency, `Variant::Regular`, registered in `apply_fonts` right after IBM Plex Serif) rather than a Unicode symbol glyph in the existing font -- this project has repeatedly confirmed live that several such glyphs render as empty tofu boxes in IBM Plex Serif's own fallback chain (see the painter-drawn pin/mute/audio-indicator dots, built specifically to dodge that), and Phosphor's codepoints live in a private-use range no other font in the chain defines, so there's no coverage ambiguity for any icon actually used here. `+ New Tab`/`Reset View` were left as text -- not part of the crowding complaint, and used as coordinate anchors by unrelated existing tests. Verified live: screenshotted the toolbar with a group present and confirmed three distinct, crisp icons (no tofu boxes) in place of the old text buttons; clicked the new-group icon and confirmed a real "Group 1" card appeared; clicked the collapse icon and confirmed it swapped to the expand icon; clicked the ungroup icon and confirmed the group dissolved and both group-only icons disappeared along with it.

**Hand-rolled graph-node decorations migrated to `egui_phosphor` icons (later session pass)**: with the icon font already wired in for the Graph toolbar, four painter-drawn shapes/glyphs that predate it were reconsidered case by case rather than migrated wholesale -- the color swatch, engine-identity dot, and animated audio waveform stayed exactly as they were (a color swatch *is* the color it shows, not a pictogram; the engine dot is the same kind of color-coded legend; the waveform's whole point is the animation a static glyph can't do), but four others were genuinely improved by a real icon:
- **Pin toggle**: the filled-vs-outline dot (meant "pinned" only once you'd learned the convention) is now a `PUSH_PIN` icon, accent-colored when pinned and weak gray otherwise -- same two-color state encoding, a self-explanatory glyph instead of an abstract dot.
- **Group-subgraph member eject button**: the bare "^" caret (ambiguous -- read as easily as "expand") is now `SIGN_OUT`, a real door-with-arrow "step out" icon.
- **Static mute indicator** (the audio dot's own muted case, see above): a plain red dot is now a red `SPEAKER_X`.
- **Graph node close buttons** (tab/group/download, all three): the literal ASCII letter `"x"` is now the `X` icon glyph -- cosmetic polish, lowest-stakes of the four since a plain "x" was never actually ambiguous.

All four are drawn on the same untransformed `overlay_painter` layer the title-strip text already uses (crisp text at any zoom), same as before -- only the drawn string changed, not the layer or sizing. Verified live via cropped/upscaled screenshots of each: a real pin glyph in both accent-blue (pinned) and weak-gray (unpinned), a clean `X` glyph in the close-button square, a red speaker-with-slash for muted, and a door-with-arrow for the eject button -- no tofu boxes in any of the four.

**Remaining hand-rolled/raw-Unicode glyphs migrated too (same pass, on request)**: back/forward (`◀`/`▶` → `CARET_LEFT`/`CARET_RIGHT`), the settings gear (`⚙` → `GEAR`, both Tab- and Graph-view toolbars), the settings popup's zoom `−`/`+` buttons (→ `MINUS`/`PLUS`), and a download node's own file icon (`egui_phosphor::DOWNLOAD_SIMPLE`, replacing a raw `\u{2913}` arrow-to-bar glyph that had been quietly working despite its own comment's claim of avoiding exactly that gamble). The first three were already a proven-safe Unicode gamble, not a real bug, but converted anyway for visual consistency now that the icon font is already loaded everywhere else in this chrome. Verified live via cropped/upscaled screenshots: clean caret glyphs for back (enabled)/forward (disabled), a real gear icon, matching minus/plus glyphs next to "100%", and a download-arrow-into-tray icon on a download node added via a temporary `TESTADDDOWNLOAD` debug hook (removed after verifying, same disposable-hook pattern used elsewhere in this project) -- no tofu boxes in any of the four.

**Remaining text buttons/toggles migrated to icons (later session pass)**: a final sweep of every `ui.button`/`selectable_label`/`small_button` text label in this app's chrome. Four groups converted:
- **Tab-view toolbar**: "Reload" → `ARROW_CLOCKWISE`, "Home" → `HOUSE`, "Screenshot" → `CAMERA`, "Print to PDF" → `PRINTER` -- these were flagged earlier in this session as the strongest remaining crowding-relief candidates but not yet implemented; done now.
- **`Muted` toggle**: swaps `SPEAKER_HIGH`/`SPEAKER_X` by state, the same icon the graph-card audio indicator's own muted case already uses, so the toolbar toggle and the graph decoration read as one concept. `selectable_label`'s own selected-state styling (accent background, dark foreground) handles the icon's contrast automatically, same as the engine picker's own selected row.
- **`Bookmark` toggle**: `BOOKMARK_SIMPLE`, same selected-state contrast handling as `Muted`.
- **Cosmetic-only "x"/"X" cleanup**: the three `small_button("x")` remove-row buttons (bookmarks/downloads popups, settings' saved-sessions list) and the find bar's own literal `"X"` close button all now use the `X` icon -- purely visual consistency with the already-migrated graph-node close buttons, lowest-stakes change in this pass.

Deliberately left as text: `Graph`/`◀ Groups` (mode-switch controls where the label names *where* it goes, which an icon alone would lose), `+ New Tab` (already decided to keep, per an earlier pass, as a test coordinate anchor), `Tab actions` (a grab-bag dropdown trigger, not a single concept an icon states cleanly), `Reset View` (rare action, already clear, not crowded), find bar's `Prev`/`Next` (a much earlier live finding already confirmed `▲`/`▼` tofu-box in this app's own font; not reattempted with the icon font in this pass, out of its stated cosmetic-only scope), and every context-menu/settings-popup action row (Rename, Dissolve, Duplicate, Copy URL, Export/Import, Close, etc. -- vertical text lists are the right convention for an open-ended action set, same reasoning already established). Verified live: screenshotted the Tab-view toolbar and confirmed four new crisp icons (reload arrow, house, camera, unmuted speaker, bookmark ribbon) with no tofu boxes; toggled Muted and confirmed the icon swapped to a crossed-out speaker on an accent-blue background; toggled Bookmark and confirmed the same accent-blue contrast with a filled bookmark ribbon; opened the bookmarks popup and the find bar and confirmed both now show a clean `X` icon in place of their old literal letter.

**Graph view filter**: the Graph's omnibar text dims every card whose title, url, name or page text misses it (`App::node_matches`, `GRAPHFILTERTEXT` sets it headlessly); see Search.

**`Ctrl+G` Tab/Graph toggle**: switches to whichever view isn't currently showing. Tab -> Graph reuses `leave_to_graph`'s own shrink animation; Graph -> Tab jumps to `self.focused` directly (`focus_tab`, not the graph node's expand animation) and updates `current_group` to match it first, same reasoning the `Ctrl+K` quick-switcher's own jump already has -- `self.focused` isn't guaranteed to be a tab at the level currently being viewed, so there's no reliable on-screen node to animate from. Neutralized while the quick-switcher is open, same as its own Escape/arrow handling. Verified live: toggled Graph -> Tab -> Graph and confirmed each switch landed correctly.

**Export/Import session to an arbitrary file**: two more settings-popup buttons alongside the named-session store, using a real native file dialog (`rfd`, new dependency) instead of the app's own `sessions/` folder -- for sharing/backing up a session outside the app. Export writes `build_session_snapshot()` to wherever picked; Import reads it back through the same `replace_running_session` teardown-then-`apply_session` flow `load_named_session` was refactored to share, so all three loading paths (startup, named-open, file-import) replay a session identically. Hidden entirely in headless mode -- a real modal file dialog blocks on OS/human input neither exists there, so it would just hang forever; `EXPORTSESSION`/`IMPORTSESSION <path>` debug commands call the same underlying save/import directly instead, bypassing the dialog, same pattern every real-keyboard-shortcut debug command already uses. Verified live: exported a 2-tab session to a path, opened 3 fresh tabs, imported it back and confirmed the graph returned to exactly the 2 exported tabs.

**Import confirmation with a tab-count diff**: picking a file via "Import Session..." (or headless `IMPORTSESSION`) no longer replaces the running session immediately -- it stages the parsed file in a new `pending_import` field, and a small centered popup shows "Current session: N tabs" against "Importing: M tabs" with Import/Cancel buttons, so swapping in the wrong file is a deliberate second click rather than one silent dialog pick. Import applies it via the same `replace_running_session` `load_named_session` already uses; Cancel just discards the staged session, leaving the running one untouched -- `import_session_from` (the old immediate-apply path) is gone, folded into this. New `CONFIRMIMPORT`/`CANCELIMPORT` debug commands click the popup's own buttons, so the whole flow is headlessly testable end to end, not just the file-load half. Verified live: built a 3-tab session and exported it, started fresh with 1 tab, ran `IMPORTSESSION` and confirmed the popup read "Current session: 1 tab" / "Importing: 3 tabs"; separately confirmed `CANCELIMPORT` left the 1-tab session untouched, then re-ran the import and confirmed `CONFIRMIMPORT` correctly replaced it with all 3 tabs in their original shape.

**Graph zoom % indicator**: the toolbar shows the camera's own zoom as a percentage (`100%` = `GraphCamera::transform`'s scale of `1.0`, one world unit per screen point -- same "100% = no scaling" meaning Tab view's own zoom already uses), previously only visible/inferrable, not readable as a number. The toolbar is built before that frame's real transform is computed, so the label shows the *previous* frame's value (`App::graph_zoom_pct`, updated right after the transform itself is) -- one frame of lag, imperceptible at any real frame rate, and far simpler than reordering the whole panel/transform computation. Verified live: read "100%" at the default framing, scrolled to zoom in, and confirmed the readout updated to "116%".

**Graph view dot-grid background (visual polish pass)**: a faint dot grid now paints behind edges/nodes in Graph view, same reference-plane idea every node-graph editor (Blender, Figma, ComfyUI) uses so panning/zooming reads as moving through a real space instead of an empty void. Spacing is fixed in *world* units (40, doubled/halved to keep on-screen spacing within a 20-80px band regardless of zoom) rather than screen units, so dots visibly spread apart zooming in and pack together zooming out -- the intended feel. The adaptive spacing step exists specifically to avoid a real perf cliff: a naive fixed 40-world-unit grid would need roughly 57,000 `circle_filled` calls a frame at `GRAPH_ZOOM_MIN` (0.15x) on a full-HD window, since the visible world rect grows as zoom shrinks; the 20-80px floor/ceiling keeps the per-frame dot count in the low thousands at any zoom level instead. Reads the *previous* frame's visible-world-rect (`App::graph_scene_rect`) rather than this frame's local `scene_rect`, since that one is already mutably borrowed for the whole `Scene::show` call -- one frame of lag, same as the zoom-% readout above.

**Curved graph edges (visual polish pass)**: parent-child edges (tab-to-tab, tab-to-download) are now a gentle quadratic-bezier bow instead of a straight `line_segment`, factored into a shared `draw_graph_edge(painter, from, to, stroke)` (now `link_gestures::paint_bow`) both edge-drawing loops call. Deliberately *not* the horizontal S-curve most node editors (React Flow, Unreal Blueprints) use -- those assume a fixed left-to-right port layout (output always on the right, input always on the left), which doesn't apply here since this graph's nodes are free-positioned by the physics simulation in any direction relative to each other. Instead the curve just bows perpendicular to the straight line between the two points, which looks right regardless of edge direction; bow amount is proportional to edge length (capped at 30 world units) so short edges between adjacent nodes don't over-bow into a visible loop. Uses `egui::epaint::QuadraticBezierShape` directly via `painter.add(...)` (egui's `Painter` has no built-in curve convenience method, unlike `line_segment`).

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Card glow (visual polish pass)**: both tab and group cards in Graph view now paint a soft colored glow behind themselves, tinted the card's own border/tag color, via a shared `draw_card_glow(painter, rect, base_corner_radius, color, inv_zoom)` -- two layered rects grown outward with decreasing alpha, no directional offset (radiates evenly, doesn't imply a light source). Went through several iterations live: a plain black drop-shadow (the first attempt, meant to give group cards visible depth against plain tab cards) turned out invisible against this app's near-black `BG` -- nothing darker to contrast against. Switched to a glow tinted the card's own border/tag color instead, which reads clearly there and doubles as an echo of the existing color-coding. Strength went through a few live rounds too (too subtle at first, then a stronger pass, then a user-picked midpoint between the two) before settling at `(85, 3.5)`/`(38, 8.5)` (alpha, world-unit growth) for both layers. Originally group-only "to visually separate from plain tab cards," but extended to tab cards too on request once the glow itself looked good -- an untagged resting tab's border falls back to a dim weak-text gray, so its glow is a faint gray halo rather than colorful; only tagged/focused/hovered tabs (and any group) get a vivid one. **Animated on request**: `draw_card_glow` gained an `intensity` multiplier (1.0 normal, up to 1.8 on hover) scaling the alpha only, not the spread -- boosting an existing glow rather than resizing the card itself, which would misalign it against its own thumbnail/label content drawn at the un-hovered rect. The hover transition itself eases via `ctx.animate_bool_responsive` (keyed per-node, fast-start-then-settle) instead of snapping instantly between the two states. **Extended to the save toast (later pass)**: the toast's own actual glow-drawing logic was split out into `card_glow_shapes(rect, base_corner_radius, color, inv_zoom, intensity) -> [Shape; 2]` (`draw_card_glow` is now a thin `painter.add(...)` loop over it) since the toast's rect isn't known until *after* its `Frame` lays itself out around the "Saved <path>" label -- unlike a graph card, whose rect is fixed upfront from `GRAPH_NODE_SIZE`/`GROUP_NODE_SIZE`. `Painter::add` only ever appends, so painting the glow "behind" content whose size isn't known yet needs the standard egui workaround: reserve two `Shape::Noop` placeholder slots *before* the `Frame`, then `Painter::set` them to the real shapes once `response.rect` exists. `ACCENT`-tinted (no border color of its own to echo, unlike a tab/group card), `inv_zoom`/`intensity` both `1.0` (screen-space chrome, no zoom scaling; no hover state to boost).

**Toolbar icon-button hover easing (later pass)**: a handful of toolbar icon buttons (Reload, Home, Screenshot, Print-to-PDF, the Settings gear, the Keyboard-Shortcuts question mark, the Settings page's own Back button) now ease their hover fill/text color via a new `icon_button_eased(ui, id, label)`, instead of snapping instantly the way every plain `ui.button` in this app still does -- egui's stock `Button` has no built-in color transition of its own, same root cause the graph cards needed their own `hover_t` easing for earlier. Deliberately scoped to just these -- easing *every* button app-wide would mean wrapping or replacing dozens of call sites across popups/context menus for a subtler payoff (unlike a graph card's snap, which was genuinely jarring against the new glow/border easing, a plain button's instant hover color is closer to how most native desktop widgets already behave). One frame of hover lag: a button's fill has to be picked before `ui.add` reveals whether the pointer is actually over its not-yet-laid-out rect, so this frame's color comes from *last* frame's hover state, stashed in `ctx.memory` per button id -- the same "acceptable one-frame lag" the zoom-% readout and the dot-grid background already accept elsewhere. **Widened (toolbar UX audit)**: three Graph-toolbar icon buttons (Close Split, Import Group, Ungroup All, Collapse/Expand Groups) had been left on plain `ui.button` despite being icon-only like the rest of this list -- an oversight, not a deliberate exception, since the stated rule above is about icon-only buttons specifically, not a curated list. All icon-only toolbar buttons now use `icon_button_eased`; text buttons ("+ New Tab", "Reset View", "Tab actions", etc.) still don't, per the same reasoning above.

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Card border/fill easing (later pass)**: the glow's hover transition eased, but the card's own fill brightness and border width/color still snapped instantly -- a half-animated feel. Both now interpolate off the exact same `hover_t` the glow already computes (one hover transition driving all three, not separately-timed ones): fill brightness `1.0 + hover_t * 0.35` (was a hard `1.35` jump), border width `1.0 + hover_t * 0.5` world units. Tab cards also ease the border *color* between its resting tint (tag color, or weak gray) and the hovered one (`text_color()`), via a small new `lerp_color32` helper (`egui`/`epaint` has no built-in `Color32::lerp`) -- group cards don't need this since their border color doesn't change on hover, only its width does. The focused-tab override (`ACCENT`, width `2.5`) stays a hard cut regardless of `hover_t`, per the existing "strongest, least ambiguous signal" reasoning for that state.

**More transitions/easing, on request (later pass)**: three more places that used to snap instantly now fade.
- **Toolbar dropdown popups** (Bookmarks, History, Downloads, Tab Actions, and Graph view's Recently Closed) fade in *and* out via `ui.set_opacity` inside each popup's own `Area`, driven by `ctx.animate_bool` on that popup's own `_open` flag -- `build_list_popup_frame` (the shared shell four of the five already use) and `build_tab_actions_popup` both gained a `fade: f32` parameter for this. The `animate_bool` call at each site is made *every frame regardless of open state*, not only while open -- egui's animation manager tracks real elapsed wall-clock time between polls, so calling it only while a popup happens to be open leaves a huge untracked gap while it's closed, and the next open jumps straight to fully visible in one frame instead of visibly easing (confirmed this the hard way before fixing it). Polling continuously works safely here because the underlying data (bookmarks/history/downloads lists, the toolbar buttons themselves) all outlive the `_open` flag going false, so drawing can keep running correctly through the fade-out, not just the fade-in. Context menus and the quick-switcher were deliberately left alone at the time -- their state is an `Option<...>` cleared the instant they close, not a persistent bool-plus-data pair, so there's nothing left to keep drawing during a fade-out without a larger restructure. **The four right-click context menus got that restructure in a later pass**: a new `fade_popup_state(ctx, id, current, cache)` takes a popup's own `Option<T>` state plus a sibling `cache: &mut Option<T>` field, polls `ctx.animate_bool` on `current.is_some()` every frame the same "continuous, not just while open" way the toolbar dropdowns' own fade needs, and returns `current`'s content while `Some` or the frozen `cache` otherwise -- `None` only once the fade has actually finished. `context_menu`/`group_context_menu`/`download_context_menu`/`tab_context_menu` each gained a `_cache` sibling field for this and now fade in/out through it. **The quick-switcher got its own fade in a later pass too**: same `fade_popup_state`/`quick_switcher_cache` shape as the context menus, but with a real `is_live` branch in `build_quick_switcher` instead of rendering the cache identically to the live state -- editing a `TextEdit` or clicking a row needs `&mut self.quick_switcher`, which no longer exists once it's actually `None`. While closing, the search box becomes a plain frozen label (the last-typed query, or the hint text if it was empty) and each match row becomes a disabled `egui::Button::selectable(...)` -- visually identical to its live counterpart, just non-editable and non-clickable, so a click on a row already fading out can't silently jump anywhere. Arrow-key/Enter/Escape handling is also gated on `is_live`, since there's no live switcher left to move a selection in or confirm.
- **The "Saved &lt;path&gt;" toast** fades in over its first 150ms and out over its last 400ms of `SAVE_TOAST_DURATION` (4s), instead of popping on and cutting off instantly. Driven directly off `shown_at.elapsed()` rather than `ctx.animate_bool` -- unlike the popups above, it already has a real wall-clock lifetime to derive opacity from, no animation-manager polling-continuity concerns apply. This app's winit loop runs `ControlFlow::Poll` (confirmed by reading `main()`), so it's already redrawing every frame regardless of input -- a time-based fade animates smoothly with no extra `request_repaint` needed.
- **The graph hover-peek preview** (the enlarged thumbnail floating beside a hovered node) fades in and out the same "poll every frame regardless" way the popups do, via a single `ctx.animate_bool(Id::new("peek_preview_fade"), peek_target.is_some())` call placed where the existing `peek_suppressed` bookkeeping already runs unconditionally each frame. Unlike a toolbar popup, this is painted directly on the Scene's own world-space painter rather than a separate `egui::Area` (so it pans/zooms with its node) -- `ui.set_opacity` still applies correctly since the peek preview is the last thing drawn in that closure each frame.

*(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Tab node context menu**: right-clicking a tab's card opens Pin/Unpin, the same 6-color swatch row groups already have, Remove from Group (only while viewing a group's own subgraph, same scope as the card's existing hover-revealed eject button), and Close -- consolidating the pin dot/color swatch/eject button/close "x" scattered across the card's own corners into one menu, same pattern `group_context_menu`/`download_context_menu` already established. The right-click hit-test chain (group, then download, then tab) picks whichever specific card was hit; missing all three still starts the rectangle-select marquee as before. Verified live: opened the menu on an unfocused tab, picked a color swatch and watched the border tint immediately, reopened and confirmed that swatch showed selected, then picked Close and confirmed the tab closed through the normal path ("Recently Closed (1)" appeared in the toolbar).

**Bookmarks**: a flat `(title, url)` list in a new `bookmarks.rs` module, persisted to `bookmarks.json` (same missing/corrupt-file-means-empty posture as `settings.rs`/`session.rs`). Tab view's toolbar gets a "Bookmark" toggle (`selectable_label`, pressed look when the focused tab's url is already saved -- same reasoning `Muted`'s own toggle already uses to avoid a star glyph this app's font might not have) and a "Bookmarks (N)" dropdown listing every saved one, click a title to navigate the focused tab there, "x" to remove. No folders/tags in this first cut -- a flat list is small enough that organizing it further hasn't been worth the extra state yet. Verified live: bookmarked a tab, confirmed the toggle went pressed and "Bookmarks (1)" appeared, opened the dropdown and confirmed the entry, removed it via "x" and confirmed the file emptied and the toggle/dropdown both reverted.

**Bookmark folders (single level)**: `Bookmark` gains an optional `folder: String` (`#[serde(default)]`, so an older `bookmarks.json` with no such field loads fine as unfoldered). The dropdown now groups entries by folder -- unfoldered first with no header, then each named folder alphabetically with a bold header -- still a single level, not a real tree, same scope limit the field's own doc comment states. Each row shows its folder (or "No folder") as a small weak label underneath; clicking it opens a real focused `TextEdit` in its place (same `renaming_group`-style draft/commit/cancel shape the group-rename feature already has, keyed by the bookmark's own url rather than an index so it can't end up pointing at the wrong row), Enter/blur commits (blank clears the folder back to unfoldered), Escape cancels. New `BOOKMARKFOLDERTEXT` debug command direct-sets the draft, same reason `GraphFilterText` exists. Verified live: bookmarked two tabs (both unfoldered), renamed one's folder to "Work", and confirmed the dropdown re-grouped into an unfoldered entry followed by a "Work" header and the renamed entry underneath, both with correct per-row folder labels -- confirmed via a completely fresh app restart reloading the saved file, after an earlier same-session screenshot of the same state showed the second label missing due to what looks like egui's own floating-`Area` auto-size lagging one frame behind when a nested widget (the `TextEdit`) is replaced by a plain `Label` of different height; a subsequent frame always showed it correctly, and the persisted JSON was correct throughout. **Found, not fixed**: while narrowing this down, one run hit `thread 'main' panicked at ... epaint/src/textures.rs:337: Dropped TexturesDelta with 1 unapplied deltas` immediately after a `SCREENSHOT` following a `MOUSEMOVE`, right after the popup had just rendered "Work" for the first time (new glyphs, so a real texture-atlas delta existed that frame). Not reproduced on two immediate retries of the same sequence, so likely a rare shutdown-timing race in the GPU render/present path rather than anything specific to bookmarks -- flagged here rather than chased further, since a minimal repro wasn't found and root-causing egui/wgpu's texture-delta lifecycle around app exit is a much larger change than this feature warrants. **Seen again** during a later icon-migration pass, same signature, again right after a burst of debug commands introduced fresh glyphs (a set of new toolbar icons rendering for the first time) -- still not reliably reproducible (an identical retry of the exact same sequence ran clean immediately after), so the "new-glyph-texture-delta plus a rapid command burst" correlation looks real across both occurrences, but a deterministic minimal repro remains elusive. **Root-caused and fixed**: `GpuState::render` acquired the surface texture (`self.surface.get_current_texture()`) *before* consuming `egui_output`'s `FullOutput`; its early-return branch for a non-`Success`/`Suboptimal` surface status (Timeout/Occluded/Outdated/Lost/Validation -- all transient, common around resize/occlusion/shutdown) returned immediately, dropping the still-owned `egui_output` parameter without ever applying or clearing its `textures_delta`. `TexturesDelta` panics on `Drop` if any `.set`/`.free` entry went unconsumed, which is exactly the observed panic -- and it only fired on frames where `ctx.run()` had just produced a real delta (new glyphs), matching both occurrences' "new-glyph-texture plus rapid burst" correlation (a rapid burst makes hitting a transient bad surface status far more likely). Fixed by moving the texture-delta apply/free/clear step to run unconditionally on `egui_output` *before* the surface-acquire check, so the delta is always fully consumed regardless of whether that frame can be presented -- the atlas sync itself never needed a valid surface, only the actual draw/present did. Verified live: rebuilt, then ran a 20-cycle headless burst (`NEWTAB`/`SCREENSHOT`/`MOUSEMOVE`/`ZOOMIN`/`ZOOMRESET` back-to-back, each cycle forcing fresh glyph textures) under `RUST_BACKTRACE=1`, exited clean via `QUIT` with no panic.

**Print to PDF**: a new Tab-view toolbar button, hand-writing a minimal single-page PDF (new `pdf.rs`, no PDF-generation crate) that embeds the same full composited screenshot the "Screenshot" button already captures, JPEG-encoded (the `image` crate's existing dependency gains its `jpeg` feature) and wrapped in a `DCTDecode` image XObject on a US-Letter page, scaled to fit and centered. Same known limitation class as this project's thumbnails/session-restore: a rasterized visual snapshot of the whole window (chrome included), not a real paginated/text-reflowable print output or page-content-only capture. Routes through the exact same `pending_screenshot` GPU-capture path a plain screenshot uses (a temp PNG, converted to the target PDF and deleted once that capture completes) rather than teaching `gpu::GpuState` anything about PDF. Hidden in headless mode like Export/Import Session (a real file dialog blocks on OS/human input neither exists there); a `PRINTTOPDF <path>` debug command bypasses the picker directly. Verified live: printed a tab to PDF and confirmed the resulting file opens as a valid single-page PDF showing the full window, correctly scaled and centered on the page.

**`Ctrl+P` pin shortcut**: pins/unpins the selected cards, or the focused one with no selection, same effect as its graph card's own pin dot or the tab context menu's Pin/Unpin entry -- previously pinning needed a mouse click on one of those. Verified live: toggled it and confirmed the focused tab's pin dot switched from outline to filled. **Superseded 2026-10-23:** a card no longer has a pin control (only a passive pin mark while pinned); Pin/Unpin for the selection is the selection bar's button, which uses the same rule.

**Browsing history**: a Tab-view toolbar "History (N)" dropdown listing every distinct `(title, url)` this session has navigated to, most-recent-first, click one to navigate the focused tab there, "Clear" to empty it. Session-only, capped at `MAX_HISTORY_ENTRIES` (200) -- unlike bookmarks, nothing here is ever written to disk. Recorded in two places: `navigate_tab` (address-bar-driven navigation, title unknown yet so the entry shows its bare url until superseded) and `EngineToHost::NavigationStateChanged`'s own handling (in-page link clicks, which `navigate_tab` never sees). Found live while testing: `navigate_tab` sets `node.url` synchronously before the engine's own confirmation round-trips back, so `NavigationStateChanged`'s `node.url != url` check was already false by the time its event arrived -- silently missing every address-bar navigation until history was also recorded at the `navigate_tab` call site itself. Verified live: navigated once (recorded with a real title from the initial load), navigated again via the address bar (recorded as a bare url), confirmed "History (2)" and both entries listed, then confirmed Clear empties it and removes the button.

**Duplicate Tab**: a new "Duplicate" entry in the tab context menu (right-click a tab's card) -- spawns a new tab at the same url and engine, sharing the original's parent and group, a sibling rather than a child of the tab it duplicated. Reuses the existing `spawn_new_tab_with_kind`. Verified live: right-clicked a tab, picked Duplicate, confirmed a new tab appeared (minimap's dot count went from 1 to 2) showing the same page content.

**`Ctrl+D` duplicate-tab shortcut**: keyboard parity for the context menu's own "Duplicate" entry, applied to the focused tab -- `App::duplicate_focused_tab_shortcut` is a one-line wrapper calling the exact same `duplicate_tab` the menu entry already uses. New headless `DUPLICATETAB` debug command. Verified live: one tab open, sent `DUPLICATETAB`, and confirmed a second node appeared showing the same page content, same as picking Duplicate from the context menu.

**Close Others**: closes every open tab except the focused one, each through the normal `close_tab` path (teardown, reparenting, the `closed_tabs` stack all still apply), not a bulk shortcut around it. Originally its own always-visible Tab-view toolbar button next to "Mute Others"; now also a row in the "Tab actions" dropdown. Verified live: three tabs open, clicked Close Others on the middle one, confirmed exactly one tab remained and "Recently Closed (2)" reflected both others closing normally.

**Copy URL**: a new entry in the tab context menu, same `ui.ctx().copy_text` mechanism the download context menu's own Copy Path already uses. Verified live: opened the menu, confirmed the entry, clicked it and confirmed the menu closed cleanly with no error and the tab itself untouched.

**Reload (`Ctrl+R`/`F5`)**: a new `HostToEngine::Reload` implemented across all four engine-hosts (`WebView::reload`/`Browser::reload`/`WebViewExt::reload`/`ICoreWebView2::Reload` -- each engine's embedding API already exposes a plain no-argument reload, unlike `GoBack`/`GoForward`'s can-go-back/forward guard) plus a new Tab-view toolbar button next to the back/forward arrows. This was a genuine gap: no reload action or IPC message existed at all before this. Verified live on Servo: reloaded the focused tab via the headless `RELOAD` command, confirmed no crash and the page came back correctly, and confirmed history's count didn't grow (same url, no new entry). CEF/WebKit/WebView2 use the same verified no-arg reload API via a trivial mirror of the already-proven `GoBack`/`GoForward` pattern in each -- not independently live-tested (WebKit is WSL2-only, per this project's standing constraint; CEF isn't the default test engine here).

**Downloads list popup**: a Tab-view toolbar "Downloads (N)" dropdown listing every entry in `self.downloads` -- the same data the graph view's own download nodes/context menu already show -- so a completed download can be found without first switching to Graph view. Click a filename to open its containing folder (`reveal_download`), "x" to remove it (`remove_download_node`); both reused directly, no new download-handling logic. Verified live via a temporary `TESTADDDOWNLOAD` debug command (removed after testing, same as the download context menu's own verification earlier): "Downloads (1)" appeared, the dropdown listed "report.pdf", and "x" removed it, correctly dropping the button entirely.

**Live download progress (byte-level, real native APIs)**: a download's own graph node/popup row used to only appear once the file was already fully saved (`DownloadNode`'s own doc comment used to call it "a **completed** download" -- there was no in-progress representation at all, checked while scoping this feature and confirmed the only IPC message that existed anywhere was `DownloadCompleted`). New `EngineToHost::DownloadStarted { path, total_bytes }` / `DownloadProgress { path, bytes_received }` messages, sent from all three engines that have a real download API. `DownloadNode` now gets created at `DownloadStarted` (not `DownloadCompleted`), gains `bytes_received`/`total_bytes`/`completed` fields, and is found-and-updated by `path` (the stable key across all three messages -- final by contract at `Started` time, since every engine has already decided the save location before that fires) on `Progress`/`Completed` rather than only ever constructed once at the end. Handled inline in the per-message match arm, not deferred the way `frame_updates`/`crashes`/etc. are -- a fast download can send `Started`, several `Progress`s, and `Completed` all within the same `try_recv` drain, and deferring node *creation* specifically would mean same-batch `Progress`/`Completed` finding no node yet to update.
- **UI**: the Downloads popup shows a live `egui::ProgressBar` (with a "X / Y" byte readout) for an in-progress download with a known total, or a plain "Downloading… X" byte count with no bar for an unknown-total (chunked-transfer) one -- a bar stuck at a guessed fraction would be dishonest. The graph view's own download-node card gets a matching thin progress bar (known-total only) drawn between the icon and filename, same `ACCENT`-filled-over-track-color idea, in world-space alongside the card's own fill/border rather than through `overlay_painter` (that one's screen-space, reserved for text to avoid blur at non-100% zoom).
- **Servo has no download support of any kind** (not new -- confirmed while scoping this: zero download-related code anywhere in `manifold-view-servo`, and its own embedding API has no download hook at all, per `EngineToHost::DownloadCompleted`'s pre-existing doc comment). Progress is therefore only ever reported by WebView2/CEF/WebKit.
- **WebView2**: `ICoreWebView2DownloadOperation`'s `BytesReceivedChanged` event (`webview2_com::BytesReceivedChangedEventHandler`, which already existed in the crate unused) alongside the existing `StateChanged`; `total_bytes` from `TotalBytesToReceive` (negative means unknown). `path` is read once, from `DownloadStartingEventArgs::ResultFilePath` at the moment the download starts, and reused as the stable key for every later event on it rather than re-read from the operation each time. Build-verified.
- **CEF**: `on_download_updated` already fired repeatedly over a download's whole lifetime (not just once at completion) -- previously only acted on `is_complete()`. Now also emits exactly one `Started` per download id (a new `started: HashSet<u32>` dedupes it, alongside the existing `reported` completion-dedupe) plus a `Progress` on every other call, from `DownloadItem::total_bytes()`/`received_bytes()`. Build-verified.
- **WebKit**: `decide-destination` (already the point `set_destination` is called from) is also where `total_bytes` is read, from the response's `Content-Length` (`WebKitURIResponse::content_length`, 0 = unknown) -- and where `path` is computed directly from the just-built `dest`, rather than round-tripped through `destination()` the way the progress/finished signals below have to (they don't have `dest` in their own closure's scope). A new `connect_received_data` handler reports `WebKitDownload::received_data_length()` (WebKit's own running cumulative total -- no manual accumulation from the signal's per-chunk delta parameter needed) as `Progress` on every chunk. **Correction, build-verified after all**: first written up as "not build-verified" here, on the assumption (from a `pkg-config webkit2gtk-4.0` probe run directly, not from an actual `cargo build`) that this WSL2 environment was missing the dev headers `manifold-view-webkitgtk`'s `Cargo.toml` needed. That probe checked the wrong package name -- `webkit2gtk-sys` 2.0.2's own `Cargo.toml.orig` only ever declares a `system-deps` entry for `webkit2gtk-4.1` (no `4.0` variant exists in this crate version at all), which this environment already has installed. Running the real `cargo build -p manifold-view-webkitgtk` inside WSL2 (not attempted the first time around) compiles clean with zero warnings -- no environment gap, no `Cargo.toml` change needed. All four engines' code is therefore build-verified.

**Home button**: a new Tab-view toolbar button next to Reload -- navigates the focused tab to `self.settings.home_page` via the existing `navigate_tab`, no new IPC message needed (unlike Reload, this is address-bar-driven navigation the engine already supports). Verified live on Servo: navigated a tab to `https://example.org/` (confirmed via the address-bar text), clicked Home (headless `CLICK` at the button's screen position), and confirmed the address bar read back the configured home page, `https://example.com/`.

**Graph minimap**: a small fixed overview in the graph panel's bottom-right corner, screen-space (drawn on the same untransformed overlay layer as node labels, so it's unaffected by Scene's own pan/zoom) -- every current-level tab/download/group as a dot, and the main view's current viewport as an outlined rect, both against the same world bounds `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect()` already uses for "Reset View" and entering/leaving a group, so the minimap and the main view always agree on "the whole graph." Clicking anywhere in it recenters the main viewport there (same zoom, just panned). Reads pointer state directly off `ui.input` rather than through a normal `ui.interact()`/`Response` click: confirmed live via temporary logging that Scene's own background pan sense (allocated over the same `scene_outer_rect` the minimap sits inside) wins egui's hit-testing for any later widget added there, so a `Response`-based click on the minimap never actually saw the pointer at all -- the same fix the rectangle-select marquee already needed for an identical reason. Verified live: zoomed into part of the graph, clicked a different spot in the minimap, and watched the main view actually pan there.

- **Tab view**: today's chrome for whichever tab is focused — address bar (type a URL, press Enter), back/forward, an engine-picker dropdown (swaps the tab live between Servo/CEF/WebKit/WebView2), favicon next to the title. The "Graph" button (or Escape) returns to the graph.

A gear icon (in either view's toolbar) opens **Settings** — default search engine (DuckDuckGo/Google/Bing; only where a search actually goes, suggestions always come from DuckDuckGo's own endpoint regardless), default starting engine for new tabs, zoom (a linear multiplier, applies to new tabs and immediately to whichever tab is currently focused — not retroactively to other already-open tabs), and home page. Every control saves immediately on change (no separate "Save" step) to `%APPDATA%\manifold\settings.json`; a missing file falls back to defaults. A value this build can't read (an unknown engine name, a zoom that isn't a number) resets only that setting, with a log line, and a zoom outside 50–200% loads clamped into it. A file that can't be read at all (not JSON, or another field of the wrong type) is moved aside as `settings.unreadable-<secs>.json` with a toast, and defaults load rather than failing to start. Zoom is implemented on all four engines: Servo (`WebView::set_page_zoom`, a direct linear factor) and CEF (`BrowserHost::set_zoom_level`, log-scale internally — converted via `log_1.2(factor)` so the same setting means the same visual size everywhere) were added along with this settings feature and verified live (visibly larger text at 130%, both engines); WebKit's `set_zoom_level` handler already existed from earlier work and receives the same message the same way — not independently re-verified live, but nothing about it should differ. WebView2 (`ICoreWebView2Controller::SetZoomFactor`, also a direct linear factor) verified live at 150%.

Typing into the address bar shows an autocomplete dropdown below it: matching open tabs first, then DuckDuckGo search-query suggestions (debounced, fetched from DuckDuckGo's own `/ac/` autocomplete endpoint). Click a row to accept it — clicking an open-tab row focuses that tab, clicking a search row navigates to that search. Up/Down move a highlight through the rows (wrapping at both ends); Enter accepts whichever row is highlighted, or falls back to navigating the typed text directly if none is. Non-URL input (no scheme, no dot, or contains a space) is treated as a search query on plain Enter too, resolved the same way as the dropdown's own suggestions. Escape dismisses the dropdown without leaving Tab view; a second Escape (or with no dropdown showing) returns to Graph view as usual.

Keyboard shortcuts work in either view: `Ctrl+T` opens a new tab (same as "+ New Tab" — a child of whichever tab is focused, staying put rather than auto-focusing), `Ctrl+Shift+T` reopens the newest node this replica closed (a tab, note, file card or whole group): the same node, with its links and group, where it was, `Ctrl+W` closes the focused tab, `Ctrl+L` focuses the address bar (Tab view only), `Ctrl+F` opens the find-in-page bar (Tab view only), `Ctrl+=`/`Ctrl++`/`Ctrl+-`/`Ctrl+0` zoom in/out/reset (same step, cap, and floor as the settings popup's own +/−/Reset buttons — confirmed live: five `Ctrl++`s took `zoom_factor` from 1.0 to exactly 1.5, visibly larger text), `F12` opens the focused tab's engine's own native DevTools, and `Ctrl+Tab`/`Ctrl+Shift+Tab` cycle focus through open tabs in the order they were opened (this graph-based UI has no tab strip to define a more meaningful order). All are checked via egui's own input polling, consuming the key so it never leaks through to a focused tab's page. **Fixed (now a real stack):** `Ctrl+Shift+T`/`REOPENTAB` used to only remember the single most recently closed tab, overwriting that one slot on every subsequent close — closing two tabs in a row made the first one unreachable. `App::closed_tabs` is now a `Vec<ClosedTab>` capped at 10 (oldest dropped first); `Ctrl+Shift+T` still just pops the last entry, unchanged for the common case. A "Recently Closed (N)" button now also appears in the Graph toolbar once anything's been closed, opening a dropdown of every remembered tab (most recent first, with its engine's color dot) — click one to respawn *that specific* tab and remove just it from the list, rather than being limited to only ever reopening in strict last-in-first-out order. Verified live: closed two different tabs (dropdown correctly read "Recently Closed (2)" and listed both URLs), clicked the top entry, and got that exact tab back with the count dropping to "1". **Superseded 2026-09-25:** the three lists became one; see the second refactor survey, item 2.

`F12`'s DevTools is a real, separate OS window the engine manages itself (not part of the tab's own captured/composited content) — confirmed live on CEF and WebView2, each popping up its own real "DevTools - <url>" window. WebKitGTK has the matching API (`WebInspector::show()`) but it's deliberately left as a no-op: this project's WebKit engine-host runs inside an `xvfb-run`-launched X session with no window manager, and a real top-level window shown with nothing to map it pegged the process at ~45% CPU indefinitely in live testing (confirmed: never returned to servicing IPC, had to be force-killed) rather than failing visibly — not safe to ship without adding a window manager to that Xvfb session, a bigger change than this warranted. Servo's embedding API has no DevTools/inspector method at all. On WebKit and Servo `F12` (and the Tab actions row) shows a toast naming the engine ("WebKit has no DevTools") instead of doing nothing (`Tag::DevTools`).

Right-clicking a link on any tab shows a minimal one-item context menu — "Open in new tab" — at the click position; a right-click that doesn't land on a link shows nothing. WebView2/WebKit query the same way `input.rs`'s own left-click handling already does (`elementFromPoint`/`run_javascript`, walking up to the nearest `<a href>` ancestor). Building this surfaced a real, generically-applicable bug: `egui_winit` reports a right mouse button press as "consumed" unconditionally, even over this project's own raw content quad (which isn't a real egui widget and has nothing there to legitimately claim the event) — confirmed live via temporary logging, and worked around by bypassing `consumed_by_egui` specifically for the right button in `window_event`'s `MouseInput` handler, the same way every left-click on content already works. That fix is confirmed correct (a live run reached the WebView2 script and correctly found a real link's `href`), but getting a screenshot of the resulting popup proved intermittent in this headless environment — the same class of timing flakiness already documented elsewhere in this README (WebKit's own headless flakiness, Servo's occasional slow navigation) — so treat the mechanism as confirmed-by-code-path rather than screenshot-verified. WebKit's side builds clean (needed bumping `webkit2gtk`'s feature to `v2_22` for `run_javascript`'s result-value API, plus a new direct dependency on `javascriptcore-rs` for `ValueExt`) but wasn't live-tested at all this session, given how much time the WebView2 side alone took.
- **Follow-up (later session pass): CEF and Servo now send this too, closing what was flagged as an inconsistently-aggregated feature during the favicon audit.** Neither needed the WebView2/WebKit JS-hack -- both have their own, cleaner native hook that hands over the link URL directly, no script round-trip:
  - **CEF**: a new `ContextMenuHandler` (`manifold-view-cef/src/context_menu_handler.rs`), following the exact same `wrap_*_handler!`/shared-`RefCell`-drained-by-the-main-loop shape `find_handler.rs`/`download_handler.rs` already use. `on_before_context_menu` reads `ContextMenuParams::link_url()`/`xcoord()`/`ycoord()` directly -- CEF already knows exactly what was right-clicked, no hit-testing needed. `run_context_menu` unconditionally returns "handled" so CEF never attempts to show a real popup, matching every other engine's own "suppress the native menu, use it only as a data source" posture; windowless/OSR mode has no window to anchor one to anyway.
  - **Servo**: overriding `WebViewDelegate::show_embedder_control` (previously left at its default no-op entirely -- this engine-host doesn't implement any of the other prompt types `EmbedderControl` also covers: `<select>`/color/file pickers, IME, `alert()`/`confirm()`) and matching `EmbedderControl::ContextMenu`. Its `ContextMenuElementInformation` carries `link_url` as a structured `Option<Url>` directly. Letting the `ContextMenu` value drop without calling `.select()`/`.dismiss()` still tells Servo "dismissed, no selection" (its own `Drop` impl sends that automatically) -- the real menu never shows, same outcome as CEF's `run_context_menu` override, reached a different way. Confirmed via a build that this path was already silently safe *before* this change too, just inert: the default no-op `show_embedder_control` still let the unhandled `EmbedderControl` value drop at the end of the call, auto-dismissing -- so right-clicking a link on a Servo tab was never a hang or a leak, it just forwarded nothing.
  - Zero `manifold`-side changes needed -- it already consumed `EngineToHost::ContextMenuLink` generically regardless of which engine sent it, confirming the earlier audit's read that this gap was purely engine-side.
- **Superseded (2026-09-27 JSON level rework): the tab's "View as JSON" button and `build_json_viewer`'s popup are removed -- a Json node is browsed in its own JSON level instead (see the JSON node entry near the end of the next section).** Kept for the debugging trail, not as current behavior. **JSON viewing: the last item on the original aggregation-audit backlog, and the first genuinely new user-facing feature the audit's own gap-analysis unblocked.** A tab's toolbar shows a `BRACKETS_CURLY` "View as JSON" button once the current page turns out to be a raw JSON response (Graph view gets the same action via the tab's context menu, for a tab other than whichever is focused) -- clicking it opens a full-screen popup (`build_json_viewer`) with the body parsed into a real collapsible tree (`draw_json_value`, one `egui::CollapsingHeader` per object/array, `{n}`/`[n]` counts shown even collapsed), not this project's own re-implementation of a raw-text/pretty-print view. Detection: once a navigation finishes loading, a script eval checks `document.contentType === 'application/json'` -- the real HTTP response header as Chromium/WebKit already parsed it, not a guess from the URL's own file extension -- and only then reads back `document.body.innerText`. `TabPayload::json_body` holds the result; cleared back to `None` the same moment the tab's own url changes for real (same gate `history`/favicon-refetch already use), so navigating away from a JSON response doesn't leave a stale button pointing at content no longer showing.
  - **WebKit and WebView2** already had a script-eval-with-a-result-callback API from the `ContextMenuLink` work above (`run_javascript`/`ExecuteScript`) -- this reuses it directly, triggered once per navigation on the `loading: true -> false` edge (WebKit's own poll loop) / `NavigationCompleted` (WebView2's real event). One real correctness difference from `ContextMenuLink`'s own href parsing: `ExecuteScript`'s return value comes back JSON-encoded, and a link href's naive strip-outer-quotes trick (safe there since a real href essentially never contains a literal `"`) is *not* safe for arbitrary page content, which can. WebView2's side properly unescapes via a new `serde_json` dependency (`serde_json::from_str::<Option<String>>`, decoding both the quoted-string and `null` cases correctly); WebKit's `javascriptcore::Value` already hands back a real Rust `String` with no manual JSON-unescaping needed.
  - **Servo** already exposed a public `WebView::evaluate_javascript` (a real callback-based eval API, unused by this engine-host until now) that hands back a structured `JSValue` enum directly -- no JSON-encoding/unescaping step at all, the cleanest of the three. Wired into `notify_load_status_changed`'s existing `LoadStatus::Complete` check; the callback needed its own `Rc`-wrapped cell (unlike this `Delegate`'s other plain-`RefCell` fields) specifically so the `'static` closure `evaluate_javascript` stores could hold an owned clone of just that one cell rather than the whole `Delegate`.
  - **CEF, later session pass: the "would need a real V8 extension" assumption above was wrong -- no extension needed, just CEF's own real mechanism for a renderer to return a value.** `Frame::execute_java_script` is still fire-and-forget, but the `cef` crate (152.1.0) already exposes the whole path needed to get a result back a different way: a `RenderProcessHandler` running inside the renderer subprocess (this same exe, re-launched by CEF itself with `--type=renderer`), `V8Context::eval` for the actual script execution, and a `ProcessMessage` round-trip to hand the string back to the browser process. New `manifold-view-cef/src/render_process_handler.rs`: `on_process_message_received` (a `check-json-body` request) enters the frame's `V8Context`, evals `document.contentType` then, only if it's `application/json`, `document.body.innerText`, and replies with a `json-body-result` message carrying the string -- silence (no reply at all) when the page isn't JSON, since `manifold` already clears `json_body` on any real URL change regardless of engine. `nav_handler.rs`'s existing `LoadHandler::on_loading_state_change` sends the request the moment `is_loading` goes to 0 -- CEF only fires that callback on a real transition, not per frame, so this needed no separate edge-detection the way WebKit's own poll loop does for the same "loading: true -> false" moment. `client.rs`'s `Client::on_process_message_received` (a different interface than the renderer-side one, despite the same method name -- `ImplClient` vs. `ImplRenderProcessHandler`) receives the reply into a new `SharedJsonBody`, drained by the main loop the same "shared-RefCell-drained-by-the-main-loop" shape `find_handler.rs`/`download_handler.rs` already use, straight into `EngineToHost::JsonBody`.
    - **One real, structural fix needed first:** this project's `execute_process` call (the reentry point CEF uses to relaunch this exe as a subprocess) passed `None` for its `App`, deliberately mirroring cefsimple's own reference example -- meaning a renderer subprocess ran under CEF's default no-op `App` and could never have had a `RenderProcessHandler` registered at all, regardless of anything in `client.rs`. Fixed by building the real `App` earlier and passing it to *both* `execute_process` and the browser-process `initialize` call -- harmless for the browser-process path, since CEF only ever calls `render_process_handler()` from inside an actual renderer subprocess.
    - Zero `manifold`-side changes needed, same as the link-context-menu work above -- the "View as JSON" button/context-menu entry was already gated purely on `TabPayload::json_body.is_some()`, with no engine exclusion to remove.
