    - Build-verified across the full workspace (excluding WebKit) with zero warnings; not live-tested this session.

Clicking a download link saves the file and shows a brief "Saved &lt;path&gt;" toast (bottom-left, auto-dismissing after 4s; clicks pass through it to the card or page underneath) — no save-location prompt, no progress UI. All three of WebView2, CEF, and WebKit have a real native download API, and each supplies its own default save location the same way a real browser's download prompt would default to one: WebView2 (`ICoreWebView2_4::add_DownloadStarting`/`DownloadOperation::add_StateChanged`) already computes a default path on its own; CEF (`DownloadHandler::on_before_download`/`BeforeDownloadCallback::cont`) and WebKitGTK (`WebContext::connect_download_started`/`Download::connect_decide_destination`) both have no default of their own and would silently never start the download without this project supplying one, so both save to `%USERPROFILE%\Downloads`/`$HOME/Downloads` plus the server's suggested filename — the same "pick the obvious default, don't build a save-as dialog" posture this project already takes elsewhere. Verified live end-to-end on WebView2: a real click on a `download`-attributed link produced the file on disk with correct content, and `EngineToHost::DownloadCompleted` arrived with the right path (confirmed via temporary debug logging) — but catching the resulting toast in a screenshot proved to be the same class of headless-timing flakiness already documented for the context-menu popup above (the completion event and any redraw after it can land right at the tail end of a scripted test's last frame), so the toast is confirmed-by-code-path/debug-log rather than screenshot-verified. CEF and WebKit build clean against the exact same handler pattern `find_handler.rs`/WebKitGTK's own find/favicon code already use successfully, but a live click-triggered download wasn't reproduced for either in the time available this session (CEF's real native mouse-injection input mechanism needs different on-screen coordinates than WebView2/WebKit's script-driven clicks, and a coordinate mismatch — not necessarily the download code itself — is the most likely reason the test click never landed on the link). Servo has no download hook in its embedding API at all, same per-engine gap as find-in-page and DevTools.

A completed download also shows up as its own node in Graph view, edged to the tab it came from (the edge is saved with the session since 2026-10-04, as is a content node's: content a tab became keeps that tab's link, and Open as Tab links the new tab to its node) — same force-directed layout/edge machinery `TabNode`s already use (`graph_layout::step` treats tabs and downloads as one flat id space), just a smaller card with a filename instead of a live thumbnail (no engine process/content behind it). Click a download node to open its containing folder with the file pre-selected (`explorer.exe /select,<path>`) — a WebKit-sourced path is translated from its WSL2-side form to the `\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\...` UNC path Explorer can actually browse first. The "x" only forgets the node, never deletes the file. Closing a download's parent tab reparents it to that tab's own parent, the same rule `close_tab` already applies to child tabs; a completed download node is saved with the session (one still in progress isn't, since it can't resume), and its file stays on disk either way. Verified live on WebView2: a real downloaded file showed up as a node with a correct edge to its source tab, right where the download-completion toast paragraph above's own debug-logged `DownloadCompleted` event said it would.

Each graph node shows a small green dot at its top-right corner while that tab's page is producing audio — a plain painter-drawn circle rather than a speaker glyph (several Unicode symbol glyphs, `▲`/`▼`/`✕` among them, are confirmed elsewhere in this app to render as empty boxes in whatever font it actually has glyphs for). WebView2 (`ICoreWebView2_8::IsDocumentPlayingAudio`/`add_IsDocumentPlayingAudioChanged`, needing `webkit2gtk`'s `v2_8` Cargo feature — bumped up from `v2_6`) and WebKitGTK (`WebView::is_playing_audio`/its `is-playing-audio` GObject notify) both expose this as a real *passive* read of state the engine already tracks, no different in kind from reading a tab's `title`. CEF has no such API short of registering an `AudioHandler`, which takes over delivering every raw PCM audio packet to the app instead of playing it through normal OS audio output — a real audio-routing feature, not a passive read, and a much bigger and riskier change than a tab icon warrants (get it wrong and CEF tabs go silent). **Later session pass: CEF sends this too now, by a different route** -- see the dedicated writeup further down. Servo's embedding API has no such property at all. **Not live-verified this session**: this project's synthetic click input (`el.click()` via `ExecuteScript`/`nav_action` — see Known limitations) doesn't carry the "sticky user activation" browsers require before an autoplay-gated API like `AudioContext` will actually start producing sound, so a real audio stream could never be triggered to confirm the indicator turns on; the API usage itself is a single documented property read, the same shape already verified working for favicon/find on both engines.

**Audio indicator upgraded to a tiny animated waveform**: the plain green dot above now only shows while muted (a static red dot, since nothing is actually playing to animate); while actually playing it's replaced by a 3-bar waveform, still painter-drawn rects rather than a speaker/equalizer glyph. Animated off `std::time::SystemTime::now()` rather than egui's own `ui.input(|i| i.time)` — **found live**, not assumed: driving the indicator through a temporary `TESTSETAUDIO` debug command (set `audio_playing` on the focused tab directly, removed after verifying, same disposable-hook pattern used elsewhere in this project) and comparing cropped/upscaled screenshots taken seconds apart showed `ui.input`'s own time reading as completely frozen across separate headless `SCREENSHOT`-triggered redraws, even though each redraw was demonstrably fresh (the newly-set `audio_playing` state itself only ever appears after one). Switching the animation's clock to a plain OS `SystemTime` read sidesteps whatever that staleness is and was confirmed to tick correctly the same way. This app already requests a redraw unconditionally at the end of every frame, so the animation needs no extra repaint request to keep running on its own. Verified live: cropped/upscaled screenshots of the indicator corner showed three distinct bars of different heights (not a dot), and a second capture two seconds later showed measurably different bar heights.

A "Muted" toggle button sits in Tab view's toolbar (right after the engine picker) — `selectable_label`'s pressed look when active, no speaker glyph risked. This is host-initiated (unlike the passive audio-playing read above), so there's no `EngineToHost` reply: `manifold` tracks a per-tab `muted` flag itself and sends `HostToEngine::SetMuted { muted }` on click. WebView2 (`ICoreWebView2_8::SetIsMuted` — the exact same interface `IsDocumentPlayingAudio` already uses) and WebKitGTK (`WebView::set_is_muted`, needing the `webkit2gtk` Cargo feature bumped to `v2_30`) both have a real native mute API; CEF also mutes now (see the dedicated writeup further down), and Servo has no audio control at all — silent no-op there only. Verified live on WebView2: clicking the button called `ICoreWebView2_8::SetIsMuted(true)` (confirmed `Ok`) and a follow-up `IsMuted` read-back on the same interface correctly returned `true` (temporary debug logging, since removed). WebKit's wiring uses the identical `WebViewExt::set_is_muted` pattern already proven for `set_zoom_level` in the same file, and builds clean, but wasn't independently re-verified live this session — this project's own documented WebKit-headless-test flakiness (see below) made every attempted repro this session fail to even reach a spawned tab within a generous wait, a spawn-timing problem rather than anything specific to this change.

`Ctrl+F`'s find bar (top-right of Tab view) searches the focused tab's real page content as you type, via each engine's own native find API — `ICoreWebView2Find`/CEF's `BrowserHost::find`/WebKitGTK's `WebKitFindController` all highlight matches directly in the page and report a live match count; Prev/Next (or Enter/Shift+Enter) step through them, the "Aa" toggle switches case-sensitivity (re-searching immediately, resets to off on a fresh search), Escape or the bar's own close button ends the search. Verified live on CEF and WebView2 (highlighting, match count, active-match tracking, and the case-sensitivity toggle all correct — e.g. searching "banana" against a page with one capitalized "Banana" and two lowercase drops the count from 3 to 2 with the toggle on); WebKit's wiring is written against the same `WebKitFindController` API this project's favicon code already uses successfully, but not build/run-verified this session (WSL2 itself failed to start in this environment — a VM-creation error unrelated to this change). Servo has no find-in-page API in its embedding surface at all — on a Servo tab `Ctrl+F` shows a toast ("Servo has no find in page") and the bar stays shut (`Tag::Find`); before that it opened (confirmed live: no crash, no hang) but never showed a match count.

Tab view's toolbar has a "Screenshot" button (next to "Muted") that saves the whole composited window — chrome and content, not just the page — as a PNG to `manifold-<unix-seconds>.png` in the user's Pictures folder (`profile::pictures_dir()`; the temp folder without a home folder), then shows the same "Saved <path>" toast the download feature uses (the two now share one `save_toast` field/`build_save_toast` renderer rather than a download-specific one). No save-as dialog, same "pick the obvious default" posture as everywhere else this project saves a file. This is the exact same capture path the headless `SCREENSHOT` debug command already uses (`GpuState::render`'s `screenshot_path` parameter, a second GPU render into a plain `COPY_SRC` texture then read back) — the button just supplies a real default path instead of a test-provided one, engine-agnostic since it works off the already-composited frame rather than anything engine-specific. Verified live: clicking it produced a real, correctly-rendered PNG of the page on disk, with the toast shown in the same screenshot.

Only the focused tab ever actually renders — every other tab's engine-host process stays alive (so its page keeps running, JS timers and all) but stops producing frames until it's focused again; its node just shows the last frame it produced. New tabs are created via "+ New Tab" (or `Ctrl+T`), or automatically as a child node when a page opens one itself (`target=_blank`/`window.open`/ctrl+click) — CEF, WebKit, and WebView2 all intercept and forward these; Servo does not (falls back to its own unhandled-popup default), see Known limitations.

If an engine-host process dies (crashes, or is killed) — focused or not — the tab automatically respawns the same engine at its last-known URL, keeping its place in the graph.

The URL carries over on an engine swap, and so do cookies between WebView2 and CEF: the old tab's cookies are pulled into manifold's cookie jar before the swap (waiting up to 2 s) and the new engine is seeded from the jar (see "Cookie jar" below). Other state (scroll position, localStorage, IndexedDB, etc.) does not — a known v1 limitation, not a bug.

The whole tab graph (which tabs, their url/engine/graph position, who opened whom, which tab is focused, which view is active) is saved on every change that matters (a tab spawns, closes, navigates, or focus/view changes) and restored at the next launch, instead of always starting over at one root tab on the home page. Like an engine swap, only the graph's shape is portable across a restart — a restored tab is a fresh navigation to its last known URL, not a resumed one (no localStorage or scroll position; a WebView2 or CEF tab does start with the cookie jar's cookies).

The session lives in `%APPDATA%\manifold\` as a replicated operation log rather than one JSON blob, so two instances open on the same profile (or a synced folder) merge instead of one clobbering the other's save:

- `session.mnf` — the shared graph: every node's kind, url/engine, group, and who opened whom. Multiple instances on the same file each append their own ops and read back the other's; a conflicting edit resolves the same way every field does (spec "Fields": last-write-wins by wall clock, actor id breaking a tie).
- `session.mnf.local` — this profile's own personal state, kept out of the shared file so it never bounces between instances: focus, camera position, each node's own position, title and live url (where a redirect or script took it without sharing). (Recently Closed is not here: it is derived from the shared document.) Two instances on one profile each write their own whole, so the next launch reopens whichever instance saved last.
- `session.mnf.prev` — a copy of the file kept from the last compaction, used to recover a snapshot if the live file's own is damaged.
- `session.mnf.damaged-<16 hex digits>` — a byte-for-byte copy of the file set aside the moment damage is found (a bad CRC, a torn tail), keyed by its generation, so repairing it in place can't lose evidence of what went wrong. The app repairs around the damage and keeps running; a toast names the file once.
- `session.mnf.unreadable-<unix seconds>[-<n>]` — a `session.mnf` that isn't a session file at all (no header) is renamed to this at startup and a new, empty session is started, with a toast naming where the old file went. Its `.prev` and `.local` move with it (`<aside>.prev`, `<aside>.local`). That launch prunes no fetched content or jar files, since the new session names none of the old one's; later launches prune against the new session, so recover anything from the set-aside copy before restarting.
- An old `session.json` (from before this format) is left in place and ignored — nothing migrates it automatically.
- Named sessions ("Save As" / "Open Session" in the Settings popup's Sessions section) are their own store files under `sessions/<name>.mnf`, same format and same `.local`/`.prev`/`.damaged-*` siblings as the main session. Deleting one (the X beside "Open", which asks first) removes `<name>.mnf` and every `<name>.mnf.*` file beside it, damaged copies included, but not another save named `<name>.mnf.<more>` or its files. A name never maps to a file if it is empty, `.`/`..` or over 200 characters, contains a separator, a control character or one of `:<>"|?*`, or is a Windows device name (`CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `LPT1`-`LPT9` and the like, in any case, even with an extension). So `OPENSESSION` can't reach outside `sessions/`, and Save As or Open with such a name toasts "Not a session name".
- `history.json` — browsing history (title/url pairs, most recent first, capped at 200), unrelated to the graph store; missing or corrupt just means empty history, same "moved aside, never fails to start" posture as `settings.json`/`bookmarks.json`.
- one `jars\<jar id>.json` per jar card, the Default included (ephemeral jars have no file; a `default.json` from an older build is left alone and unused) — the cookie jars (below). On Windows each is `MJR1` followed by a DPAPI blob for the current user; elsewhere `MJR1` then plain JSON in a file created 0600 (keyring protection is a follow-up). A file that cannot be read is renamed to `<file>.unreadable-<unix seconds>[-<n>]` (for example `<jar id>.json.unreadable-...`) and that jar starts empty; only the 5 newest of those per jar are kept, since each holds credentials. If the file cannot be read or moved aside at all, that jar's cookie changes are not saved that session, so it is never overwritten. Saves go through `<file>.<pid>.tmp` and a rename; the aside and temp names use each file's own name.

**Cookie jar** (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-jars-design.md`). Manifold keeps a Default jar card (made at first start, never closed), whose file is `jars\<its id>.json`, shared by every WebView2 and CEF tab on it (a tab can use a named jar instead: Per-tab jars, below), so a login made in one engine is present in the other and survives a restart or an engine swap. A tab's host is seeded from its jar when it connects, before its first navigation; manifold pulls each tab's cookies back every 30 s, before a sleep or swap (waiting up to 2 s) and once at quit (waiting up to 1 s). A pull round's changes are written once its replies are in, at most 2 s after the first change (plus one event-loop wake, about 100 ms; longer while a modal dialog holds the loop): a crash in that window loses those cookie changes, on top of what tabs set since their last pull (up to 30 s), which a crash loses anyway. New tabs are seeded from memory, so they never see the lag. A cookie a tab changed replaces the jar's (an expiry within 1 ms of the old one is not a change); a cookie a tab reported earlier and no longer has is deleted (a logout); a seeded cookie the tab never reported is never deleted by it. Not built yet: sending new cookies to tabs already running (a tab sees another tab's later login only after it respawns), Servo and WebKit (they get no cookies and contribute none). WebView2 tabs run InPrivate, so WebView2 keeps no localStorage, IndexedDB, cache or site permissions across restarts any more: only cookies persist, through the jar. Each live WebView2 host also gets its own named InPrivate profile, from a pool of eight names (`manifold_0` to `manifold_7`; a name is reused only after its host has exited, and starts signed out), so WebView2 tabs no longer share one in-memory cookie store (`gate jar` two-tab run, 2026-10-03); manifold deletes each profile's leftover directory when the host goes and sweeps the rest at startup (`process_manager::sweep_webview2_profiles`). WebView2 lists every profile name it has seen in its `Local State` (about 1 KB each); the pool keeps that to the most WebView2 tabs ever open at once, at least eight (spec, WebView2 isolation). Two instances on one profile overwrite each other's jar file (last save wins); that is not supported in v1.

**Per-tab jars** (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-per-tab-jars-design.md`). Each tab uses one jar: Default, or a named jar picked (or made, "New Jar...") from the tab's right-click menu, so two tabs can be signed in to one site as two accounts. A tab or group can have a `jar` edge to a jar card (its menu's Jar page, or "Use jar..." in Links): a tab uses its own edge, else the nearest enclosing group's, else the Default. An edge reaches a jar on the source's own level or above; a group's edge also reaches a jar inside it. A move that takes an edge out of reach clears it, with a toast; one that arrives from another device is kept and warns (the tab uses the Default). "Inherit" clears the tab's own edge. Tabs a tab opens (Duplicate, links its page opens in a new tab) copy its jar. A live tab whose jar changes keeps running on its old jar until it switches by sleeping, under the idle-sleep rules (not while focused, on screen, pinned, playing audio, loading, downloading or holding a changed form), and wakes on the new one; "Switch Jar Now" (its menu, or a click on Tab view's jar dot) replaces its host at once. A card on a named jar, or waiting to switch, shows a jar chip (an arrow from the old jar's dot while waiting). Jars are nodes in the synced session, so jar edges and names sync between devices; cookies do not (each device keeps its own jar files). A jar is its id, not its name: names need not be unique, and where two jars share one (compared by the clean form below, any case) each is shown with a marker derived from its id, "#1a2b", drawn smaller, monospace and dim after the name (plain text such as a confirm dialog says "Name · #1a2b"); the built-in Default jar is shown italic with a "built-in" marker ("Default (built-in)" in plain text), so a user jar named Default stays distinct. A typed name that copies a marker is drawn as plain name text. Every group, jar, saved-session and bookmark folder name is cleaned when it is committed: control, zero-width and bidi characters are dropped (emoji sequences such as 👩‍💻 keep theirs), odd spaces collapse to one, and the name is at most 64 characters. A stored name that still holds such a character (from sync or older data) shows it as a dim `⟦200B⟧` placeholder (`<U+200B>` in plain text), with a hover naming it. A note's first line, where it serves as the note's title, is shown the same way; the note itself is never changed. Settings shows a marker beside the name field, which edits only the name; a narrow card's chip ellipsizes the name and keeps the marker. A jar in an opened or imported file is always a new, empty jar, even beside a same-named one (a group export holds no jars; its tabs keep naming jars by id). Settings "Jars" lists Default and each named jar with its tab count: rename, toggle Ephemeral (no file, forgotten at quit; turning it on deletes a saved file, so it asks first when there is one, and a locked jar's unreadable file is never touched), Clear Cookies, and Delete (its tabs use Default; its files, unreadable copies included, go with it, and a jar deleted on another device while this one was off has its files deleted at the next start); clearing and deleting ask first. After Clear Cookies, a running tab on that jar keeps nothing it signs in to until it has switched ("cleared means cleared"). Servo and WebKit tabs ignore jars (their chip says so).

The Settings popup's Sessions section also has "Export..."/"Import..." for a portable JSON snapshot of the current session (`{"nodes": [...]}` plus this profile's personal state) — this is the one place `.json` is still the right extension, since it's an interchange format, not the live store. Command-line tools work on the same `.mnf` files directly: `manifold.exe --dump <file.mnf>` prints its frames and decoded document as JSON, `--check <file.mnf>` reports any damage or a torn tail (exit 0 clean, 1 damaged), and `--import-json <in.json> --out <file.mnf>` builds a fresh store file from a JSON snapshot (used by `scripts/drive.sh`'s `SEED`, see below).

Each engine-host writes its own log to `%TEMP%\manifold-<tab_id>.log`. `MANIFOLD_CEF_VERBOSE=1` turns Chromium's verbose log back on for the CEF host.

**Image view**: clicking an image card maximizes it into Item view (the same animated expand/shrink a tab card uses; Escape or the "Graph" button returns). The toolbar is the shared Item toolbar frame -- "Graph" at the left, the Help/Settings chip at the right -- with the image's own content between: its filename and pixel size, a zoom chip (−, percentage, +, Fit; the same chip the Graph toolbar uses), and Open Containing Folder / Copy Path. The image opens at Fit (contained, never above 100%); the mouse wheel zooms about the cursor (10%–1600%), dragging pans (never past the image's edges), and `Ctrl+=`/`Ctrl+-`/`Ctrl+0` zoom it. A maximized image is restored on relaunch (at Fit). Tab-only shortcuts (Ctrl+W, F5, …) keep acting on the last focused tab, as they do from Graph view.

## Branches

Since 2026-10-06, `main` is the only long-lived branch and releases are tags. (From 2026-09-25 to then, work went on `dev` and `main` moved only at releases through `git merge --no-ff dev`; `dev` was merged into `main` and retired. The release merges `v0.1.0`, `v0.2.0` and `v0.3.0` remain in `main`'s history.)

- Commit and push to `main`. Feature branches and worktrees branch from `main` and merge back into it.
- A release is a version bump commit on `main` (workspace `version`, plus `Cargo.lock`, plus a new top entry in `CHANGELOG.md`), then an annotated tag `vX.Y.Z` on it whose message is that entry, made by `target/debug/gate.exe release-tag vX.Y.Z` (it refuses unless the tree is clean, the workspace version is X.Y.Z and the changelog has the entry, builds `manifold vX.Y.Z`, a blank line and the entry body with the same code `release-check` reads it back with, and tags with `--cleanup=verbatim`, which the default cleanup would not do to the `###` headings; a hand-built message once failed `release-check` on the pushed tag, 2026-10-25), then pushing the tag runs `release.yml` (see Releases), which leaves a draft release for you to read and publish. `git tag -l` and `git log --first-parent --decorate` list the releases.
- Releases run on GitHub Actions (decision `docs/decisions/2026-10-18-hosts-in-the-release.md`, which replaced the `hosts-KIND-FP` design of `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-ci-release-design.md`). Pushing an annotated tag `vX.Y.Z` runs `release.yml`: `gate release-check` (tag message is `manifold vX.Y.Z`, a blank line and the `CHANGELOG.md` entry's body; workspace version), the downloadable hosts (CEF: `manifold-view-cef.exe`, static CRT; Servo: `manifold-view-servo.exe` at 129 MB plus its ANGLE `libEGL.dll` and `libGLESv2.dll`, dynamic CRT; the Servo build adds about 40 minutes) with a manifest of their sizes and SHA-256 hashes (`dist/manifold-vX.Y.Z-windows-x64.host-pins.json`), the embedded WebView2 host and PDF view, `manifold.exe` built with `MANIFOLD_HOST_PINS=<that manifest>` (`build.rs` embeds it and `crates/manifold/src/host_pin.rs` parses it: the hash compiled into the exe is the trust anchor, the URL is not trusted, and a host's URL is `releases/download/v<exe version>/<file>-<platform>`), `gate package`, `gate release-verify` (the host files in `dist/` match the manifest), then a draft release (the draft is the publish gate; run by hand on a branch, `release.yml` does the same build without a release or tag and uploads `dist/` as an artifact, so a workflow change needs no throwaway version) holding the exe, its `.sha256`, the embedded hosts' hash records, the manifest and each host as `FILE-PLATFORM`. A build with no manifest (dev, the gate) pins nothing; a debug build also reads `MANIFOLD_HOST_PINS_FILE` (the gate's engine-states shots use it). WebKit's host is built by the `webkit-host` job (`ubuntu-22.04`, the oldest Ubuntu with `libwebkit2gtk-4.1`, so its glibc starts on older distros) and pinned under `linux-x64` (dry run 40006630810, 2026-10-23: the ELF is in the manifest and `dist/`, all libraries resolve in WSL Ubuntu, glibc floor 2.34; not yet started under a real tab). The release is that one exe: CEF's, Servo's and WebKit's hosts are downloaded on demand into `engine-hosts/` (WebKit's is a Linux ELF pinned under `linux-x64`; WSL runs it straight from that Windows folder, and a built host beside the exe or in the dev tree's `target-linux/debug/` wins over the download; WSL itself, a distro named Ubuntu, `xvfb-run` and the WebKitGTK libraries are not part of the download), each checked against its pinned hash, with a per-engine `<engine>.host` marker listing the installed files' hashes (written last; the 5 s disk poll reads only the marker) and a re-hash when a tab spawns. A host is pinned by hash, so **after an update the downloaded host reads Update needed and must be reinstalled** (Settings, Engines; every release rebuilds its hosts and the output is not bit-reproducible). An engine whose host this build pins no download for, and that has no sibling host, reads Unavailable ("this build has no Servo download"); dev builds and Linux CI pin nothing and keep sibling lookup. `gate package [exe]` runs the finished exe alone in a temp folder (see Packaging); `gate install-check cef|servo EXE` (a published release only) has that exe install the engine from the network, as a Download click does (v0.7.1: installed in 28 s, run `install-check-v071b`; v0.8.0: CEF 28 s, Servo 25 s, run `install-check-v080`).
- Packaging: the release is `dist/manifold-v<version>-windows-x64.exe` from `release.yml` (about 27 MB with the 0.9 MB WebView2 host embedded; embedding the 4.6 MB PDF view as well measured 23.5 MB -> 28.8 MB on a non-static-CRT release build, 2026-10-12; no zip, no README or PACKAGING.md beside it). `gate package [exe]` (default: the newest `dist/manifold-v*-windows-x64.exe`; a draft release's exe works too) copies that exe ALONE into a temp folder and checks it: it imports no `vcruntime140`/`msvcp140` (`llvm-objdump`; the release links the C runtime statically via `RUSTFLAGS="-C target-feature=+crt-static"`, set by `release.yml` and `hosts.yml` only, and CEF's wrapper is built `/MT` by `cef-dll-sys` already), it runs headless with `APPDATA` redirected, `VIEWSTATE cef` and `servo` read Not installed (with the download size when the release's pin manifest pins them), a WebView2 tab loads a local page with the host extracted from the embed, and a PDF node renders its fixture through the extracted PDF view (the one download: PDFium, once).  A local CEF build needs `depot_tools` off `PATH` (the CI runner has none); a CMake cache from an earlier failed CEF build (`target/release/build/cef-dll-sys/<hash>/out/build`) must be deleted by hand. The downloaded Servo host and its `libEGL.dll` are the exception to the static CRT (mozjs and mozangle are `/MD`): they import `vcruntime140`/`msvcp140`, so Servo needs the Visual C++ Redistributable even though `manifold.exe` does not (the Servo download dialog warns when `vcruntime140.dll`, `vcruntime140_1.dll` or `msvcp140.dll` is missing from System32). Unsigned.
- Embedded PDF view: the same mechanism for `MANIFOLD_PDFIUM_HOST` (`manifold-view-pdfium.exe`, extracted to `engine-hosts/pdfium/` at the first PDF render; `release.yml` records its hash in `dist/manifold-<tag>-windows-x64.embedded-pdf-view.sha256`, a second file next to the WebView2 host's; `gate release-verify` checks both). The PDF view exe is `manifold-view-pdfium` in the dev tree too, found beside `manifold.exe` before the embedded copy; `manifold.exe` itself no longer links PDFium's bindings. The crates are `manifold-view-*`; the four web view exes are `manifold-view-*` too (before the next release they were `manifold-engine-*`).
- Embedded WebView2 host: a build with `MANIFOLD_WEBVIEW2_HOST=<path to the host exe>` (set by `release.yml`) carries that host inside `manifold.exe` and extracts it to `engine-hosts/webview2/` at the first WebView2 spawn (`crates/manifold/src/embedded_host.rs`). Dev builds and the gate leave the variable unset, embed nothing and use the sibling host, so neither exercises the embed; only `MANIFOLD_WEBVIEW2_HOST=<abs path> cargo test -p manifold embedded_host` (use a separate `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, as the variable changes the build) and a release build do.
- Packaging policy: packaged builds offer CEF on demand, opt-in, never as the default engine, and never bundled (spec `2026-10-02-engine-install-design.md`).
- Release checklist: if the `cef` crate moved, re-pin CEF for every pinned platform (`target/debug/gate.exe cef-pin [--platform <id>] <prefix>`, a download per platform; spec `2026-10-02-engine-install-design.md`, "Platforms") before tagging.
- A hotfix for an old release branches from its tag as `release/x.y`, is tagged like a release, and the branch is deleted after. The fix is also applied to `main` (cherry-pick or merge).
- Everything on `main` up to `2a88b50` predates the branch convention and is the pre-release baseline.

## Search

The omnibar finds a card by what its page says, not only by its title and url. Each tab's visible text is asked of its engine a moment after a load ends and before the tab sleeps (`QueryPageText`, protocol 6) and appended to `text.log` in `search/` under the profile folder (`profile::search_dir`). Nothing leaves the device: the index is a hand-rolled BM25 in memory (`search/index.rs`), rebuilt from `text.log` on a worker thread at every start and never saved. A tab in an ephemeral jar is never read, and turning a jar ephemeral deletes its tabs' text at the next pass. `search/sync.rs` keeps the index equal to the document once a second: it fingerprints every card's text (title and url, a note's text, a name, a file name, the stored page text) and queues what changed, so a rename, a note edit (a second after the edit, not per keystroke), a close and a permanent delete need no hook each. Settings, "Clear search index" deletes `text.log` and empties the index; it says the same in a line above the button.

A query is the AND of its words, the last one a prefix, ranked by BM25 (K1 1.2, B 0.75). The Graph dims cards the omnibar text misses, by title, url or page text (`App::node_matches`); the dropdown lists up to five tabs and up to five other cards, each with a snippet of the page text when that is what matched.

Startup build, measured 2026-10-23 on a 71 MB `text.log` (5 000 pages of 1 500 words, release): loading the log takes about 0.1 s, building the index from it about 19 s on the worker thread (see PARKED.md, "Saved search index"). Search works on what is built so far; the dimming and rows fill in as it finishes.

**Suggest groups and Arrange by topic** (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-search-and-categorization-design.md`). Each card's text becomes a vector (`search/lsa.rs`: tf-idf rows, a seeded randomized SVD to 64 dimensions, so the same texts give the same bits in any order); a cosine says how much two cards talk about the same things. *Suggest groups* (toolbar sparkle, omnibar command, `SUGGEST`) clusters the level by average linkage (`search/cluster.rs`: cosine 0.2, at least 3 cards, at most 8 groups, at most 400 cards; 400 cards take about 100 ms in a release build) and offers each cluster as a ring round its cards and a chip with Accept and Dismiss. Nothing is grouped until accepted, an accept is one undo step, and an offer stands only while the level's cards are the ones it was made for. *Arrange by topic* (toolbar, omnibar command, `ARRANGE`; `search/arrange.rs`) seeds the cards at their first two embedding components and settles the layout 300 steps with an extra spring from each card to its three most similar neighbours (cosine at least 0.30, spring weighted by the cosine; `graph_layout::Edge::weight`). Pinned cards and groups stay. It writes the document positions as one undo step and refuses above 300 cards. The ordinary settle that runs when a level's cards change knows nothing of topics, so it relaxes an arrangement a little (30-45 % of a rest length on average, prototype `graph_layout/prototype.rs`); keeping the similarity springs in later settles is parked.

## Headless mode (automated testing)

```bash
target/debug/manifold.exe --headless
```

`--script <file>` reads the commands from a file instead of stdin (EOF quits, as for stdin), and `--profile-dir <dir>` (or `--profile-dir=<dir>`) uses `<dir>` as the profile folder instead of `%APPDATA%manifold`. Together they let a run with no stdin and no way to set `APPDATA` stay isolated, such as one started by the elevated `Samply` task (`~/.claude/SAMPLY.md`), which otherwise would use the real profile. Any other argument, or a flag missing its value (a bare `--profile-dir`, an empty one, or one followed by another flag), is refused: manifold prints `manifold: <why>` and exits 2, rather than running on the real profile.

Runs the same app, but the window is positioned far off-screen (not `--visible(false)` — a genuinely invisible window's swapchain never successfully acquires a frame on Windows, DWM permanently reports it occluded; an off-screen-but-visible window keeps rendering normally while never overlapping the user's actual screen) and driven entirely by a text-command protocol read from stdin, one command per line, instead of real OS input. This exists so live GUI testing of this app doesn't have to fight Windows' foreground-lock (`SetForegroundWindow` can be silently refused while the user is active on their own machine — see project memory) or contend visually with whatever the user is doing. `EXPORTSTATE`, `EXPORTSESSION` and `EXPORTGROUP` replace their file atomically (written beside it, then renamed over it), so a script may read one as soon as it exists; `SCREENSHOT` writes in place, so wait for its size to settle.

Debug commands read and write any path the user can (the `EXPORT*` and `IMPORT*` commands, `SCREENSHOT`); they are for driving manifold from scripts and are not a security boundary.

Commands:
- `MOUSEMOVE <x> <y>`, `MOUSEDOWN <x> <y> [left|right|middle]`, `MOUSEUP <x> <y> [left|right|middle]`, `CLICK <x> <y> [left|right|middle]`, `DOUBLECLICK <x> <y>` — dispatched as genuine synthetic `winit::event::WindowEvent`s, not a logic bypass, so they exercise real egui hit-testing exactly like real input would.
- `SCROLL <x> <y> <delta_x> <delta_y>`
- `ADDRESSBAR <text>` — in Tab view, focuses the address bar, then types the text over its contents as egui key/text events, so suggestions update through the real `TextEdit` path.
- `ENTER`, `ESCAPE`, `ARROWDOWN`, `ARROWUP` are real `egui::Event::Key` presses injected into the next frame's input, not calls into the handlers. `winit::event::KeyEvent` has no public constructor, so they enter at the egui level; see `debug_control`'s doc comment. Headless mode marks the window focused for egui, which otherwise reports no widget focus in an unfocused window.
- `ENTER` — in the focused address bar, navigates using its text or the highlighted suggestion.
- `ESCAPE` — dismisses the suggestion dropdown, or leaves Item view (a maximized tab or image) for Graph view if none is showing.
- `ARROWDOWN` / `ARROWUP` — moves the address-bar dropdown's highlighted row (wrapping at both ends); a following `ENTER` accepts it.
- `EGUITEXT <text>` — types into whichever egui text field has focus ("New Jar..."'s field, a group rename), as an `egui::Event::Text` in the next frame's input; `BACKSPACE` is a real Backspace press there. With `ENTER`/`ESCAPE` they drive those fields end to end. Leading and trailing spaces are trimmed off the text.
- `TYPE <text>` — sends each character to the focused tab's page (not the address bar).
- `KEYPAGE <name>` — sends a KeyDown+KeyUp of the named key (`Enter`, `Backspace`, `ArrowLeft`, a single letter/digit, etc.) to the focused tab's page.
- `NEWTAB` / `CLOSETAB` / `FOCUSADDRESS` / `NEXTTAB` / `PREVTAB` — the `Ctrl+T`/`Ctrl+W`/`Ctrl+L`/`Ctrl+Tab`/`Ctrl+Shift+Tab` shortcuts' exact same effect, called directly (same rationale as `TYPE`/`KEYPAGE`: no public way to synthesize a real `Ctrl`-chorded `KeyboardInput`). `FOCUSADDRESS` is the one command among these that isn't instant — it drives a real `egui::Response::request_focus()`, which (immediate-mode) only takes visible effect starting the *next* frame, so it also forces a redraw itself rather than relying on some later, unrelated command to eventually trigger one.
- `SCREENSHOT <path>` — saves the next composited frame (chrome, any open dropdown, and content) as a PNG, read back directly from the GPU rather than `PrintWindow`/`CopyFromScreen` (see project memory's gotchas on GDI-handle exhaustion and foreground-lock unreliability with that approach). Unlike the toolbar's Screenshot button it shows no "Saved <path>" toast, which would cover the bottom-left of the window for 4 s, in later screenshots and under a script's next clicks.
- `EXPORTSTATE <path>` also lists what the last finished frame drew, so a probe asserts on state instead of pixels: `identities` (every `Identity::paint` icon: `kind` spinner|image|glyph|tile, `text`, screen `rect`; the JSON preview header's glyph is not logged, since it paints through `Identity::text`), `rows` (each labelled icon row of the omnibar dropdowns, history and bookmarks: `text`, `rect`, `selected` = the highlighted or focused one; a closed tab's row has no label, so it is not listed) and `open_popup` (the `AnchoredPopup` open, by name) and `group_suggestions` (the groups "Suggest groups" offers that still stand: `label` and `cards`). Recording is in `identity.rs` (`begin_icon_frame`, `drawn_icons`, `drawn_rows`). `gate trail` asserts the strip's and address bar's tiles and the dropdown's rows through them. `TOGGLEPOPUP recentlyclosed` opens the graph toolbar's Recently Closed list.
- `ADDFILE <path> [x y]` — adds a file as the node its content makes (image, PDF, JSON, note, tab, or a download card); `x y` is a window point, like a drop there.
- `NEWJSON [x y]` — a new inline JSON card (empty object) at a window point; `DETACHJSON <id>` — move a file JSON card's value into the synced document and drop its path (toasts and changes nothing past the 10,000-leaf cap).
- `JSONEDIT <op> <pointer> [args]` — edit the open inline JSON level: `set <ptr> <json>`, `add <ptr> <key> <json>`, `insert <ptr> <index> <json>`, `remove <ptr>`, `rename <ptr> <to>`, `move <ptr> <to>` (`root` is the root pointer; `<json>` is the rest of the line). One undo step each; `json_level` reports `"editable"`.
- `JSONSELECT [pointer]`, `JSONTOGGLE [pointer]`, `JSONMORE [pointer]`, `JSONRELAYOUT`, `JSONSEARCH <text>` — drive the current JSON level (double-click a Json card to enter one): select an entry (empty pointer: the root) and pan to it if it's off-screen, collapse/expand a card, page a card's rows, clear saved card positions and relayout, and search keys and scalar values. `EXPORTSTATE`'s `json_level` (below) is `null` off a JSON level.
- `MOVETOGROUP <id> <group>` — the node menu's "Move into <group>" with explicit ids (a Join drop, which saves); ignored, with a note on stderr, unless the menu would offer that group (a sibling group). `EXPORTSTATE`'s `mirror_groups` maps every node id to its group as the instance shows it.
- `CLOSENODE <id>` — closes node `id` (any kind) as its card's Close or Delete does, setting `closed` on it so it lists in Recently Closed, and saves; an unknown id is ignored with a note on stderr. (It replaced `CLOSEGROUP`.) `EXPORTSTATE`'s card key `stored_type` is the type string as stored. It equals `type` except for an `image/*` other than `image/png`, a different case, or a type this build doesn't know, whose `type` is `application/vnd.manifold.unknown`.
- `REOPENNODE <id>` — reopens closed node `id` as the Recently Closed popup does (`reopen_node`), whichever replica closed it; `REOPENTAB` reopens the newest one this replica closed. `EXPORTSTATE`'s `closed` lists the Recently Closed ids newest first (`Doc::closed_roots`), and each entry of `links` has `shown`, true when both ends are cards on this replica (a link to a closed node stays stored and reads false).
- `GOBACK` / `GOFORWARD` — the focused tab's Back and Forward chips: a move in the tab's trail (the engine's own Back/Forward when its list agrees, else a load of the entry's URL), and at the trail's first page Back goes to the card the tab was opened from, Forward there back to the tab (`trail_nav.rs`; spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-trail-design.md). `EXPORTSTATE`'s `trail` maps each tab with a trail to `{entries, depth, url, back_fork, forward_fork, lin_at, lin_len, left_to, back, forward}`: `depth` is 0 at the first page, `back`/`forward` are the chips' enablement, `back_fork`/`forward_fork` whether the chips show a branch marker.
- `LINK <a> <b>` — links card `a` to card `b`, as `a`'s "Link to..." row and a click on `b` do, and saves; a refusal (`NotLive`, `DifferentLevel`, `Exists`, ...) goes to stderr. `UNLINK <a> <b>` removes the link between them in either order (silent when there is none) and saves. `OPENEDFROM <child> <parent>` sets `child`'s opener, as its "Opened from..." row does. `EXPORTSTATE`'s `links` lists every stored link as `{id, from, to}` (hidden or dangling ones included), and `connecting` is pick-target mode's `{from, edge, targets}` or null. `EXPORTSTATE`'s `edges` lists the lines drawn at this level as `{kind, link, from, to, screen}` (`screen`: the curve's middle, t = 0.5, where a probe clicks a link line). `link_handles` maps each card that would show connect handles to its `{top, right, bottom, left}` screen rects. `connect_drag` is a running connect-drag's source or null, `dragging` the card being dragged or null.
- `RENAMEGROUP <id> <name>` — commits `name` as node `id`'s new name, as its rename field's Enter does (any node; a group takes the clean name, another node stores `name` as an override of its label, and an empty name clears it; an unknown id is ignored with a note on stderr), and saves.
- `DUPLICATENODE <id>` — duplicates node `id` (the whole selection when `id` is in it) beside the original, as the context menu's Duplicate does: a tab through `duplicate_tab`, anything else, a group's subtree included, through a pure snapshot (`duplicate.rs`), offset by 40 px; saves. `RENAMEGROUP` with no arguments is still `F2`.
- `NEWJAR <name>` — a new jar card at the current level, as the Jar page's "New Jar..." makes, with no edge; prints its id. `USEJAR <id> <jar-id|none>` — tab or group `id` draws its `jar` edge to that jar card, or clears it with `none`, as a Jar page row does. `JAREPHEMERAL <id> on|off` — jar card `id`'s Make or Stop Ephemeral, confirmed. `EXPORTSTATE` cards carry `jar`, `effective_jar` and `is_default`.
- `SETNOTETEXT <id> <text>` — note `id`'s box typed to hold `text` (the rest of the line, possibly empty) and blurred, which commits it (resolving any remote text held meanwhile) and saves.
- `MOVENODE <id> <x> <y>` — card `id` dragged to world point (x, y) and released: its document pos is written as a drag release writes it, and saved. `EXPORTSTATE`'s `mirror_names` (groups), `mirror_texts` (notes) and `mirror_pos` (every node) map ids to the name, text and document pos the instance shows, null where `EXPORTSESSION` leaves the field out.
- `RESIZE <width> <height>`
- `LABEL <name>` makes a label (or finds the one with that name) and puts it on the selected cards; `LABELSELECTED <name> on|off` toggles it on the selection; `LABELRENAME <old> <new>`, `LABELDELETE <name>`, `LABELMENU` (opens the selection bar's menu) and `SELECTCARDS [id...]` (sets the selection). `EXPORTSTATE` lists `labels` and each card's `labels`.
- `SUGGEST` — the toolbar's Suggest groups: clusters this level's cards by page text and keeps the clusters as offers (`EXPORTSTATE` `group_suggestions`). `ACCEPTSUGGESTION <n>` groups the n-th offer's cards (from 0) in one new group, one undo step. `ACCEPTSUGGESTIONLABEL <n>` labels the n-th offer's cards instead (one undo step). `ARRANGE` — the toolbar's Arrange by topic: lays the level's cards out by page text (one undo step; refused with a toast above 300 cards).
- `WAITTABS [secs]`, `WAITIDLE [secs]`, `WAITINDEX [secs]`, `SLEEP <secs>` — hold the commands after them, in order (default 60 s at most for the first three). `WAITINDEX` waits until every live http(s) tab outside an ephemeral jar has the text of the page it shows, and the search index has taken it in (an engine that never answers `QueryPageText` holds it to the timeout); `WAITTABS` waits until no tab spawn is in flight; `WAITIDLE` until typed input (`ADDRESSBAR` text, queued keys) has landed, no view transition runs, the Graph camera isn't easing or gliding (while the Graph shows), no address-bar suggestion is on its way and the focused tab isn't loading, all for 3 frame ends in a row (it draws frames itself until then), so use it between `ADDRESSBAR` and `ENTER` and after a new tab's spawn, instead of a guessed `SLEEP`. A tab that hasn't reported its first load yet counts as not loading, so after an `ENTER` wait for something the page does (a request at its server) before `WAITIDLE`.
- `SETVISIBLE <true|false>` — sends `SetVisible` directly to the focused tab, bypassing `focus_tab`/`leave_to_graph`'s own visibility changes (which always come bundled with a `Resize`, since Tab vs. Graph view's chrome heights differ). For testing the background-tab suspend/resume path in isolation.
- `QUIT` — exits cleanly. Also sent automatically on stdin EOF, so a driving script that closes its write end doesn't leave the process hanging.

`gate smoke` (`crates/gate/src/smoke.rs`, the Rust port of the former `headless-smoke-test.sh`) is a runnable example (and a real regression smoke test): drives the address-bar autocomplete flow end to end through one headless instance on stdin and asserts each screenshot was actually produced and isn't suspiciously small, plus app state read back through the debug-only `EXPORTSTATE` command (whether it's in Tab view, the focused tab's URL, the dropdown's rows and which one is highlighted). It also covers Graph-view groups: entering and leaving one, tab drops inside one (reparent, the cycle refusal), ejecting a selected tab, and the overloaded and empty groups' border colors, read off a screenshot (read with the `image` crate). It also closes a group holding an image and a nested group and checks that `REOPENTAB` brings back the whole subtree, still nested. Those click cards where `EXPORTSTATE`'s `cards` puts them, polling until two exports agree, because the camera eases after entering a group. That's not visual-regression testing, but it catches wrong behavior as well as crashes. It polls that state instead of sleeping for DuckDuckGo's suggestions (a fixed 3 s wait was once too short, and an Escape with no dropdown up correctly left Tab view), and compares the searched query against the exact row it clicked or highlighted, so DuckDuckGo's changing order doesn't matter. `EXPORTSTATE` is separate from `EXPORTSESSION` because `EXPORTSESSION`'s JSON is the persisted import/export format, not a place for transient UI state. On a JSON level, `EXPORTSTATE`'s `json_level` gives the root node id, whether it is inline and its top-level `keys`, each visible card's pointer/position/collapsed state/row and page counts, the selected pointer and the current search's matches — the new block added for this covers entering a dropped JSON file's level, `JSONSELECT`/`JSONSEARCH` reaching it, and the back button leaving it. A card whose window has moved (`skip` > 0) also gives `earlier`, the screen point of its `+ N earlier` row (null otherwise); the smoke test's last JSON block drops a 1000-entry array, `JSONSELECT`s `/items/900` (the window moves, skip 900), `CLICK`s that point and checks the window grew up a page (skip 880). It runs in its own throwaway `APPDATA` profile (starting from just the root tab) and leaves other running manifold windows alone. Run it after `cargo build -p manifold`. Note for anything driving this from Git Bash/MSYS: a `SCREENSHOT` path travels over stdin, not argv, so it's not auto-translated from a POSIX path (`/c/Users/...`) the way a command-line argument to a spawned native exe would be — convert with `cygpath -w` first. Its suggestions come from a local server (`suggest.rs`'s `/ac/`, via the `MANIFOLD_SUGGESTIONS_URL` override of the autocomplete endpoint), so the address-bar checks no longer need the network (2026-10-02); the group-card checks stay timing-flaky (see Open below).

To run one session from a file of commands by hand: `target/debug/manifold.exe --headless --profile-dir DIR --script commands.txt` (`DIR` names the manifold folder itself, so the real `%APPDATA%\manifold` is never touched; the file's last line should be `QUIT`). A session to start from: `manifold.exe --import-json session.json --out DIR\session.mnf` builds the profile's session file from a JSON snapshot first. `gate profile` (crates/gate/src/profile.rs) is a worked example that writes its own command file and prints a per-phase table of redraws a second, frame time, input latency and CPU from the app's timing trace (`MANIFOLD_TRACE=<path>` writes Chrome trace events of the `frame`, `egui` and `render` spans, input arrivals and egui's repaint requests, `telemetry.rs`; Perfetto opens it; a script's `MARK label` cuts it into phases; `gate profile graph-idle --visible` measures a window with nothing to do); it needs a release exe at least as new as every source file under `crates/`, and says to rebuild otherwise. When `RUN_DIR` is set (`~/.claude/runs/run.ps1`), `gate profile` also writes the table's one-line summary as that run's `headline.txt`. `gate ab NAME SCENARIO [SECONDS] [--repeats N] [--quiet PCT] LABEL:VAR=value,...` (crates/gate/src/ab.rs) runs `gate profile --visible` once per arm per repeat, interleaved and each through `run.ps1` as `ab-NAME-LABEL-rN`, waits for total CPU under PCT (default 15) before each run, and prints the median frame time (with its range) and CPU per phase and arm; an arm is an environment switch such as `WGPU_BACKEND=vulkan`. `--debug-commands` runs the same file in a real window (`gate profile graph-frames 10 --samply --visible`), which tells egui it has focus, so scripted keys and clicks need no OS foreground (the elevated samply task cannot take it, and Windows blocks input injected from a non-elevated process into an elevated window). Piping commands on stdin to `--headless` also works (`gate launch-forms` launches that form).

`gate two` (`crates/gate/src/two/`, the Rust port of the former `two-instance-test.sh`) runs two (later a third, then a fourth, one at a time) headless instances on one shared, isolated `session.mnf` profile, exercising the store's multi-writer merge end to end. The scenarios keep the script's numbers 1 to 12 and run in the order 1, 2, 8 to 12, 3 to 7: (1) instance A imports a two-tab group and instance B adds a note, then both instances' `EXPORTSESSION` node sets agree; (2) A imports 200 notes -- enough ops to cross `COMPACT_MIN` and trigger a compaction on save -- while B stays open, B adds another note afterward, and both still agree with nothing lost (`session.mnf.prev` confirms A actually compacted); (8) A moves a new note into a new group (`MOVETOGROUP`) and B follows; (9) A and B move one note into two different groups back to back and end on one of them, nothing lost, no cycle; (10) A moves group G into H while B moves H into G, and both end on the same acyclic nesting without hanging; (11) A renames a group (`RENAMEGROUP`) and B follows, then both rename it back to back and end on one name; (12) A sets a note's text (`SETNOTETEXT`) and B follows, then both set it back to back and end on one text that a further save doesn't undo, then A moves the note (`MOVENODE`) and B's mirror and document follow. Scenarios 8 to 12 compare each node's group, name, text and pos in what A shows, what B shows (`EXPORTSTATE`'s `mirror_groups`, `mirror_names`, `mirror_texts`, `mirror_pos`) and the document. (3) A navigates a new tab to the nonce site, a local site that redirects to a fresh url on every load, and B's copy follows once without the two navigating each other (the site's request count must stop climbing); (4) A clicks a link on that site and B follows, then A's page navigates by script and B doesn't; likewise a single-page app's url change is followed from a click, not from a timer (the engine reports how each navigation started; the server counts urls asked for twice); (5) A navigates the shared tab to a local JSON file and B follows, and the document ends with exactly one more Json node, not one per instance; (6) A adds a note and is killed (`TerminateProcess`, not power loss -- the kill always comes right after an `EXPORTSESSION`, which saves first, so this exercises a replica vanishing, not recovery from a torn write), B exits cleanly, and a fresh third instance opened on the same profile still has A's note; (7) that third instance, now alone, types a url whose page navigates by script and quits, and a fourth reopens the tab at the script's url (the personal-state `live_url`), not the typed one. The nonce site is `crates/gate/src/nonce.rs`, served in process by a thread of `gate`, so the test reads its request counters directly; the JSON asserts (shared-node multiset, group, field agreement, no cycle) are `crates/gate/src/session.rs`. Every assertion prints a `PASS:` or `FAIL:` line (66 labels), polled until agreement instead of a fixed sleep. The stop rule: a failed precondition stops the run at once, a failed observation lets its scenario finish and then stops the run, because later scenarios build on the same document. `ENGINE=cef` runs scenarios 3, 4, 5 and 7 in CEF instead of WebView2. Build with `cargo build -j 8 -p manifold -p manifold-view-webview2 -p gate`.

`target/debug/gate.exe all LOG [full|light|ui]` (crates/gate/src/all.rs; was scripts/gate.sh) is the pre-merge gate: `gate two` three times (`full`, the default) or once (`light`), then `gate smoke`, `gate launch-forms`, `gate page-text`, `gate click-count`, `gate iframe-typing`, `gate surface`, `gate search`, `gate suggest-groups`, `gate arrange` (all four skipped in `ui`), `gate link`, `gate unknown-type`, `gate closed`, `gate trail`, `gate sleep` history on WebView2 (stage `sleep-history`) and `gate layout` once each, one after another, writing progress and verdicts to `LOG` (a `GATE <stage> start` and `GATE <stage> exit=` line per stage, `GATE done` last) and each run's output to `LOG.two-N.log`, `LOG.smoke.log`, `LOG.launch-forms.log`, `LOG.page-text.log`, `LOG.search.log`, `LOG.suggest-groups.log`, `LOG.arrange.log`, `LOG.link.log`, `LOG.unknown-type.log`, `LOG.closed.log`, `LOG.trail.log`, `LOG.sleep-history.log` and `LOG.layout.log`. Use `full` for any change to sync, the store, the mirror, session save/restore or an engine, and `light` when only UI code that writes the store or reaches an engine (gestures, menus) or scripts changed. `ui` runs `cargo test` of the three CI crates, `gate link`, `gate unknown-type`, `gate closed`, `gate trail`, `gate sleep` history and `gate layout` only (no `gate two` or smoke run), for drawing, input and view-state modules that import no store, sync or network module, and for docs. `gate tier [BASE]` (crates/gate/src/tier.rs; also `--stdin`) prints the tier a change needs from the paths it touches (advice only; with nothing changed since HEAD it asks for the commit the last gate ran at). After each stage `LOG` gets that stage's `TIMING <step> <seconds> <start>` lines with the CPU load at the step's start and its mean (`LOG.cpu` is the in-process sampler's record of the load, joined by time), and `GATE slowest steps` lists the ten longest. Build first with `cargo build -j 8 -p manifold -p manifold-view-webview2 -p gate` (the second crate is the WebView2 host, which `-p manifold` does not build; the probes refuse a stale host), then launch it detached at low priority from the repo root: `cmd //c start "" //b //belownormal target/debug/gate.exe all LOG light`. Measured 2026-10-09 at 30-35% CPU from other sessions, so upper bounds: `full` 5.5 minutes (`gate two` 57-68 s a run, smoke 70 s, launch-forms 23 s, link 29 s; measured 2026-10-11 at 38-45% CPU from other sessions), `light` 3.5, `ui` 2 (2026-10-09); under the old shell script (scripts/gate.sh, retired 2026-10-13), 2026-10-09: `full` 11 minutes (two-instance 106-136 s a run), 2026-10-08 under heavier load: 11.5 minutes at 35-90% CPU; it records CPU load and Defender state at the start and end. Running the runs in parallel was tried on 2026-10-07 and was worse: 53 minutes (the CPU is the limit), and all four runs failed on timing (scenario 5 and 7 waits, one smoke check) while other sessions and a probe also loaded the machine. It stays sequential. Fixed flake (2026-10-14): `gate link` failed about 1 run in 10 (a drag from a link's end or a click on its middle never grabbed the link: `retarget_preview` None, or the delete missed). Cause: every launch re-settles the cards with a force layout whose result varies with the node ids, and often packs the three tabs and the Default jar card edge to edge, so a card lay across the link curve at the probe's point (or within egui's 5-point hit radius of it, or over a handle) and took the press. Measured with a diagnostic dump of egui's hit-test at the press (the press's `hits.click` was a card, a card control or a link handle, not the Scene background). The stage now puts the cards at fixed world points (`spread` in crates/gate/src/link.rs, MOVENODE) at the start of the two instances that drag or click: 0 failures in 110 runs (80 with the diagnostic, 30 without), against about 1 in 10 before it. The app is right to give a press on a card to the card; the curve is under it. Seen once in 110 runs (2026-10-14): `edge_pt` found no link edge at s8 right after the restart. `WAITTABS` returns when no spawn is in flight, not when the store has delivered the cards and link nodes, so (2026-10-15) `Run::start` now waits for the restore by what it produces (three cards and the link edges the run expects) and fails naming the cards and edges it saw if they never arrive. The race itself was never reproduced, so the cause is likely but unproven; if the new "restore incomplete" error ever appears, its edges dump says whether the app delivered the link late.

Opt-in engine stages, in any mode, after `sleep-history`: `WITH_ENGINES="servo webkit"` adds `trail-servo` and `sleep-servo` (history, crash, restart) for `servo`, and `trail-webkit` and `sleep-webkit` for `webkit`, which also needs `SITE_HOST=<ip>` (the WSL gateway) and fails without it. The hosts must be set up first, see "Engine setup for the gate". Each stage's output goes to `LOG.<stage>.log`.

`gate scale <tabs>...` measures what a big session costs at startup: for each count, one headless instance on a fresh isolated profile restores that many tabs of a local page (served by the gate itself), checks the focused tab is live and every other tab asleep, and prints the time that took and, 15 s later, its process tree's process count, private memory and working set, broken down by process name. `ENGINE=cef` uses CEF tabs. Since 2026-10-08 the instance runs BelowNormal and the priority call sits inside the timed restore, so "restored in" figures are not comparable with earlier recorded runs.

`gate key` checks what a real page sees when the host sends keys (`KEYPAGE`): for WebView2 (default) or CEF (`VIEWS="cef"`, or `VIEWS="webview2 cef"` for both), a headless instance loads a local page (served in process by crates/gate/src/probes.rs) that reports each keydown's `code`, `key` and `keyCode`, and the script compares them with what the shared `key_codes!` table says each engine should send (CEF sends no `code`; modifiers are VK 16 on WebView2, 160 on CEF). Run it after editing `KeyCode` or an engine's key path. Scripts that feed the app through a growing command file must use `tail -n +1 -f`: plain `tail -f` starts with only the last 10 lines, so if it starts after the script has written them, the earliest commands vanish (this made the probe fail intermittently until 2026-09-30). Servo, WebKit and the winit table are not covered.

`gate page-kind` checks the page-kind detection on WebView2 (default) or CEF (`VIEWS=cef`): a tab restored at a local `application/json` response must become a Json node, one at an `image/png` an Image node, and a plain HTML page must stay a live tab (read back through `EXPORTSTATE`'s `cards[].type` and `loaded`, via `State` in `crates/gate/src/state.rs`; `SETTLE=secs` lengthens the 6 s wait for a slow machine). Servo and WebKit run the same `PAGE_KIND_SCRIPT` but are not covered. Both probes serve their pages in process (`probes.rs`). `gate hscroll` checks horizontal wheel scrolling the same way (a 6000 px wide page must move right on a positive dx, then back), and `gate key`, `page-kind`, `hscroll`, `bad-url` and `failed-spawn` each take 9-24 s (all in crates/gate/src/probes.rs; they replaced the bash probes, whose Git Bash forks cost seconds each).

`gate chrome-shots-sizes` runs `gate chrome-shots` at several window sizes (`SIZES="400 300,800 600,1920 1080"`, `VIEWS=` defaults to the views that click no fixed coordinate) into `target/shots-sizes/<W>x<H>/`, to look at a small or a large window. `gate chrome-shots` (crates/gate/src/shots.rs, with `shots_img.rs` for the sheet and hover report and `shots_net.rs` for the download server and fake proxy; it replaced the bash script and its Python helpers) screenshots the browser chrome in every view, to iterate on the UI (about 5 min for all of the default list; `VIEWS="tabview settings"` picks some, `SIZE="1280 800"`, `OUT=`, `CROP_PX=` tune it, and the hover and tab-stop knobs are env vars named in shots.rs). One headless run on a throwaway profile with a seeded session (a tab, a group holding a tab, a note, an image, a JSON file; every tab is `about:blank` so no page content competes). Per view it writes `target/shots/<view>.png` (the whole window), `target/shots/crop/<view>.png` (the top strip, toolbar and address bar) and `sheet.png` (all views in a grid; the crops and sheet). Views: Graph (plain, hover, tab/group/note context menus, a link and the tab menu's Links section, a hovered link line, a card's connect handles and a connect-drag mid-way, filter), inside a group, a JSON level, Tab view (plain, address-bar dropdown, find bar, Tab actions), settings page, shortcuts help, quick switcher. Card positions come from `EXPORTSTATE` (clamped to the window, since a card can hang off the edge) and each shot waits until two exports agree, so the camera has stopped easing. There is only a dark theme (`theme.rs`), so there is one set of images. Also the Split pane, the Recently Closed popup, and the Bookmarks, History and Downloads popups (opened from the settings page, in a taller window so they open below their buttons; the profile is seeded with rows for them). Also the new-node menu (right-click on empty canvas), a toolbar tooltip, the image viewer, the engine pickers (settings default, settings Current Tab, Tab view Split), the search-engine dropdown, a JSON bar tooltip, and a toast (opening a session that does not exist), a real download in flight and finished (`download-progress`, `download-real`), and the new-node menu zoomed out (`newnode-zoom`). Also the CEF install states (`engine-states-picker`, `engine-states-settings`, `engine-install-confirm`: one extra instance on a copy of the exe walks Not installed, Failed, Installing, Installed, Update needed and Custom). The opt-in `hover-*` and `focus-graph` views check that hovering or focusing a widget changes nothing else (see Hover no longer resizes buttons). Build first: a stale exe is refused.

`gate sleep` checks manual Sleep and idle auto-sleep on WebView2 and CEF (`CASES=idle`, `VIEWS=cef` to narrow): a seeded tab is put to sleep with `SLEEPNODE id`, or by `IDLESWEEP 9999` then `IDLESWEEP 2` three seconds apart, and woken again, and `EXPORTSTATE` must read the tab card as loaded, not loaded, loaded (`cards[].loaded`; the card's `type` stays `application/vnd.manifold.tab` throughout). Two more cases use a tall page (`/tall`) that reports its scroll position to the server: `scroll` scrolls it with PageDown, sleeps and wakes it, and the woken page must end within 2 px of where it was; `dirty` fills its input, and the idle sweep must leave it awake while `SLEEPNODE` still sleeps it. CEF runs from `target/debug/cef-runtime/`, so run `scripts/sync-runtime-deps.sh` after building or the probe runs an old CEF engine.

`gate unresponsive` suspends a WebView2 engine host and types into its page: the card must read `unresponsive` with a "Not responding" toast, `RESTARTVIEW` must bring it back, and `QUIT` with a second suspended host must end within 10 s. Not part of the gate (it suspends processes).

`gate jar` checks the cookie jar end to end in a throwaway `APPDATA` (about 5 min; build first): a WebView2 tab logs in at `scripts/cookie_server.py` on `jar.localhost` (a name, so host-only and domain cookies differ; `JAR_PROBE_HOST=127.0.0.1` for an IP), the jar file must then hold one HttpOnly, host-only `sid` (DPAPI-protected), and fresh CEF and WebView2 runs must send it on their first request. A two-tab run reports, without failing either way, whether a second WebView2 tab sees the first one's login (it does not: each live host has its own profile). Three runs check the pooled WebView2 profile names: a name reused after a browser restart starts signed out; a tab that logged in and closed hands its name to the next tab in the same live browser, which must be signed out; and ten tabs opened and closed in turn must add no name outside the pool to WebView2's `Local State`. Two runs check per-tab jars: `perjar` seeds named jars Alice and Bob with a WebView2 tab on each signing in as its own probe user (`/login?u=alice`, `/login?u=bob`), and each login must land in its own jar's file with the default jar untouched; `jarswitch` restarts on that profile, focuses Alice's tab, moves it to Bob with `SETJAR Bob` and `SWITCHJARNOW`, and its next `/whoami` must come back as Bob. `switchnow` (with a keeper WebView2 tab holding the browser open) signs a tab in as alice on jar Alice, moves it to the empty jar Bob with `SWITCHJARNOW`, and its reload and next `/whoami` must be signed out; it then signs in as bob, and `switchclear` restarts on that profile, clears Bob (`CLEARJAR Bob`) and switches now: the tab must be signed out again, and after a pull and the quit pull Bob's jar must hold no `sid`. `RUNS="perjar jarswitch"` runs only those (jarswitch needs perjar). It prints counts, flags, cookie names, probe usernames and the test domain only, never values; host `jar:` log lines come from `%TEMP%\manifold-<tab>.log`.

`gate layout` (`crates/gate/src/layout.rs`; about 5 min, one launch per window size and page kind, build first) opens every menu, dialog and dropdown the headless app can (the shortcuts dialog, quick switcher, empty-canvas menu, the tab, note and group menus, and the Settings page's Bookmarks, History and Downloads popups) at 800x600, 480x360 and 1280x800, and checks two things per popup and size. *Inside the window*: every `popups` entry of `EXPORTSTATE` (the visible Foreground and Tooltip layers' rects, with the `window` size, from `App::popups_state`) lies within the window. *Nothing shows through*: the popup is shot over the demo cards, then again with every other card moved far away, and its rect (inset 4 pt for the shadow and rounded corners) must be pixel-identical within `BLEED_PIXELS` (a blinking caret). Card text or a chip painting over a popup, or a popup drawn under the cards, makes them differ. The quick switcher compares only its header: its tab list changes as titles load. Each shot waits with frames drawn, since a command draws one frame and `SLEEP` none. A failure prints the differing pixels' box; `LAYOUT_KEEP=<dir>` copies the failing PNG pairs there. It cannot see a layering fault that looks the same both ways, or a popup opened by a toolbar click (those commands are coordinates). The three Settings popups open through `TOGGLEPOPUP` (it opens the page for their anchors) in their own instance; with no cards on that page their no-bleed check can only catch a fault in the page itself, so it is the inside-the-window check that counts there (all pass, 2026-10-12). First run (2026-10-11): the shortcuts dialog was wider than a 480 px window and the tab menu taller than a 360 px one; the dialog now narrows and wraps, and `build_list_popup_frame` scrolls any list taller than the window.

`gate link` (`crates/gate/src/link.rs`, the port of the former `link-probe.sh`) checks links end to end (about 30 s; build first): five headless runs at BelowNormal on one throwaway profile seeded with three `about:blank` tabs (a, b, and c below a). The first `LINK`s them and `EXPORTSTATE`'s `links` must hold one link from the first to the second; after a restart it must still be there with the same id and ends; `UNLINK` with the ends in the other order removes it, and a third run must still have none. The third run links them again, deletes the link by clicking the middle of its curve (`EXPORTSTATE`'s `edges[].screen`, which must be clear of both cards), links them by dragging from the first tab's right handle onto the second, which must not move the first, then unlinks and links them again by releasing 15 points outside the second (inside the drop tolerance), with `EXPORTSTATE` taken mid-drag listing handles on every card. The fourth run must still have that link, then drags from the curve near b's end (`edges[].near_to`) onto c: mid-drag `connect_drag` is a and `retarget` the link, and afterwards the same Link node must run a to c; a drag from the c end ended with Esc must leave it as it was; a second link a to b dragged from its b end onto c (already joined to a) must be deleted, leaving a to c, and the a to c link dragged from its a end and released on empty canvas must be deleted. Mid-drag `retarget_preview` must read retarget over c, refused over the fixed card, and delete over the duplicate's c and over empty canvas. The fifth run must have no link.

`gate unknown-type` (`crates/gate/src/unknown_type.rs`) checks the unknown-type card end to end (about 30 s; build first). Two headless runs at BelowNormal share a throwaway profile seeded with a tab, a group and one node of type `application/x-from-the-future`. The first run finds the card (EXPORTSTATE `type` `application/vnd.manifold.unknown`, `stored_type` the real string), moves it (`MOVENODE`, `mirror_pos`), renames it, links it to the tab, duplicates it and moves it into the group. The second run checks that the group, the link and the stored type and name of both cards persisted, then closes the card (`CLOSENODE`): it leaves the graph and the export but stays stored, and `EXPORTSTATE` `closed` lists it. `REOPENTAB` brings back the same node, in its group and with its link to the tab.

`gate closed` (`crates/gate/src/closed.rs`) checks the closed field end to end (about 2 min; build first). One throwaway profile is seeded with a group holding a note, an outside note linked to the inner one, and 21 more notes. Instance A closes the group (`CLOSENODE`): `EXPORTSTATE` `closed` lists it, the card leaves the graph and its link reads `shown` false; `REOPENNODE` brings back the same ids, the member in its group and the link shown. Then A and a second instance B on the one profile close notes in turn, each waiting until the other lists the close: both list the same history newest first. The 21st close makes both evict the same oldest node.

`gate trail` (`crates/gate/src/trail.rs`) checks the trail end to end (about 6 s; build first; `ENGINE=servo` runs it on a Servo host copied beside `manifold.exe`, built with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-servo`). One throwaway profile is seeded with tab A on a local site the gate serves itself. A opens tab B (`NEWTAB`), which goes to two more pages; `GOBACK` twice walks B's pages, a third `GOBACK` focuses A (opened-from), whose `EXPORTSTATE` `trail` then names B in `left_to`, and `GOFORWARD` returns to B. A new page from B's first page makes a sibling of the second (`entries` 4, `depth` 1, `back_fork` true). After the fork, `TOGGLEVIEW` to Graph view must list four `"virtual": "trail"` cards (one per page; the click itself is tested in `trail_strip.rs`, as the gate has no world-to-screen click). A last page calls `history.back()` itself (a timer) and must read as a Back: no new entry, Forward offered. Then, in Graph view at 100%, a real press and pointer moves drag tab A's card over the strip's second page (`dragging` names A and the two rects overlap), a screenshot is kept as `drag-over-strip.png` in `RUN_DIR` for a look at the layering (not asserted), and the card goes back. Then B goes to a second site (another port, so another origin) that serves a red `/favicon.ico`; from tab A the address bar's dropdown must lead B's row with an `image` identity (this caught a typed navigation never asking for the new origin's favicon).

**WebKit under the gate (passes, 2026-10-15):** `ENGINE=webkit gate trail` passed 6 of 6 runs in about 7 s each. Setup: the host built in WSL (`CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-linux cargo build -p manifold-view-webkitgtk`), the gate's page server reachable from WSL (it binds `127.0.0.1`, which WSL cannot reach: set `SITE_HOST` to the WSL gateway IP, `wsl.exe -d Ubuntu -- ip route`, the default route's `via`, and the server binds and is addressed there; `ENGINE=webkit SITE_HOST=<ip> gate trail`), and Windows Firewall allowing inbound for `manifold.exe` and `gate.exe` (a Block rule stops the host connecting). Two bugs made earlier runs hang or crash-loop. (1) `wsl.exe` inherited manifold's piped stdin, which carries the gate's debug commands, and took them: every engine host is now spawned with a null stdin (`2876bfe`). (2) The host sent `uri()`, the pending page from the moment a load starts, with `can_go_back`/`can_go_forward`, which follow the history at commit, so a poll could pair a new url with the old page's flags and a page-script Back read as a fork (failed 4 of 6 runs); the host now sends the back/forward list's current item, which moves with the flags. With `VIEWS=webkit SITE_HOST=<ip>`, `gate sleep` passes the manual, idle, scroll and dirty cases (2026-10-15); its history case (Back twice and Forward once after a wake) passes too, after two fixes: a committed move holds the origin's late settled report (`nav_history.rs`), and the probe waits for the trail to reach the new url before the next press. Two concurrent `xvfb-run -a` took distinct displays in 10 of 10 tries. Servo (2026-10-15): `VIEWS=servo gate sleep` passes crash, restart and history (3 of 3 after the fix). After a wake Servo reports the old page's settled load around the move's own reports, in varying order (before the target's loading report, between its loading and settled reports, or after); the committed move holds that report until its deadline whatever order it comes in, and `handle_view_message` keeps the tab's url and title when `observe` says the report was held that way. `gate sleep` history on WebView2 flakes under CPU load (about 1 in 6 to 1 in 2), with or without that change: the 3 s `MOVE_WINDOW` expires between the origin's loading and settled reports, the target's arrival then forks a child of the origin, and the second Back lands on the origin. With a 30 s window it passed 10 of 10 (2026-10-15). It runs in `gate all` as `sleep-history`, so a failure there under load is this flake: rerun it on an idle machine. Open, not gated. `gate sleep` history is not in `gate all` yet: it passes on an idle machine (WebView2 3 of 3, WebKit 2 of 2) but failed 5 of 5 on WebView2 with the CPU at about 99% from other sessions, and passed 3 of 3 there with `MOVE_WINDOW` raised from 3 s to 30 s (checked 2026-10-15). Cause: under saturation the engine's report for a plain Back's target takes more than the 3 s move window, the move is dropped, and the target then arrives as a new page, a fork. That is the window working as designed, not a trail bug, and `gate all` assumes an idle machine anyway; the stage can be added once a full run on an idle machine passes it. On Servo the entry the Back moves to has its url rewritten to the old page's (the trail shows `/p3` for the `/p2` entry), which the probe reads as `back=p3`. A try that kept the held report from the tab (`observe` returning false and the event loop returning early) hung the headless export, because the early return skips the repaint the export waits on.

`gate launch-forms` (crates/gate) launches the exe once in each form its launchers and session tools use (about 6 s; build first), on a temp profile and under `timeout 60`, and checks the exit codes: `drive.sh` 0, `--headless --profile-dir --script` 0 (the form `json-pan-profile.sh` uses), `--import-json`/`--out` 0, `--dump` 0, `--check` 0, and a headless run with a misspelled `--hedless` refused with exit 2 and `unknown argument "--hedless"` on stderr. `--debug-commands` is not launched (it opens a window; a unit test covers it). Both probes run in `scripts/gate.sh`, in `light` and `full`.
