`gate focus-loss` checks that losing window focus mid-drag ends the drag, for a connect-drag and a card drag (about 80 s; `CASES=connect` or `CASES=card` for one; build first). It steals the OS foreground for a few seconds per case, so don't type meanwhile. Each case is one headless run at BelowNormal on its own throwaway profile seeded with two `about:blank` tabs. `connect` presses the left tab's right handle and drags halfway to the right tab; `connect_drag` must be set. `scripts/foreground.ps1` then gives the instance's off-screen window the foreground, checks `GetForegroundWindow`, and at once takes it for a throwaway off-screen form (which it closes; the foreground goes back to the window that had it, unless something else took it meanwhile or it was a manifold window). That real `Focused(false)` must clear `connect_drag`, and neither a late button-up over the right tab nor a click on it may link. If Windows refuses the foreground it prints `SKIP` and exits 0. `card` drags the left tab by its centre until that is over the right tab (`EXPORTSTATE`'s `dragging` must name it), steals the same way, and then `dragging` must be null, the tab's document pos saved (`mirror_pos`), the tab must stay put while the pointer moves on with the stale button down, and a late button-up back over the right tab must group nothing (a drop of a tab on a tab groups them). `NO_STEAL=1` is a control run: the drags survive, the late button-up links (`connect`) or groups (`card`), so those checks FAIL.

`gate bad-url` checks that an engine host given an initial URL it can't open shows `about:blank` instead of panicking (under a minute when it passes, about 3 when it fails; build first; `VIEWS` defaults to `webview2`). For a tab stored with the url `not a url`, and one with no url, a headless instance opens the tab (`GRAPHDOWN`, `GRAPHOPEN`) and polls `EXPORTSTATE`; the tab must end loaded with `focused_url` `about:blank` (proof the host reached its initial navigate), and its host log must hold the host's `opening about:blank` line and no `panicked`. Hosts log to `%TEMP%\manifold-<tab>.log`, so the probe points `TEMP`/`TMP` into its own directory.

`gate surface` (`crates/gate/src/surface.rs`; `SURFACE outdated|lost` makes the next frame see that window-surface status, so `gpu.rs`'s reconfigure and recreate branches run without an eGPU replug; `EXPORTSTATE`'s `surface_recoveries` counts them and a screenshot follows each; no engine needed; the counter shows the branch ran, not that the new surface is the one drawn to, 2026-10-24), `gate iframe-typing` (`crates/gate/src/iframe_typing.rs`; a click and `TYPE abc` into an iframe of another origin, which reports each edit to its own site; passes on WebView2, CEF, Servo and WebKit, 2026-10-24, with the CEF and WebKit caveats in `PARKED.md`), `gate click-count` (`crates/gate/src/click_count.rs`; `gate all` runs it with the others; `DOUBLECLICK` and `TRIPLECLICK` land on a one-line page, which reports each release's click count and its selection: a word after two, the paragraph after three; passes on WebView2, CEF and WebKit, 2026-10-24; Servo reports the counts but selects nothing, see `PARKED.md`), `gate page-text`, `gate search`, `gate suggest-groups` and `gate arrange` (`crates/gate/src/{page_text,search,suggest_groups,arrange}.rs`; `gate all` runs them in `full` and `light`; `gate page-text` takes `VIEWS=servo|cef|webkit` for the other engines, passing on all four 2026-10-24, with `SITE_HOST=<wsl gateway>` for webkit) check the Search milestone end to end. `page-text` loads a page on the local site and reads back the text the app stored (`TEXTOF`); `search` finds a card by a word only its page holds (`WAITINDEX`, then `omni_matches`); `suggest-groups` offers and accepts a group from six notes; `arrange` moves the cats nearer each other than the rust notes (stored positions from `EXPORTSESSION`) and undoes it. The last two need no engine; the first two need `cargo build -p manifold -p manifold-view-webview2`.

`scripts/foreground-watch.ps1` checks that a headless instance never takes the OS foreground (about 12 s; build first; `powershell -File scripts/foreground-watch.ps1 [-Engine cef] [-Permissive] [-Visible]`). It starts one `--headless` instance on a throwaway `APPDATA` with one tab, polls `GetForegroundWindow` every 50 ms and logs each change (window, class, title, process), each window the instance and its engine host create, and each of their threads' own active window, then ends with `VERDICT foreground unchanged` or `VERDICT foreground taken by ...`. Whether a background process may take the foreground depends on the moment (refused while you are typing in another app, allowed when nothing has it or the foreground lock timed out), so `-Permissive` forces the worst case: an off-screen form takes the foreground first and opens it to every process (`AllowSetForegroundWindow(ASFW_ANY)`); it steals the foreground for the run. Found 2026-10-07: winit's default `SW_SHOW` activated the headless window, and the WebView2 host's window activated on its first `ShowWindow` and on `MoveFocus`; either took the foreground whenever allowed (once out of a fullscreen game). Now the headless window is created `with_active(false)` and the host window `WS_EX_NOACTIVATE`. `-Visible` runs a normal on-screen window (`--debug-commands`) instead: it must take the foreground and still have it at `QUIT`. Before the fix the WebView2 host took it from a normal window too, on every WebView2 tab start; after it, three of four `-Visible -Permissive` runs kept it, and in one the window lost it (to `msrdc`, 100 ms after the host's `MoveFocus`). Rechecked 2026-10-07 against a control host built without `MoveFocus`: with it, 9 of 10 visible runs (`-Visible`, `-Visible -Permissive`) kept the foreground through `QUIT`, holding it 4 to 12 s after the host's `MoveFocus` where that came early enough to see; without it, 9 of 9 kept it; headless `-Permissive` was unchanged in 3 of 3 each. The one loss went to a freshly spawned WSLg `msrdc` (its `wslhost.exe` started 6 s before) whose RemoteApp window took the foreground 1.3 s before the engine host even started, so `MoveFocus` played no part, and the original loss to `msrdc` was most likely the same. Without `MoveFocus` the host's thread never activates its window at all. `MoveFocus` stays.


**Engine setup for the gate** (each engine's host built and placed once; then `ENGINE=<name>` or `VIEWS=<name>` picks it for a stage, `VIEWS` winning when both are set, or `WITH_ENGINES` adds it to `gate all`). Run from the repo root, 2026-10-24:
- *Servo* (a cold build is about 33 minutes, a warm one seconds): `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-servo cargo build -j 6 -p manifold-view-servo`, then `cp target-servo/debug/manifold-view-servo.exe target/debug/`.
- *CEF* (needs the CEF download that `cef-dll-sys` makes): `cargo build -j 6 -p manifold-view-cef`, then `target/debug/gate.exe sync-runtime-deps` (it copies the host and runtime into `target/debug/cef-runtime/`).
- *WebKitGTK* (WSL Ubuntu with `webkit2gtk` and `xvfb`): `wsl.exe -d Ubuntu -- bash -lc 'cd /mnt/c/.../manifold && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-linux cargo build -j 6 -p manifold-view-webkitgtk'`, find the gateway with `wsl.exe -d Ubuntu -- ip route` (the default route's `via`), and run the stage with `SITE_HOST=<that ip>`; Windows Firewall must allow inbound for `manifold.exe` and `gate.exe`.
- Check one with `VIEWS=<servo|cef|webkit> gate page-text` (4-5 s); `trail` and `sleep` take `ENGINE=` / `VIEWS=` the same way.

**Building Servo on its own:** `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target-servo cargo build -p manifold-view-servo` keeps its dependency tree out of `target/` so it can be deleted alone. Measured 2026-09-30: 3.9 GB for a full debug build (33 minutes at `-j 6`), almost all in `build/` (`mozjs_sys` 0.8 GB), so there is little to trim; `target-servo` is git-ignored.

Both scripts run `target/debug/manifold.exe` as it is, and `cargo check`/`test`/`clippy` don't rebuild it, so both refuse an exe older than any source it was built from (read from cargo's `manifold.d` dep-info; `gate fresh`, crates/gate/src/fresh.rs). The WebView2 host beside it (`manifold-view-webview2.exe`, read from its own `.d`) is judged the same way. Build with `cargo build -p manifold -p manifold-view-webview2` (`-p manifold` alone does not build the host), or set `ALLOW_STALE=1` to run the old one on purpose.

**Keyboard-shortcut reference popup**: `F1`, or a new `?` icon button next to Settings on both Tab and Graph toolbars, opens a centered popup listing every global shortcut `handle_global_shortcuts` handles (New Tab, Close Tab, Reopen Closed Tab, Duplicate, Next/Prev Tab, Quick Switcher, Tab/Graph toggle, New Group, Rename Group, Pin, Focus Address Bar, Find, Reload, Zoom in/out/reset, DevTools, and this popup itself) -- so a shortcut added over the session's course isn't only discoverable by reading the source. Static content, no staged state -- just an `Area`/`Frame` with a scrollable two-column `Grid` and a Close button. New `TOGGLESHORTCUTSHELP` debug command for headless parity. **Bug found and fixed while wiring up the button**: the first version passed `anchor_rect: None` to the shared `clicked_outside_popup` helper (see the click-outside dedup above) since a `?` button has no state-driven position to defer past -- but that meant the very click that opened the popup, still live in `ui.input()` the same frame, was itself seen as an "outside" click (correctly outside the popup's own rect, which the settings gear's button also is -- the reason it always passed its own rect there) and closed the popup back immediately. `F1` never showed the bug since a key press isn't a pointer click. Fixed by threading the button's own `Response::rect` through as `anchor_rect`, same as every other anchored popup already does. Verified live: `F1` toggled the popup open and closed repeatedly; separately confirmed the button now does too, matching the gear icon's own behavior.

## Refactor candidates (survey, 2026-09-24)

*Historical: the line numbers and type names below predate the session-store rewrite and the 2026-09-30 `main.rs` split.*

All eight done (item 8's fetcher half was ruled not worth it). Ranked by value for the risk.

1. **Done (fixed): Remove from Group behaved two ways.** A tab card's hover eject button moves the node to the group's *parent* (`build_graph_ui`, `node.group = grandparent`). The tab menu's and the item menu's "Remove from Group" set `node.group = None`, which sends a node in a nested group straight to the top level. The tab menu's doc says it has the same scope as the eject button. Fix: one `App::remove_from_group(id)` that all three call.
2. **Done: headless keys as real egui key events.** `ENTER`/`ESCAPE`/`ARROWDOWN`/`ARROWUP` set four `pending_debug_*` flags that the UI code ORs into its `key_pressed` checks, and `ADDRESSBAR` pins the bar with `debug_address_bar_pin` instead of focusing it. Pushing `egui::Event::Key`/`Text` into `raw_input` in `RedrawRequested` would test the real keyboard path. It would also remove the flags, the ORs, the pin, and the leaked-arrow class of bug (an injected event lasts one frame). The "no public `KeyEvent` constructor" reason is about winit, not egui. Needs a check that a focused `TextEdit` doesn't eat the keys the dropdown reads, which is already true for real key presses. Done that way (the smoke test passes). It also surfaced that egui reports no widget focus in an unfocused window, so headless `FOCUSADDRESS` had never actually focused the bar; headless now marks the window focused for egui.
3. **Done: menus and popups share one frame.** `build_list_popup_frame` exists, but 15 popups hand-build the same `Area` + `Frame` (`TOOLBAR_FILL`, weak stroke) with drifted corner radii (2/3/4/6) and margins (4/6/8/10/14). The link, tab, group and tab-actions menus are menus, and should use the helper. Dialogs (JSON viewer, import confirm) can keep their larger margins. This is a small visual change, so decide the canonical margin. Done as two tiers: `popup_frame` (radius 4, margin 6) for every menu and dropdown, and `dialog_frame` (radius 6, margin 14) for the centered dialogs. The find bar and save toast use `popup_frame` with their own wider margin. The tab-view hover chip keeps its borderless frame. The address-bar dropdown now subtracts the frame's margin from its width, so it lines up with the bar instead of overhanging it.
4. **Done: tab and group menus into `NodeMenuConfig`.** `build_tab_context_menu` (168 lines) and `build_group_context_menu` (180) each carry a copy of the color-swatch row and their own Remove from Group and close handling. Extending `NodeMenuConfig`/`NodeMenuAction` (pin, duplicate, copy URL, swatches, rename, dissolve, export) gives one builder and one apply function. Do this after 1 and 3. Done: a menu is now a list of `NodeMenuItem`s (button, color row, separator) that `node_menu_config` builds from the frozen `NodeMenuView`. One `build_node_context_menu` draws every kind, and one `apply_node_menu_action` applies the result. The group rename field is a mode of that builder. Behavior is unchanged, apart from a little less space around the tab and group menus' separators, and closing a tab or group from its menu no longer saves the session twice. A group's own menu still has no Remove from Group, though groups nest.
5. **Done: graph actions as an enum.** `build_graph_ui` (2.5k lines) defers about 30 click results through separate `Option`/`bool` locals, applied by a long `if let` chain. A `Vec<GraphAction>` with one `apply_graph_action` shrinks both ends. Check for ordering dependencies between the current `if`s first. Done: `build_graph_ui` pushes `GraphAction`s and applies them in click order through `apply_graph_action`. Card buttons that act on one node (✕, pin, eject, swatch, mute, reveal, open) are `GraphAction::Node(id, NodeAction)`, the same actions the right-click menu applies. The one real ordering dependency was commit-rename before Leave Group. It's gone at the source: `CommitRename` carries the typed name. Enter and Leave Group share `switch_group_level`. The empty-graph early return now applies every toolbar action, not just New Tab. Entering and leaving a group was verified later, once headless mode had `DOUBLECLICK`. Two `CLICK`s count as a double-click only when their frames land within egui's 0.3 s delay, and slow headless frames break that. `DOUBLECLICK` puts both clicks in one frame. The smoke test now enters a group by double-clicking it and leaves with the back button.
6. **Done: seven per-kind lists and minimap loops.** `tabs_at_level` … `groups_at_level` and seven near-identical minimap dot loops could be one loop over a draw-order `[ItemNodeKind; 7]` constant The right-click hit test already lists that order. Small and safe. Done, then replaced in 2b-6 by `Caps::draw_layer` and `App::level_scan`: `GRAPH_DRAW_ORDER` drove the per-level lists (`at_level`, still destructured into named lists for the kind-specific loops), the LOD count, settle key, minimap bounds and dots, the right-click hit test and `current_level_settle_key`.
7. **Done: Tab and Group cards onto the shared frame.** All seven kinds now go through `draw_graph_card`: one loop over the draw layers (then `GRAPH_DRAW_ORDER`), per-kind differences in `match` tables (`card_style`, `card_border`, `card_controls`, `drop_action`, `paint_card_body`). Every kind eases its hover with glow and fill brightening, shows the swatch (always) and the eject button (hover, in a group), and closes on middle-click; every node menu has the Colors row. One drop table covers every kind, so tabs can join a nested group's card and any card can leave through the back button. See `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-24-tab-group-card-frame-design.md`. Reparent (a tab dropped on a sibling tab inside a group) and its cycle refusal are confirmed headless under full CPU load; an earlier failure was the probe exporting positions mid camera-ease after entering the group, not the drop. (Fixed since: a merged-away group's stale hover and rename state, now cleared like a dissolved one's; an ejected tab staying in the rectangle selection; the selection wash tinting the thumbnail.)
8. **Done (engine half): one host-reader helper.** The four engine crates each copy the same "forward `HostToEngine` into an mpsc until `Shutdown`" reader thread, which could be one `ipc_protocol` helper. `favicon_fetch`/`pdf_render`/`redirect_fetch`/`suggestions` share a thread + channel + `tick()` shape. Each copy is short, so these are low value. Done for the engines: `ipc_protocol::transport::spawn_host_reader` is the one reader thread, and it turns a dropped control socket into `Shutdown`. Only WebKit and Servo did that before. CEF and WebView2 just ended the thread, and their main loops' `try_recv` can't tell a hung-up channel from an empty one. So if manifold died without sending `Shutdown`, those engines would keep running, most likely stopped only by the Job Object. Checked with `cargo check` for `ipc-protocol` and WebView2 only: CEF's build script needs the CEF download, and WebKit and Servo don't build on Windows. The four fetchers were left alone: they differ in what `tick()` does with results, so a shared helper would save little.

## Refactor candidates (second survey, 2026-09-24)

*Historical: the line numbers and type names below predate the session-store rewrite and the 2026-09-30 `main.rs` split.*

Items 1-5 are done; each says how. Ranked by value for the risk. Line numbers are for `crates/manifold/src/main.rs` at `73f40cf` unless stated otherwise. Item 5 has since moved most of that code into modules, so look a name up with grep rather than by line. main.rs was 13,931 lines then (2,548 now; see the 2026-09-30 update under item 4 for current function sizes). Its longest functions were `build_graph_ui` (1,149 lines, ~10833), `paint_card_body` (637, ~5607), `build_settings_page` (456, ~4065), `drain_tab_events` (438, ~12919), `window_event` (420, ~12088, of which `RedrawRequested` is ~215), `handle_debug_command` (349, ~12560), `build_tab_view_ui` (343, ~10362), `render_tab_toolbar_content` (213, ~10138), `handle_tab_spawn_result` (155, ~5263) and `build_bookmarks_popup` (147, ~8271).

1. **Done: carry a tab's spawn inputs in one struct.** `spawn_tab_async` (~5218) takes 11 positional arguments: `id, parent, group, pinned, color, zoom_factor, url, pos, vel, purpose, kind`. `TabSpawnOutcome` (~3151) repeats the same 10 fields and adds `generation` and `result`. Six of the seven call sites (`apply_session` ~5006, ~5067, ~5104, ~5164, ~6904, `import_group_export` ~9367) pass the same `None, false, None` for `group`/`pinned`/`color`. Only `respawn_tab` (~7325) passes real values. Every attribute that has to survive a respawn is threaded through by hand in four places: the signature, the outcome, `respawn_tab`, and `handle_tab_spawn_result`. The README says `color` was added "the same way `pinned` already was", and `zoom_factor` followed. Proposed: a `TabSpawn { id, url, kind, pos, vel, parent, group, purpose, carry: TabCarry }`, where `TabCarry { pinned, color, zoom_factor }` has a `Default` that takes zoom from settings. Then `TabSpawnOutcome { spawn: TabSpawn, generation, result }`. The next carried attribute becomes a single field. Small and mechanical, and the compiler finds every site. Check first: `zoom_factor` defaults to `self.settings.zoom_factor` at some sites and to a local `zoom` at others (~5110, ~5170), so `Default` can't hide that difference. Done: `spawn_tab_async(TabSpawn)`, with `TabSpawn::new` taking the zoom explicitly (so no `Default`) and `TabSpawn::respawn` reading parent, group, position and `TabCarry` off the old node for both swap-finish and crash recovery. Checked headless by killing a pinned tab's engine host: crash recovery brought it back pinned.

2. **Done: make Recently Closed one chronological list of subtree snapshots.** Closed items live in three lists, each with its own cap, push-and-trim code and reopen path. `closed_tabs` (`MAX_CLOSED_TABS` 10, push ~7207) holds `ClosedTab`. `closed_groups` (`MAX_CLOSED_GROUPS` 5, ~9212) holds `group_export::GroupExport`. `closed_nodes` (`MAX_CLOSED_NODES` 10, ~4630) holds `ClosedNode`. `build_recently_closed_popup` (~4531) draws them as three sections, groups first, not in close order. It tracks a separate `reopen_*_index` for each list. `Ctrl+Shift+T` only reaches `closed_tabs` (~8172). The split also loses data (reproduced headless 2026-09-25: closing `grp-0 { image, grp-1 { tab } }` listed two entries, "Inner (1)" and the image, and reopening both left them loose at the top level with the outer group gone). `close_group` (~9209) snapshots through `export_group` (~9303), which records only the group's *direct tab* members. A nested subgroup is closed recursively and pushed as its own top-level `closed_groups` entry. Note, Image, PDF and JSON members go to `closed_nodes` without their group. A group with no direct tabs isn't recorded at all, even when its subgroups have tabs. So reopening a nested group brings back a flattened group, a separate sibling group and loose top-level cards. `close_group` also has to truncate `closed_tabs` back afterwards (~9218, ~9228) to cancel the side effect of its own `close_tab` calls. Proposed: one `closed: VecDeque<ClosedEntry>` with a single cap. Each entry is a subtree snapshot (root node plus descendants, with kind payloads, relative positions and group links). There is one `record_closed` and one `reopen_closed(index)`. `Ctrl+Shift+T` pops the newest entry of any kind, which is how browsers behave. Nested closes append to the outer snapshot instead of pushing their own entries, so the truncate hack goes away. Check first: whether `GroupExport` must stay as it is for the Export Group file format. If so, keep it for files and give the undo list its own type. Also decide whether Ctrl+Shift+T should reopen notes and groups (a behavior change). Done: `closed: Vec<ClosedEntry>` capped at `MAX_CLOSED` (20). An entry is the closed node plus, for a group, every node under it at any depth, each with its old id, parent, group, position, tag colour and a `ClosedPiece` (`closed_entry`, unit-tested). Closing is two steps: `close_node` records one entry, then `destroy_node` (`destroy_group`/`destroy_tab`) removes everything without recording, so the truncate hack is gone. `reopen_closed` gives every node a fresh id, remaps links inside the entry, and keeps a link outside it only if that node still exists (else the top level). `Ctrl+Shift+T` reopens the newest entry of any kind, as the user chose. `GroupExport` stays for the Export Group file format only. Checked headless: closing the reproduction's `grp-0` listed one "Group 0 (3)" entry, and REOPENTAB rebuilt `grp-2 { image, grp-3 { tab } }`. Two limits: an "Expand as Graph" leaf closed on its own isn't recorded (each would push a real entry out of the cap), and a file card reloads its file, so one deleted since is toasted and skipped (a PDF shows its error card).

3. **Done: `drain_tab_events`: collect, then apply one message at a time.** The function (438 lines, ~12919) holds a `get_mut` borrow of one tab node while it drains that tab's `events_rx`. Anything that touches other nodes has to be deferred. There are six deferred collections: `frame_updates` (a `HashMap` so each tab keeps only its newest frame), `swap_finishes`, `crashes`, `open_tab_requests`, `redirect_content_tabs`, and `download_events`. The last is a function-local `DownloadEvent` enum (~12991) that exists only to keep Started/Progress/Completed in order across kinds. The comments at ~12968-12990 explain all of this as working around the borrow. Proposed: first drain every tab into a `Vec<(tab_id, EngineToHost)>`, keeping only the latest `FrameReady` per tab (that coalescing is the confirmed WebView2 memory-growth fix and must stay). Then apply each message through a `handle_engine_message(&mut self, tab_id, msg)` that has full `&mut self`. This matches what `GraphAction`/`apply_graph_action` did for the graph UI. Arrival order is kept for free, and most of the collections and their comments go away. Check first: the current code applies each kind as a batch after the loop (frames, then swaps, then crashes, and so on). Keep an order that still matters, such as a `Crashed` arriving in the same drain as a `FrameReady`, and keep the single `save_session` after `nav_url_changed`. Done: the drain keeps each tab's newest `FrameReady` (and collapses a run of same-path `DownloadProgress`), queues everything else as `(tab_id, EngineToHost)` in arrival order, and applies each through `handle_engine_message`, which returns whether the url changed; one `save_session` follows, then the fetcher ticks and the frame uploads as before. All six deferred collections and `DownloadEvent` are gone. Order changed only where it was an artifact: a crash or swap now applies where it arrived, so a same-drain `save_session` can miss a tab that's mid-respawn, which the save after the respawn completes restores. Checked headless: the smoke test passes, killing a pinned tab's engine host brings it back pinned, and picking another engine in Settings reaches `finish_swap`. Found while checking that last one: a swap to an engine whose binary isn't built fails its respawn, and the tab was then gone for good, saved session included. Fixed: `finish_swap` has to drop the old engine before spawning the new one (their named pipes would collide), so it now sets `TabSpawn::fallback_kind` to the old engine; if the spawn fails, `handle_tab_spawn_result` toasts "Couldn't switch to X: ... Kept Y." and respawns the tab on the old engine with the same carry (pinned, color, zoom). Checked headless: a swap to CEF (not built here) keeps tab-0 on WebView2, in the exported session too. A crash respawn that failed on the tab's own engine still lost the tab, and so did a session or group restore whose engine couldn't start. **Fixed:** a failed `Respawn` or `Restore` spawn now leaves a failed-tab card (`NodePayload::FailedTab`, `FailedTabPayload`) where the tab was, with a warning icon, the url, and the engine and error as its tooltip. It is saved, exported, copied and reopened (Recently Closed) as the tab it stands in for, via `NodePayload::saved_tab`, so nothing is lost across restarts. Clicking it, or Enter while it's focused, retries on the same engine (`retry_failed_tab`; the card shows a spinner meanwhile). Its menu adds "Open in Default Engine" when that differs from the engine that failed. A success replaces the card with the live tab under the same id; a failure leaves a fresh card. A retry's outcome uses the card's placement as it is when the outcome arrives, and is dropped if the card was closed meanwhile (`App::failed_tab_retries`). Tab view shows only live tabs, so a focused failed card falls back to Graph. Checked headless (seed `target/followups/restorefail`, a session with a second tab on CEF, which isn't built here): the card appears and the exported session keeps tab-1 on CEF (`restorefail.txt`); a click retries and fails back to the card (`restorefail2.txt`); "Open in Default Engine" turns it into a live WebView2 tab (`restorefail3.txt`). Not probed: a crash respawn that fails; it takes the same `Respawn` failure path the retry probe exercised. (Renamed since, by lazy tabs: the card is `NodePayload::Unloaded` / `UnloadedTabPayload` in its `Failed` state, the retry is `wake_tab`, and `failed_tab_retries` is `wakes_in_flight`.) Seen while checking: the graph's decoration layer (card labels and icons, and the minimap) can paint over a card's context menu. That's older than this change and affects every card. Fixed since: see Known limitations (`overlay_painter`, `Order::Middle`).

4. **Split the longest functions along their seams (pure extraction).** `paint_card_body` (~5607) is one `match` with seven arms of 45-150 lines: Download ~5622, Note ~5717, Image ~5800, Pdf ~5860, Json ~5946, Group ~5990 (150 lines), Tab ~6141. Each arm becomes `paint_<kind>_body` and the `match` becomes a dispatcher. `build_graph_ui` (~10833) has clear phases. The toolbar is an inline ~280-line closure (~10894-11176); the Tab and Image views already have `render_tab_toolbar_content`/`render_image_toolbar_content`, so Graph is the odd one out. Then come the settle check (~11207), keyboard navigation (~11232-11271), rectangle select (~11389), camera tween (~11443), minimap drawing (~11480-11528) and its click (~11951), the `Scene` contents (~11531-11868, ~340 lines) and pan momentum (~11897-11951). The toolbar can push `GraphAction`s like the card buttons already do. `RedrawRequested` (~12293) splits into running the egui frame (~12311), computing content panes (~12381), render plus screenshot/PDF (~12428) and headless end-of-frame work (~12483). `build_settings_page` splits by its headings: Settings ~4105, Current Tab ~4177, Lists ~4238, Sessions ~4270, Bookmarks ~4342, with the popups it anchors at ~4446-4520. No behavior change is intended. The risks are borrow-checker friction where a closure captures several locals, and hit-test order inside `Scene`, which must not change (the notes-click bug shows why). Do this before item 5, so that whole functions move instead of fragments. Done for `paint_card_body`: it is now a seven-line dispatcher over `paint_download_body`, `paint_note_body`, `paint_image_body`, `paint_pdf_body`, `paint_json_body`, `paint_group_body` and `paint_tab_body`. Each takes only the parameters it uses (only Group needs the `CardView`), each arm's leading comment became its doc comment, and comments that pointed at "the Tab arm" now name `paint_tab_body`. A whitespace-insensitive diff shows only the new signatures and the dispatch; the smoke test passes. Done for `build_graph_ui` too (1,149 lines to 264): the toolbar is `render_graph_toolbar`, which returns a `GraphToolbar` (its pushed actions, the zoom-chip requests and the three button rects) that `build_graph_ui` destructures into the same locals as before; then `handle_graph_keys`, `handle_rect_select`, `advance_graph_camera` (group tween, else pan momentum), `draw_graph_minimap` (returns rect, world bounds and scale for `handle_minimap_click`), and inside the `Scene` `paint_dot_grid`, `paint_edges`, `draw_graph_cards`, `draw_pending_new_node_menu` and `draw_peek_preview`; after it `track_pan_momentum`. Call order, and so hit-test and paint order, is unchanged. The settle check and the empty-graph early return stay inline (a dozen lines each). Checked: the whitespace-stripped line multiset differs only by the new signatures, calls and struct; comments that said "below"/"above" across the new boundaries now name the function; clippy's warning count is unchanged (37, all older); the smoke test passes; and a headless probe (`target/followups/graphsplit.txt`) right-drag-selects a tab (toolbar shows Group Selected (1)), clears it with Escape, opens the new-node card with a right-click and recenters with a minimap click. Done for `build_settings_page` (456 lines to 37): its request locals are one `SettingsRequests`, each heading is a `settings_*_section` method (general, current tab, lists, sessions, bookmarks) that fills it, and after the scroll area `apply_settings_requests` runs the deferred effects (dialogs, save/open/delete, mute, screenshot, print) and `build_settings_popups` the anchored popups. Checked headless by clicking through Settings: zoom +, Mute, the History popup and the default-engine picker (`target/followups/settings.txt`). Done for `RedrawRequested` (213 lines to 26): `run_egui_frame` (input adjustments, then `build_ui`), `render_frame` (which calls `content_panes_points` and hands a finished capture to `handle_screenshot_result`, the PNG toast or print-to-PDF conversion), and `headless_end_of_frame` (the ADDRESSBAR retry and `quit_after_redraws`); the crash-state record, drain, transition check and the unconditional `request_redraw` stay inline. Checked: the line multiset differs only by the moves, and comments that pointed at `RedrawRequested`'s handling now name the method; the smoke test (screenshots, QUIT) passes. The print-to-PDF branch isn't reachable headless (its button is hidden there); it moved unchanged. Item 4 is done. **Update 2026-09-30 (second pass, pure extraction, one commit each):** `render_graph_toolbar` 309 to 65 lines (`toolbar_breadcrumb`, `toolbar_group_chip`, `toolbar_zoom`, `toolbar_filter`, `toolbar_recently_closed`, `toolbar_selection`, `toolbar_spawn_error`); `build_tab_view_ui` 312 to 187 (`build_respawn_placeholder`, `build_address_suggestions`, `build_split_pane_label`, `build_content_spinner`); `build_graph_ui` 267 to 212 (`update_graph_lod`, `apply_camera_requests`, `settle_level_if_changed`, `apply_zoom_requests`, `build_graph_popups`); `materialize` 233 to 21 (`update_live_node`, `insert_materialized`). What is left is deliberately not split: `window_event` (235), `handle_tab_spawn_result` (229), `drain_tab_events` (227) and `handle_debug_command` (350) are flat `match` dispatchers whose length is their arm count, so splitting moves code without making it easier to read; `build_graph_ui`'s remaining body is the `Scene` closure, which borrows `self` and its locals together; `build_tab_view_ui`'s is the toolbar destructure and action dispatch, where a split would thread ten flags through a struct. The zoom chip (`ZoomChipAction`, `render_zoom_chip`, `step_scene_rect`, `in_reading_order`, `ZOOM_STEP`) moved to `zoom_chip.rs` with its two tests.

5. **Done: split main.rs into modules.** Only the helpers are in separate modules today (`gpu`, `session`, `settings`, `debug_control`, `process_manager`, `graph_layout`, `group_export`, the four fetchers, `bookmarks`, `pdf`, `json_file`, `crash_report`). All UI and state sits in one file with two `impl App` blocks (~3852-11985 and ~12511-13360). Rust makes this cheap. A child module can write `impl super::App { ... }` and still read `App`'s private fields and call its private methods, because privacy extends to descendant modules. Lowest risk first, since these are free items with no `App` access. `node.rs`: `NodePayload`, `GraphNode`, the payload structs and `ItemNodeKind`/`GRAPH_DRAW_ORDER` (~2543-3070). `card.rs`: `CardStyle`/`CardView`/`card_border`/`card_controls`/`drop_action`/`paint_card_control` (~678-1290). `theme.rs`: color consts, `apply_style`, `apply_fonts`, `heading_text`, `toolbar_panel`, `icon_button_eased`, `chip_*`, `lerp_color32` (~1289-2130). `engine_info.rs`: `engine_label`/`engine_color`/`EngineTier`/`engine_pros`/`engine_cons`/`build_engine_picker_popup` (~1777-2005). `image_view.rs`: the zoom and pan math (~2220-2343). `input.rs`: `translate_*`/`winit_modifiers_to_bits` (~13360-13456). The tests in `mod tests` (~13506-13931, 425 lines) move alongside. Then come the `impl App` areas: `graph_ui.rs` (`build_graph_ui`, `draw_graph_card`, `paint_card_body`, `apply_graph_action`), `tab_view.rs`, `settings_page.rs`, `popups.rs` (bookmarks, history, downloads, recently closed, quick switcher, find bar, shortcuts help, JSON viewer, import confirm, ~8271-9140), and `event_loop.rs` (`ApplicationHandler`, `handle_debug_command`, `drain_tab_events`). Check first: methods defined inside a child module are private to that module, so any method called from outside it needs `pub(super)`. Make one move per commit and keep `cargo check` green after each, because this diff conflicts with any work in progress on main.rs.

   Done in 15 commits (`8651713`..`c8d85fb`); main.rs went from 14,314 lines to 5,816. Free items: `node.rs` (the node model), `card.rs` (the card frame types), `theme.rs` (palette, style, fonts, the popup/dialog frames, `toolbar_panel`, `icon_button_eased`, `chip_*`, `toolbar_chip`, `lerp_color32`, `apply_dark_titlebar`), `engine_info.rs`, `image_view.rs` (the zoom and pan math) and `input.rs`, each test moving with its code. `impl App` areas: `settings_page.rs` (with `SettingsRequests`), `popups.rs` (the popups the plan lists, plus tab actions, `save_json_body_as_node` and the popup helpers `clicked_outside_popup`, `build_list_popup_frame`, `fade_popup_state`), `tab_view.rs` (the item toolbar every item shares, `build_item_ui`, the Tab view), `graph_ui.rs` (`build_graph_ui` and everything it draws or handles, `apply_graph_action`, `GraphAction`/`GraphToolbar`) and `event_loop.rs` (`ApplicationHandler`, the per-frame methods from item 4, `dispatch_window_event`, the debug commands, `drain_tab_events`, `handle_engine_message`; main.rs's second `impl App` held only these). Two changes from the plan: `draw_graph_card`, `card_view` and the `paint_*_body` painters joined `card.rs` rather than `graph_ui.rs`, next to the frame types they use, and the Image view's UI methods (`render_image_toolbar_content`, `apply_image_zoom_action`, `build_image_view_ui`) joined `image_view.rs`. Each module starts with `use super::*;`. Moved items, named fields and methods are `pub(crate)`; `App`'s fields stay private, which child modules can still read. Each move was scripted and checked: the removed lines equal the new file's lines once `pub(crate)` is stripped, `cargo check` has no new warnings, the 27 tests pass, clippy stays at 37 warnings, and the headless smoke test passes (28 PASS). Still in main.rs: `App` and its `Default`, the session save/restore, tab spawning, group and focus operations, node insertion, shortcuts, the node menu and `build_ui`. These have no single seam yet; splitting them further is optional. **Update 2026-09-30:** the "still in main.rs" list above has since moved too: `closed.rs`, `node_menu.rs`, `view_modes.rs`, `graph_nav.rs`, `shortcuts.rs`, `fetched.rs` and `tabs.rs` (see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-main-split-and-engine-table-design.md`); main.rs is 2,733 lines.

6. **Done: one mechanism for anchored popups.** Eight toolbar and settings popups repeat the same ~20-line block. Each has its own `*_open: bool` field (`bookmarks_open`, `history_open`, `downloads_open`, `tab_actions_open`, `recently_closed_open`, `default_engine_picker_open`, `current_engine_picker_open`, `split_engine_picker_open`, ~3639-3689). Each has its own `ctx.animate_bool(Id::new("<name>_popup_fade"), open)` (~4447-4493, ~10665, ~10677, ~11975), an `if fade > 0.0 { if let Some(rect) = <anchor>_button_rect`, and a `clicked_outside_popup` close (13 calls). The three engine pickers (~4469-4520, ~10675-10694) are copies of each other apart from what "picked" does. The popups are exclusive only by accident: clicking a second button counts as a click outside the first. A headless command or shortcut can open two at once. Proposed: `open_popup: Option<AnchoredPopup>` (an enum of the eight), one `show_anchored_popup(ui, which, anchor, fade, body)` helper, and one engine-picker call that takes an `on_pick` target. Check first: egui 0.36's `egui::Popup` already provides toggle-on-button, close-on-click-outside and only-one-open-at-a-time through its popup memory, so it may replace both the enum and `clicked_outside_popup`. It has no fade, though, so the eased open and close (and `fade_popup_state` for the menus) would need to sit on top of it. Done without `egui::Popup`, which fits badly here: its memory keeps a popup open only while `show()` runs each frame, so a popup can't be drawn fading out after it closes; `show()` returns nothing once closed; and it shares one open slot per viewport with ComboBoxes and menus. Instead the eight `*_open` bools are one `open_popup: Option<AnchoredPopup>` (`popups.rs`), so opening one popup closes any other, whether by click, shortcut or headless command. Buttons call `toggle_popup`; closes go through `close_popup`, which clears only if that popup is still the open one, so a popup fading out can't close the one that replaced it (unit tests `opening_a_popup_replaces_the_open_one` and `a_fading_popup_cannot_close_its_replacement`). `App::anchored_popup_fade(ctx, which, anchor)` is the shared fade: it returns the anchor rect and fade while the popup is open or still fading out. A fading popup takes no clicks (`interactable` is whether it's open). The three engine pickers are one `App::build_anchored_engine_picker`, which returns the picked engine; each caller applies it (save the default, swap the tab, open a split). Rather than a `show_anchored_popup(..., body)` closure, each popup keeps its own builder and calls `anchored_popup_fade`, because the builders borrow `self` mutably. Checked headless: in Settings, History opens and toggles off, opening the default-engine picker closes History, and picking an engine closes the picker (`target/followups/popups1.txt`). Downloads is replaced by History (`popups2.txt`). In Tab view, Split opens, Tab actions replaces it, and a second click closes it (`popups3.txt`); a click on the page closes Split, and one on the address bar closes Tab actions and focuses the bar (`popups4.txt`).

7. **Done: group App's scattered state into sub-structs, starting with focus requests and find.** `App` has about 110 fields (~3176-3716). Several sets are kept in sync by hand. Five `pending_focus_*` flags (`rename` ~3217, `quick_switcher` ~3282, `address_bar` ~3502, `find_bar` ~3614, `bookmark_folder` ~3663) are each set by a shortcut and `mem::take`n where the widget is built (~8346, ~8812, ~9014, ~9411, ~10330, ~10953). They could be one `pending_focus: Option<FocusTarget>`, or dropped by giving each `TextEdit` a stable `Id` and calling `ctx.memory_mut(|m| m.request_focus(id))`. The find bar is five fields (`find_bar_open`, `find_text`, `find_match_case`, `find_result`, `pending_focus_find_bar`, ~3603-3614) that `close_find_bar` resets by hand. Its doc comment has also come loose: it sits on `closed_tabs` (~3528-3536), and `find_bar_open` itself has no doc. That becomes `FindState` with `Default`. Other clusters: the address bar and suggestions (`address_bar_text`, `address_bar_focused`, `open_tab_suggestions`, `search_suggestions`, `dropdown_highlighted`, `debug_address_bar_typing`), the graph camera (`graph_scene_rect`, `graph_zoom_pct`, `graph_camera_tween`, `pan_momentum`, `pan_drag_history`, `graph_lod_simplified`, `graph_settled_key`), and the per-kind id counters `next_{tab,download,group,note,image,pdf,json}_id` (~3306-3325). Check first: `request_focus` on an id whose widget isn't built this frame. Ctrl+L from Graph view switches views, so the address bar doesn't exist until the next frame. That is why these flags exist, so try the egui route on one widget before replacing all five. Done for focus requests and find. The five flags are one `pending_focus: Option<FocusTarget>`, and each widget takes its request with `take_focus`. egui's own `request_focus(id)` wasn't used: it survives only one frame without its widget (egui `memory/mod.rs`, `Focus::end_pass`), the group-rename `TextEdit` is built in two places (card and context menu), and `debug_input_pending` reads the address-bar request as "needs another frame". (Ctrl+L acts only in Tab view, so the view switch the check worried about never happens.) The find bar is `find: FindState` (`popups.rs`), and `FindState::close` is the one reset. That fixed a bug: the two paths that close the bar because its tab was destroyed reset it by hand and skipped match case, so after closing a tab with "Aa" on, the next find session started case-sensitive. Checked with unit tests (`closing_find_keeps_the_text_and_resets_the_rest`, `a_focus_request_is_taken_once_by_its_own_widget`) and headless (`target/followups/focus7.txt`): on the old build "Aa" stayed on after CLOSETAB; now it resets, the text is kept, and the find bar and address bar both take focus. The smoke test passes. Done for the address bar too: `address_bar: AddressBar` (`tab_view.rs`) holds the text, focus, both suggestion lists, the fetcher and the highlight. `AddressBar::dismiss` is the one way to close the dropdown (Escape, Enter, accepting a suggestion), and `combined()` replaced `App::combined_suggestions`. `debug_address_bar_typing` stays on `App` with the other headless-debug fields. That fixed a bug: dismissing only cleared the lists, so a search-suggestion fetch still in flight landed afterwards and refilled them. The dropdown then hung over the loaded page with the bar unfocused, and because `editing_address` counts non-empty suggestions as editing, the bar stopped following the tab's URL. `dismiss` now calls `SuggestionFetcher::cancel`, which bumps the generation so the late response is dropped. Leaving Tab view had the same result: only the highlight was reset, so the typed text and dropdown came back over whichever tab was shown next (`target/followups/leave-sugg.txt`: 8 suggestions after TOGGLEVIEW twice). `build_ui` now ends the edit (`dismiss`, unfocus) on any frame Tab view isn't showing, which covers every exit at once, and `AddressBar::editing` is the one definition of mid-edit. Checked with a unit test (`a_cancelled_fetch_cannot_deliver`) and headless (`target/followups/stale-sugg.txt`): on the old build, 8 suggestions were showing 3 s after Enter, over the results page; now there are none, in three runs, and the bar shows the URL. The smoke test passes. Done for the graph camera: `camera: GraphCamera` (`graph_ui.rs`) holds the rect, zoom readout, tween, momentum and drag history. `jump_to` and `ease_to` each cancel whatever animation was running, and `advance` replaced `App::advance_graph_camera`. `graph_lod_simplified` (a render latch) and `graph_settled_key` (layout settle) stay on `App`, since neither is camera state. That fixed two bugs. A tween started mid-glide left the momentum in place, and it resumed when the tween ended, pulling the camera off the level just framed (unit test `easing_the_camera_stops_a_glide` fails without the fix). Separately, only `switch_group_level` re-framed the camera, while the quick switcher (Enter and click) and the view toggle set `current_group` directly. Jumping to a tab in another group and back to Graph showed that group with the old level's framing: an empty canvas, cards at x≈4600 in an 1100-wide window (`target/followups/cam1.txt` with `far.group.json`). Those paths and `dissolve_group` now go through `switch_group_level` (via `show_level_of`), and the cards land on screen. The smoke test passes. A loaded session had the same gap: the camera isn't saved, and `apply_session` (startup and Open/Import Session) never framed what it restored, so a session whose nodes sat away from the origin opened on an empty canvas (`target/followups/cam2.txt` with `far.session.json`: cards at x≈4600). `apply_session` now sets `GraphCamera::frame_restored`; once the restored tabs have spawned, Graph view settles and fits the level if none of its nodes is on screen, and otherwise keeps today's framing. Done for the id counters: `ids: NodeIds` (`main.rs`, with `next_id`), and the download id now comes from `next_id` too instead of its own `format!` and increment. **Superseded:** `NodeIds` is gone since `08fd35d` ("node ids come from the journal's counter"); `alloc_node_id` (`node_store.rs`) draws from the store's journal, and only `fallback_ids` remains on `App`, for when no store is open. No bug there: downloads aren't saved in a session, and `apply_session` already raises every saved kind's counter past its restored ids (groups included, once they were saved; see below).

   Groups are now saved in a session (2026-09-25). `session.json` gains `groups` (id, name, position, color, parent group), `current_group` and `next_group_id`, and every node gains `group` and `color` (`SessionPlace`, flattened so `pos_x`/`pos_y` stay where they were); tabs also gain `pinned`. All new fields default, so older files load unchanged (`session_without_counters_loads`). A node naming a group the file lacks lands at the top level. Groups made by "Expand as Graph" are marked `GroupPayload::from_json` and still aren't saved, like their leaves; a node the user put inside one, and the viewed level, are saved under the nearest saved group around it (`App::saved_group`). Group edits used to change nothing that saved, so `apply_graph_action`, the node menu's actions and `new_group_shortcut` now call `save_session`. A tab's zoom is still not saved. Checked with a unit test (`a_node_keeps_its_group_and_color_through_a_save`) and headless: `target/followups/grp1.txt` seeded with `grpseed/session.json` (nested groups, colors, a pin, a dangling group, `current_group`) exports the same state back, and `NEWGROUP` lands in `current_group` and in the autosaved file; `grp3.txt` expands a JSON file, enters it, adds a note, and the export has no JSON groups or leaves, with the note and the level under `grp-1`. The smoke test passes.

8. **Put per-key tables on `manifold_view_protocol::KeyCode`.** The 56-variant `KeyCode` (`crates/manifold-view-protocol/src/messages.rs` ~38) is translated in six hand-written tables. Host: `translate_key_code` (now `input.rs`; main.rs ~13391 at `73f40cf`, winit to `KeyCode`). CEF: `key_code_to_vk` (`manifold-view-cef/src/input.rs` ~9, Windows VK). WebView2: `key_info` (`manifold-view-webview2/src/input.rs` ~255, VK plus DOM `code` plus DOM `key`). WebKit: `crates/manifold-view-webkitgtk/src/input.rs` ~154 (GDK keysyms). Servo has two: `key_to_servo_key` and `key_to_servo_code` (`manifold-view-servo/src/main.rs` ~456, ~514). Supporting one more key (punctuation or F-keys, which are all missing) means editing all six. The variant names already are the DOM `code` strings. Proposed: `KeyCode::dom_code() -> &'static str` and `KeyCode::windows_vk() -> u16` in ipc-protocol. CEF's table and two thirds of WebView2's reduce to calls. Servo's `Code` can come from `Code::from_str(key.dom_code())`. WebKit's GDK table and the host's winit table stay, because they are genuinely different spaces. A small rider: the identical `document.contentType` probe script is pasted in Servo (~126), WebKit (~419) and WebView2 (`nav_state.rs` ~159), each with its own `"json\n"`/`"type\n"` reply parsing. That could be one `ipc_protocol` constant plus a `parse_content_probe(&str)`. Risk: only ipc-protocol, the host and WebView2 build on this machine. CEF needs its download, and WebKit and Servo build only under WSL2, so verifying those three means the WSL2 build plus a live typing test per engine.

### Considered and rejected

- **`handle_debug_command` (349 lines):** already a flat dispatcher, and almost every arm is a one-line call into the same `*_shortcut` method the keyboard uses. It is long only because the command list is long. `ExportState` (~12741, ~48 lines inline) is the one arm worth pulling out, and it can go with item 5.
- **Make `ViewMode::Item` carry the viewed image:** the invariant that `viewed_image` is `Some` only in Item view is already contained by `showing_tab_view`/`showing_image_view` (~3878) and has five writers. Making it a type would complicate Settings' `settings_return_view` round trip for little gain.
- **Store `Session` as one tagged `Vec<SessionNode>`:** it would collapse the per-kind loops in `build_session_snapshot`/`apply_session` (~4808, ~4921), but it changes the on-disk format of `session.json` and saved sessions, which then needs a migration. Groups not persisting is a documented decision, so there's no second driver for the change.
- **One engine-side outbox instead of per-message `Rc<RefCell<Option<_>>>` cells** (CEF 8, WebKit 7, WebView2 7, Servo 2): tidier, but the cells differ on purpose (latest wins, queued, or deduplicated on change, e.g. audio playing). Three of the four crates can't be built on Windows, so the verification cost is high for code that isn't causing bugs.
- **Merge the duplicated `as_*` accessors on `NodePayload` (now `Runtime`) and `GraphNode`** (~2568-2631, ~2923-2959): trivial one-line delegations. Since 2b-6 one macro (`runtime_accessors!`, `node.rs`) generates both sets; folding the two enums' accessors together further would be harder to read than what it saves.
- **One global id counter instead of seven:** ids like `note-3` are persisted, and `bump_past_id` is per prefix, so this touches session compatibility for no behavior gain. Grouping the counters into one struct is covered by item 7.
- **Add a `send_to_tab(id, msg)` helper:** there are 30 `nodes.get_mut(..).and_then(as_tab_mut)` sites, but only 11 are immediately followed by a `send`. The saving is a line each, so it's best done opportunistically while touching those sites for items 3 or 4.

## Known limitations

- **Fixed (audit 2026-10-01): our shortcut chords also reached the page in Tab view.** `handle_global_shortcuts` `consume_key`s Ctrl+T/W/L/R/F/D/P/K/G/Tab/zoom and F1/F2/F5/F12 from egui's input, but `window_event`'s `KeyboardInput` arm forwarded the raw winit event to the engine whenever `!consumed_by_egui`, and egui-winit's `consumed` means "a text field has focus", not "a shortcut took it". The engine got the KeyDown and the typed character for each chord as well as the shortcut firing. Now `shortcuts::claims_chord(code, modifiers)` names those chords and the arm drops their press, text and release (`App::swallowed_keys` remembers the key so the release is dropped even if Ctrl is let go first). Keys we don't claim (Ctrl+C/V/X/A/Z/S...) still go only to the page. `claims_chord` is a second list that must track `handle_global_shortcuts` (unit-tested, not derived from it). Only the predicate is tested: real key events can't be synthesized (see `debug_control`), so the end-to-end drop against a page logging `keydown` is unverified.

- **Fixed:** "extreme lag on startup," reported live by a user, longstanding and unrelated to any recent change. Root-caused via temporary per-phase timing instrumentation through `main()`/`resumed()`/`GpuState::new` (removed after measuring): of a ~1.3-1.5s total time from process start to the first real frame, `wgpu::Instance::new()` alone accounted for ~640ms (over 40%) -- `wgpu::InstanceDescriptor::new_without_display_handle()`'s own default `backends` is `Backends::all()`, which on Windows also loads/probes the `GL` (ANGLE-backed) secondary tier this app never ends up using (`adapter.get_info()` confirmed it always lands on Vulkan or DX12 in practice). Fixed by explicitly restricting `backends` to `wgpu::Backends::PRIMARY` (Vulkan/Metal/DX12/WebGPU only) -- wgpu's own recommended set for exactly this "skip the legacy fallback tier" reason. Verified live: `Instance::new()` dropped to ~130-160ms across two repeated timing runs (from a consistent ~640ms baseline), with the same adapter (AMD Radeon RX 9060 XT via Vulkan) still selected either way -- no behavior change, purely faster. Request-adapter/request-device/pipeline-creation (shader compile) remain real, likely-irreducible costs of talking to the GPU driver and weren't touched; a `wgpu::PipelineCache` to skip shader recompilation across runs is a possible future win but out of scope for this fix.
- **Fixed:** the gear-icon Settings dropdown overflowed off the bottom of a small window, reported live by a user. Root cause: it was a fixed-position `Area` with no `ScrollArea` and unbounded height (grows with the saved-sessions list), only ever anchored below the gear button -- nothing clamped its height to the window's own. Fixed by turning it into a real `ViewMode::Settings` page instead of a popup: a top toolbar (Back button, title) plus a window-filling `ScrollArea`, so height is never an issue regardless of window size or list length. The gear button on both Tab and Graph toolbars now calls `toggle_settings_page` (enter from whichever view was showing, remembered in `settings_return_view`; a second click, `Escape`, or `Ctrl+G` returns to it) instead of opening the old popup. Content is left-aligned with a capped 480pt width (not centered -- tried centered first, changed on request) so long lines stay readable without stretching across a wide window. Verified live by the user directly (not this session's own automated testing, held off on per the user's own request): opened the page, requested the layout be left-aligned instead of centered (applied, see the 16pt-inset `Frame` wrapper around the content).
- **Fixed:** some headings/labels (`.strong()` text -- "Settings", "Sessions", "Bookmarks" section headers among them) rendered black on this app's own always-dark backgrounds, reported live by a user. Root cause: `apply_style`'s `ctx.all_styles_mut` only overrode specific widget-state fields on top of *whichever* base preset (`egui::Visuals::dark()`/`Visuals::light()`) egui had already loaded into that style slot; under a light OS/window theme, every field the function didn't explicitly touch -- including `.strong()` text color -- kept the light preset's own near-black default, invisible against `BG`/`TOOLBAR_FILL`/etc., which are forced dark unconditionally regardless of OS theme. Fixed by resetting `style.visuals = egui::Visuals::dark()` for both style slots before applying this app's own cosmetic overrides, so every untouched field starts from a coherent dark-appropriate default no matter which theme the OS reports. Verified live in this dev environment's own (already-dark) OS theme that headings still render correctly post-fix (light gray/white, clearly legible); the light-OS-theme trigger path itself wasn't independently reproduced, since nothing in this app's headless protocol can force winit to report a specific OS theme. **Recurred, different root cause, fixed:** "Settings" (and every other `.strong()` heading) still rendered black afterward, reported live again. Real cause this time: `RichText::strong()`'s color comes from `Visuals::strong_text_color()`, which egui defines as `widgets.active.text_color()` -- and `apply_style` deliberately sets that field to `Color32::BLACK`, needed for pressed/selected-button text to stay readable against the `ACCENT`-colored fill those states use. Headings aren't drawn on an `ACCENT` background, so they inherited black-on-dark from the same field for an unrelated reason. Fixed at each of the six `.strong()` call sites (`Settings`/`Sessions`/`Bookmarks`/`Import Session` popup headers, the bookmarks-folder bold header, and the new Keyboard Shortcuts popup's own title) with an explicit `.color(TEXT_PRIMARY)`, leaving `widgets.active`'s black untouched since pressed/selected buttons still need it. Verified live: all reported headings confirmed legible after rebuild.
- **Fixed:** a crash while a session was still restoring could drop tabs from it. Restored tabs spawn in the background and each one saved `session.json` as it landed, so until the last landed the file lacked the rest (a temporary log in `save_session` showed `tabs=1 in_flight=7` … `tabs=8 in_flight=0` for an 8-tab session). `save_session` now skips while `restore_spawns_in_flight` is nonzero, and the last restored tab's landing saves, including anything changed meanwhile. Spawns time out (10 s, or 45 s for WebKit), so saves wait at most that long. Checked headless with `target/followups/restseed`: only one write for the whole restore, all 8 tabs, and a `NEWGROUP` sent before any tab landed is in it (`rest3b.txt`).
- Session state other than cookies (localStorage, IndexedDB, scroll position) is not portable across an engine swap. Cookies carry between WebView2 and CEF through the cookie jar; a swap to or from Servo or WebKit keeps only the URL.
- Only the focused tab starts its engine at launch; every other saved tab restores asleep (a dimmed card with its title, host and favicon; favicons are cached in `%APPDATA%\manifold\favicons` for 7 days) and starts when opened, which takes a second or two. An opened tab stays loaded until it is closed, the app restarts, or you put it back to sleep by hand: "Sleep" in a tab's menu, "Sleep Tabs" in a group's (the tab on screen and the split pane's tab stay live). Before sleeping, the host asks the page for its scroll position and whether a form was changed and sleeps on the answer (a query unanswered after 5 s is settled by the next idle sweep, within about 15 s: a manual Sleep then sleeps the tab anyway, without a scroll to restore, so a hung page can still be slept; an idle one just stays awake). Waking reloads the url and scrolls back to the saved position once, after the first load, unless you scroll first; the position is kept in memory only, so it is lost when the app restarts. Other page state (form input, a playing video, script state) is still lost. Settings can also put background tabs to sleep after 10, 30 or 60 minutes out of sight (off by default; tabs playing audio, pinned tabs, tabs still loading and tabs with a form field changed from its default stay awake; manual Sleep sleeps a changed form anyway; the check covers form fields and `contenteditable` only, not state a page keeps elsewhere, and downloads are not checked yet). Live-tested on WebView2, CEF and Servo (all four probe cases pass). WebKit answers the same query and compiles but has not been run: its engine host lives in WSL2, which cannot reach the probe server on Windows loopback, and the plan is to test it on a native Linux build rather than add a WSL workaround. A plain timer for now; a memory-pressure heuristic is a possible follow-up (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-lazy-tabs-design.md`, Deferred). So memory grows with the tabs opened, not the tabs saved. Each opened tab costs, measured 2026-09-26 (debug build, N copies of a local ~60 KB page, memory 15 s after every tab spawned, before lazy tabs): WebView2 ~78 MB private (~135 MB working set), an engine host of ~34 MB plus its share of Edge's processes (all tabs share one browser process; 100 open tabs made 206 processes, 8.3 GB private); CEF ~177 MB private (~330 MB working set) and six processes (25 open tabs: 151 processes, 4.8 GB private). Real pages weigh several times more. Restoring 100 tabs now (2026-09-27, `gate scale`, then `scale-test.sh`): WebView2 8 processes, 620 MB private, settled in 2.3 s; CEF 7 processes, 587 MB. The host alone is ~410 MB of that. Tabs another instance creates, a reopened Recently Closed entry and an imported group arrive asleep too.
- CEF tabs share cookies only through the cookie jar: each is seeded from it when it starts and pulled back into it later, so a login reaches a CEF tab that starts afterwards, not one already running. Each CEF engine-host gets its own throwaway `root_cache_path`, `<profile>\cef-cache\<manifold pid>-<n>` (`profile::cef_cache_dir()`, passed as `VIEW_CACHE_DIR`), and browsing data itself stays in memory. Chromium's process singleton lets only one browser process use a given cache root, so shared logins would need one CEF process hosting every CEF tab, which gives up one process per tab. Dropping an `EngineTab` deletes its dir, and startup (`process_manager::sweep_cef_cache`) removes leftovers from killed runs, skipping any dir a live CEF, such as another instance's, still holds open. WebView2 tabs share one browser process but not cookies: each live host has its own named InPrivate profile, `manifold_0` to `manifold_7` (lowest free first, freed once the host has exited; more concurrent tabs get names past 7), so WebView2's `Local State`, which lists every profile name it has seen, stops growing. Its leftover `WV2Profile_manifold_<n>` directory is deleted the same way (`process_manager::sweep_webview2_profiles`).
- **Blink (CEF) tabs run unsandboxed.** The CEF host starts Chromium with `--no-sandbox`: a renderer exploit in a CEF tab runs with the user's rights. WebView2 tabs keep Edge's sandbox. Enabling it needs CEF's sandbox bootstrap bundled with the host.
- **Open: WebView2's user-data folder sits outside the profile.** `manifold-view-webview2` passes a null folder to `CreateCoreWebView2EnvironmentWithOptions`, so its data lands next to the exe (`target/debug/manifold-view-webview2.exe.WebView2/`), outside `profile::dir()` and so outside the tests' isolated APPDATA. Routing it through `profile.rs` (an env var from the host, like CEF's `VIEW_CACHE_DIR`) would fix both. Logins no longer depend on it: tabs run InPrivate and get their cookies from the cookie jar. Each host's named profile still writes caches and an empty cookie database there (`EBWebView\WV2Profile_manifold_<n>`), which manifold deletes, plus one `manifold-profile-<n>.lock` per profile slot. Only a host whose runtime lacks InPrivate falls back to this shared profile; such a tab is neither seeded from the jar nor pulled into it, so the user's real cookies there never reach the jar.
- **Open: `gate smoke` (formerly `headless-smoke-test.sh`) is flaky on WebView2 graph interactions.** On 2026-09-26 it passed 1 of 3 runs on an unchanged tree, with different checks failing each time: "double-clicking the group card enters the group" (twice), "dropping a tab onto its own child is refused", "ejecting a selected tab also drops it from the selection". Not yet diagnosed; machine load wasn't checked. **2026-09-30:** under load from other sessions' builds it also failed with cygwin `fork` errors and "tab spawns still in flight"; two runs on a quiet machine (CPU under 10%) passed. **2026-09-30 (later):** three runs in a row passed at ~50% CPU on the current tree, so the flake is load-dependent, not a command-ordering bug. **2026-09-30 (last):** could not reproduce on demand: 3/3 passed with 8 BelowNormal spinner processes holding CPU at ~93%, and 3/3 beside another session's live shared-lease GPU training job (GPU activity not sampled). **2026-10-02:** of two more runs on the current tree (Defender real-time protection off, CPU not sampled), one failed three address-bar checks ("Enter searches the highlighted row", "Escape closes the dropdown...", "a headless screenshot shows no toast"; the focused tab had navigated to a duckduckgo `?q=weather` search, so these depend on the live suggestion fetch and a real network), and the other was cut short with no verdict; a different area from the graph-interaction failures above. If it recurs, keep that run's log and note the CPU and GPU load. **2026-10-02 (later):** after the suggestions moved to a local server, 1 of 4 runs failed "double-clicking the imported group's card enters it" (the others passed); its JSON was wiped by the next run and CPU was not sampled, so the cause is still unknown. `check_query` now prints the state it saw (`tab_view`, `current_group`, tabs, groups) on a failure, so the next one says whether the double-click was missed or the group opened wrong. Its `coproc` feed is not the `tail -f` file feed that lost commands in `key-probe.sh`/`scale-test.sh`, both since ported (see below). **2026-10-11:** not reproduced since the port to Rust (`gate smoke`, `crates/gate`): 10 of 10 consecutive runs passed (54 checks each, about 52% CPU from other sessions, Defender real-time protection off), with stable section times (for example `address-bar` mean 12.3 s, max 14.8 s) and no `arrow_nav` screenshot timeouts. The old shell version's fork overhead under load is the likeliest cause of the earlier failures, but the cause was never confirmed, so this stays open until a gate on a loaded machine has passed a few more times. **2026-10-19:** it is back, and it is not from that day's changes: `gate smoke` alone on the commit before them (db6727a, run 5 of 8, 1 failure) missed the double-click into the empty group at `reopen-nested` with the same three failures, and at HEAD 2 of 4 runs alone and 2 of 2 light gates failed one (the group card in `group-drops`, the imported group in `pair`, the empty group at `reopen-nested`, plus once `two-1` scenario 4, "B follows A's link click"). The state it saw each time was `tab_view=False group=None` with the cards where they were, so the double-click was missed, not late, and two of the three sites aim at a freshly settled state, so a stale position is not the cause. Every `DOUBLECLICK` is one frame of move, press, release, press, release (`debug_click`). `gate dblprobe` (`ROUNDS`, default 100; not part of `all`) double-clicks one settled group card in and out: 0 missed of 300 on a quiet machine (half with a hover frame first) and 0 of 100 with 14 BelowNormal spinners holding the CPU at 100%. So a double-click on a settled card never misses by itself, with or without load, and the smoke misses come from the state the sites reach it in (each follows a Tab-view exit, an import or a closed dialog). **Cause found (2026-10-19):** egui makes a release a triple click when the click before the last was under 0.6 s ago, at any position (`input_state` `triple_click` checks the time of that click but the distance to the last one only), so a `DOUBLECLICK` within 0.6 s of an earlier click (the smoke sites follow a `CLICK` such as Reset View) read as count 3 and `double_clicked()` stayed false. The probe's "after Reset View" round missed 18 of 20 with no pause, 7 of 10 after 0.1 s and 0 of 10 after 0.3 s (the double click's own frame time adds the rest). `debug_click` now waits out 650 ms since the last debug click before a count-2 click; that round then misses 0 of 20. A 350 ms wait looked like a fix once and was not (8 to 10 of 10 missed on rerun). **Not enough (2026-10-19, later):** with the 650 ms wait, a light gate still missed the empty group at `reopen-nested`, and 1 of 3 smoke runs alone missed the imported group in `pair`. Egui measures the window in frame times, but the wait ran on the clock of `debug_click`, and a slow frame stamps a click later than that clock, so the gap in frame times could still be under 0.6 s. `debug_click` now reads egui's own clock instead: before a count-2 click it runs a frame and sleeps until `pointer.time_since_last_click()` (public, in frame time, counts every click) is past 0.6 s plus 50 ms; the smoke also waits for idle after the confirm dialog's Escape (not shown to matter by itself). A variant that ran each click's frame at once and timed from the app's clock passed 6 of 6 and a light gate; this one passed 6 of 6 smoke runs alone (all 74 checks, 2026-10-19, CPU not sampled). **Cause found (2026-10-20):** a virtual trail-page card can be drawn over the group card's center, which is where the gate aimed (`State::xy`), so the double-click landed on the trail page. Two kept failing runs (`docs/runs.md`, `smoke-diag4-x20`) both had trail page `...:0:2` over the group's center; egui had counted a clean double click. `State::xy` now aims at the first point of the card that no other card covers. Smoke alone passed 24 of 24 (`smoke-xyfix-x24`) against about 6 failures in 52 runs before. **Recurred and fixed (2026-10-24):** smoke alone failed 7 of 12 (`smoke-canvasfix-x12`). The toolbar's zoom chip had moved, so the gate's "Reset View" click at `326 17` landed on the chip's `100%` readout (which keeps the pan) and the fit never happened; the empty group sat at y 704-814 in a 639 px window and every double click aimed at it missed (`reopen-nested`, `pair`, and the `borders` screenshot checks). The gate now clicks chrome by name, not pixel: `Instance::click_control(name)` finds the control in the state export's `controls` (every `NamedTip` logs its tooltip and rect; `state::FIT_VIEW`, `state::BACK`), and a control that is not on screen fails naming it. `State::xy` also prefers a point on the canvas (`xy_stays_on_the_canvas`). Smoke alone passed 12 of 12 (`smoke-fitfix-x12`) with the pixel constant and again with named controls (`smoke-named-x12`).
- **Unexplained, not reproduced since:** on 2026-09-25, 2 of 4 back-to-back local runs of a multi-session probe (`target/cardframe/stage5.py`, git-ignored) aborted with `scripts/drive.sh` returning 3840. No check failed and the app printed nothing. 3840 is not an exit status: Git Bash reports a death by signal N as the Windows exit code N<<8 (`exit 15` gives 15, `kill -TERM $$` gives 3840), so something outside the script sent its bash SIGTERM. That rules out a command failing under `set -e`, which gives a plain exit status. The sender is unknown. Ruled out by 22 clean runs since (13 without `set -e`, 8 with an ERR trap, 1 with the Bash tool moving the run to the background mid-way). The failures came while the disk was briefly full and other sessions were busy; if it recurs, note what else was running.
- **Fixed (2026-09-30, from the full-codebase review): the IPC layer trusted its peers.** (1) The WSL WebKit engine's control port listens on every interface (WSL2 NAT can't reach loopback), and `accept_remote` took the first connection, so any host on the network could become a tab's engine. Now `spawn_webkit_tab` makes a random 16-byte token per spawn, passes it as `VIEW_TOKEN`, and `accept_remote(listener, timeout, &token)` drops any connection that doesn't send it first (`connect_remote` sends it); an intruder can't displace the real engine. Not done: binding only the WSL gateway address. It needs a `wsl.exe` round trip before every bind (a cold WSL boot already takes seconds) for little gain over the token. The token is visible in the `wsl.exe` command line to the same user's processes only. `manifold-view-webkitgtk` reads the token but has only been built under WSL by hand, not in CI: rebuild it there after pulling. (2) `MessageChannel` refuses a length prefix above `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` (128 MiB) before allocating, so a bad peer can't force a huge allocation. (3) Remote-path frames (`FrameReady` with inline pixels) are checked against their width, height and the GPU's max texture size before upload (`gpu::frame_is_valid`); a bad frame is logged and dropped. (4) `SharedFrameBuffer::open_existing` refuses a file whose length isn't `width*height*4`. Also from that review: an engine's name, store code, binary, label and texture format are one `EngineDesc` table (`EngineKind::desc()`, exhaustive), and an unknown engine name in a session file is now an error rather than a silent Servo; favicon cache names are injective (`_` plus two hex digits for every other character); new nodes go through `place_node`, and creating one from a JSON level leaves the level first (spec `2026-09-30-place-node-design.md`). Builds before the first release aren't expected to read each other's files, so none of these keep a compatibility fallback.
- **Fixed (2026-10-09, wave 3a, from the engine survey; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-wave-3a-engine-hardening.md`).** (E11) The engine installer's download stops reading at the pinned size (`a069090`) and deletes its `.part` if the stream runs past it, instead of reading to the end of whatever the server sends. (E12) Redirect fetches run at most four at once, the rest wait in a queue (`net::Throttle`, `8d1d847`); favicon fetches use the same type instead of a private copy of the same queue (`7d0bcea`). (E6) The WebKit engine's command line carries the start URL as hex in `VIEW_INITIAL_URL_HEX` (`e612076`), so page-controlled text reaches the WSL shells only as hex; `VIEW_TAB_ID` is dropped. **The WebKit host must be rebuilt in WSL after pulling** (an old host ignores the new variable and opens its default page); the new host has been compiled there (`cargo check`, Ubuntu, 2m38s) but not run. `VIEW_INITIAL_URL` is still what the CEF, Servo and WebView2 hosts read. **Parked or held:** E7, owner-only local pipes plus a check of the connecting peer's PID (plan tasks 4 and 5), is held for a manual cross-user check that has not been done; a random pipe-name suffix is parked with E10 (frame-file naming), to be done together; a free-space check before the install download is parked (outside this wave's items); streaming redirect fetches to disk is parked because `content::passes` needs the whole body in memory; launching WebKit through `wsl.exe --exec` (no shell at all) is parked, since the hex command line already keeps page text out of the shell.
- **Fixed (2026-10-10, wave 3b, from the engine survey; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-10-wave-3b-engine-io.md`).** (E1) A send to an engine is queued and written by a per-tab writer thread (`process_manager/engine_io.rs`), and `ipc_protocol` writes each message in one `write` (`d21e7d3`, `5967c23`). An engine that stops reading no longer freezes the app, and neither does closing its tab or quitting. While an engine is behind, pointer moves and resizes merge (latest wins) and wheel steps add up; nothing else merges or reorders. A tab whose queue fills (256) while a write has been blocked for 50 ms, or whose write has been stuck for 2 s, shows a warning badge and a "Not responding" toast, and its menu offers Restart Engine and End Engine (headless `RESTARTVIEW` and `ENDVIEW`; `EXPORTSTATE` cards carry `unresponsive`). Nothing is killed automatically; messages refused while the queue was full are lost, and a restart starts from the tab's URL. (E3) Frames go to one slot per tab, other engine messages through a bounded channel (256) that blocks the reader when full, readers wake the event loop (`UserEvent::EngineMessage`, coalesced), and one drain applies at most 64 per tab (`b517a09`, `1647c1a`). The engine's own inbox is bounded the same way (`ENGINE_INBOX`, `ed9d7a8`), so an engine whose main loop is stuck backs the app's writer up too. **Rebuild every engine host after pulling:** the wire format is unchanged and `PROTOCOL_VERSION` is not bumped, but a host built before has an unbounded inbox. CEF, Servo and WebKit were not built or run with the bound (WebView2 was: `gate unresponsive`). A WebKit tab force-closed while its writer is stuck leaves that thread until the Linux host exits (closing the Windows-side socket does not unblock it). The probe measured detection at about 2.5 s after the input (the 2 s stuck-write timer plus an idle tick), not through a full queue: 1000 typed characters did not fill it. **Parked:** E2 (frame path, by decision); sends from the engine host side (by decision); the engine-side blocking waits of E9 (engine source changes); a separate host-to-engine connection for WebKit; a Restart affordance in Tab view (the badge and menu rows are on the Graph card).
- **Fixed:** spawning a WebKit tab used to be able to freeze the entire app (unresponsive to all input, including headless-mode commands) for anywhere from sub-second to multiple hours. Two root causes, both hit on every WebKit spawn: (1) `ipc-protocol`'s `accept_remote()` called a plain, unbounded `TcpListener::accept()` on the main event-loop thread, so a slow WSL2/Xvfb/GTK boot blocked everything, not just that tab — fixed with a bounded 45s connect timeout that fails cleanly into the existing spawn-error UI path instead of hanging. (2) On tab close/crash-recovery, `Drop for EngineTab` force-killed the WebKit case's `wsl.exe` relay with zero grace period — that only kills the Windows-side process, not the actual Linux-side tree (Xvfb, GTK, the engine-host binary itself), leaving orphans running indefinitely; fixed by waiting up to 3s for a graceful WSL2-side exit first. The same unbounded-`accept()` pattern also existed in `accept_local_pair` (Servo/CEF's same-machine spawn) — lower risk since there's no cold-VM-boot equivalent, but hardened the same way regardless (a 10s bound, much shorter than WebKit's 45s since a same-machine child that hasn't connected within a few seconds has failed, not merely booted slowly). **Fixed (follow-up):** the `Drop for EngineTab` fix above only runs on manifold's own clean exit path — it never fires at all when manifold.exe itself is force-killed (`taskkill /F`, a crash) rather than exited normally, since no Rust destructor runs on that kind of process death. In that case, `manifold-view-webkitgtk`'s main loop kept polling GTK, capturing frames, and silently re-attempting now-dead sends forever — confirmed live, ~45% CPU indefinitely, wholly orphaned (no Windows-side process left to tear it down).
