Root cause: its reader thread already detected the dead control socket (Windows still closes the TCP connection on process death regardless of Drop, so `recv` reliably got an `UnexpectedEof`) but only broke its own loop silently instead of telling the main loop to exit. Fixed by having that `Err` path synthesize a `Shutdown` into the same channel the real message uses, reusing the existing clean-exit code path — see `manifold-view-webkitgtk/src/main.rs`'s reader thread. Only covers a parent that's actually gone (socket closed by the OS), not one merely hung-but-alive with the socket still open — no `Err` would fire in that case.
- **Frame path (2026-10-11, survey E2; plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-11-frame-path.md`, measurements in its Results).** Every engine offers each frame to one `ipc_protocol::FrameSink`, which compares it row by row against the frame file (or, for WebKit, an in-memory store), copies only the rows that differ and reports them as `FrameReady::damage`; an unchanged frame sends nothing. The intermediate copies are gone (WebView2's `Vec`, CEF's `to_vec`), as are each engine's own create/recreate/retry code, `SharedFrameBuffer::write_frame` and WebKit's `frame_due`. The app keeps the damaged span in the tab's one-frame slot (piled-up frames union their rows, a size change makes it whole) and `GpuState::upload_frame` writes only that band, unless the texture is new, resized or marked stale (a frame was skipped). Thumbnails are dirty flags, re-rendered inside `GpuState::render`'s encoder only for thumbnails egui draws that frame (the downsample shader takes its box size from `dpdx`/`dpdy`, so there is no uniform); `free_tab_texture` flushes a dirty thumbnail first. CEF tells its browser when a tab is hidden (`was_hidden`), so hidden tabs stop painting. Measured first (2026-10-11, load 15-65%, Defender off): WebView2 and CEF already send nothing for a static page, so the saving is on pages that animate: a whole 1080p upload was 8.5 ms of app-thread CPU and a 64-row band is 0.6 ms; the removed copies are worth 40-60 ms/s of one core. The ignored benches `handoff_timing` (`frame_buffer.rs`) and `upload_timing::frame_upload_timing` (`gpu.rs`) are the evidence. Frame files are named by generation (`manifold-frame-<tab>-g<N>.bin`, protocol 4): `FrameReady::generation` names the file, an engine starts a new file whenever the frame size changes and deletes the previous one, and the app keeps one mapping per engine in `EngineTab` (`engine_io::FrameMap`), reopening only when the generation changes; a same-size frame of another generation merges as whole. The per-frame map cost at 1920x1042 fell from 2.8-2.9 ms (`open_existing` and touching every page) to 0.09-0.13 ms. Not done: creating WebView2's capture device on the app's adapter (it captures on the iGPU, the app renders on the eGPU; the cross-adapter copy is unmeasured). Servo and WebKit were run live headless (2026-10-11): frames, screenshots, animation and one `-g<N>` frame file per Servo engine (none for WebKit, which is inline) all work; a static Servo page used to stay stretched after a resize (fixed: a resize forces a paint). CEF's sink offer costs about 2.9-3.5 ms per paint even in a release host: 2.4-3.0 ms of it is the cold first read of CEF's paint buffer, which the old `to_vec` paid too (3.3-4.8 ms), so the sink saves only about 0.7 ms a paint there. CEF now offers `on_paint`'s dirty rows as a hint (`FrameSink::offer_hinted`): only those rows plus `HINT_PAD` = 8 each side are read, so a page that animates a small spot costs about 0.24 ms a paint instead of 2.8 ms (debug host, 1920x1080 spinner); every `HINT_FULL_EVERY` = 30th hinted offer compares the whole frame, since CEF's rect is not exact. Full-viewport and multi-spot pages are unchanged.
- **Fixed:** a headless `ENTER`/`ESCAPE`/`ARROWDOWN`/`ARROWUP` command immediately followed by `QUIT` with no delay between them (e.g. a fast test script) could exit the app before the earlier command was ever consumed, silently dropping it — `DebugCommand::Quit` used to call `event_loop.exit()` synchronously with no guarantee any frame ran first to process the pending flag. Fixed by deferring the exit one frame (`quit_after_next_redraw`) whenever a pending debug flag is still set when `QUIT` arrives, so the normal frame that would have consumed it gets to run first; a plain `QUIT` with nothing pending still exits immediately as before. Verified live via the exact back-to-back `ENTER`+`QUIT` sequence (temporary tracing confirmed the deferred path engages only when it should). **Same gap found and fixed for `SCREENSHOT`**: a `SCREENSHOT` immediately followed by `QUIT` had the identical problem (`pending_screenshot` is only consumed inside a `RedrawRequested`) — found live while testing a WebKit root-tab spawn, whose own cold-start delay made the race far more likely to actually trigger (a burst of queued stdin commands processed back-to-back the instant the blocking spawn call returns). `quit_after_next_redraw`'s trigger condition now also checks `pending_screenshot.is_some()`.
- **Fixed:** a headless `ENTER`/`ESCAPE`/`ARROWDOWN`/`ARROWUP` could be silently ignored for seconds after entering Tab view, e.g. `ADDRESSBAR <url>` then `ENTER` 3 s after the click didn't navigate. Those commands are dropped while a view transition runs, and a transition only ended inside a drawn frame; with frames stalled on a busy GPU (see the slow-frames entry above), one that finished its 220 ms long ago still counted as running. Confirmed by trace (`transition=true` when the ENTER arrived). `handle_debug_command` now ends a transition whose time is up before handling any command, and a dropped key command is logged (`debug: Enter ignored: ...`) instead of vanishing. Checked by removing the per-frame `finish_transition_if_done` call to simulate a stall: ENTER still navigated.
- **Fixed:** a headless `ARROWDOWN`/`ARROWUP` sent before the address bar's suggestions arrived stayed pending past the `ENTER` that navigated, then highlighted a row in the next edit's dropdown. Reproduced with `ARROWUP` + `ENTER` right after an `ADDRESSBAR`: a later plain `ENTER` searched the last suggestion instead of the typed text. `build_tab_view_ui` now takes both flags every frame, whether or not the dropdown is up, the way a real key press disappears. Found in an audit (2026-09-24) of command guards that read state only a drawn frame updates. The other guards were fine: `WAITTABS` counts spawns through `UserEvent`s, the quick switcher's and bookmark-folder rename's state is set and read by commands, `ESCAPE` is consumed in both Tab and image view, and `transition` is handled by the entry above.
- **Fixed (test side):** the smoke test's click on the root graph node sometimes missed, leaving the app in Graph view so every later command was ignored. It waited a fixed 4 s after launch, but the root tab spawns asynchronously. Traces showed a missed click arriving either before the spawn finished (no node at all), or just after it, before any frame had drawn the node (a click is hit-tested against the last drawn frame). A stale pre-`RESIZE` layout was ruled out: clicks right after `RESIZE` hit 11 runs out of 11. The smoke test now starts with `WAITTABS` and enters Tab view with `GRAPHOPEN` (5 runs out of 5 with no startup sleep at all). Any script that clicks a node should `WAITTABS` first. The "not yet drawn" half is fixed in the app: in headless mode a frame is now also rendered *before* each pointer command (`MOUSEMOVE`/`MOUSEDOWN`/`MOUSEUP`/`CLICK`/`SCROLL`), since egui hit-tests against the last frame's widgets. A `CLICK` on the root node right after `WAITTABS` went from 0 runs out of 3 to 3 out of 3.
- **WebKit tab input (mouse, keyboard)** is wired up via synthetic GDK events, same as Servo/CEF. An earlier attempt at this caused a runaway-CPU event storm and was reverted (see project memory) — the fix was attaching a real `GdkDevice` to each synthetic event (`gdk_event_set_device`), which the first attempt never did. Verified via repeated live keystrokes/clicks with no CPU escalation, and (after the frame-freeze fix below) correct on-screen text — no dropped or doubled characters. Scroll is implemented the same way but not yet cleanly live-verified (test-harness input-injection reliability issues, not a known code problem).
- **Headless testing against WebKit specifically is measurably flakier than the other two engines.** The exact same test script, unmodified, has been observed to succeed and then completely fail (a command's handler never even firing) on back-to-back runs, whenever a concurrent WSL2/Xvfb/GTK process is involved — confirmed directly via temporary tracing, not assumed. Not a deterministic app bug (Servo/CEF headless tests don't show this); budget generous real wall-clock time (10s+ before the first command, several seconds between each subsequent one) for any WebKit headless test, and re-run once before treating a no-effect result as a real regression.
- **Fixed:** an engine-swapped-to-WebKit tab's rendered frame used to go permanently stale after respawn (title/URL kept updating, the visible content never did) — `GtkOffscreenWindow`'s backing surface never actually adopted a post-spawn `Resize` message's size, so the old size-matching gate on sending `FrameReady` never passed even once. Fixed by sending whatever size the captured pixbuf actually is, rather than requiring it to match what was requested. Verified live (a real page navigated to and rendered correctly after an engine swap).
- Page-opened tabs pass `page_tab_gate.rs` (2026-10-04): only `http(s)://`, `about:blank` and `manifold://` URLs (also for the context menu's "Open in new tab"), only with a user gesture the engine reports (WebView2: `IsUserInitiated` *and* a click/key within 5 s), and at most 5 per source tab per 10 s; a drop toasts "Blocked pop-ups from this page" once per 10 s.
- Page-triggered new tabs (ctrl+click, `target=_blank`, `window.open`) work for CEF, WebKit, and WebView2 tabs — verified live for WebKit and WebView2 (clicking a `target=_blank` link spawned a real child node in the graph at the right URL; for WebView2, confirmed via process count going from 1 to 2 with no second window ever becoming visible). Servo tabs don't send these — its popup-interception API doesn't fit this project's process-per-tab architecture (see `ipc_protocol::EngineToHost::OpenTabRequested`'s doc comment). **Confirmed safe, not just assumed:** clicking a `target="_blank"` link on a Servo tab is a clean no-op — `servo`'s `WebViewDelegate::request_create_new` has an empty default body when left unimplemented (as this project leaves it), so the click registers (visible focus outline) but nothing else happens — no crash, no hang, no phantom tab, no same-tab navigation, confirmed via a live headless-mode test with a `data:` URI test page.
- **WebView2 mouse input is real OS-level native injection; keyboard is not (and can't be).** Mouse down/up/move/wheel — clicks, drag-selection, scrolling — go through `ICoreWebView2CompositionController::SendMouseInput`, real events that Chromium's own input pipeline handles exactly as it would a physical mouse (hit-testing, focus-on-click, drag-selection and scroll-target resolution all come for free). This only became possible once hosting moved to the Composition controller; see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-20-composition-hosting-migration-design.md`. **Keyboard stays script/CDP-driven, permanently:** WebView2 exposes no `SendKeyInput` equivalent on any hosting mode, so `KeyDown`/`KeyUp` use `CallDevToolsProtocolMethod("Input.dispatchKeyEvent")` (CDP-injected keys *are* trusted by Chromium, so Enter submits and Backspace deletes) and `Char` uses CDP `Input.insertText`, which types into whichever frame has focus (an iframe's field too) and keeps its order with the keys. It used `execCommand('insertText', ...)` until 2026-10-04, which ran in the top document only and went through `ExecuteScript`, a separate channel from the keys.
  - **Earlier rounds that concluded native input was impossible were wrong, and it's worth knowing why.** Raw `WM_*` messages to `Chrome_RenderWidgetHostHWND` (with and without `MoveFocus` first) really are ignored by Chromium. `SendMouseInput` really did fail in an early spike — but under the *windowed* hosting controller, which isn't what that API is for. Retried against the Composition controller it was always meant for, it works.
  - Mouse coordinates crossing the IPC boundary are **physical pixels of the engine's rendered surface**, the same space as `HostToEngine::Resize` — not CSS pixels. Confirmed live on a 125%-scaled monitor: converting to CSS pixels first (correct for the old `elementFromPoint` path, wrong for `SendMouseInput`) made every click land at 80% of the intended distance from the content's top-left corner.
  - **`KeyDown`/`KeyUp`, later session pass: no longer no-ops.** The earlier claim here -- "WebView2 has no CEF-style synthetic key-event injection API" -- was wrong. `ICoreWebView2::CallDevToolsProtocolMethod` runs *any* Chrome DevTools Protocol method against the tab's own page, and `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` is exactly the mechanism tools like Puppeteer use for `page.keyboard.press()`: a CDP-injected key event is treated as trusted by Chromium's input pipeline, so — unlike a hand-dispatched DOM `KeyboardEvent`, which is what all three earlier OS-level rounds were ultimately trying to work around — it *does* trigger default actions (Enter submits a form, Backspace deletes a character, arrow keys scroll/move a text cursor). `input.rs`'s new `dispatch_key_event` builds the CDP `windowsVirtualKeyCode`/`code`/`key`/`modifiers` parameters from `ipc_protocol::KeyCode` (a small lookup table) and `ipc_protocol::modifiers` (remapped to CDP's own, differently-ordered bitmask), leaving `Char`'s `execCommand('insertText', ...)` path untouched — the physical-key and produced-text halves of `InputEvent` stay exactly as separated as `ipc_protocol`'s own doc comment already describes. Build-verified with zero warnings; not live-tested this session.
- **Favicons, rearchitected: fetched by `manifold` itself, not aggregated from each engine's own native API (later session pass).** Previously each engine had its own favicon mechanism, and they were genuinely inconsistent: Servo/WebKit/WebView2 each worked via a different native API, and CEF's never worked at all (`on_favicon_urlchange` fires but its `icon_urls` list is always empty under CEF's windowless/Alloy runtime -- a confirmed platform limitation, not fixable from that engine-host's side; see git history for the original per-engine root-cause writeups, now removed along with the code they described). On reflection this whole approach was the wrong shape for a *multi-engine* browser -- "roughly corresponding features aggregated per engine" instead of one real, uniform implementation. Replaced with `manifold`'s own `favicon_fetch` module: on every real url change (the same gate `history`/`nav_url_changed` already use), it fetches `GET {origin}/favicon.ico` directly over HTTP (`ureq`, already a dependency for search suggestions) and decodes it (`image::load_from_memory(...).into_rgba8()`, with `image`'s `ico`/`webp`/`bmp`/`gif` features added alongside its existing `png`/`jpeg` for this). This covers all *four* engines identically, including Servo, which had no favicon support of any kind before. Doesn't parse a page's own `<link rel="icon">` (this project doesn't parse page HTML at all) -- the plain `/favicon.ico` guess is the same fallback every browser's own native favicon logic ultimately uses anyway, and covers the overwhelming majority of real sites.
  - Same "spawn a thread, `mpsc` channel, main loop `tick()`s it" pattern `SuggestionFetcher` already established, deduplicating concurrent requests for the same origin via an `in_flight` set rather than generation-tagging (a favicon fetch has no notion of "stale" the way a debounced search query does).
  - The one-time `rustls`/`ureq` TLS/DNS warm-up (see the startup-crash-loop fix below) used to live inside `SuggestionFetcher::new()` specifically; pulled out into a shared `suggestions::warm_up_https_client()`, called once from `App::default()` before either fetcher is constructed -- a second network consumer doing its own redundant warm-up (or worse, racing a real request against the *other* consumer's not-yet-finished one) would have silently reintroduced the exact crash risk that fix was for.
  - `GpuState`'s favicon texture cache is now keyed by **origin**, not tab id -- two tabs on the same site share one texture instead of each independently fetching/decoding/uploading its own, and a favicon now genuinely outlives the specific tab that first triggered its fetch. `favicon_id`/`upload_favicon`'s two render call sites (toolbar icon, graph card) look up by `favicon_fetch::url_origin(&node.url)` instead of the tab/node id. No longer tied to tab-close cleanup (an origin can outlive every tab that visited it) -- instead bounded by a new `MAX_CACHED_FAVICONS` (200) FIFO eviction, the same bounded-cache posture `MAX_CLOSED_TABS`/`MAX_CLOSED_GROUPS` already have elsewhere.
  - `ipc_protocol::EngineToHost::FaviconChanged` is gone entirely, along with every engine-host's own favicon plumbing: Servo's `notify_favicon_changed`/`to_rgba`, WebKit's `FaviconDatabase` setup/fetch/`favicon_to_rgba`, CEF's `on_favicon_urlchange`/`fetch_favicon`/`SharedFaviconUrl` (and its now-unused `image`/`ureq` dependencies), and WebView2's entire `favicon.rs` module. Build-verified across all four engine-hosts (including WebKit inside its actual WSL2 build environment) and the full `manifold` workspace, all clean with zero warnings -- not yet live-tested this session, per the user's own standing request to hold off on further automated headless runs.
- **Graph view's node model unified: tab, download, and group nodes all now share one `GraphNode`/`NodePayload` shape.** Prompted by a request to make Graph view an extensible "arrange anything" canvas (notes, embeddable files, JSON compute graphs), which the previous three-hardcoded-types-in-three-separate-`HashMap`s model (`TabNode`/`DownloadNode`/`GroupNode`) couldn't support. Of the architecture options considered (one big tagged enum; trait objects; a shared struct plus a payload enum), picked the shared-struct approach: `GraphNode` holds the fields every kind shares (`parent`, `group`, `pos`, `vel`, `color`), `NodePayload` holds only what's kind-specific (`TabPayload`: the engine handle, `url`/`title`/nav state, `muted`/`pinned`/`swap_target`; `DownloadPayload`: `path`, byte progress; `GroupPayload`: just `name`, since tag color moved to the now-shared `GraphNode::color`). `App`'s old `tabs`/`tab_order`, `downloads`/`download_order`, and `groups`/`group_order` are all now the one `nodes: HashMap<String, GraphNode>` + `node_order: Vec<String>` pair — all three kinds' id spaces were already disjoint (`"dl-"`/`"grp-"` prefixes, tab ids their own separate scheme, since `graph_layout::step` always treated every node as one flat id space regardless of kind), so merging needed no id remapping. Done as three deliberately separate, incremental steps rather than one big-bang rewrite — download (~35 call sites), then group (~40), then tab last (~130, by far the largest, touching nearly every part of the file: navigation, IPC event handling, session save/load, quick-switcher, context menus, drag/drop, crash/swap recovery, headless debug commands). Used the compiler as the completeness check at each step: delete the old fields, rebuild, fix every resulting error site in turn — guarantees nothing was silently missed, unlike a manual grep sweep; the tab step alone worked through over 140 compiler-reported sites this way. Two real bugs surfaced along the way that weren't type errors, so the compiler couldn't catch them on its own: (1) during the group step, `downloads_at_current_level()` filtered only by `node.group == current_group`, which happened to also match every top-level group node (a group's own `group` field is always `None`, same as `current_group` at the top level) once groups joined the same map — silently leaking group cards into download-only iteration; fixed with an explicit `n.as_download().is_some()` kind filter, with the group-side equivalent factored into its own `groups_at_current_level()`. (2) during the tab step, the IPC event-drain loop holds a live mutable borrow of one tab's own `self.nodes` entry for its entire per-tab `try_recv` drain (needed continuously, since the loop condition itself re-reads it) — before this merge, download bookkeeping lived in a separate `self.downloads` field, so creating/updating a download node from inside that same loop was a disjoint, unproblematic borrow; once download bookkeeping moved into the *same* `self.nodes` map the tab-event loop already borrows from, `DownloadStarted`/`Progress`/`Completed` handling would have been a second, conflicting mutable borrow of the same field. Fixed by deferring all three download event kinds into one ordered `Vec<DownloadEvent>` (preserving relative order across kinds, not just within one, so a fast download's whole `Started`→`Progress`→`Completed` sequence arriving in a single drain still applies in the right order) and applying them after the per-tab loop finishes, the same deferred-effects pattern `frame_updates`/`crashes`/`swap_finishes` already used for other reasons. Build-verified across `manifold` and the full workspace (excluding WebKit, which needs its own WSL2 build) with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session, per the user's own standing request to hold off on further automated headless runs.
- **Fixed:** node card decorations (a tab's title/pin icon, etc., painted via `overlay_painter` -- a dedicated untransformed `Order::Foreground` layer used for crisp text at any zoom, see that painter's own doc comment) rendered on top of the keyboard-shortcuts popup and several other Graph-view popups (recently-closed, and the group/download/tab context menus) instead of underneath them, reported live. Root cause: within one egui `Order` tier, layers paint in the order they're first touched each frame -- `build_shortcuts_help`/etc. were called from `build_graph_ui` well before the Scene/node-rendering block that first creates `overlay_painter`, so that layer (created later in the same frame) always painted on top regardless of which popup was actually open. Fixed by moving all five popup-building calls to the end of `build_graph_ui`, after `overlay_painter`'s layer already exists for the frame -- the button rects they need (`shortcuts_help_button_rect`/`recently_closed_button_rect`) are computed earlier in the function but read fine from the new call site, since egui's own Area logic tracks a popup's position across frames regardless of exactly when in the frame it's built. **Later: that root cause was wrong, and the overlap was back** (seen 2026-09-25 under egui 0.36: a failed tab's context menu under its own url, and the minimap). `GraphicsLayers::drain` paints, within each `Order`, the Area layers in stacking order and then any bare layer that isn't an Area, such as `overlay_painter`'s, after all of them, whenever it was first touched. So a bare Foreground layer covers every Foreground popup. **Fixed at the root:** `overlay_painter`'s layer is `Order::Middle`, above the Scene (Background) and below every popup, menu and toast (all Foreground Areas). The popup calls stay at the end of `build_graph_ui`, which is harmless. Also, a card's hover tooltip (a missing file's or failed tab's error) no longer shows while a card menu is open; it had covered the menu of the card just right-clicked. Checked headless: a failed tab's menu, and the keyboard-shortcuts popup, now cover the card labels and the minimap; marquee selection still works (`graphsplit.txt`).
  - Found and fixed a duplicate loop while in this area: `group_merge_requested`'s handler reparented tabs/downloads off the dissolved group's id via two back-to-back, functionally identical loops over `self.nodes.values_mut()` -- a leftover from the node-model migration above, where a same-shaped `self.tabs`/`self.downloads` pair collapsed onto the one `self.nodes` map, and a blind rename turned both into the same loop without the duplication being noticed at the time. Collapsed to one loop; no behavior change.
- **Notes: the first genuinely new Graph-view node kind, built on top of the unified `GraphNode`/`NodePayload` model above rather than another migration of a pre-existing type.** The actual original ask that motivated that whole refactor -- Graph view as an extensible "arrange anything" canvas, starting with the easiest new kind (a plain sticky note; images/PDFs/JSON-compute-graph nodes were assessed separately as moderate-to-much-larger follow-on work, not attempted here). `NodePayload::Note(NotePayload { text })` -- no engine process or IPC behind it, unlike every other kind so far. Created via the Graph toolbar's new `NOTE_PENCIL` button (any level, like "+ New Tab", not top-level-only like "+ New Group" — a note is a plain member of whichever subgraph it's created in) or the headless `NEWNOTE` debug command. Rendered as a warm-yellow card (`NOTE_CARD_COLOR`, deliberately unlike every other card's dark chrome tone, so it reads as "a physical note" at a glance) whose body is a real editable `egui::TextEdit`, not overlay-painted static text the way every other kind's title strip is -- editing happens directly in place, on the graph. Only the card's header strip (not the text body) is drag-sensitive, via its own separate interact rect, so clicking inside the text to place a cursor or select text is never mistaken for the start of a card drag; a second, hover-only interact over the whole card (harmless to layer under the text widget, since a pure hover sense claims no clicks) drives the close-button reveal and hover bookkeeping so hovering the text body still counts. Persisted in `session.json` (`session::SessionNote`: `id`/`text`/`pos_x`/`pos_y`) -- checked with the user first, since a note being user-authored content (unlike the mostly-display-preference state this file otherwise holds) is a real schema decision: confirmed persisting it was the right call, unlike groups/pin state/tag color, which stay session-only. Like a tab's `parent`, a note's own group membership isn't persisted either (matching `SessionTab`'s existing scope, which doesn't persist `group` for tabs today), so a restored note always lands at the top level regardless of which subgraph it was created in. `restore_session`'s own "nothing to restore" check was widened from `tabs.is_empty()` to `tabs.is_empty() && notes.is_empty()`, so a session with notes but zero tabs isn't silently discarded.
  - Found and fixed a third instance of the same duplicate-loop artifact the group-migration commit already found one of: `dissolve_group`'s own group-membership-clearing had two back-to-back, functionally identical loops over `self.nodes.values_mut()`, from the same tabs/downloads-to-one-map merge. Collapsed to one; no behavior change.
  - Refactored `remove_download_node` into a generic `remove_node` (same body -- plain `self.nodes`/`node_order` removal plus the stale-`dragging`/`hovered` guard `close_tab`/`finish_swap` also apply) once note deletion needed the identical logic; neither kind has a process, membership, or file to additionally tear down the way closing a tab or dissolving a group does.
  - **Follow-up: note right-click context menu and drag-into-group, the two gaps the initial Notes cut deliberately left for later.** `build_note_context_menu` (Copy Text, Remove from Group when grouped, Delete) mirrors `build_download_context_menu`'s exact shape -- same `fade_popup_state`/`clicked_outside_popup` pattern, a new `note_context_menu`/`note_context_menu_cache` pair, and a `hit_note` check slotted into the existing right-click hit-test priority (group, then download, then note, then tab). Dropping a dragged note onto a group's card joins that group, the same gesture a tab already has -- unlike a tab, dropping a note onto another *ungrouped* note doesn't auto-create a new group, since that "two loose items dropped together" convenience is tab-specific UX this doesn't try to replicate. A group's own member-count label (bottom of an empty/collapsed group card) was silently counting every kind already, but read `"N tabs"` regardless -- harmless before, since only tabs could ever join a group; inaccurate now that a note can too, so reworded to `"N members"`.
- **Images: the second new Graph-view node kind, following Notes onto the same unified model.** `NodePayload::Image(ImagePayload { path, width, height })` -- like a note, no engine process or IPC behind it, but unlike a note (and every other kind) its card isn't a fixed size: `image_node_card_size` scales the decoded picture's own `width`x`height` down (never up, so a small picture isn't blurrily stretched) to fit inside a capped `IMAGE_NODE_MAX_SIZE` box while preserving its real aspect ratio, and every place a fixed `*_NODE_SIZE` constant gets unioned into a bounds rect or hit-tested against (`fit_all_nodes_scene_rect`, the right-click hit-test) computes this per-node instead. Added via the toolbar's new `IMAGE_SQUARE` button (a real file picker, `!self.headless`-gated same as every other file-dialog action here) or the headless `ADDIMAGE <path>` command, both funneling through `insert_image_node`/`insert_image_node_with_id` -- decodes the file once via `image::open` (already a dependency, same crate favicon decoding uses) and registers it as its own GPU texture (`GpuState::images`, a new node-id-keyed map alongside `thumbnails`/`favicons`, freed one-for-one via `free_image` on node removal -- unlike `favicons`' origin-keyed sharing/FIFO eviction, there's no cross-node sharing to do and no unbounded-growth risk, since it's bounded by however many images the user deliberately adds). Persisted in `session.json` (`session::SessionImage`) as just the file path, re-decoded at restore rather than duplicating the picture's own bytes a second time -- if the file has since moved or been deleted, that one node is silently skipped rather than failing the whole session load. Card interaction (drag, hover-revealed close button, right-click context menu with Open Containing Folder/Copy Path/Remove from Group/Delete, drop-onto-a-group-card) mirrors Notes' own follow-up work above, minus the header-strip-only-drag workaround Notes needed -- an image card has no embedded interactive widget competing for clicks, so the whole card is plain `Sense::click_and_drag()` like a tab or download.
  - Found and fixed a fourth instance of the recurring duplicate-loop artifact: `replace_running_session` ("Open Session" replacing the whole running session with a different saved one) had `self.nodes.clear(); self.node_order.clear();` twice back-to-back. Collapsed to one; no behavior change.
  - **Fixed, reported live:** a created/edited/moved/deleted note never actually made it into `session.json` unless some unrelated tab action (navigate, focus, close) happened to trigger a save. Root cause: `save_session` is deliberately *not* a timer (see its own doc comment -- "a save that's actually continuous... every mutation calls `save_session`") -- every note-mutating path (`new_note_shortcut`, dragging a note, editing its text, `note_close_requested`) was missing its own `save_session()` call, the same explicit-call-per-mutation discipline every tab-mutating path already has. A brand-new note created and typed into, with no tab ever touched in that run, was pure in-memory state that vanished the instant the app closed. Fixed by adding the call at each site: on creation, on drag-release (not continuously while dragging -- same "commit once, not every frame" reasoning as `save_session`'s own design), on the note's `TextEdit` losing focus (commits on blur, not per keystroke, so typing doesn't hit disk on every character), and on deletion.
- **PDFs: the third new Graph-view node kind, following Images onto the same unified model.** `NodePayload::Pdf(PdfPayload { path, outcome: Option<Result<PdfOutcome, String>> })` -- rendered via a rasterizing library, unlike every prior kind, so this one first needed real crate research (live crates.io/GitHub data, not cached knowledge, per explicit request) rather than an obvious pick. Ruled out `mupdf-rs` (AGPL-3.0 -- would copyleft-infect the whole project) and `poppler-rs` (binds `poppler-glib`, GTK/Linux-first -- would reintroduce the GTK-on-native-Windows problem this project has structurally avoided, since WebKitGTK only ever runs inside WSL here); confirmed `lopdf` (despite its own huge download count) does no rendering at all, just parsing. Landed on `pdfium-render` (MIT/Apache-2.0, wraps Google's PDFium, most actively maintained of the rendering options, and its `image_025` Cargo feature returns a plain `image::RgbaImage` -- the exact type this project's GPU-upload path already wants) plus `pdfium-auto` (the most-adopted/transparent of the binary-sourcing helper crates -- confirmed by reading its actual source that `bind_pdfium_silent()` downloads+caches the real `pdfium.dll` from `bblanchon/pdfium-binaries` lazily at first *runtime* call, not via a `build.rs` fetch, so `cargo build` itself stays offline-friendly and -- corrected an assumption stated mid-research -- no `sync-runtime-deps.sh` step is needed the way CEF/Servo's own bundled runtimes require).
  - **Version-pin bug found and fixed:** adding `pdfium-render = "0.9"` directly (latest) alongside `pdfium-auto 0.3.1`'s own internal pin of `pdfium-render 0.8.37` resolved as two distinct, incompatible `Pdfium` types in the dependency graph -- `cargo build` failed with a `Pdfium`-vs-`Pdfium` type mismatch until diagnosed via `cargo tree -i pdfium-render` (showing both versions present) and fixed by pinning manifold's own dependency to the exact `pdfium-render = "=0.8.37"` to unify the graph.
  - New `pdf_render::PdfRenderer` (same "spawn a thread, `mpsc` channel, main loop `tick()`s it" pattern `favicon_fetch` already established) does the render off the UI thread -- both the PDFium bind (a real first-time network fetch, once per machine) and the render itself are slow enough to matter more than a favicon's own small HTTP fetch. `insert_pdf_node_with_id` inserts the node immediately with `outcome: None` ("still loading") rather than checking the file synchronously the way `insert_image_node_with_id` does -- a bad/missing PDF path surfaces later as `Some(Err(String))`, not as "node never created," a deliberate difference from Image's fail-fast posture given the render is now genuinely asynchronous. The card renders three states: a spinner while `None`, a `FILE_X` glyph (hover shows the error) when `Some(Err(_))`, or the rendered first page plus a small "N pages" label once `Some(Ok(_))`. Reuses `GpuState`'s own `images`/`upload_image`/`image_id`/`free_image` map verbatim -- a rendered PDF page is just another RGBA bitmap by the time it reaches the GPU, no PDF-specific texture handling needed. Persisted in `session.json` (`session::SessionPdf`) as just the file path, same as `SessionImage` -- width/height/page count are all re-derived by rendering the first page again at restore, not duplicated.
  - **Bug found and fixed during self-review (not user-reported): eager PDFium bind at app startup.** The first `PdfRenderer::new()` draft called `bind_pdfium_silent()` immediately inside the spawned thread, before waiting for any request -- meaning every app launch would attempt a PDFium bind (and potentially trigger a real first-time download) even if the user never adds a PDF that run. Fixed by moving the bind to lazily happen on the *first* actual request (`Option<Result<Pdfium, String>>` outside the loop, `get_or_insert_with` inside it), the same "don't pay a cost nothing asked for yet" reasoning behind this project's own startup-lag fix above.
  - **Bug found and fixed during self-review (not user-reported): orphaned GPU texture on delete-during-render-in-flight.** The `pdf_renderer.tick()` drain loop originally called `gpu.upload_image()` unconditionally for every finished render, without checking whether that node still existed -- deleting a PDF node while its render was still in flight would already have run `free_image` at delete time, so the later-arriving result would upload a texture nothing would ever free (a real leak, not just wasted work). Fixed by checking `self.nodes.get(&id).and_then(|n| n.as_pdf()).is_none()` and discarding the stale result before any upload or mutation.
  - Added via the toolbar's new `FILE_PDF` button (a file picker, same `!self.headless`-gated posture as Images) or the headless `ADDPDF <path>` command. Context menu (Open Containing Folder/Copy Path/Remove from Group/Delete) and drag/drop-onto-group mirror Images' own exactly. Build-verified across the full workspace (excluding WebKit) with zero warnings, including a full CEF/Servo rebuild confirming no cross-crate breakage from adding `pdfium-render`/`pdfium-auto` to `manifold` alone -- not yet live-tested, per the user's own standing convention of build-verifying and leaving live testing to them.
- **Navigating straight to an image url redirects the tab into a real `Image` graph node**, instead of leaving it sitting there as an ordinary tab showing whichever native image-viewer page each engine happens to render for a direct image response. Detected the exact same way as `JsonBody` -- once a load finishes, the same `document.contentType` script/`V8Context` eval every engine already runs for JSON detection now also checks for an `image/` prefix, disambiguated over one round trip via a `"json\n"` prefix on the JSON-body outcome (safe since it's prepended before any real page content). `manifold` doesn't try to read the image's bytes back out of the engine at all -- a `redirect_fetch` module does `manifold`'s own independent `GET` of the tab's current url (same "own uniform HTTP fetch, engine-agnostic" posture `favicon_fetch` already established) on a background thread, validates the bytes actually decode as an image, and saves them to a temp file. Once that completes, `redirect_tab_to_image` reuses `insert_image_node_with_id` completely unchanged -- a redirected image is indistinguishable from one added via "+ Image" from that point on: same GPU upload path, same session persistence (a since-cleared temp file is silently skipped at restore, same as any other missing local image file), same context menu. Reparents the tab's own children to its own parent and clears the same dragging/hovered/focused guards `close_tab` does, but doesn't push to the recently-closed-tabs list -- this isn't a user-initiated close to remember as reopenable. Guards against the tab being closed or re-navigated while the fetch was still in flight by re-checking, once the fetch completes, that the tab still exists and is still showing the exact url that was fetched -- a stale in-flight fetch is silently dropped rather than converting the wrong page. Build-verified across the full workspace (including WebKit inside WSL2) with zero warnings. Live-tested 2026-09-30 on WebView2 and CEF by `scripts/page-kind-probe.sh`.
  - **Generalized, later session pass: dispatch to node kind by mime type, not a bare per-kind signal.** The original `ImagePage` message carried no data at all -- every engine decided for itself "yes, this is an image" and sent a fixed sentinel. Replaced with `ipc_protocol::EngineToHost::RedirectableContent { content_type: String }`: every engine now forwards the *real* `document.contentType` string for any non-JSON response, and `manifold`'s own `redirect_fetch::classify` is the one place that maps a content type to a `RedirectKind` (today just `Image`, matching an `image/` prefix). This means a future redirectable kind is a `manifold`-only change -- widen `classify` (and add one match arm in the `redirect_fetcher.tick()` dispatch) -- not a script/eval change repeated across all four engine-hosts. `RedirectFetcher`/`RedirectResult`/`fetch_to_temp_file` (renamed from the image-only `ImageRedirectFetcher`) are already shaped for more than one kind: `validate` branches on `RedirectKind` to confirm the downloaded bytes actually match before ever touching a node, and the `tick()` drain dispatches on it to call the right `redirect_tab_to_*` function.
  - **Superseded 2026-09-27 (content dispatch, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-content-dispatch-design.md`): JSON is now converted automatically, "Save as Node" and `build_json_viewer` are gone, and the reasoning below no longer applies.** Kept for the trail.
  - **JSON deliberately isn't in the automatic allowlist, despite being detected through the exact same mechanism.** A JSON response is often a live, refetchable API call worth keeping as an ordinary tab (re-navigate, copy the url, hit reload for fresh data) -- unlike an image, which has nothing left to offer once redirected. Automatically destroying a live API tab to replace it with a frozen snapshot would be a real regression dressed up as a feature. Instead, the JSON viewer popup (`build_json_viewer`) gained a "Save as Node" button, shown only when the popup is showing a tab's own `json_body` (not already a saved `JsonPayload` node, which has nothing further to save) -- `save_json_body_as_node` writes the in-memory body to a temp file and reuses `insert_json_node_with_id` unchanged, the same "downloaded bytes become a real file first" pattern the automatic kinds use. The tab itself is never touched: no fetch, no teardown, no reparenting -- a node is *added* alongside it, not a replacement. This is the general shape for any future kind where the source tab is still worth keeping: automatic + destructive for kinds with nothing left to offer, opt-in + additive for kinds that are still useful as a tab.
  - Build-verified across the full workspace (including WebKit inside WSL2) with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session.
- **Superseded below (2026-09-27 JSON level rework): double-clicking a Json card no longer opens `build_json_viewer`'s popup — see the JSON node entry near the end of this section.** Kept for the debugging trail, not as current behavior.
- **JSON: the fourth new Graph-view node kind, following PDF onto the same unified model.** `NodePayload::Json(JsonPayload { path, value: serde_json::Value })` -- like Notes/Images, no engine process or IPC behind it, and unlike PDF/Image it has no GPU texture of any kind: the card is drawn with plain egui widgets, a fixed `JSON_NODE_SIZE` box showing a `BRACKETS_CURLY` icon plus `json_summary`'s own one-line shape readout (`{ 5 }` for an object, `[ 12 ]` for an array, or the scalar's own compact `Display` form, truncated past 24 characters). The full tree only ever shows in a popup -- double-clicking the card reuses `build_json_viewer` completely unchanged, the exact same full-screen collapsible-tree popup a tab's own "View as JSON" already opens. That reuse needed one small generalization: `App::json_viewer_tab` (a tab id) became `json_viewer_node` (any node id), and the popup now looks up either a tab's raw `json_body` (reparsed on the fly, with its existing invalid-JSON fallback) or a JSON node's already-parsed `value` directly (no reparse, and no fallback needed -- a JSON node's `value` was already validated at creation).
  - Added via the toolbar's new `BRACKETS_CURLY` button (a file picker, same `!self.headless`-gated posture as Image/PDF) or the headless `ADDJSON <path>` command, both funneling through `insert_json_node`/`insert_json_node_with_id` -- reads and parses the file synchronously (`std::fs::read_to_string` + `serde_json::from_str`), returning `false` on a bad/missing/non-JSON file, same fail-fast posture `insert_image_node` already has (a JSON parse is just as fast as decoding an image, so there was no reason to give this `Pdf`'s own background-render treatment). Persisted in `session.json` (`session::SessionJson`) as just the file path, same "re-derive, don't duplicate" posture Image/PDF already have -- a since-moved-or-deleted file is silently skipped at restore rather than failing the whole session load.
  - Card interaction (drag, hover-revealed close button, right-click context menu with Open Containing Folder/Copy Path/Remove from Group/Delete, drop-onto-a-group-card) mirrors PDF's own exactly, minus the `gpu.free_image` call on delete -- a JSON node has no texture to free, same as a note. Deleting (or closing) a JSON node whose own content is currently showing in `build_json_viewer` also clears `json_viewer_node`, so the popup doesn't linger pointing at a node that no longer exists.
  - Considered, not done here (*done later: a tab whose page is JSON becomes a JSON node through content dispatch, 2026-09-27*): converting a JSON-viewing *tab* directly into a permanent JSON node (mirroring the image-url-redirect feature above) -- a natural follow-up, but a separate feature from "add a JSON node from a local file," which is what was actually asked for this pass.
  - Build-verified across the full workspace with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session.
- **Split view: engine-vs-engine, Tab view's toolbar "Split" picker.** Prompted by a survey of what other browsers offer (Arc's Spaces/command bar, Vivaldi's tab tiling, Zen's 4-pane split) looking for the one idea genuinely specific to *this* project's own architecture rather than a copy of something any single-engine browser already does -- comparing the same url rendered by two different engines side by side, live, is something no other browser can do at all. Picking an engine from the new toolbar dropdown spawns a real second tab at the focused tab's current url (`App::start_split`, a new `SpawnPurpose::Split`), parented to it and sharing its group, and shows it in a second, right-hand pane once it finishes spawning.
  - **The core assumption this overturned:** `GpuState` had exactly one `TabTexture` slot for the whole app, with its own doc comment explicitly reasoning "since only the focused tab ever produces frames (background tabs are `SetVisible(false)`), a single slot (not a per-tab map) is enough" -- true until a second tab could also be live at once. Became `tab_textures: HashMap<String, TabTexture>`, freed via a new `free_tab_texture` whenever a tab stops being visible (unfocused, split closed, or the tab closes outright) so it doesn't silently accumulate one full-resolution texture per tab ever focused.
  - `GpuState::render`'s own signature changed from one `(content_rect_px, focused_tab_id)` pair to a `panes: &[((f32,f32,f32,f32), &str)]` slice (0 for Graph view, 1 for ordinary Tab view or a `Transition`, 2 for a split). This needed no new scissor-rect machinery: the existing hand-rolled content pipeline already draws a fullscreen triangle clipped to whatever `set_viewport` rect is currently active, so compositing two panes into disjoint screen regions is just calling the existing per-pane draw function twice (first clears the color attachment, second loads it) -- a new `record_content_layers` helper is the whole change, both the on-screen and the offscreen screenshot/PDF-export path route through it identically.
  - **Input stays primary-only in this first cut.** The secondary pane is a real, live, fully-rendering tab -- but keyboard input and chrome (address bar, back/forward, reload, find, zoom, mute, screenshot) still only ever target the primary (left) pane; the secondary pane is view-only, with just a small floating engine-label overlay and a divider line drawn by real egui on top of both raw content quads (it has no toolbar of its own to put a label in). Mouse events gate on a new `split_divider_x_px` -- events landing right of it are simply not forwarded, rather than accidentally driving the primary tab's page from what looks like a click on the other pane.
  - Each pane resizes to exactly half the content width (`App::content_size_px`, no divider gap reserved) rather than the full window, so each engine's own page layout (media queries, etc.) reflects its real on-screen footprint -- both panes are re-resized whenever the split opens, closes, or the window itself resizes.
  - A split only ever makes sense paired with the specific tab it was opened against: switching primary focus to a different tab (`focus_tab`) always closes it first; closing either half of the pair (`close_tab`) clears the whole thing rather than leaving a dangling reference to a node that no longer exists. Closing the split itself (`close_split`) only unpairs the secondary tab -- it remains a normal, closable graph node, exactly like any tab not currently shown in a split.
  - **Crash recovery double-checked, not just assumed correct:** the secondary pane's own engine-host can crash and go through the exact same `respawn_tab`/`SpawnPurpose::Respawn` path any other tab's crash recovery uses, which only ever restores visibility for whichever tab is `self.focused` -- reasoned through explicitly and fixed by also treating "is this id currently `self.split_tab`" as reason enough to stay visible and get resized on respawn, so a crash on the secondary pane specifically doesn't silently leave it blacked out until the user notices and reopens the split by hand.
  - New headless commands `SPLIT <servo|cef|webkit|webview2>` / `CLOSESPLIT` mirror the toolbar exactly, for deterministic testing without needing to click through a `ComboBox` at coordinates that shift with the toolbar's own layout.
  - Build-verified across the full workspace with zero warnings. This is the largest single change to the render pipeline this session made (the previous single-texture-slot assumption ran through the whole content-compositing path) -- live-testing it was attempted, but hit the same sandboxed-bash-script path restriction documented on the WebView2 key-dispatch entry above before the app itself ever launched; the user's real `session.json` was backed up first and restored byte-identical afterward, untouched either way.
- **CEF audio indicator and mute, later session pass: the "would need a full `AudioHandler`" verdict above only applied to a real native read/write. It doesn't apply to an approximation built on the `RenderProcessHandler`/`V8Context` machinery the JSON-viewing work above already added.** Muting is a plain write, so it's the simpler half: `HostToEngine::SetMuted`'s CEF handler calls the already-available `Frame::execute_java_script` (fire-and-forget, no `RenderProcessHandler` involvement needed at all) to set `.muted` directly on every `<video>`/`<audio>` element on the page.
  - The audio-*playing* indicator is a read, so it does reuse the `V8Context::eval` + `ProcessMessage` round trip -- but on a new timer, not the existing per-navigation one: there's no single "audio started" event to hook the way a page load has, so `manifold-view-cef`'s own main loop now polls every `AUDIO_PLAYING_POLL_INTERVAL` (1s), sending a new `check-audio-playing` request regardless of `visible` (background-tab audio still needs an accurate dot, same as every other engine's own passive read). The renderer's reply evals `Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('video,audio')).some(el => !el.paused && !el.muted)` and answers with a real boolean `ProcessMessage` argument (`ListValue::set_bool`/`bool`) rather than a string.
  - **Real, stated tradeoff, not hidden:** both directions are a media-element-only approximation -- accurate for the common case (YouTube, most players), but blind to audio a page produces through the Web Audio API alone with nothing playing through an actual `<video>`/`<audio>` tag (some games/synths). WebView2/WebKit's own native reads have no such gap; this is the price of not taking over CEF's whole audio pipeline for it.
  - The dedupe against "only sent when the value actually changes" (`ipc_protocol::EngineToHost::AudioPlayingChanged`'s own contract, needed since this is now a poll rather than a real event) happens in `manifold-view-cef`'s own main loop, not in the shared `SharedAudioPlaying` cell itself, which is simply overwritten with the latest reply every tick.
  - Build-verified across the full workspace with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session.
- **Notes render as real Markdown now, dual-mode.** `NotePayload` gained an `editing: bool` (session-only, like `TabPayload::pinned` -- a restored note always starts rendered regardless of what it showed when the app last closed; a freshly created note starts `editing: true` so typing works immediately). `editing: true` shows exactly what Notes always have -- a real, focused `TextEdit`; `editing: false` renders `text` as real Markdown (`egui_commonmark`) instead, read-only, with the whole card (not just the header strip) clickable to switch back to editing -- the same "click to edit, blur to render" split Obsidian/Notion use, since a rendered view has no natural place to put a text cursor.
  - `egui_commonmark = { default-features = false, features = ["pulldown_cmark"] }` -- deliberately drops the crate's own default-on `load-images` feature: a note's body is user-typed text, and rendering an `![alt](url)` inside it would otherwise fire a real, unreviewed HTTP GET the instant a note renders, purely from typing a URL into it -- the same passive-tracking-pixel concern email clients disable remote image loading for by default.
  - **Bug found and fixed live, not assumed correct: a click squarely on a rendered note never registered at all.** The card's own click-and-drag `ui.interact()` was registered *before* the Markdown content it draws -- but `ui.scope_builder`'s own child `Ui` (used to constrain `CommonMarkViewer::show` to the card's text area) allocates space over most of that same region, and -- the identical "later-registered content wins hit-testing over an earlier same-region interact" rule this README already documents for the minimap/Scene-background bug -- that later allocation was silently winning the pointer every time, leaving the card's own interact permanently unhovered no matter where you clicked. Confirmed via temporary debug logging comparing `ui.interact()`'s reported `hovered()`/`clicked()` against the real pointer position and the note's own projected screen center, which matched almost exactly while `hovered()` stayed `false` for hundreds of consecutive frames -- ruling out a coordinate/timing issue before looking at registration order instead. Fixed by moving the card's own interact to *after* the Markdown render (the editing branch, where the header strip and text body never overlap, was never affected and keeps its original order). Verified live afterward: a click on a rendered note switched it to show raw Markdown source in a real `TextEdit`, exactly as intended.
  - Real, accepted limitation for this pass: `CommonMarkViewer` has no built-in scroll (its own `show_scrollable` is flagged upstream as "buggy in scenarios more complex than the example"), so Markdown longer than the fixed `NOTE_NODE_SIZE` card overflows past its bottom edge rather than scrolling or growing the card -- same visual tradeoff a plain `TextEdit` would have without a `ScrollArea` wrapped around it.
  - Build-verified across the full workspace with zero warnings; live-verified this time (see the bug writeup above) rather than left untested.
- Graph-view thumbnails are downscaled (box-sampled to at most 256×192) before upload — Tab view's own live content stays full resolution.
- **Fixed:** two bugs found while building address-bar autocomplete. (1) The address bar's typed text used to snap back to the tab's real URL mid-keystroke — egui's own focus tracking transiently leaves the `TextEdit` for one frame the moment the suggestion dropdown's buttons first appear, so a naive `if !focused { resync }` fired on that blip; fixed by also treating non-empty suggestions as "still editing." (2) The exact same blip also hid the dropdown's own `Area` (buttons included) on the one frame a click landed on a row, silently swallowing the click — clicking a suggestion looked like it did nothing. Fixed the same way: gate the dropdown on the broader "still editing" check, not raw focus. (3) Separately, the very first HTTPS request `manifold`'s process ever makes (`ureq`/`rustls`'s one-time TLS setup plus a cold DNS+TLS handshake) was slow enough, the first time it happened to coincide with the user's first search query, to trip Servo's engine-host into a crash/respawn loop; fixed with a throwaway warm-up fetch at startup so that one-time cost is absorbed before the user can hit it.
- **Fixed:** a background Servo tab refocused from Graph view didn't repaint — content stayed black indefinitely even though chrome (title, spinner) kept updating and the engine-host process was alive. Root cause: `SetVisible(true)`'s capture-and-`FrameReady`-send block was gated on `notify_new_frame_ready` having fired again on its own, which never happens for a static page that received no input while hidden — the gate stayed shut forever. Fixed by forcing one repaint on the `false`→`true` transition instead of waiting for Servo to decide something changed. **Verification gap, now narrowed further (not fully closed):** added a `SETVISIBLE <bool>` headless debug command specifically to toggle a tab's visibility with zero `Resize` involved (the earlier suspicion — that `focus_tab`'s always-bundled `Resize` was masking the race — needed a way to rule it out). With `Resize` fully out of the picture, a negative-control test (temporarily reverting the fix, rebuilding, retesting) *still* didn't reproduce a black screen — meaning `Resize` wasn't the (or wasn't the only) masking factor. Something in Servo appears to keep re-arming `notify_new_frame_ready` periodically even for a page that looks static (a compositor heartbeat, caret blink, or similar), so `frame_ready` is essentially never caught sitting `false` at the exact instant a real toggle happens within a scripted multi-second window. The fix remains correct by inspection and provably safe either way (worst case: one harmless extra repaint) — the `SETVISIBLE` command is kept as a tool for whoever next has better luck (or a way to force Servo fully idle first) pinning down the precise repaint cadence needed for a deterministic repro.
- **Bookmarks/History/Downloads folded into one "Lists" toolbar dropdown, and all three now show even with an empty list.** Previously each was its own button (`"Bookmarks (N)"`/`"History (N)"`/`"Downloads (N)"`), individually gated on `!self.X.is_empty()` — meaning the toolbar's own width shifted as lists filled up, and there was no way to discover any of the three existed before ever adding a bookmark, visiting a page, or downloading a file. Replaced with one always-visible "Lists" button (`App::lists_menu_open`) opening `build_lists_menu`, a small dropdown listing all three with their live counts; picking a row just sets that list's own `_open` flag exactly as its old standalone button did — `build_bookmarks_popup`/`build_history_popup`/`build_downloads_popup` themselves are otherwise unchanged, same `Area`/`Frame` popup each already used.
  - **Two real bugs found while making the lists reachable when empty, neither reachable before this change.** (1) All three popups' own close logic auto-closed on `|| self.X.is_empty()` — harmless dead code while the triggering button only ever existed for a non-empty list, but would have closed a just-opened popup on the very next frame the instant the new always-visible button made opening an empty one possible at all. Removed the empty check from all three (`bookmarks_open`/`history_open`/`downloads_open` now only ever close on an explicit action or a real outside click), and each popup now shows a plain "No bookmarks/history/downloads yet." label when its list is empty instead of rendering blank. (2) The old "Downloads (N)" button counted `self.nodes.len()` — every graph node (tabs, notes, images, PDFs, JSON, groups included), not just downloads — which was wrong the same way before this change, just never obviously so, since happening to be non-empty was all that decided whether the button showed at all. `build_lists_menu`'s own count (and the downloads popup's own empty check) now correctly filter to `n.as_download().is_some()`.
  - Build-verified (`manifold` alone, then the full workspace excluding WebKit) with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session.
- **Download/Image/PDF/JSON/Note context menus deduplicated into one shared `build_node_context_menu`.** Same real duplication the Lists dropdown above found in the toolbar, one layer down: these five right-click menus were each a hand-copied ~90-line function -- identical `Area`/`Frame`/fade/`clicked_outside_popup` scaffolding, differing only in which buttons show ("Copy Text" for Note; "Open Containing Folder" + "Copy Path" for the other four) and what a click on one actually does (`reveal_image`/`reveal_pdf`/`reveal_json`/`reveal_download`, each kind's own `as_X().path` accessor, and per-kind delete cleanup: `gpu.free_image` for Image/PDF, clearing `json_viewer_node` for JSON, neither for Note/Download). The shared scaffolding — already `build_list_popup_frame`, reused here rather than re-duplicated a third time — is now drawn exactly once by `build_node_context_menu`, configured per call site by a small `NodeMenuConfig` (which items show, the delete button's exact wording, the popup width) and returning which single `NodeMenuAction` (if any) was clicked; each of the five `build_X_context_menu` methods shrank to that one call plus its own short `match` applying the kind-specific effect. Pure refactor, no behavior change — including one deliberately *preserved* quirk: the download menu still has no "Remove from Group" item regardless of whether the download actually has one, exactly as before this pass (not something this dedup introduced, and not fixed here either, since that would be a behavior change beyond what was asked).
  - Build-verified (`manifold` alone, then the full workspace excluding WebKit) with zero warnings; not yet live-tested this session.
- **Tab view's and Graph view's toolbars shared their trailing "Keyboard Shortcuts (F1)" / Settings-gear pair as two hand-copied, byte-identical blocks -- extracted into one `build_help_settings_cluster`.** Both toolbars need a way to reach shortcuts help and Settings regardless of which view is showing, but that's exactly the shape of duplication the Lists/context-menu dedups above already found elsewhere -- unlike those two, the two toolbars themselves are otherwise genuinely different (canvas ops vs. page navigation), so only this one shared trailing pair was extracted, not the whole toolbar. Returns the help button's own rect, which each caller still needs for its own `build_shortcuts_help` popup call further down -- that popup itself isn't duplicated, just the button that opens it. Pure refactor, no behavior change; build-verified with zero warnings.
- **Tab view's toolbar relaid out, and Engine/Mute/Lists/Screenshot/Print-to-PDF moved into Settings.** The toolbar had grown to a dozen-plus buttons crammed to the address bar's *left*, pushing the address bar itself almost to the row's far end -- backwards from Chrome/Firefox/every mainstream browser, which put nav controls, then a wide address bar taking most of the row, then a small handful of page-action icons to its right, then an app/overflow menu at the far end. Reordered to match: Graph / back / forward / reload / home / favicon-or-spinner, then the address bar (now given most of the row via a `trailing_reserve` estimate for what's left, the same hand-estimated-width tradeoff the old `gear_reserve` already used, just sized for everything now drawn after the address bar instead of only the gear), then Bookmark (mirrors the star at a real address bar's own right edge) / View-as-JSON (conditional) / Split + Close Split / Tab actions, then the trailing Shortcuts-help/Settings-gear pair.
  - Engine picker, the per-tab Mute toggle, the Lists dropdown (Bookmarks/History/Downloads), Screenshot, and Print to PDF all moved into a new "Current Tab" section (Engine/Mute/Screenshot/Print to PDF) and a new "Lists" section (Bookmarks/History/Downloads, replacing the toolbar's own three-into-one-dropdown fold from earlier in this pass -- no longer needed once these buttons left the width-constrained toolbar for a scrollable page with room for three plain buttons) on the Settings page. Both sections read/act on `self.focused` directly -- it isn't cleared by entering Settings, so "Current Tab" means exactly the tab that was focused right before Settings opened, same tab the old toolbar controls acted on. The real tradeoff, not hidden: engine-swapping (this project's own signature feature) and muting now cost a trip through a separate page-with-a-Back-button instead of an in-place toolbar toggle -- a deliberate decluttering choice, not an oversight.
  - The Bookmarks/History/Downloads popups (`build_bookmarks_popup`/`build_history_popup`/`build_downloads_popup`) are unchanged either way -- only what anchors and triggers them moved, from the old toolbar "Lists" button's rect to these three new Settings-page buttons' own rects, proving out the same fade/`Area`/`clicked_outside_popup` mechanism works identically from within a `ScrollArea` page as it did from a fixed toolbar `Panel`.
  - Verified live: screenshotted the reorganized Tab toolbar (address bar now wide and near the front, action icons to its right, no overlap or clipping at the default window size); clicked into Settings and confirmed the new "Current Tab" (Engine dropdown showing the real focused tab's engine, Mute, Screenshot -- Print to PDF correctly hidden, same `!self.headless` gate it always had) and "Lists" sections render; clicked "History (1)" and got the anchored popup showing the one real visited page; clicked "Bookmarks (0)" and confirmed the empty-list fix from earlier in this pass still holds from this new trigger location -- a real "No bookmarks yet." message, not an instantly-closing popup. Session state backed up before this test and restored byte-identical after.
- **`reveal_download`/`reveal_image`/`reveal_pdf`/`reveal_json` deduplicated.** Four near-identical 6-line functions (open the containing folder with the file pre-selected), differing only in how each kind's own `path` gets read. Extracted the shared `explorer.exe /select,<path>` spawn into one `App::reveal_in_explorer(path)`; each `reveal_*` is now just its own path lookup plus one call. Pure refactor, no behavior change; build-verified with zero warnings.
- **Graph-view Note/Image/Pdf/Json card loops deduplicated: the hover-revealed close button, and the drag/drop-into-group/hover bookkeeping, were each hand-copied once per kind.** Unlike the toolbar and context-menu dedups above, this one touches the canvas's own hit-testing code -- the exact area this project has already been bitten by once on registration order (see the Notes-render-as-Markdown entry's own "click never registered" bug further down this file). Handled carefully: the extraction changes *what calls what*, never the *order* anything is registered or drawn in, so the specific class of bug that bit Notes before can't have been reintroduced by this change -- every `ui.interact()`/paint call still happens in exactly the sequence it always did, just from inside a shared function instead of copy-pasted inline.
  - `draw_node_close_button` (free function -- doesn't need `&mut self`): the 18x18 hover-revealed "X" at a card's top-right corner, colors passed as parameters rather than hardcoded, since Note's card deliberately uses a different (warm, black-alpha) palette than Image/Pdf/Json's shared dark-chrome one (see the Notes entry's own doc comment for why). Returns whether it was clicked; each of the four call sites is now one call plus a one-line `if` setting its own `*_close_requested`.
  - `App::finish_draggable_node`: applies a card's drag delta, and on drag-release, drops it onto whichever group card it's now sitting on top of, plus the `self.hovered`/`self.dragging` bookkeeping every card needs -- 100% identical logic across all four kinds (confirmed by direct comparison before extracting, not assumed), so this one needed no parameters at all beyond `id`/`response`/`is_hovered`. Tab/Download/Group each keep their own separate drop logic (a tab can also drop onto another tab to form a new group; a group can't drop onto a group), so this only ever covers the four kinds whose drop behavior is genuinely identical.
  - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live verification was attempted but not completed cleanly this pass: testing against the real running session's own populated graph (several tabs plus an existing note) worked for creating a test note and screenshotting it, but hovering it to check the close button's own reveal didn't visibly trigger in the headless capture (most likely a timing/redraw nuance between the synthetic `MOUSEMOVE` and the next `SCREENSHOT`, not something diagnosed further this pass) -- rather than iterate further against real user data, this was verified by direct before/after code comparison instead (every extracted statement checked against its four original copies, confirming identical order and behavior), the same "build-verified, not independently live-tested" posture this README already uses elsewhere when live testing isn't practical in the time available.
  - **A real mistake happened during this attempt, corrected, disclosed here rather than left unmentioned:** the test session-backup step silently failed once (a `cp` to `/tmp` that produced no file, not caught until after the fact), and the subsequent test note creations/view-mode toggles were saved into the user's actual `session.json` before that was noticed. Recovered by hand-editing the file back to its known-good state (removing the stray test notes, restoring `view_mode_tab`/`next_note_id`), verified field-by-field against the values a working backup captured immediately after -- no real data (the user's actual tabs, or their own pre-existing note) was lost, but this should not have been possible to happen in the first place; the fix going forward is to verify a session backup actually exists on disk before running any test that can mutate it, not just assume a `cp` with `2>/dev/null` succeeded.
- **"+ Image"/"+ PDF"/"+ JSON" folded into one Graph toolbar "+ File" button.** All three were only ever a file picker (differing purely in extension filter) followed by a call to the matching `insert_*_node` -- the same "dispatch by type, one allowlist" shape `redirect_fetch::classify` already established for auto-redirected tabs, applied here to a picked file's own extension instead of a fetched `Content-Type` (there's no HTTP response header to read a real MIME type from for a file already on disk). New `App::add_file_node` shows one picker filtered to every supported extension at once, then dispatches on the chosen file's extension to `insert_image_node`/`insert_pdf_node`/`insert_json_node` -- all three unchanged, this only replaced the three thin picker-wrapper functions (`add_image_node`/`add_pdf_node`/`add_json_node`) that used to call them. `ADDIMAGE`/`ADDPDF`/`ADDJSON <path>`'s headless equivalents are untouched -- they already bypassed any picker, calling their own `insert_*_node` directly, so they don't go through this dispatch at all.
  - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified what's actually reachable in headless mode: confirmed the Graph toolbar renders correctly with the button count reduced (the new "+ File" button is hidden under the same `!self.headless` gate the three it replaced already had, same as it should be, so this is visually a no-op in headless mode -- the dispatch logic itself isn't headless-reachable at all, same limitation the three functions it replaced already had), and confirmed `ADDJSON` still creates a real JSON node correctly (checked `session.json` directly rather than a screenshot, to rule out a view-toggle timing issue) -- `add_file_node`'s own dispatch logic wasn't independently click-tested (would need a real file-picker interaction), consistent with this project's existing posture for every other file-dialog-gated feature. Session backed up (and verified to actually exist on disk this time, see the fix noted just above) and restored byte-identical after.
- **The Export Session/Bookmarks/Group "Save As" dialogs deduplicated; their own Import counterparts deliberately left alone.** All three exports shared the exact same five-line tail (a native save dialog defaulting to one filename, then on success a `Saved <path>` toast) -- extracted into one `save_dialog_with_toast(default_name, filter_label)`, a plain free function rather than an `&mut self` method: a method taking `&mut self` couldn't accept a closure that also wants to mutate `self` (e.g. `self.build_session_snapshot().save_to(path)` immediately followed by `self.save_toast = ...`) without a borrow conflict, so it just returns the picked path plus the pre-built toast text and lets each caller do its own two-line write-and-toast. Each of the three export call sites shrank from ~8 lines to ~3-4. The three *imports*, by contrast, are only superficially similar (all `pick_file` + a `load_from`) but genuinely differ in what happens next: Session's stages into `pending_import` behind a confirm/cancel dialog (replacing a whole session is a bigger, more destructive operation), while Bookmarks and Group apply immediately with no confirmation -- unifying that would mean either taking away Session's confirm step or adding one to the other two, a real behavior/feature decision, not a pure refactor, so left as three separate `if` blocks. Pure refactor on the export side, no behavior change; build-verified with zero warnings.
- **Groups nest.** The single biggest structural limitation this README kept repeating ("no nesting in this first cut... `GraphNode::group` is a single `Option<String>`, not a path") turned out not to need a path type at all -- a group's own `GraphNode::group` can now itself be `Some(parent_group_id)`, and walking that field upward from any node already reconstructs the whole ancestor chain (`App::group_breadcrumb`), the same way `subtree_ids`/`dissolve_group` already walked one link of it. `App::current_group` still names whichever level is showing (`None` at the true top); leaving a group now goes to *its own parent* (possibly another group), not straight to the top, so backing out of a deeply-nested group takes as many steps as it took to get in -- or one breadcrumb click to jump straight to any ancestor.
  - **One real design fork, resolved by asking rather than guessing:** dragging one group's card onto another already meant something -- merge (dissolve the source, flatten its members into the target). Nesting could plausibly have repurposed that same gesture to mean "nest instead." Asked the user directly rather than picking; the answer was to leave that gesture alone entirely (still merge, unchanged) and create nesting only through "+ New Group"/Import Group/"Group Selected" while already viewing a group's own subgraph -- zero risk to an already-shipped gesture.
  - `groups_at_current_level` gained the same `n.group == self.current_group` filter every other kind's own `*_at_current_level` already had (a group's own membership used to be structurally impossible to be anything but top-level, so no filter was needed before) -- every caller that used to wrap it in its own `self.current_group.is_none()` "top level only" gate (the group-card render/hit-test loop, the minimap dots, `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect`, `current_level_settle_key`, the right-click hit-test, and the "New Group"/"Import Group"/"Ungroup All"/"Collapse Groups"/"Group Selected" toolbar buttons) had that gate removed -- the filtered list itself now does the level-scoping, so the wrapper was redundant once fixed and wrong once nesting existed.
  - `dissolve_group` reparents members to the dissolved group's *own parent* now, not unconditionally to the top -- read before removing the group, so a top-level group's parent (`None`) still means "become top-level" exactly as before. The hover-revealed "eject" button and dragging a member onto the "◀" back button both got the same fix (`remove_from_group_requested`'s handler): one level up, not straight to the top.
  - New `App::group_breadcrumb` walks the ancestor chain and drives the toolbar's own trail -- root-most first, current group last (rename-able, same `TextEdit` as before), each ancestor a clickable button that jumps straight there by reusing `group_entered` (the exact "switch `current_group`, ease the camera" effect double-clicking a group card already triggers -- it doesn't care which direction that is, so no new mechanism was needed for "jump to an ancestor" versus "drill into a child").
  - **A real, stated gap, not hidden:** dragging an *existing* tab onto a group's card to join it is deliberately still top-level-only. Inside a group's own subgraph, dropping a tab onto a sibling tab already means something else (re-parent it there) -- resolving that overlap with "join this group instead" wasn't part of this pass. A nested group still gains members today via "+ New Tab"/"Group Selected" while viewing its own subgraph, or (for Note/Image/Pdf/Json specifically, which have no competing sibling-drop gesture of their own) by dragging an existing one onto a nested group's card directly -- `App::finish_draggable_node` (already shared across those four kinds from the dedup above) had its own top-level-only gate removed, since nothing conflicts for them the way it does for tabs. Closed since (survey item 7): one drop table (`drop_action`) resolves the overlap by target, a group card before a tab card, so a tab now joins a nested group's card too.
  - Session-only, same as groups always were (`GroupPayload`'s own doc comment) -- nesting depth included, so this needed no `session.json` schema change at all.
  - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified creating a group at the top level, double-clicking to enter it (confirmed via screenshot: the toolbar correctly showed "◀ Group 0" plus "+ New Group" now available *inside* that empty subgraph, something structurally impossible before this change), and confirmed groups don't survive a process restart (matching the session-only design) by checking a fresh launch's own graph. Didn't independently reproduce a second nesting level or the multi-crumb breadcrumb live -- headless coordinate-targeting for a second double-click proved fiddly in the time available (a stray click landed on the address bar instead of the intended button once, harmlessly) -- confirmed by code review instead, since the second level uses the exact same generic mechanism (`current_group`/`groups_at_current_level`/`group_breadcrumb`) already verified working for the first. Session backed up and restored byte-identical throughout (verified to actually exist on disk first, per the fix noted earlier in this file).
