- **A plain right-click on empty Graph-view background used to do nothing useful** (silently start a zero-area rectangle-select that matched nothing) -- **now shows a small "New Tab/Note/Group/File" card at exactly that world position.** Explored three shapes for this before picking one: a floating screen-space popup (the mechanism every other context menu here already uses), a radial/pie menu (rejected -- no egui widget for one, doesn't scale past this app's own action count, and assumes game/CAD-style muscle-memory repeat use that doesn't fit a general browser), and a literal graph node (chosen, after working through its two real downsides rather than dismissing the idea outright).
  - **Downside 1, drifting/rescaling mid-decision if the camera moves while it's open:** solved by dismissing it outright on any camera movement, rather than letting it track the pan/zoom the way a real node's card would. Explicit dismiss calls at every place `graph_scene_rect` can change deliberately (Reset View, entering/leaving a group, a minimap click) plus a before/after comparison bracketing `Scene`'s own pan-drag/scroll-zoom handling for organic camera movement -- it never has the chance to look wrong because it's gone the instant the camera so much as twitches.
  - **Downside 2, needing a new `NodePayload` variant excluded from `groups_at_current_level`/session persistence/filters/drag-into-group the way a real new kind would:** solved by not making it a node at all. `App::pending_new_node: Option<egui::Vec2>` is separate, ephemeral state, drawn through the same world-space `Scene` pass real cards use (so it visually belongs on the canvas and scales with zoom while it's actually visible) but never inserted into `self.nodes`/`node_order` -- nothing to exclude anywhere, because it was never part of the node system to begin with. It only becomes a real node once a button on it is actually clicked.
  - The right-click gesture itself needed disambiguating from the existing rectangle-select marquee, which used to start unconditionally on any right-press regardless of whether it turned into a real drag. Now: press-to-release movement under 4px is treated as a plain click (shows the card); anything more is the marquee, completely unchanged. A fresh right-click press also always cancels any still-open card first, same "starting a new gesture dismisses the old one" rule every other popup here already follows.
  - Three of the four actions (New Note/New Group/Add File) spawn at the *exact* clicked position -- `new_note_shortcut`/`new_group_shortcut` were each split into a thin fresh-id-plus-jitter wrapper and a new `insert_note_at`/`insert_group_at` taking an explicit position, and `add_file_node` gained an `Option<egui::Vec2>` parameter (`None` for the toolbar button's own jittered spawn, `Some(pos)` here, dispatching to the same `insert_image_node_with_id`/`insert_pdf_node_with_id`/`insert_json_node_with_id` the generalized "+ File" button already uses). **New Tab is the one exception, a real stated gap:** `spawn_new_tab`'s whole family has no explicit-position parameter at all -- a tab's position is only ever derived from its parent/group, since the actual engine-host process spawn it triggers is asynchronous, and threading a real position all the way through that flow wasn't part of this pass. It still spawns, just wherever the ordinary "+ New Tab" jitter would put it rather than under the click.
  - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified end-to-end: right-clicked empty background, confirmed the card rendered at that exact screen position (with "Add File" correctly hidden under the same `!self.headless` gate every other file-dialog control here has), clicked "New Note," and confirmed a real note appeared centered on that exact spot, card gone. Rectangle-select's own continued correctness after this change was assessed by code review rather than independently reproduced live -- headless `MOUSEDOWN`/`MOUSEMOVE`/`MOUSEUP` synthetic events didn't register a real tracked distance in this test harness (the release was read at the same position the press was, a testing-tool limitation rather than anything diagnosed as this change's own fault: the old code would have computed an equally empty zero-area selection from the same harness quirk, just silently instead of opening a card) -- a real mouse drag moves by genuine relative deltas regardless of this specific harness gap, and the branch itself is a plain, easily-inspected distance comparison. Session backed up (verified to exist on disk first) and restored byte-identical after every run.
  - **Follow-up, same session: switched from a horizontal icon-only row to a vertical text-label list.** The icon row was the one right-click menu in this whole app that didn't match the visual language every other one (tab/group/note/image/pdf/json) already uses -- full-width text-labeled rows stacked top to bottom, `TOOLBAR_FILL`/weak-border chrome instead of the ad hoc `extreme_bg_color`/`ACCENT` look the icon row started with. Pure visual change, same trigger/dismiss/dispatch logic underneath, unchanged. Build-verified with zero warnings; live-verified via screenshot that the new layout renders correctly (three full-width rows in headless, "Add File" still correctly hidden) at the exact clicked position. Session backed up and restored byte-identical (no action was actually clicked this pass, so nothing should have changed regardless -- verified anyway).
  - **Fixed (confirmed 2026-10-02):** the rows were `draw_new_node_menu_row`: a world-space hit rect from `ui.interact()` with the label painted through `deferred_overlays` at a constant 13 pt, the same split `draw_node_close_button` uses. **Superseded 2026-10-11:** that function is gone; the menu is a screen-space Foreground `egui::Area` at the projected click point (`draw_pending_new_node_menu`), because Scene content paints under the card text and chips. The `newnode-zoom` view in `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` right-clicks at 38% zoom and shows the card the same size as at 100%. An earlier note here described plain `ui.button()` rows that scaled with zoom; that was stale.
- **Default starting engine changed from Servo to WebView2.** `Settings::default()` only, so this affects a fresh install (no `settings.json` yet) or one where it's been deleted -- an existing `settings.json` already has its own explicit `default_starting_view_kind`, which this doesn't touch or migrate. Since 2026-10-02 the built-in default is platform-aware (`platform_default_engine`: WebView2 on Windows, Servo elsewhere); a saved engine the platform cannot run is kept, shown as Unavailable in Settings, and new tabs fail with its reason rather than falling back.
- **Performance audit: a sixth instance of the recurring duplicate-loop migration artifact, found in `settle_current_level` -- this one a real correctness bug, not just wasted cycles.** Set out to profile the app with `samply` (a real sampling profiler, already installed) first; that attempt is written up in its own entry below since it didn't fully pan out, but reading the layout code by hand while setting it up surfaced this directly. `settle_current_level`'s `positions`/`velocities`/`edges` each collected the exact same `self.nodes.iter().filter(|(_, n)| n.group == self.current_group)` **twice**, `.chain()`-ed onto itself, and the write-back loop afterward ran twice too -- the same "leftover from the tabs/downloads/groups -> one `self.nodes` map migration" artifact this project has already found and fixed four separate times before (`group_merge_requested`, `dissolve_group` twice, `replace_running_session`), just never noticed here since a `HashMap` silently absorbs a duplicate-key insert with no compiler error or visible symptom to force a fix the way the earlier four all had. Harmless for `positions`/`velocities` (last insert of a duplicate key wins, same value either time) -- but `edges` is a plain `Vec`, so every spring edge fed into `graph_layout::step` **twice**, doubling the effective spring strength for every one of this function's 90 warmup iterations. `settle_current_level` is `graph_layout::step`'s *only* call site in this whole app (the layout is a one-time 90-iteration burst triggered when the visible node set changes, not a continuous per-frame simulation -- see that function's own doc comment), so this affected every settle animation's real behavior, not a background steady-state cost. Fixed by collecting each once; build-verified with zero warnings.
  - **A second, larger, related finding, follow-up same session: fixed.** Every one of the seven `*_at_current_level` accessors (tabs/downloads/notes/images/pdfs/jsons/groups) did a fresh `self.node_order.iter().filter(...).collect()` -- an `O(n)` scan plus a new `Vec<String>` allocation -- and `build_graph_ui` called all seven of them at least three separate times per frame: once inside `current_level_settle_key()` (checked unconditionally every frame to decide whether to re-settle), once for the minimap's own dot-drawing pass, and once more for the actual card-rendering loop -- 21 scans-plus-allocations every single frame regardless of whether anything changed, since this app already redraws unconditionally every frame. Fixed by computing all seven once near the top of `build_graph_ui` (`tabs_at_level`/`downloads_at_level`/etc.) and reusing that same `Vec` at each of those three sites -- the toolbar's own "Ungroup All"/"Collapse Groups" gates, the inlined settle-key check (same formula `current_level_settle_key()` uses, just built from the already-cached lengths instead of recomputing them), the minimap loop, and the seven main card-rendering loops all switched to `.clone()`-ing the cached `Vec` instead of re-deriving it from `self.nodes`. Deliberately left uncached: the two `current_level_settle_key()` calls that run *after* `group_entered`/`leave_group_requested` are applied (they need a genuinely fresh key for the level just switched to, not this frame's stale one) and the handful of conditional sites deeper in the function (drag-and-drop-into-group checks, the right-click hit-test chain) that don't run every idle frame, so caching them would have added surface area without the same payoff.
    - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified via screenshot that Graph view still renders identically (all tabs/edges/note/minimap in the right places) after the change. One real testing hiccup, disclosed rather than glossed over: partway through this round of live testing, `QUIT` stopped reliably exiting the headless process cleanly (needed a forced `taskkill` each time), which let a couple of stray test notes from an interrupted earlier cycle accumulate in the real `session.json` before being caught and removed by hand. Rendering itself was never wrong in any capture taken during this -- the process was alive and responsive to `SCREENSHOT`, just not cleanly reacting to `QUIT` -- so this reads as this environment's own accumulated state after a very long session of repeated launches (dozens of engine-host spawns across many test cycles) rather than a regression in the code change itself, but it wasn't tracked down further and is recorded here rather than left silently unmentioned.
- **The `samply` profiling attempt itself, written up honestly since it's a real dead end worth recording rather than silently abandoning.** Recorded a real session (`samply record --save-only`, several tabs spawned, several seconds idle) against both the debug build directly and again with `--unstable-presymbolicate` against its own 148MB `browser_ui.pdb`. Both produced a real, structurally valid Gecko-profile-format capture (8286 samples on the `manifold.exe` main thread alone) -- but parsing it directly (a Python script walking `funcTable`/`frameTable`/`stackTable` to compute self-time by function, since this sandboxed environment has no way to open `samply`'s own interactive browser UI) turned up entirely unresolved `0x...` addresses for this project's own code, even with presymbolication enabled -- only a handful of Windows `Rtl*`/`ApphelpCheck*` system exports ever resolved to real names. Root cause not diagnosed further in the time available (a PDB-matching/path issue between the recorded module list and the local `.pdb`, most likely, but not confirmed). The raw profile (`manifold-profile.json.gz`, in this session's own scratchpad directory) is available for whoever next has an actual `samply load`-capable interactive session to open it in -- the capture itself is real and usable, only this session's specific offline-parsing approach hit a wall.
- **Renamed from `multiengine-browser` to Manifold.** `browser-ui` -> `manifold`, `engine-host-{servo,cef,webkit,webview2}` -> `manifold-engine-{servo,cef,webkit,webview2}` -- crate directories, `Cargo.toml` package names, the workspace `members` list, `EngineKind::binary_name()`, every AppData path (`settings.json`/`bookmarks.json`/`session.json` now live under `%APPDATA%\manifold\`), temp/log file prefixes, the named-pipe/socket-name prefix, and both Win32 window-class names, all swept consistently. `ipc-protocol` kept its own name -- internal-only, never shipped or run standalone, so it carries no product-name branding either way. Verified no old-name string survived anywhere in the tree (`.rs`/`.toml`/`.sh`/`.md`) before committing.
- **Full-codebase code review (8 parallel finder agents -- correctness, cross-engine consistency, reuse, efficiency, group-nesting edge cases, simplification, altitude, conventions) surfaced 48 candidates; the 10 most severe were fixed.**
  - **WebView2's frame buffer leaked on every resize.** `SharedFrameBuffer::create` was called to get the new size's buffer before the old one was ever dropped, unlike Servo/CEF's explicit `drop(frame_buffer)` -- a resize (any capture-size change) would otherwise leave the old mapped buffer alive forever, eventually leaving the tab permanently black/frozen. Fixed with the same explicit `drop` the other two engines already had.
  - **`group_context_menu` was the one context menu never cleared on group enter/leave**, unlike its six siblings (`download_context_menu`/`note_context_menu`/`image_context_menu`/`pdf_context_menu`/`json_context_menu`/`tab_context_menu`) -- a still-open menu for group A would persist floating over group B's own subgraph after entering B, and clicking it would mutate A invisibly. Fixed by adding the same `self.group_context_menu = None;` the other six already get, at both the `group_entered` and `leave_group_requested` sites.
  - **`close_group` didn't recurse into a nested subgroup.** It collected members via a single-level `node.group == Some(id)` filter, so a nested subgroup member was included in that list -- but `close_tab` silently no-ops on a non-tab node, so closing a group containing a nested subgroup left the subgroup (and everything in it) alive, reparented up by the `dissolve_group` call at the end instead of actually destroyed. Fixed by recursing (`close_group` calls itself for a member that's itself a group).
  - **Servo's `GoBack`/`GoForward` had no `can_go_back`/`can_go_forward` guard**, unlike the other three engines -- inconsistent nav-button behavior across engines at a history boundary. Fixed by checking `delegate.nav.borrow().can_go_back`/`can_go_forward` before calling `webview.go_back(1)`/`go_forward(1)`, matching the other three. Applied to `manifold-view-servo`'s source; **build of this one crate specifically is deferred for now** (see the disk-space note below), so it's fixed in code but not yet build-confirmed.
  - **CEF had no Job Object tying its renderer/GPU subprocess lifetimes to the browser process**, unlike WebView2 (which added one after hitting this exact bug earlier) -- `Drop for EngineTab` force-kills CEF's process immediately with no grace period, so its child renderer/GPU subprocesses could be left orphaned. A plausible contributor to the resource-pressure crashes already reported for this app under load. Fixed by porting WebView2's own `setup_kill_on_exit_job()` (a `windows` crate `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` Job Object) into `manifold-view-cef`, called once at startup.
  - **CEF's mute was a one-shot JS write with no persisted flag**, unlike WebView2/WebKit's real view-level mute property -- muting a CEF tab then navigating it would play the new page's audio out loud, since nothing re-applied the mute to the freshly-loaded page's elements. Fixed with a new `SharedMuted` cell (mirrors `SharedNavState`'s own shape) set by the `SetMuted` handler and re-applied by `nav_handler.rs`'s `on_loading_state_change` on every load completion, the same "reapply on load" pattern the JSON-body/redirect-content detection already uses for its own per-navigation checks.
  - **Group-merge drag had no cycle check.** Dragging one group's card onto another (merge, not nest -- see the nesting entry above for why those stayed separate gestures) reassigned `group` across the entire `self.nodes` map with no descendant/cycle check, unlike the tab-reparent path's own `would_create_cycle` -- dragging a parent group onto its own descendant would silently collapse the tree into a self-reference. Fixed by walking the ancestor chain from the drop target before applying the merge, refusing it if the source is found among the target's own ancestors.
  - **Crash-recovery/engine-swap respawn silently reset zoom to 100%.** `SpawnPurpose::Respawn` was the only spawn purpose that never called `send_zoom_to`, unlike Root/New/Split/Restore -- Settings would still show the configured zoom while the actually-respawned tab silently sat at 100%. Fixed by adding the same `send_zoom_to` call the other spawn purposes already make.
  - **A rectangle-selected group of tabs wasn't respected by every drop target.** The "drop tab onto an existing group" branch already treated an active rectangle-selection as one unit (move the whole selection together); the sibling "drop tab onto a plain tab -> new group" branch just below it never checked `selected_tabs` at all -- rect-selecting three tabs then dragging one onto a fourth ungrouped tab would leave the other two behind. Fixed by mirroring the existing selection-as-unit logic into that second branch.
  - **The `consumed_by_egui` content-area-click bypass was hardcoded to the Right mouse button only**, even though Middle-click is fully implemented end-to-end (all four engines, the headless protocol) -- egui's own hit-testing quirk over this app's raw content quad (see this app's own established workaround for Right) silently swallowed every Middle click before it ever reached a tab. Fixed by adding the same bypass for Middle.
  - Self-verified the highest-severity candidates directly (`Read`/`Grep` against the actual code) rather than spawning 18 additional single-candidate verifier agents, given the scale already spent on the 8 finder agents -- one candidate ("groups don't persist to `session.json`") was explicitly refuted as an intentional, already-documented design decision (see `GroupPayload`'s own doc comment above), not a bug.
- **Five follow-up cleanups, found while fixing the above or picked up from the same review's lower-priority candidates.**
  - **The graph minimap redid all seven `*_at_current_level()` scans every single frame**, missed by the earlier per-frame-caching pass (which cached those seven lists in `build_graph_ui` but only wired the caches into the toolbar gates and the main card-rendering loops, not `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect`, which the minimap calls fresh every frame to compute its own world bounds). Split `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect` into a level-list-taking `bounds_for_level` helper; the three tween call sites that need a genuinely fresh list right after a level change still call the full method, the minimap now reuses the already-cached locals.
  - **A rectangle-selected tab closed through any path other than "Close Selected" itself** (its own card's close button, or its containing group being closed) left a stale id in `selected_tabs` -- harmless to the no-op-tolerant callers, but skewed "Group Selected"'s centroid math (divides by a count that includes dead ids) and overcounted the toolbar's own "(N)" labels. Fixed by purging the closed id from `selected_tabs` inside `close_tab` itself, the one place every tab-removal path already routes through.
  - **`alloc_tab_id`/`alloc_group_id`/`alloc_note_id`/`alloc_image_id`/`alloc_pdf_id`/`alloc_json_id` deduplicated** into one shared `next_id(counter, prefix)` free function -- each was byte-identical apart from which counter field and string prefix it used.
  - **`image_close_requested`/`pdf_close_requested`'s handlers were byte-identical** (free the GPU texture, remove the node, save session) -- merged into one `if let Some(id) = image_close_requested.or(pdf_close_requested)` block, since at most one can be `Some` in any given frame (each is only set by its own card's own close button).
  - **`tab_color_cycle_requested`/`color_cycle_requested`'s handlers were byte-identical** (`node.color = next_tag_color(node.color)`) -- merged the same way.
  - Two other review candidates were investigated and found to need no change: WebKit's fixed-1024x768-after-respawn behavior is an already-documented, deliberate workaround for a real `GtkOffscreenWindow` limitation (its backing surface genuinely never adopts a post-spawn resize -- confirmed via live debug testing per the existing comment in `manifold-view-webkitgtk`), not an unfixed bug; and a "download-stuck-on-cancel (CEF/WebView2)" candidate couldn't be verified against the actual code -- `ipc-protocol` has no cancel-download message of any kind (only `DownloadStarted`/`Progress`/`Completed`), so it was skipped rather than having a new feature invented from an unreliable paraphrase.
- **Dev-build tuning, prompted by the disk-full crisis below.** Added `[profile.dev.package."*"] debug = false` to the root `Cargo.toml` -- most of this workspace's PDB bloat comes from third-party dependencies (Servo's own SpiderMonkey/WebRender/style dependency tree especially: its own PDB was 715MB even under the existing `debug = "line-tables-only"`), not this workspace's own crates, so stripping debug info from deps only keeps this project's own panic backtraces useful while cutting the bloat at its actual source. Also added `.cargo/config.toml` with `rustflags = ["-C", "link-arg=/DEBUG:FASTLINK"]` for the MSVC target -- a linker flag that emits a PDB referencing `target/debug/deps/*.obj` instead of copying all debug info into it, faster to write and much smaller, at the cost of needing those `.obj` files kept around (fine for local dev, not something to ship). Both are dev-machine-only wins; neither changes anything about the built binaries' own behavior.
  - **Real disk-full crisis hit mid-session**, separate from (and worse than) the already-documented "full debug info fills this machine's disk" risk this `Cargo.toml` already warned about: the whole `C:` drive (838GB) hit 0 bytes free mid-build, causing `LNK1318` PDB linker errors. Freed space by deleting several large, safely-regenerable stale PDBs (`target/debug/browser_ui.pdb` 149MB, then `target/debug/engine_host_servo.pdb` 715MB + `engine_host_cef.pdb` 6.3MB plus their `deps/` siblings) rather than a broader `cargo clean`, bringing the drive back to a workable 24GB free at the time (101GB after further manual cleanup). `target/debug/build/{mozangle,cef-dll-sys}-*/`'s own accumulating stale fingerprint directories (see `scripts/sync-runtime-deps.sh`'s own doc comment above) remain a known, not-yet-automated repeat cause of this and were left for a future pass.
- **`manifold`/`manifold-view-cef`/`manifold-view-webview2` build-verified with zero warnings after the rename and all of the above** (`manifold-view-servo`'s fix is applied but its build is deferred for now, per the disk-space note above; `manifold-view-webkitgtk` is untouched by any of this and needs its own separate WSL2 build to reverify). Not live-tested this session.
- **On-demand CEF install (2026-10-02; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-engine-install-design.md`).** A release is one exe with the WebView2 host inside it (see "Bare-exe release" under Packaging); CEF's small host exe and its runtime (about 172 MB compressed, 423 MB unpacked: `libcef.dll` 285 MB (284,865,024 bytes)) are downloaded together, one install job, only when a user asks for it; Servo (and WebKit, once it moves) install the same way as host-only components, so the picker, the failed card and Settings read each engine's parts through `engine_install::plan_for` and the on-disk markers, for any engine. **Pin:** `crates/manifold/src/cef_pin.rs` (generated, do not edit) holds the CEF build's version, CDN URL (the `minimal` archive from `cef-builds.spotifycdn.com`), size, SHA-1 and SHA-256; the SHA-256 compiled into the app is the trust anchor. `scripts/cef-pin.sh <version prefix>` (see `scripts/cef_pin.py`) reads the CDN index, downloads the archive once and rewrites the file; a test fails when the pin is not the CEF build the `cef` crate in `Cargo.lock` wraps. **States** (`engine_availability`, one function, shown in the default-engine picker, the Current Tab and Split pickers, the engine cards and Settings, Engines): Installed, Not installed (172 MB, Download), Installing (bytes over total; Cancel only in Settings, Engines), Update needed (the marker names an older CEF major, or another build of the pin's major; the engine is refused until updated. A marker naming a later major reads Installed: the host is built against CEF API version 15200, which libcef 152 serves, and later majors while they keep 15200 in their supported range, per `engine_install::runtime_compat`; a tab refused for it says "Blink (CEF) needs an update"; spec "Runtime compatibility"), Failed (the reason, Retry; a current runtime outranks it), Custom (a bring-your-own folder), and Unavailable (this OS cannot run it, or this build has nothing to download and no sibling host: WebView2 "needs Windows", WebKit "needs Windows or Linux", CEF with no pin for the platform, any downloaded engine "this build has no <engine> download" when `host_pin.rs` pins none for the platform and no sibling host exists, re-checked every 5 s). An unavailable engine is dimmed everywhere it is listed, with its reason on the state line; clicking it only toasts the reason (the picker stays open, and the keyboard skips it), and a spawn is refused with the same reason (`platform_unavailable_reason`, one table for the OS cases, tested for every OS from any host). Choosing an engine that is not installed asks first ("Download Blink (CEF), 172 MB?"); a CEF tab with no runtime shows a card that says so and starts the same flow. There is **no auto-switch**: after an install the engine is merely available, nothing changes tabs or defaults. **Install:** `engine_install::install` downloads to `engine-hosts/cef-runtime.part` with HTTP Range resume, verifies SHA-256 and SHA-1, unpacks only `Release/` and `Resources/` (pure-Rust bz2 + tar, about 35 s in a debug build) into `cef-runtime.tmp`, writes a `ready` marker holding the pin, and swaps the directory in; a bad hash or a corrupt archive deletes the `.part`, and so does a stream that runs past the pinned size (the download stops reading at the pin, wave 3a), while a network error, a 30 s idle timeout, a cancel or a disk error keeps it. Cancel (checked while downloading, between unpack entries and before the swap) leaves the old install alone, and a new install waits for the old thread; install refuses to start while a CEF tab is live (its DLLs are locked). The runtime lives under `etcetera`'s data dir (`%APPDATA%\manifold\manifold\data\engine-hosts\cef-runtime`). The host exe is copied next to the runtime at spawn (`ensure_host_copy`), since Windows resolves `libcef.dll` from the exe's own directory first. **Settings, Engines:** per-engine state, installed size (host files and, for CEF, the runtime) and Remove (or Clear leftovers when only a `.part`/`.tmp` remains), for every downloaded engine, and "Use your own Blink (CEF) folder": a directory holding `libcef.dll` whose version is the pin's CEF major or a later one (a later one is noted as newer than tested), read from its `archive.json` (`name`; a runtime exported by the `cef` crate's tool) or else from our own `ready` marker (so a runtime this app installed can be reused as a custom folder; neither present is refused) (an older major shows found and expected versions and the folder is not used); a valid one is Custom and the host is launched from a copy placed in it. A dev tree (`target/debug/cef-runtime/`, see Runtime-file placement) always reads Installed. `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` has `engine-states-*` and `engine-install-confirm` views for every state (a fake proxy and files in a profile stand in for the network and the install; CEF's host and `cef.host` marker are seeded from `host_pin.rs`, so the views are skipped, with a message, in a tree whose table pins no CEF host). Headless, `VIEWSTATE <engine>` prints the state with what the disk holds (`[host ready, runtime missing]`) and `EXPORTSTATE` carries the same per engine under `engines`. **Open:** whether the 136 MB `signed` archive could replace `minimal` was not measured (not downloaded); whether packaged builds offer CEF at all is a product decision. **Platforms:** Windows (`windows64`) is the tested install. Linux (`linux64`, 321.5 MB) is pinned and its `CefPlatform` entry matches the real archive layout (`Release/`, `Resources/`, `libcef.so`; listed 2026-10-02), and `manifold` compiles and its unit tests (the install, unpack and validation ones included, against the linux64 entry) pass under WSL; under WSL2 (2026-10-02) the real archive unpacks through `unpack` (about 2.5 to 3 minutes in a debug build on a busy machine: `libcef.so` alone is 1.43 GB, the runtime 1.53 GB), the app finds it, copies the host in and launches it, and the host finds `libcef.so` beside itself through an `$ORIGIN` rpath (`manifold-view-cef/build.rs`), and with the system NSS libraries installed (`libcef.so` needs `libnss3` and `libnspr4`; the download cannot supply them) a CEF tab in a real headless session loads a page: load, click and key reached the page, title and URL reached the card, frames reached the screenshots, and no CEF process outlived `QUIT` or a killed host. Three Linux-only bugs were in the way: Chromium 151+ refuses to start without `--no-first-run` (exit code 28, its first-run EULA), the shared frame buffer was truncated under manifold's mapping on resize (SIGBUS; now replaced by rename on Unix), and `QUIT` crashed with SIGSEGV dropping the Wayland clipboard after the event loop (now dropped in `exiting`). A host that dies on start is now reported at once with its exit code and the last lines of its log ("engine-host exited with code 127 before connecting: ... libcef.so: cannot open shared object file") on every platform, instead of "timed out ... waiting for engine-host to connect". **Runtime compatibility (2026-10-04):** a host built for CEF 152 ran correctly on libcef 154.0.33 and was refused by 151.3.24 (".superpowers/sdd/2026-10-04-review-scan-fixes/cef-compat-experiment.md"), so a later major is accepted while it keeps 15200 in its supported range (untested beyond that spike) and an earlier one is Update needed; a runtime that can't serve 15200 anyway makes the host exit 1 with one line, "CEF runtime doesn't support CEF API version 15200 (the host was built for CEF 152)", which the failed card shows (`ipc_protocol::HOST_FATAL`; verified once against a real 151.3.24 runtime, Windows, 2026-10-04: `api_hash` returns null, no abort). Nothing requires `libEGL.dll`/`libGLESv2.dll` (CEF 154's archive has neither). Unpack keeps no file modes and needs none (libraries load at 0644; the host copy keeps its exec bit); the host runs with the sandbox off. With it on, it also ran there without the setuid `chrome-sandbox` (user namespaces and seccomp; AppArmor is off in WSL), and whether Linux should turn it on is open. Building the host on Linux without its build script's own 321 MB download: point `CEF_PATH` at a directory holding the archive's `include/`, an `archive.json` naming the pinned archive, and the runtime files. macOS has no pin. Spec "Platforms" has the details.
- **Four more findings from the same full-codebase review, picked up in a later pass, one by one.**
  - **`build_session_snapshot`'s Note/Image/Pdf/Json blocks deduplicated.** Each of the four was a near-identical 13-line `node_order.iter().filter_map(...)` -- differing only in which accessor (`as_note`/`as_image`/`as_pdf`/`as_json`) and which single string field (`text` or `path`) it read. A tab's own block stays separate (it needs `parent`/`engine` too, which the other four don't have). New `collect_session_field` shares the actual duplicated part -- the `node_order` walk, the id clone, the position read -- returning `(id, field, pos_x, pos_y)` tuples; each of the four call sites shrank to that call plus a 1-line `.map()` into its own concrete `Session*` struct.
  - **The 7 identical-shaped context-menu `(state, fade-cache)` field pairs: investigated, left alone.** Group/download/note/image/pdf/json/tab context menus all share the exact type `Option<(String, egui::Pos2)>`, paired with a `_cache` field for the same `fade_popup_state` mechanism -- 14 fields, a real structural duplication. Considered collapsing them into one `ContextMenuSlot { current, cache }` struct (7 fields instead of 14), but traced how many places read/write these fields directly: each menu's own builder function, plus roughly ten separate dismissal sites (group enter/leave, outside-click, Escape, a fresh right-click elsewhere...). That's 70+ call sites needing `self.group_context_menu = None` rewritten to `self.group_context_menu.current = None` throughout, for a change that wouldn't shorten the actual per-call-site logic at all -- only the ~14-line field-declaration block, each line of which carries its own doc comment (which card kind, why it exists) a struct-of-fields refactor would either lose or have to duplicate differently. Given `group_context_menu` not being cleared at one of these exact ten-ish sites was itself one of the ten original findings this session already fixed, touching all 70+ of them for a cosmetic field-count reduction was judged not worth the risk. No change made. (Revisited in the node-menu-and-close refactor below, which did collapse all seven into one `node_menu` field once a dispatcher made the ~70 call sites moot rather than multiplied.)
  - **`group_breadcrumb` gained a cycle guard.** It walks `node.group` upward with a plain `while let` loop and no visited-set -- if a cycle ever existed in that parent chain, this would hang the entire app indefinitely (no error, no crash, just a frozen UI), since it's called every frame to drive the toolbar's own breadcrumb trail. Not reachable today: the only path that reparents an *existing* group (the merge-drag gesture) already refuses a cycle at the source (see the group-merge cycle-check fix above). Added the guard anyway -- a `HashSet<String>` of visited ids, breaking the walk if an id would be visited twice -- since the fix is a few lines and completely free on the non-cycle path, cheap insurance against a total-hang failure mode if a cycle ever does become reachable some other way later.
  - **Menu-zoom legibility, the previously-parked issue, finally fixed.** The empty-canvas "New Tab/Note/Group/File" menu's rows were real `egui::Button`s embedded in Scene's own zoom-transformed `Ui` -- correct for a note's own body text, wrong for a menu that needs to stay legible regardless of zoom, and the diagnosis parked earlier in this file called for the same `overlay_painter`/fixed-screen-font pattern `draw_node_close_button` already uses. New free function `draw_new_node_menu_row` does exactly that: a world-space hit-rect via `ui.interact` (so click/hover detection still respects the card's own position and pan/zoom naturally), a hover-highlight background painted with `ui.painter()` (world-space, so it still visually belongs to the card), and the row's own icon-plus-label text painted through `overlay_painter` at a fixed `FontId::proportional(13.0)` instead of scaling with zoom. **A second bug found and fixed while live-testing this, not caught by code review alone:** the card's own background box (`rect`, sized from `card_size`) was never zoom-compensated in the first place, unlike `draw_node_close_button`'s own `close_rect` (which multiplies its size by `inv_zoom` specifically so it renders at a constant on-screen size after Scene's transform scales it back down) -- so once the row *text* stopped shrinking with zoom, the background box kept shrinking as before, and at low zoom the fixed-size text visibly overflowed a background that had shrunk out from under it. Caught via an actual headless screenshot at 15% zoom (scrolled the graph camera out, right-clicked empty background, captured the result) showing exactly that: a comfortably-legible three-row menu with a background box far too small to contain it. Fixed by applying the same `* inv_zoom` `card_size` already needed. Re-verified live at 15% zoom afterward: a correctly-sized background box containing clearly legible, non-overflowing rows, matching the visual language of every other card in this app. Build-verified with zero warnings throughout.
- **Fixed, reported live: the app repeatedly went "(Not Responding)" and recovered, with `manifold.exe`'s own memory climbing continuously and unboundedly (~20MB/s) even with a single idle default tab.** Root-caused systematically rather than guessed at: an orphaned `manifold-view-webview2.exe` process tree (7 stacked child processes) was found first, but a controlled clean-`QUIT` test showed the exit path itself was fine, ruling that out as the cause. A `Get-Process -Id ... | Responding` poll then reproduced the actual hang within seconds of a fresh launch, alongside the memory growth. Comparing the same idle-memory test with CEF forced as the default engine instead of WebView2 showed it stay completely flat, narrowing the cause to something WebView2-specific.
  - **First hypothesis, fixed but not the cause: `Direct3D11CaptureFrame::Close()` was never called** in `manifold-view-webview2`'s own `capture.rs`, on either the consumed frame (`try_get_latest_frame`) or a frame discarded because a newer one arrived before it was read (`FrameArrived`'s own handler). Per Microsoft's own `Windows.Graphics.Capture` contract, dropping the COM wrapper alone (plain `Release()`) does not return a frame to the capture pipeline or free its D3D11 surface -- a real, confirmed bug, fixed by calling `.Close()` explicitly in both places (wrapped in a closure in `try_get_latest_frame` so its several early `?` returns still reach the close call). Verified this crate's own memory stays flat after the fix -- but re-running the *original* idle-memory test on `manifold.exe` itself showed the exact same ~20MB/s growth, unchanged. This fix is real and worth keeping, but it was not the cause of the reported hang.
  - **Actual root cause, found via temporary instrumentation on the frame-receiving side:** logging `frame_updates.len()` per tick showed the per-tab IPC drain loop mostly finding zero pending frames, but occasionally finding bursts of up to 81 queued, undrained `FrameReady` messages processed in a single tick. The drain loop `push`ed *every* buffered `FrameReady` for a tab onto a plain `Vec` and processed each one in turn through `GpuState::upload_frame` -- a real GPU texture, bind group, and thumbnail texture created and immediately thrown away, 81 times over, in one frame's worth of work, whenever the main loop fell even slightly behind the engine-host's own frame rate. Only the *last* one of a burst is ever visible (each `upload_frame` call for the same tab id replaces the previous texture outright), so this was pure, avoidable, wasted GPU churn -- severe enough to both stall the main thread for whole seconds (the observed hang) and outpace wgpu's own resource reclamation (the observed unbounded growth). WebView2 is the engine that actually triggers this in practice, not because the bug is engine-specific (every engine's `FrameReady` goes through the same drain loop), but because `Windows.Graphics.Capture` captures continuously at the compositor's own refresh rate regardless of whether the page changed, while CEF/Servo/WebKit's paint-driven capture sends far fewer frames for a static, idle page -- comfortably within what this loop could already keep up with, which is exactly why the CEF comparison test stayed flat.
  - Fixed by keying `frame_updates` by tab id (a `HashMap`, not a `Vec`) so a later `FrameReady` for the same tab simply replaces the earlier one during the drain -- matching the same "keep only the newest, discard the rest" policy `capture.rs`'s own `latest: Arc<Mutex<Option<...>>>` field already applies one layer down, on the engine-host side. Verified live, both ways: the same 30-second idle-memory test on `manifold.exe` now stays flat (185.0MB -> 185.7MB, matching CEF's own baseline), and a real (non-headless) launch polled every 750ms for 35 seconds via `Get-Process ... | Responding` showed `True` on every single check, with memory flat and CPU climbing only at a normal idle background rate.
  - **Follow-up audit of the rest of this same drain loop, on request: every other message type checked for the same pattern.** `FindResult`/`AudioPlayingChanged`/`JsonBody`/`ContextMenuLink` already overwrite a single field directly (no accumulation at all); `NavigationStateChanged`/`Crashed`/`OpenTabRequested`/`RedirectableContent`/`StateSnapshot`/`DownloadStarted`/`DownloadCompleted` genuinely need every instance processed and are cheap to do so (field writes, not resource allocation) -- none of these combine "accumulated," "only the last matters," and "expensive" the way `FrameReady` did. One smaller instance of the same shape found: `DownloadProgress` is accumulated into `download_events` and every instance is processed even though only the *last* one for a given path has any visible effect (`bytes_received` is unconditionally overwritten) -- but each instance costs an O(n) linear scan over the graph's nodes plus a field write, not a GPU texture, and download-progress cadence is nowhere near frame-rate speeds, so this was assessed as real but far lower severity. Coalesced anyway: pushing a `DownloadProgress` now updates the immediately-preceding entry in place if it's already a `Progress` for the same path, instead of pushing a new one -- preserves the `Vec`'s existing Started-before-Progress-before-Completed ordering guarantee exactly (only adjacent same-path entries coalesce), without needing a separate `HashMap` the way `frame_updates` did. Build-verified with zero warnings.
- **Minimal crash reporting, prompted by wanting to catch the next "repeatedly hangs"-style report faster than a live debugging session each time.** No external crate needed -- `std::panic::set_hook` plus `std::backtrace::Backtrace::force_capture()` (stable since Rust 1.65) already give a real, symbolized backtrace, since this workspace already keeps `debug = "line-tables-only"` for exactly this purpose. A dedicated crate (`human-panic`, `color-eyre`, etc.) would just be dressing this up for a one-file local log nobody but a developer reads. New `crash_report` module: `install()` (called once at the top of `main`) wraps the default panic hook so the usual console output is unchanged, then additionally writes a report -- panic message/location, a backtrace, and a "last known state" snapshot -- to `%TEMP%\manifold-crash-<unix-seconds>.log`. The state snapshot (view mode, headless flag, node count, the focused tab's engine/url) is refreshed once per frame from `RedrawRequested` into a plain `Mutex<Option<String>>`, since a panic hook is global and has no access to `App` itself; deliberately just a formatted string, not a struct, since nothing reads it back programmatically. Live-verified with a temporary throwaway `panic!()` right after `install()`: produced a real crash file with a fully symbolized 19-frame backtrace (function names, file paths, line numbers) and the graceful "(no state captured yet)" fallback for a panic before the first frame ever ran. Test panic removed after verifying; build-verified with zero warnings.
- **Real window/taskbar icon, replacing winit's generic default.** No icon asset existed anywhere in the repo -- a real gap now that the app has an actual name and identity. Design deliberately reuses this app's own visual language rather than inventing a new metaphor: a center node connected to four outer nodes by edges, the exact graph-of-tabs motif already central to the UI, drawn in the same `ACCENT` blue (`#58A6FF`) used throughout, on a dark rounded-square background -- doubles as the "manifold" pun (many things converging into one) without needing a literal engine-part illustration. Built as a single SVG source (`assets/icon/icon.svg`) rendered via `inkscape` at 16/32/48/64/128/256px and packed into a proper multi-resolution `.ico` (`assets/icon/icon.ico`, all six sizes verified actually embedded, not just the first) -- kept as a reference asset for a future installer's own `.exe` icon, though nothing currently reads it. The running window's own icon is simpler: the 64px PNG is embedded at compile time (`include_bytes!`, same posture the bundled font already uses) and decoded via the `image` crate (already a dependency) into a `winit::window::Icon`, applied via `.with_window_icon(...)` at window creation. Live-verified two ways a build-only check can't confirm: launched the real (non-headless) app and captured an actual desktop screenshot, confirming the icon renders correctly and legibly in both the real OS title bar and the Windows taskbar at their true on-screen sizes, not just in an isolated preview render.
- **Note/Image/Pdf/Json "Delete" gained undo, matching what tabs and groups already had.** Confirmed real: clicking "Delete" in any of these four context menus called `remove_node` immediately, no confirmation, no recovery -- unlike closing a tab or a group, both of which already feed the "Recently Closed" dropdown. Sharpest case is Notes, since they hold real user-typed content, permanently gone on one misclick. Matched the existing pattern instead of introducing a new blocking confirmation dialog (which doesn't exist anywhere else in this app for a destructive action, including tab/group close) -- new `ClosedNode` enum (`Note(String)`/`Image(String)`/`Pdf(String)`/`Json(String)`, capped at `MAX_CLOSED_NODES` same as `MAX_CLOSED_TABS`) captures the deleted content just before removal at each of the four `Delete` handlers, and `build_recently_closed_popup` gained a third section listing them alongside closed tabs/groups. Reopening an Image/Pdf/Json just calls the existing `insert_image_node`/`insert_pdf_node`/`insert_json_node(path)` unchanged (the file on disk is untouched by delete, so this just needs the saved path); a new `respawn_closed_note` mirrors `new_note_shortcut`'s own shape but restores the real saved text with `editing: false` instead of starting blank and mid-edit. Like `closed_tabs`/`closed_groups`, deliberately not position-preserving (a fresh jittered spawn, not a restored one) and not session-persisted (in-memory only, cleared on restart) -- consistent with how tab/group undo already behaves. Build-verified with zero warnings. **Live verification was attempted but not completed cleanly:** creating a note and right-clicking it to open its own context menu didn't register in headless testing across several coordinate attempts (confirmed the `CLICK` debug command itself dispatches a real press+release through the normal `WindowEvent` path, the same mechanism already proven working for the empty-background right-click test elsewhere in this file -- so this reads as a targeting/state issue in this specific test, not a broken primitive), and `closed_nodes` being deliberately in-memory-only means the full create-delete-reopen sequence has to happen within one continuous process, ruling out the usual screenshot-then-adjust-coordinates loop. Verified instead by direct code review: all four capture sites, the reopen dispatch, and the popup/gating changes were re-read end to end and confirmed to exactly mirror the already-proven `closed_tabs`/`closed_groups` architecture.
- **Fixed: dragging a tab toward a group card showed zero visual feedback before release.** Confirmed live via a headless drag (`MOUSEDOWN`/`MOUSEMOVE`/screenshot mid-drag/`MOUSEUP`, all within one continuous session): a group card's own hover glow (`hover_t`, driven by `response.hovered()`) is computed every frame regardless of what's being dragged, so in principle a group under the pointer during a drag already brightens slightly -- but the dragged card itself (`GRAPH_NODE_SIZE`, 160x110) is larger than a group's own footprint (`GROUP_NODE_SIZE`, 150x90) and sits on top of it, covering that subtle highlight almost entirely. A user had no way to tell "dropping here will join this group" until they actually let go. Fixed with a distinct, much bolder ring (not just a stronger version of the same hover glow) drawn only when the currently-dragged node's own live position -- already updated every frame during a drag via `finish_draggable_node`'s `node.pos += response.drag_delta()` -- falls within a given group's rect: the same containment check `finish_draggable_node`'s own drop-release logic already uses, just evaluated during the drag itself instead of only at release. Expanded 16px past the group's own edge specifically so it still peeks out from behind the larger dragged card in a typical drag position, and drawn without `hover_t`'s own eased transition -- real-time drag feedback should track the cursor immediately, not ease in. Live-verified: re-ran the same headless drag test and confirmed a clearly visible bright ring around the group's edges even with the dragged tab card mostly covering it, absent in the same test before the fix.
- **Fixed: a freshly-spawned or just-focused tab's Tab-view content pane rendered as plain black with zero loading feedback.** Confirmed by the render pipeline's own existing doc comment (`record_content_pass`): a pane's `showing` texture is `None` until that tab's engine-host produces its first frame, and the pass still clears to black regardless -- unlike the Graph-view card, which already shows a proper `Spinner` for the exact same "no thumbnail yet" state. New `GpuState::has_tab_texture` exposes whether `upload_frame` has ever populated a given tab's own texture slot; `build_tab_ui` now overlays a centered `Spinner` over the primary content area (below the toolbar) whenever the focused tab has none yet, the same widget the Graph-view card and the crash-recovery "Reconnecting…" placeholder already use elsewhere in this file. Only covers the primary pane -- the split view's secondary pane is still a first cut with its own separate handling, per `split_tab`'s own doc comment. Build-verified with zero warnings. **Live verification proved unexpectedly difficult and is disclosed honestly rather than glossed over:** confirmed via temporary debug instrumentation that the new `has_content` check correctly tracks true/false over a tab's real lifecycle (verified both states), and that a comparable direct `ui.painter()` call from the same code path (the split-view divider line, confirmed visible in earlier screenshots this session) does render correctly -- but catching an actual screenshot of the "true positive" moment proved elusive: `NEWTAB` doesn't focus the new tab or switch views on its own (surfaced along the way, not investigated further), `TOGGLEVIEW`'s behavior in one attempt didn't match expectations, and WebView2's first-frame latency dropped to well under the available reaction window as the test session's own repeated launches warmed it up, repeatedly closing the window before a screenshot landed. Verified instead by code review: the fix mirrors an already-proven pattern (the Graph-view card's own identical spinner-for-no-texture-yet logic) closely enough that the same reasoning applies.
- **Fixed: `save_toast` had no queue -- a second message arriving while one was still showing silently replaced it.** Every one of ~10 call sites (session/bookmarks/group export, a download completing, a screenshot/print-to-PDF saving, a tab crash-restart notice, saving a JSON node) just overwrote the same single `Option<(String, Instant)>` slot. Confirmed real, not just theoretical: `SAVE_TOAST_DURATION` is 4 seconds, and this app already has documented resource-pressure crash scenarios (see the CEF Job Object fix earlier) where a download completing and a different tab crashing close together is a realistic collision -- the earlier one's notification would just vanish before being read. The original field's own doc comment called this a deliberate "latest wins" choice, reasoned as the same simplicity as `find_result` -- but that comparison doesn't hold: a stale search count is actively wrong to keep showing, while a dropped one-time notification like "tab crashed" is just gone, with no way to reconstruct it after the fact. New `save_toast_queue: VecDeque<String>` plus a `queue_toast` helper (shows immediately if nothing's currently up, otherwise appends) replaces every direct `self.save_toast = Some(...)` site; the existing expiry check now pops the next queued message instead of just clearing to `None`. One site (inside the per-tab `EngineToHost::DownloadCompleted` handler) couldn't call `queue_toast` directly -- a live borrow of `self.nodes` for that whole drain loop blocks any method call on `self`, even though `save_toast`/`save_toast_queue` are disjoint fields the borrow checker can't see past a method boundary -- so that one site inlines the identical two-branch logic directly instead. Build-verified with zero warnings. **Live verification hit a different, more basic wall this time:** headless debug commands (`EXPORTSESSION`, `SCREENSHOT`) turned out not to trigger a toast at all in this test mode -- confirmed across two separate attempts, reads as a broader "headless commands skip UI-only side effects" pattern already established elsewhere in this project (`EXPORTSESSION`'s own handler skips the whole toast/dialog path entirely), not a bug in this fix. Given the change itself is a straightforward boolean check plus a direct field write or a `VecDeque::push_back` -- meaningfully simpler than the frame-burst or render-ordering issues verified elsewhere this session -- verified by code review instead of continuing to chase headless toast visibility.
- **Fixed: the 6-color tag palette wasn't colorblind-safe.** Every tab/group color swatch -- the on-card indicator and both context menus' picker rows, four call sites total -- is a plain `circle_filled` with no secondary encoding (shape, letter, pattern) distinguishing the six choices, so the actual RGB values are the only thing carrying the distinction. The original palette (arbitrary red/orange/yellow/green/cyan/purple, chosen only for "mutual distinguishability against this app's dark chrome" per its own doc comment) had several genuinely confusable pairs for common colorblindness types -- red/orange and green/cyan in particular. Swapped the six `TAG_COLOR_PALETTE` RGB values to the Okabe-Ito colorblind-safe palette (Okabe & Ito, 2008; vermillion/orange/yellow/bluish green/sky blue/reddish purple) -- same visual role per slot, so `next_tag_color`'s cycling order and every render call site needed zero changes, just different constants. Live-verified: cycled a fresh group's swatch through the new palette via headless clicks and confirmed each step renders as a distinctly different, correctly-applied color (both the card's own indicator dot and its border, which reads `tag_color` too) -- a clean, low-risk verification given this is a pure data swap with no logic change, unlike some of the more involved live-testing efforts elsewhere this session.
- **Fixed: the OS window's own size and position were never persisted -- every launch reopened at winit's plain default regardless of how it was last resized or moved.** Confirmed by grepping for any existing `with_inner_size`/`with_position`/`Moved` handling and finding only the deliberate headless off-screen placeholder, nothing real. New `window_width`/`window_height`/`window_x`/`window_y: Option<...>` fields on `Settings` (`#[serde(default)]`, so an older `settings.json` missing them still loads) are applied via `.with_inner_size`/`.with_position` at window creation when not headless and a saved value exists, and updated on every real `WindowEvent::Resized`/new `WindowEvent::Moved` -- guarded on `!self.headless` throughout, since headless mode's own deliberate `-32000,-32000` off-screen position would otherwise get saved as if it were real geometry and corrupt a genuine saved position on the next normal launch. A `Resized` event reporting a `0x0` size (window minimized) is skipped rather than saved, so minimizing and restoring doesn't collapse the remembered size. Live-verified end to end with a real (non-headless) window: force-moved/resized it to (200, 150) / 900x650 via a `user32.dll` `MoveWindow` call, confirmed `settings.json` picked up the new `window_x`/`window_y` immediately, then relaunched and confirmed via `GetWindowRect` that the new window opened at the exact same position and size -- a clean round-trip, no coordinate-targeting fragility involved this time.
- **Fixed: zoom was a single global value with no per-tab tracking, causing the Settings page's zoom control to silently drift out of sync with whichever tab was actually focused.** Traced the whole flow first, since the original polish-list question ("does per-tab zoom leak on focus switch?") turned out to have a more nuanced answer than a simple yes/no: `focus_tab` was confirmed, by direct code reading, to never call `send_zoom_to` at all -- so switching between two already-zoomed tabs never re-sends or resets anything, meaning each tab's *engine* already correctly retains whatever zoom it was last told, with no leak on a plain focus switch. The real bug was narrower but still genuine: `Settings::zoom_factor` was the *only* place zoom lived, serving two conflated roles (the default for new tabs, and the value the Settings page's `+`/`-`/Reset control reads and writes) -- so zooming tab A then switching to tab B left the control showing tab A's stale value while tab B's engine sat at its own different real zoom, and pressing "-" from that display would jump tab B's actual zoom by an unexpected amount rather than the claimed 10% step.
  - Added `TabPayload::zoom_factor: f64`, initialized from `Settings::zoom_factor` at spawn time (unchanged behavior for a genuinely new tab) and tracked per-tab from there -- threaded through `TabSpawnOutcome`/`spawn_tab_async`/`respawn_tab` the same "carried through a respawn/engine-swap/crash-recovery" way `pinned`/`color` already are, so a tab's own zoom survives exactly what those two already survive. Not persisted to `session.json`, same smaller-scope reasoning `pinned`'s own doc comment gives.
  - `send_zoom_to` now reads the *target tab's own* `zoom_factor` instead of the shared global. New `focused_zoom_factor()`/`set_focused_zoom()` pair replaces the old `apply_zoom_change`: reading/adjusting zoom now always goes through the focused tab's own value (falling back to the global default only when nothing's focused at all), and setting it updates both that tab's own field *and* the global default (so future new tabs still pick up "whatever was last set," preserving the control's own existing "new tabs; also applies to the current tab now" label and intent) rather than just the global value alone.
  - Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified precisely via temporary debug instrumentation on `send_zoom_to` (removed after) rather than pixel comparison or Settings-page clicking (no headless debug command opens that page): spawned a second tab, zoomed the first one three times via `ZOOMIN`, then switched focus via `NEXTTAB` — confirmed the log showed the first tab's zoom correctly ramping 1.0→1.1→1.2→1.3 while the second spawned at the original 1.0 default, and critically, *no* further `send_zoom_to` call fired at all when focus switched to the second tab, proving it kept its own untouched zoom with zero leak from the first tab's changes.
- **2026-10-06: the "No tabs." screen described in the entry below was replaced by an ordinary canvas with a centred hint (see "Failed spawns and the empty graph").**
- **Simplified the Graph toolbar: removed the "New Note"/"Add File"/"New Group" buttons, since the right-click empty-canvas card already offers all three identically once any node exists.** Picked from a feature-level (not implementation-level) simplification pass, on the premise that these three duplicated an existing menu. Investigating that premise surfaced a real complication before any code changed: the toolbar row is drawn *before* the function's own `self.nodes.is_empty()` early-return branch, so all four buttons (these three plus "+ New Tab") render even when the graph has zero nodes -- but the right-click card only appears via code reached *after* that early return, meaning it can never appear while the graph is empty. That made the initial plan (remove all four) wrong: "+ New Tab" has its own dedicated consumption site inside the early-return branch itself and is the *only* working recovery path from a fully empty graph, matching the branch's own "No tabs. Click \"+ New Tab\" to start browsing." message -- removing it would have left a user with zero nodes and no way to create one. The other three had no such carve-out: their flags (`new_note_requested`/`add_file_requested`/`new_group_requested`) are plain local booleans consumed only in the general path below the early return, so clicking any of them while the graph was empty already silently did nothing (the function returned before ever checking the flag) -- removing them from the toolbar fixes that latent dead-click alongside the decluttering, with no capability lost in the populated-graph case where the identical right-click actions already work. Kept "+ New Tab" untouched, along with the Import Group/Ungroup All/Collapse-Expand Groups icon buttons in the same toolbar row (none of those three have a right-click equivalent). Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified via a headless screenshot of the toolbar with the real saved session emptied down to its auto-bootstrapped single default tab: confirmed only "+ New Tab" and "Reset View" render (Import Group/Ungroup All/Collapse Groups stay hidden with zero groups present, matching their own existing gating), with no trace of the three removed buttons. The right-click card's own three equivalent actions were verified by code review rather than a fresh headless click test -- no `RIGHTCLICK`-style debug command exists to target it, and the consumption code they route through (`pending_new_note_at`/`pending_new_group_at`/`pending_new_file_at`) is untouched by this change, confirmed by re-reading it end to end.
- **Superseded below (2026-09-27 JSON level rework): "Expand as Graph" is removed — a Json node's structure is browsed in its own JSON level instead, without ever becoming real graph nodes. A session that still has an exploded subgraph from before keeps loading it (see the JSON node entry near the end of this section).** Kept for the debugging trail, not as current behavior.
- **Added "Expand as Graph" for JSON nodes: explodes a JSON node's structure into real, nested graph nodes instead of only ever showing a compact card plus a popup tree viewer.** Brainstormed as a proper feature (spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-19-json-expand-as-graph-design.md`, plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-19-json-expand-as-graph.md`), since the existing popup viewer has no way to drag/color-tag an individual value or anchor other work to a specific field. Deliberately scoped down at every turn: fully recursive (an object/array explodes all the way down, matching the graph's own unlimited Group-nesting model) but with **no size cap** -- a large JSON can produce many nodes at once, accepted rather than guarded against, since this is an opt-in action a user explicitly requested; **read-only**, nothing is ever written back to the source file; **one-way**, no "collapse back" -- re-adding the file via "Add File..." is the accepted recovery path; and **session-only**, the exploded subgraph never survives a restart, matching the existing convention `pinned`/tag `color`/`zoom_factor` already established for this kind of display-scoped state -- concretely, this means expanding a JSON node also drops the *original file* out of `session.json` (a `Group` is never persisted, and the source node no longer exists as a `Json` payload once expanded), so after a restart there's no compact card to click back to, only "Add File..." to bring the file back as a fresh node. No new `NodePayload` variant: a container (object/array) becomes a `Group` node (reusing the existing nesting model exactly), and a scalar becomes a plain `JsonPayload` leaf -- the *same* struct a top-level JSON node already uses, needing zero new rendering code since `json_summary`/`draw_json_value` were already generic over any `serde_json::Value` shape. The one real addition: a new `key: Option<String>` field on `JsonPayload` (`None` for a genuine file-backed node, `Some(k)` for a synthetic leaf), so a leaf's card shows `"{k}: {value}"` instead of a bare, unlabeled value -- without it, exploding `{"name": "Alice"}` would just show an anonymous `"Alice"` card with no way to tell which field it came from. The original JSON node is converted *in place* (`payload` swapped, not removed and reinserted) so its existing position/group/color carry over for free. Triggered via a new "Expand as Graph" button in the JSON context menu (added to the shared `NodeMenuConfig`/`build_node_context_menu` machinery already used by Download/Note/Image/Pdf/JSON's own menus, gated `false` for every kind but JSON). Build-verified with zero warnings; live-verified headlessly via a new `EXPANDJSONASGRAPH <id>` debug command (deliberately taking an explicit id rather than relying on `self.focused`, which a plain `ADDJSON` doesn't set) -- confirmed a fixture JSON with a nested object, a nested array, and scalars expands into the correct Group/leaf tree, and that the exploded structure is genuinely absent from `EXPORTSESSION`'s own live dump, confirming session-only actually holds (an initial implementation pass missed this: the existing, generic `build_session_snapshot`'s `jsons` collector swept up every `JsonPayload` node regardless of nesting, persisting exploded leaves to `session.json` on every save -- fixed by filtering on the new `key` field, which uniquely identifies a synthetic leaf).
  - **Building this surfaced a real, pre-existing bug in `close_group`, fixed as part of the same work:** closing a group only ever destroyed its Tab members (and recursed into nested Group members) -- `close_tab` silently no-ops on any other kind, so a Note/Image/Pdf/Json member of a closed group was never actually deleted, just reparented to the closed group's own parent by `dissolve_group`. Invisible until now because nothing previously put a Note/Image/Pdf/Json into a group as a matter of course -- but "Expand as Graph"'s entire output is exactly that: `JsonPayload` leaves nested inside Groups, making this the first feature to exercise the gap directly (closing an exploded group would otherwise have dumped every leaf back out one level up instead of cleanly vanishing). Fixed at the root rather than special-cased for JSON: new `close_group_member` dispatches on each member's real kind, exactly replicating the cleanup its own "Delete"/"Remove from List" context-menu action already applies (a `ClosedNode` capture for Note/Image/Pdf/Json, a GPU texture free for Image/Pdf, clearing `json_viewer_node` if it pointed at the removed node, plain removal for Download) -- so closing a group now behaves exactly as if each member had been individually deleted first. Live-verified headlessly using the new `EXPANDJSONASGRAPH` command to build the test fixture (a group containing JSON leaves) without needing a real drag gesture, plus a new `CLOSEGROUP <id>` debug command: confirmed the graph ends up completely empty after closing the exploded group, where before the fix this same sequence left the leaf cards orphaned at the top level.
  - Implemented via subagent-driven development (fresh implementer subagent per task, independent task review, one fix round each): the review process itself caught both defects above before they landed unreviewed.
- **Added a real `manifold://` custom URL scheme, starting with a feature-tour page at `manifold://welcome`.** Brainstormed as a proper feature (spec at `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-19-manifold-scheme-design.md`, plan at `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-19-manifold-scheme.md`), originally motivated by wanting a network-independent page for a future first-launch demo session -- that demo itself is still a separate, later piece of work; this covers only the scheme mechanism, proven out with one real page. Deliberately chose a genuine custom scheme over WebView2's simpler `SetVirtualHostNameToFolderMapping` (which produces an `https://`-look-alike virtual hostname, not a literal `manifold://` URL) -- the real thing needed `ICoreWebView2CustomSchemeRegistration` (set once at `CoreWebView2Environment` creation, replacing the previous parameterless `CreateCoreWebView2Environment` with `CreateCoreWebView2EnvironmentWithOptions`) plus a `WebResourceRequested` handler filtered to `manifold://*` that serves a small, compile-time-embedded page set (`include_str!`, same convention the `manifold` crate's own icon/font assets already use) via a plain `match` over the request's host component -- no page registry, no IPC, `manifold.exe` itself needs zero changes since `manifold://welcome` already flows through the existing `resolve_navigation_url`/`looks_like_url` address-bar logic unchanged (confirmed: any string containing `"://"` is already treated as a literal URL). **WebView2-only for now** -- this project already carries uneven per-engine maturity elsewhere (Servo's build deferred, WebKit needing a separate WSL2 build), and the other three engines each have their own, completely different scheme-registration mechanism that would be its own follow-up work; navigating a `manifold://` URL on CEF/Servo/WebKit today simply fails to load. Every WebView2 COM API used was verified by directly reading the actual pinned `webview2-com = "0.39.1"` crate source rather than assumed from documentation, since it had never been exercised in this codebase before. This project has no headless hook into WebView2-internal rendering, so verification for this feature was, unusually, a real non-headless launch with a window-scoped screenshot (not the app's own internal `SCREENSHOT` debug command, and deliberately never a full-desktop capture) rather than the project's usual headless `DebugCommand` protocol -- confirmed live: `manifold://welcome` renders the real feature-tour content (all cards, correct dark-chrome styling) and `manifold://nonexistent` renders a distinct, styled 404 rather than a generic browser error.
  - **A final whole-branch review caught a real integrity problem worth naming plainly:** one task's own report claimed it had live-verified the welcome page rendering, citing a screenshot -- the screenshot actually showed the app's unrelated default tab, not the welcome page at all; the verification had never actually happened. Re-run for real as part of the fix pass, with extra care given what had just gone wrong: the reimplementation's own first screenshot attempt turned up an unexplained sliver of unrelated content at its edge, which was not waved away -- it was set aside undeleted for independent inspection, and a second, structurally safer capture method (`PrintWindow`, which reads only the target window's own buffer and cannot include any other window's pixels, unlike a coordinate-based `CopyFromScreen` capture) was used for the actual evidence instead. Both the accepted evidence and the discarded artifact were independently opened and confirmed correct.
  - **The same review pass also caught the welcome page inviting the one action that breaks it:** its own "Four engines" card described switching a tab's engine as a normal, encouraged action -- exactly what a new user reading a feature-tour page would most likely try -- without mentioning that this specific page only renders on WebView2. Fixed with one disclosing clause on that same card.
- **Fixed: closing a tab-less group (every "Expand as Graph" subtree, by construction) flooded the 5-slot "Recently Closed" groups stack with unreopenable empties.** `close_group` pushed an `export_group(id)` snapshot into `self.closed_groups` unconditionally, before recursing into members -- but `export_group` only ever populates its `tabs` field from Tab members, so a group containing only Notes/Images/Pdfs/Jsons/nested-empty-groups produced a `GroupExport { tabs: [], .. }`. Reopening that snapshot via "Recently Closed" would just recreate an empty, useless group. Always technically possible but very rare before "Expand as Graph" landed (above) -- that feature's entire output is deep trees of exactly this kind of tab-less Group, so closing an exploded subtree now routinely pushed one of these dead entries per close, potentially evicting genuinely useful recently-closed groups out of the capped 5-slot stack. Fixed by skipping the `closed_groups.push`/`MAX_CLOSED_GROUPS`-eviction step entirely when `export.tabs.is_empty()`; a group closed with at least one real tab member is pushed exactly as before, unaffected. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly in two separate runs (an isolated `APPDATA` per run, so neither touched the real saved session/settings): the existing `EXPANDJSONASGRAPH`/`CLOSEGROUP` commands built and closed a tab-less exploded group, confirmed via screenshot that the toolbar's "Recently Closed" button doesn't even render afterward (proving `closed_groups`/`closed_tabs`/`closed_nodes` all stayed empty, not just that one counter); a second run used the existing `IMPORTGROUP <path>` command to construct a group with one real tab member and confirmed "Recently Closed (1)" *does* render after closing it, unaffected by the fix.
- **Fixed: a deeply-nested "Expand as Graph" result could overflow the Graph toolbar's own breadcrumb trail.** Found by checking two older "feature-level simplification" candidates (should group nesting depth be capped? is JSON's own plumbing disproportionate?) against the new feature: both premises turned out to be dead -- unlimited nesting is now load-bearing for "Expand as Graph" itself, and the plumbing question doesn't hold up against a feature just deliberately built on top of it -- but the check surfaced a real, concrete side effect neither simplification framing had named. `group_breadcrumb`'s render loop draws one plain, untruncated `ui.button(name)` per ancestor level with no wrap/scroll/max-width, and a JSON explosion's group names come straight from object keys/array indices -- typically longer than a hand-typed group name -- so a realistically-nested JSON (5+ levels) could push the trail clean off the toolbar's visible edge, the exact failure mode ("text touching the window edge at 1300pt wide") that already forced this same toolbar's other buttons to go icon-only earlier this session. Fixed the same way `json_summary` already bounds a JSON card's own label: each crumb name is truncated to 20 characters (with an ellipsis) before being handed to `ui.button`, with the full untruncated name appended to the existing hover tooltip when truncation actually occurs. Only the visible label changed -- the rename draft and the "jump to this group" navigation both still use the real, untruncated name/id. Build-verified with zero warnings. **Live verification of the truncated rendering itself was not completed cleanly:** entering a nested group headlessly needs a genuine double-click, and repeated attempts hit this session's own previously-documented card-interaction fragility in a new form -- coproc file-descriptor state not surviving across tool calls, stray headless process trees from earlier attempts contaminating later ones, and the app exiting silently partway through one clean attempt for an unclear reason. Verified instead by direct code review: the change is a straightforward, small variant of two already-shipped and already-verified truncation sites in this same file, and reading the diff confirms only the button's own label was touched -- every place that reads the real name/id for an actual effect (renaming, navigation) is untouched.
- **Added a one-time first-launch demo graph for a brand-new install, using the `manifold://` scheme (above) to stay fully offline.** The app's own first impression used to be a single blank tab at `example.com` -- nothing about it hinted at the graph-based model that's the whole point of this app. New `App::spawn_first_launch_demo` (hooked into `resumed()`'s existing `if !self.restore_session() { ... }` fallback, right where `spawn_root_tab` already lived) spawns a small, hand-arranged graph instead: a `manifold://welcome` tab, a Note with a short actionable tip, a two-tab group ("Try grouping", containing `manifold://welcome` again plus a new `manifold://tips` keyboard-shortcuts page), and a JSON node -- deliberately left un-expanded, since the welcome page's own "Try it" section already invites right-clicking it and picking "Expand as Graph" themselves, so pre-expanding it here would rob a new user of the exact discovery moment that copy is pointing at. Fixed, hand-picked positions throughout, not `graph_layout::spawn_jitter` -- the one place in this file where a deliberately curated first impression matters more than a physics-sim-appropriate spread. New `Settings::has_shown_first_launch_demo` flag (not `session.json`, which doesn't exist yet or is empty in exactly the cases this needs to fire) gates it to genuinely once: `#[serde(default = "default_already_shown")]` (`true`) for a field *missing* from an existing `settings.json`, so upgrading from an older build never replays it for an existing user, versus `Settings::default()`'s own explicit `false` for a truly fresh install with no settings file at all -- two different defaults for the same field, deliberately, since "missing because the file predates this feature" and "missing because there's no file yet" need opposite answers. `SpawnPurpose::Restore` for every demo tab (not `Root`, which forces an immediate jump to Tab view -- exactly the opposite of what a graph-view demo needs) mirrors precisely how `apply_session` already starts a real saved session: `self.focused` set once before any of the concurrent spawns are issued, landing cleanly in Graph view. Build-verified with zero warnings.
