Live-verified headlessly across three scenarios: a fresh-install-shaped session/settings pair produces exactly the intended layout (confirmed via screenshot -- the JSON card correctly shows its 3 keys, the note's full text renders, the welcome tab's own real WebView2-rendered content is visible in its thumbnail, the group card is correctly labeled); re-running the identical fresh-install setup reproduces the identical layout; and, critically, emptying the session while `has_shown_first_launch_demo` is already `true` (simulating a returning user who closed everything) correctly falls back to the plain single-tab `example.com` bootstrap instead of replaying the demo. One real bug caught by this same live testing, not just code review: the group's two member tabs were first positioned via `graph_layout::spawn_jitter` anchored at the group's own position, but that function's jitter radius is calibrated for spreading a new tab away from a busy live layout -- large enough that one member tab landed embarrassingly far from the group it belonged to, confirmed directly in the session dump before being caught. Fixed with a small fixed offset instead, matching this whole feature's own "hand-picked positions" design.
  - Added a dev-only `TRIGGERFIRSTLAUNCHDEMO` debug command so the demo graph can be re-spawned on demand while testing, without hand-editing `session.json`/`settings.json` each time -- adds a fresh copy to whatever's currently open rather than clearing anything first, and never touches `has_shown_first_launch_demo` itself, matching every other debug command's own "acts on current state" posture.
- **Added `--debug-commands`, a CLI flag independent of `--headless` that accepts the exact same stdin-driven `DebugCommand` protocol on a completely normal, visible, on-screen window** (real position, file dialogs available, window geometry saved) instead of headless mode's off-screen one. Motivated directly by how painful verifying the `manifold://` scheme and the first-launch demo (both above) turned out to be: neither has a headless hook into WebView2's own rendering, so both needed real, non-headless launches verified via OS-level screen capture (`CopyFromScreen`/`PrintWindow` PowerShell scripts) instead of this project's own much simpler headless `SCREENSHOT` command. Checked first rather than assumed: read every one of the 11 `self.headless` call sites in `main.rs` and confirmed `SCREENSHOT`'s own implementation (`GpuState::render`) was never gated on `headless` at all -- it already just captures whatever was actually drawn -- so a single flag change was enough. `main()`'s stdin-reader gate becomes `if headless || debug_commands`, while `app.headless` itself stays exactly what `--headless` alone controls, so none of the other 10 headless-only behaviors (off-screen positioning, disabled file dialogs, skipped geometry saving) apply to a `--debug-commands`-only launch. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified end to end, immediately validating the whole motivation: launched with `--debug-commands` (no `--headless`) on a real visible window, confirmed `SCREENSHOT` captures the app's actual on-screen content directly with no OS-level capture trick needed, then combined it with `TRIGGERFIRSTLAUNCHDEMO` + `GRAPHOPEN` to capture the first-launch demo's `manifold://welcome` tab's own real, WebView2-rendered content in Tab view -- the exact capability that would have simplified this session's own two immediately-preceding features.
- **Added `HELP`/`?` and an actual error message for an unrecognized debug command, replacing total silence in both cases.** The stdin debug-command protocol (`debug_control::parse_line`) had grown to ~70 commands over this session with no way to list them short of reading the source, and a typo'd or misremembered command name was a same-as-success no-op -- indistinguishable from the command genuinely doing nothing, a real source of friction across many of this session's own headless tests. New `COMMAND_HELP` table (`(usage, one-line description)` per existing command, kept next to `parse_line` with an explicit "update this in the same commit" note) backs a new `HELP`/`?` command that prints the full reference to stderr; `spawn_stdin_reader`'s own dispatch loop now distinguishes a genuinely blank line (stays silent -- just a stream artifact, not something anyone typed) from anything else that failed to parse (prints `Unrecognized debug command: "..." -- try HELP`). No way to generate the help table from the match arms themselves (Rust has no runtime reflection over a `match`'s own string patterns) -- accepted as a second, hand-maintained list that can in principle drift from `parse_line`, the cheapest way to give this protocol any discoverability at all against that constraint. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly: `HELP` printed all 76 entries correctly formatted; a blank line produced no output; `FOOBARBAZ` produced the expected error naming the exact bad input; `?` reproduced the identical help output as `HELP` itself.
- **Fixed, reported live: a Graph-view node's own overlay decorations (title/label text, pin/audio/close/eject icons, group name/count labels, the empty-canvas menu's row labels) visibly lagged behind the node's own card while panning the canvas at high speed.** Root-caused by reading `egui::Scene`'s actual `show()` source (this workspace's pinned 0.36.2) rather than guessing: `overlay_painter` deliberately draws on a screen-space layer `Scene` never transforms (its own doc comment explains why -- crisp icon-font glyphs at any zoom, not blurry rescaled ones), so anything painted on it converts its own world position to screen space manually via a `project` closure built from `self.graph_scene_rect`. But `Scene::show` only writes the caller's `scene_rect` argument back *after* the entire content closure returns for the frame -- a live pan/zoom drag is folded into `Scene`'s own *internal* transform and applied to its transformed layer *before* the closure runs, which is why ordinary card content (borders, thumbnails, drawn via `ui.painter()` on that same transformed layer) is always correctly positioned, while `project` -- built once, before `Scene::show`, from the not-yet-updated field -- is unavoidably one frame stale on every single panning/zooming frame. Invisible at rest; a growing pixel gap the faster the camera moves, since a fixed one-frame timing lag becomes a bigger on-screen offset the more ground the camera covers in that frame. No way to ask `Scene` for "this frame's real transform" mid-closure -- the value doesn't exist from the caller's side until the closure is already done. Fixed by deferring: every `overlay_painter` call inside the closure (~28 call sites across every node kind, plus the two small shared helpers `draw_node_close_button`/`draw_new_node_menu_row`) now queues a boxed closure capturing its own world position and paint parameters into a `DeferredOverlays` list instead of painting immediately; once `Scene::show` returns and `self.graph_scene_rect` reflects the frame's real, final pan/zoom, `project` is recomputed fresh and every queued closure is invoked with it. Deliberately not a redesign of `overlay_painter` itself (the untransformed-layer choice for crisp text stands, confirmed correct) -- just moving *when* the already-correct positions get painted. Build-verified with zero warnings on the first attempt despite the diff's size (~28 call sites converted, one new type). Live-verified headlessly: a populated graph (tab/note/group/JSON cards, one already showing real WebView2-rendered thumbnail content) renders with every label/icon in its correct position at rest, identically to before the fix; panning the canvas via a synthetic drag moves every card and its own decorations together with no visible misalignment. The transient one-frame gap itself is inherently unscreenshot-able (any settle time between the pan and the capture lets it self-correct, and a headless test has no way to grab the exact next frame after a live pan input) -- verified by the code-level fix being architecturally sound (confirmed against `Scene::show`'s actual source, not assumed) rather than by directly capturing the single-frame artifact.
- **Added viewport culling to every Graph-view render loop, the single highest-leverage fix for a graph with far more nodes than fit on screen at once.** Every per-kind loop (tabs, downloads, notes, images, pdfs, jsons, groups) was doing full `ui.interact`/paint work for every node in the current level regardless of whether it was actually visible -- fine at the small scale this app has been tested at so far, but a straightforward source of wasted work as graphs grow. Each loop now skips a node entirely (`continue`, right after computing its own `rect`) when `!rect.intersects(self.graph_scene_rect)` -- world-space bounding-box overlap against the current viewport, the same rect `Scene`'s own camera already tracks -- with one deliberate exception: the node currently being dragged (`self.dragging`) is never culled, since `ui.interact`'s own per-frame drag tracking is what keeps a drag alive at all, and skipping it the instant it crossed the visible edge would freeze it in place mid-drag. Both edge-drawing loops (tab-parent and download-parent edges) use a different check on the same rect -- `Rect::from_two_pos(parent_pos, node_pos).intersects(self.graph_scene_rect)`, the *segment's* bounding box, not either endpoint alone -- since an edge with one node on-screen and its other end off-screen still needs to render the visible portion. The minimap (drawn before `Scene::show`, off `fit_all_nodes_scene_rect()`) and the rectangle-select marquee (raw screen-space pointer coordinates) both needed no changes, confirmed by reading each rather than assumed. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly, after an initial test-construction dead end: placing two *parent-linked* tabs far apart in `session.json` doesn't actually test culling, since this graph's own live spring-physics (`graph_layout::step`, `REST_LENGTH` 320) immediately pulls linked nodes back toward each other regardless of their session-recorded positions, moving both off the intended test coordinates before a screenshot could be taken -- caught by cross-checking `EXPORTSESSION`'s own live dump against the coordinates just written, not assumed from a confusing screenshot. Switched to unlinked tabs (no spring between them) for a clean test: confirmed a node at world (1600, 0) is genuinely culled from the main canvas while a node at (0, 0) renders normally, with both still present in the minimap's own unfiltered dot list; confirmed via `EXPORTSESSION` snapshots taken mid-drag that dragging a node continuously past the visibility boundary (world 438 -> 1237 -> 2025, tracking the mouse linearly throughout) never freezes it; and confirmed, using a parent-linked pair with one end dragged off-screen, that the connecting edge still renders across the boundary while the off-screen node's own card correctly does not.
- **Added a second render-cost fix on top of culling: level-of-detail (LOD) rendering, dropping thumbnail textures and title/label text once a dense enough set of nodes is on screen at once.** Surveyed against two other candidates first (freezing the force-directed physics step for off-screen nodes, and a spatial index for hit-testing/repulsion) -- physics-freeze was dropped once `settle_current_level`'s own doc comment surfaced that this layout already only runs as a one-shot settle-to-convergence burst on node-count/group change, not continuously every frame, so there's no ongoing per-frame cost left to freeze; a spatial index was set aside as premature until a profile shows the O(n) cull scan or O(n^2) repulsion burst actually costing time at real node counts. LOD survives that scrutiny because it targets *paint* cost specifically -- the one thing culling's own viewport check doesn't help with once a graph has, say, 200 nodes all genuinely on screen at once. Chosen over a pure zoom-level threshold: counting post-cull *visible* nodes (reusing the exact per-kind rect/size the cull check already builds, via a new shared `count_visible_nodes` helper) targets the actual cost driver -- a dense cluster of many small-but-individually-zoomed-in cards -- rather than a proxy that misses that case. `LOD_SIMPLIFY_COUNT`/`LOD_RESTORE_COUNT` (60/45) give the flip a hysteresis gap rather than one shared threshold, so a count sitting right at a boundary doesn't flicker every frame; the new `App::graph_lod_simplified` field persists across frames and only flips on crossing whichever side applies to its current state. Per kind, only the two genuinely expensive parts are skipped -- GPU texture blits (thumbnail/favicon/image/pdf-page/group-member-preview) and always-drawn `overlay_painter` text (titles, filenames, pin icon, JSON brackets/summary, group name/count) -- while backgrounds, borders, color-tag swatches, hover-gated close/eject buttons, and progress/filter-dim overlays are left alone: every `ui.interact`/click-handling call site is untouched, so simplified rendering never risks breaking click-to-open, drag, pin, mute, or color-cycle. A note's own live `TextEdit` (while actively being edited) is explicitly exempted from ever being hidden by LOD -- only its *read-only* Markdown preview is skipped -- so simplification can never yank an in-progress edit out from under the user. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly across several scenarios, deliberately using cheap Note nodes (no engine process) for the threshold/hysteresis mechanics and a small mix of real tabs for the tab-card-specific path: 70 clustered notes render with backgrounds/borders only, no text or icon, confirming the simplify side; 30 notes render with full title text and icon, confirming the restore side; a wide 70-note spread, panned live (no restart) from "70 visible, simplified" down to "5 visible, full detail," confirmed the hysteresis gap is a real persisted flip driven by the live viewport, not a per-frame recompute; a drag performed on a note card while simplified moved it by exactly the expected screen-to-world delta, confirming `ui.interact` and click/drag handling are genuinely untouched by the LOD branches; and a mixed session (5 real tabs + 65 notes, crossing the threshold) showed the focused tab card rendering with its plain background and accent focus border but no thumbnail, title, favicon, or pin icon, confirming the tab loop's own (larger) set of gated call sites behaves the same way. Images/pdfs/jsons/groups/downloads were not each individually screenshotted -- they follow the identical narrower pattern (one texture-or-text block gated behind the same flag, no interleaved interact logic) already exercised by the two most complex loops (tabs and notes), so those were verified by direct code review instead of a full screenshot pass per kind.
- **A general (not just large-graph) performance audit found four candidates; three were fixed, one was reconsidered and dropped.** Unlike culling/LOD (which target scenarios with unusually many nodes), these all cost cycles on every single frame regardless of graph size.
  - **Fixed: `truncate_to_width` re-ran a real font-shaping call in a binary search, every frame, for every visible label.** For a card's title/filename/group-name, that's ~6-7 calls into `ctx.fonts_mut(...).layout_no_wrap(...)` plus a fresh `String` allocation per binary-search step -- unconditionally, even though a label's text and on-screen width are almost always identical to what they were last frame. Memoized on `(text, font size, max width)` via a new `App::truncate_cache: HashMap<TruncateCacheKey, String>` -- `f32` isn't `Hash`/`Eq`, so the key uses `.to_bits()` for both the font size and the width, and skips font family entirely since every call site in this app builds its `FontId` via `FontId::proportional`. Capped at `TRUNCATE_CACHE_CAP` (2000 entries) and cleared outright past that rather than evicted incrementally -- no LRU bookkeeping for what should, in steady state, only ever hold a small multiple of "how many labeled cards are visible at once." All 4 call sites (tab title, download filename, group name x2) updated to pass the cache through. Build-verified with zero warnings.
  - **Fixed: a single tab card's own render code looked up `self.nodes.get(&id)` seven separate times** -- once each for position, color, title/url/engine-kind, muted, audio-playing, pinned, and (in the end-of-loop filter-dim check) title/url again -- a fresh hash + lookup for the same key every time, paid every frame for every tab regardless of graph size. Consolidated into one lookup right after the cull check and `ui.interact`, extracting everything the rest of the card's render needs (cloning `title`/`url` once, since they're needed past the point where `self.nodes` gets borrowed again for later `&mut self` mutations like drag/hover bookkeeping) into local bindings reused for the rest of the iteration -- behavior-preserving, confirmed by checking that nothing between the original lookups mutated this same node first. Applied the same consolidation, at smaller scale (2 lookups down to 1), to the Download/Image/Pdf loops' own "main render" + "end-of-loop filter-dim check" pairs, threading the already-extracted path through instead of the filter-dim check re-fetching it; the JSON loop already had no redundancy to fix. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly: baseline render (title/favicon/engine dot all correct), `TOGGLEPIN` toggling the pin icon's color, and a drag moving the card by exactly the expected screen-to-world delta (confirmed via `EXPORTSESSION`) -- all matching pre-refactor behavior, since the fix only changes *where* each value is read from, never what it evaluates to.
  - **Fixed: `favicon_fetch::url_origin` (a couple of string scans plus a `format!` allocation) was recomputed from scratch every frame, for every visible tab, in both of its per-frame call sites** (the Graph-view tab card, and the Tab-view toolbar's own identity-chip favicon) -- for a URL that's stable between navigations. New `App::cached_favicon_origin` memoizes it in a `favicon_origin_cache: HashMap<String, Option<String>>`, capped/cleared the same way `truncate_cache` is. The Tab-view call site needed one adjustment beyond swapping the call: it previously read `&node.url` directly from a live borrow of `self.nodes`, which can't coexist with the cache method's own `&mut self` -- fixed by cloning the URL out first. The third call site (inside the IPC handler that stores a newly-fetched favicon under its own origin key) was left uncached: it only runs once per actual fetch completion, not once per frame. Build-verified with zero warnings.
  - **Reconsidered and dropped: caching the 7 `_at_current_level()` lists** (`tabs_at_level` etc.), which `build_graph_ui` rebuilds via a full filter+clone scan over every node, every frame, regardless of whether the current level's membership actually changed. The obvious approach -- gate the rebuild on `current_level_settle_key()`, the same `(group, count)` signal the physics-settle burst already uses -- has a real correctness gap: a same-frame swap (one node removed, a different one added at the same level) leaves the count unchanged, so a cached list would silently miss the new node not just for one frame but indefinitely, until some *other* count-changing event happened to refresh it -- a materially worse failure mode than the physics-settle trigger's own use of the same imprecise signal, where a missed trigger is merely cosmetic (a new node stays un-spread rather than invisible). Doing this correctly needs a real generation counter bumped at every actual `node_order` mutation -- found 22 separate call sites that insert or remove from it, each one a place the counter could be missed as new code is added later. Given the fix this scale of invasiveness buys is a plain `Vec` filter+clone (cheap, not real GPU/text-shaping work like the two fixes above), the risk of a silently-invisible node was judged not worth it. Left as a possible future follow-up if a profile ever shows this scan actually costing time at real node/group counts.
- **A visible but idle window draws at 10 Hz instead of every vsync (2026-10-01).** Timed the frame on a release build (temporary instrumentation, since removed): `drain_tab_events` ~0.2 ms and the egui pass ~0.3 ms, but `render_frame` ~15 ms, nearly all of it the `Fifo` present wait, at ~4 ms of CPU per frame: ~23-26% of a core at 60 fps with nothing happening. Vulkan vs DX12 and `desired_maximum_frame_latency` 1 vs 2 changed it by no more than the noise (21-32%; the machine was loaded by other sessions), so the fix is fewer frames. `App::is_idle` (real window, no view transition, no activity for `IDLE_AFTER` = 1 s): `about_to_wait` then uses `WaitUntil` at `IDLE_FRAME` = 100 ms (or the sooner `repaint_delay` egui reports) instead of `Poll`, and `RedrawRequested` stops re-requesting itself. Activity is any window event or `UserEvent`, any engine message other than a frame, a burst of `BUSY_FRAMES` = 6 engine frames in a second (video; an idle WebView2 page sends ~2.4/s), and egui asking for a repaint itself (`repaint_delay` zero). Result: ~6-7% of a core in Graph and Tab view (the engine side re-measured per process afterwards: the WebView2 engine host 0%, its msedgewebview2 children 0-1% in both views, so the earlier "~18% engine" figure, a sum over every msedgewebview2 process on the machine, was not ours). The save toast, camera tween and pan momentum call `request_repaint` while they run (so they stay at 60 fps), and the audio waveform calls `request_repaint_after(50 ms)`. Result with the 100 ms interval: ~2-3% of a core in Graph and Tab view (it was 6-7% at 50 ms). The full-rate floor (a frame every vsync, as while interacting) was re-measured with idling forced off, three runs each: Vulkan 21/17/18% of a core, DX12 18/17/16%, so the backend is not worth switching for (and DX12 would need DXC, see `~/.claude/LONG_RUNS.md`). Audited the rest of the time-based drawing for the same gap and found none: `egui::Spinner` and `animate_bool` request their own repaints, the view `Transition` is excluded from idle, and the graph layout is a one-shot settle (`settle_current_level`), not per-frame physics. Async results (suggestions, favicons, fetches) are polled by the idle frame, so they show up to 100 ms late. A video's frame burst ends idle by itself. Headless never idles.
- **A minimized window no longer redraws (2026-10-01).** Re-measured the idle cost of `ControlFlow::Poll` on a release build (one card, Graph view, machine load 18%): `manifold.exe` used ~22% of a core visible and ~16% minimized. `about_to_wait` now switches to `WaitUntil(now + 100 ms)` while the window is minimized and runs only the frame-free bookkeeping (`minimized_tick`: store, engine messages, idle sleep, transitions); the `RedrawRequested` arm for a minimized window builds and presents nothing and doesn't re-request a redraw. Result: minimized ~0-1% of a core; restoring resumes full-rate redraw (~20%). Not changed: a visible window still redraws every frame (~18-22% idle), so the full `Wait` conversion below remains open; headless never counts as minimized. Also on minimize, the tabs on screen get `SetVisible(false)` (`hide_tabs_for_minimize`) and `true` again on restore: with the release WebView2 engine, Tab view on a static page went from ~36-38% of a core visible to ~8% minimized (it was ~15% with only the host throttled; machine load 8%); content rendered after restore. Found on the way: minimizing sent the page a 0x0 resize, which crashed the WebView2 engine ("Tab crashed, restarted" after restore); `Resized` now skips the tab resize for a zero-sized window (no toast in a minimize/restore run afterwards, one run each way). Not measured: a minimize during a page load (manual check still open).
- **Alt+Left / Alt+Right are Back and Forward (2026-10-15).** Two rows in the `CHORDS` table (`shortcuts.rs`) that call `navigate_history` on the focused tab, the one entry point the chips and `GOBACK`/`GOFORWARD` use, so they walk the trail the same way (opened-from at a first page included). `claims_chord` now compares the held Ctrl, Alt and Super to the chord's exactly (Shift stays ignored for Ctrl and Alt chords), so the page no longer also gets an Alt chord; it replaces the old Ctrl-or-nothing special case and claims the same chords as before for those two cases. Listed in the F1 reference. **Mouse buttons 4/5 (2026-10-16):** `side_button_nav` (`trail_nav.rs`), called from the `MouseInput` handler on a press the app sees (not consumed by egui, Tab view), steps the focused tab through `navigate_history` like Alt+Left/Right. `translate_mouse_button` still drops them, so the page never also receives one. Unchecked: whether a native-window engine (WebView2) delivers a press over its page to the app at all; where it doesn't, the engine's own Back runs and the trail follows by inference, as before. Not driven headless (`CLICK` takes only left/right/middle).
- **A tab keeps its Back/Forward list when its engine is replaced (2026-10-01).** Sleep, a crash restart or an engine swap starts a new engine at the current URL only, so Back was greyed out (a minimize that crashed the engine did the same). `nav_history.rs` keeps a per-tab list on the host (`App::nav_history`, fed by `NavigationStateChanged`, capped at 50). Until the engine is replaced the engine's own history is used; after `engine_reset` (on spawn landing) the tab is "hosted": Back/Forward `Navigate` to the neighbour URL and the toolbar enables from the list. Limits: a hosted Back is a fresh load (no scroll or form state); redirects and `pushState` are approximated; the list is saved to `tab-history.json` in the profile (machine-local, keyed by node id, at most 50 entries per tab also when loaded, closed tabs' lists dropped at every save, not in the shared session store, and dropped for a tab whose URL no longer matches its list's current entry), so a restart keeps it too (`CASES=restart`); native restore (Servo `notify_history_changed`, WebKitGTK session state) is deferred to the Linux build. Headless `GOBACK` / `GOFORWARD` drive it; `CASES=history gate sleep` passes on WebView2 and CEF. `CASES=crash` kills the engine host mid-session and passes on both too; the engine-swap path shares `engine_reset` but has no live test. Superseded 2026-10-15 by the trail (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-trail-design.md): the list is a tree with an engine-list shadow instead of a `hosted` flag, a closed tab keeps it, and Back at a tab's first page goes to its opened-from card.
- **Investigated a 5th audit candidate (why does the app redraw continuously at all, even idle?) and fixed the one safe piece of it.** Root cause is `event_loop.set_control_flow(ControlFlow::Poll)` -- not the scattered `window.request_redraw()` calls, which are nearly redundant once Poll is already spinning the loop continuously regardless. Confirmed via reading, not assumed, that this is deliberate and genuinely load-bearing: the save-toast's fade in/out and the audio waveform animation are both driven directly off wall-clock `Instant`/`SystemTime` deltas with no `request_repaint` call at all, relying entirely on "we're already redrawing every frame anyway" -- and engine-to-host frame delivery (WebView2/CEF/Servo capture) has no event-loop-wake path of its own either (`EventLoopProxy`/`UserEvent` is only used for tab-spawn results and debug commands), so live tab content also depends on Poll's continuous spinning to get noticed promptly. Measured, not just reasoned about: a real (non-headless) launch with one idle default tab showed ~22% of one CPU core burned continuously doing nothing, concentrated in `manifold.exe` itself rather than the WebView2 engine-host child process. Converting to event-driven `ControlFlow::Wait` was investigated and explicitly **not** attempted here -- doing it correctly needs every wall-clock animation moved to explicit `ctx.request_repaint_after` scheduling *and* an explicit wake-the-loop call added to every async frame-delivery path across all four separate engine-host crates; missing even one creates a "frozen until an unrelated event happens to wake the loop" bug (a stuck fade, a stopped waveform, a frozen video frame) -- a large, cross-cutting architectural project of its own, not a bounded fix.
  - **The one piece that *was* safe to fix on its own: `present_mode: surface_caps.present_modes[0]`** ([gpu.rs](crates/manifold/src/gpu.rs)) had no reasoning behind it at all, unlike `ControlFlow::Poll` -- just "whichever mode the backend happens to list first," which wgpu's own spec doesn't guarantee is the throttled `Fifo` (vsync-locked) mode. Under continuous `Poll`-driven redraw, an untethered `Immediate`/`Mailbox` surface would present fully uncapped with no vsync throttle at all -- a materially worse version of the same idle-cost problem, on whatever backend/driver combination happens to order its present modes differently than this development machine's does. Fixed by requesting `wgpu::PresentMode::Fifo` explicitly -- the one mode every wgpu backend is required to support, so no fallback is needed. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified via the same real-launch CPU measurement as above: ~19.8% of one core over a 5-second idle sample, versus ~22.1% before the fix on the same machine -- a modest, not dramatic, drop, honestly reported rather than oversold. Read together with the investigation above, this says the surface was likely already resolving to something close to `Fifo` on this particular machine even before the fix; the value of making it explicit is defensive (guaranteed-correct behavior on any backend, not just this one), not a measured fix for the bulk of the idle cost. The remaining ~20% is the deeper, unaddressed cost: running this app's full per-frame update-and-render pipeline once every vsync tick, continuously, even at rest -- exactly the `ControlFlow::Poll` architecture question this investigation deliberately left alone.
- **Fixed: the previous entry's own `truncate_cache` memoization did nothing during an active pan/zoom gesture -- caught while specifically asked to look at pan/zoom performance, not the earlier general audit.** All 4 call sites derive `max_width` from `zoom_scale`, a continuous `f32` that changes on effectively every frame while scrolling/pinching to zoom -- and the cache had been keyed on that width's *exact* bit pattern, so during a smooth zoom the key was different every single frame, meaning a 100% cache-miss rate (the full binary-search-with-font-shaping cost, every frame, for every visible label) during exactly the gesture where avoiding that cost matters most. Fine at rest, useless in motion. Fixed by bucketing: `max_width` is now floored to the nearest `TRUNCATE_WIDTH_BUCKET` (4px) *before* it's used as both the cache key and the actual search bound, so nearby zoom levels share a cache entry instead of each minting a fresh one. Sound because a truncation result is a step function of width -- it only actually changes at the exact point where one more character's glyph would fit -- so a 4px bucket rarely straddles one of those points; when it does, the visible cost is "very occasionally one character more truncated than strictly necessary," never overflow past the card's own edge (flooring, not rounding, keeps the bucketed width always <= the true available width). Panning alone was never affected by this -- it doesn't touch `zoom_scale`. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified headlessly across a real zoom range on a single tab: 89% zoom correctly shows the truncated "Example Dom…", 242%/300% zoom correctly shows the full untruncated "Example Domain" once it fits -- confirming both branches of the bucketed function still produce the same results as before, just computed less often.
- **Investigated a specific panning-smoothness hypothesis and retracted the initial finding after a control test.** Added temporary frame-timing instrumentation (an `eprintln!` in `WindowEvent::RedrawRequested`, removed after) and drove a scripted pan via headless debug commands: idle frames averaged ~40ms, a 30-step synthetic pan averaged ~88ms -- looked like a real, roughly 2x panning-specific cost. A control test overturned it: 30 rapid *no-op* debug commands (`GRAPHFILTERTEXT`, no camera movement at all) averaged ~104ms, *worse* than the actual pan -- proving the slowdown was the debug-command dispatch pipeline itself (a background stdin-reader thread hopping through `EventLoopProxy` into the main loop has real per-command overhead), not panning. That path is irrelevant to real users, who never drive this app via stdin; real mouse-drag panning goes through winit's native event dispatch, a different and much cheaper route this tooling can't script or measure. No code change from this investigation -- the one confirmed real panning-smoothness bug (decoration lag during a fast pan) was already fixed earlier via the `DeferredOverlays` refactor; beyond that, headless debug-command tooling turned out to be the wrong instrument to validate or refute further panning-specific claims, and reporting an inconclusive result honestly seemed better than either fabricating a fix or silently dropping the question.
- **Switched `App::nodes` from `std::collections::HashMap`'s default SipHash to `foldhash` as its hasher.** SipHash is deliberately DoS-resistant against adversarial keys -- a guarantee this map has no use for (every key is an app-generated id like `"tab-0"`, never derived from untrusted input) and pays for on every one of the many `self.nodes` lookups per frame across the Graph-view render loops. Surveyed three alternatives before picking one: `rustc-hash`/`FxHashMap` would work but is a genuinely new dependency; `hashbrown::HashMap` (which already has `foldhash` as its own default) is already in this workspace's dependency tree, but swaps `self.nodes`'s actual concrete type away from `std::collections::HashMap`, carrying real (if likely small) API-compatibility risk across the 50+ call sites touching this map in this file, for no offsetting benefit right now -- `hash_raw_entry`-style APIs it would additionally unlock have no identified use here yet, and switching to `hashbrown::HashMap` later, if a concrete need for that ever shows up, costs exactly the same small effort it would today. Landed on `foldhash` used directly as a plain `BuildHasher` for `std::collections::HashMap` (`HashMap<String, GraphNode, foldhash::fast::RandomState>`, aliased as `NodeMap`) -- already fully resolved in this workspace's tree as `hashbrown`'s own default-hasher dependency (two `hashbrown` versions are already pulled in transitively via `wgpu`/`naga`), so this is a zero-net-new-dependency hasher swap, not a new crate; `self.nodes` keeps its literal `std::collections::HashMap` type, so every existing call site needed no changes beyond the initial type alias. Build-verified with zero warnings on the first attempt. Live-verified headlessly: a two-tab parent/child session round-trips correctly through `EXPORTSESSION`, and both tabs, their connecting edge, and their card content render identically to before the change.
- **Added pan momentum: releasing a background-pan drag with enough speed now keeps the camera gliding and decelerating instead of stopping dead** -- the "flick to scroll" feel most pan/zoom canvases (Figma, Google Maps, Miro) already have. New `PanMomentum { initial_velocity, start, start_center }`, recomputed fresh from elapsed time every frame the same way the existing `GraphCameraTween` already is (never accumulated frame-to-frame, so it can't drift with frame-time variance) -- exponential decay (`v(t) = v0 * e^-kt`) has a closed-form displacement, so applying it is one formula evaluation per frame, not an integration loop. The one design question worth calling out: where does the release velocity come from? Confirmed by reading egui 0.36.2's own pinned source first that `PointerState` already tracks a smoothed `velocity()` internally -- but using that raw *screen-space pointer* velocity directly was rejected in favor of tracking the *camera's own* recent world-space positions instead (`pan_drag_history`, a small ring of recent `(time, scene-center)` samples): the camera's own value is what `PanMomentum` actually needs, already in the right units, with no zoom-scale conversion or sign relationship to get right, and it naturally excludes anything that moves the raw pointer without actually panning the camera. History is deliberately only sampled while a genuine background pan is held down (pointer down, no node grabbed, no `GraphCameraTween` in progress) and cleared the instant any of those stop being true, so a single-frame minimap-click jump or an unrelated earlier gesture can never leak a bogus velocity into a later release. A fresh grab of the background immediately cancels any momentum still gliding -- "grab it to stop it," matching the same convention a real flick-scroll surface already has. Build-verified with zero warnings on the first attempt. Live-verified headlessly via temporary trajectory logging (removed after): confirmed a brisk release (measured ~940-1140 world-units/sec from the tracked history) produces a textbook decaying glide, its center converging smoothly toward the exact analytically-predicted asymptote (`start_center + v0/k`); confirmed grabbing the background again mid-glide stops all further momentum application on the very next frame, with a subsequent stationary click correctly computing zero velocity and never restarting it; and confirmed via before/after screenshots that a slow, deliberate drag-and-release does not glide at all, staying exactly where released.
- **Fixed: `GpuState::upload_frame` allocated a brand new GPU texture (and, for the main frame, a new bind group) from scratch on every single uploaded frame, for a live tab that's continuously producing same-sized frames the overwhelming majority of the time.** This was the actual dominant cost identified in the "app feels slow" investigation above -- confirmed as the right target by reading `create_texture_with_data`'s own doc comment on `TabTexture`, which openly said "recreated on every uploaded frame, no dirty-tracking." `TabTexture`/`ThumbTexture` now also keep the `wgpu::Texture` itself plus its `width`/`height` (and, for the main frame, `format`), so `upload_frame` can compare a new frame's dimensions against what's already there: matching dimensions reuse the existing texture in place via a new shared `write_full_texture` helper (`queue.write_texture`, no new GPU resource at all); a real size/format change (a resize, or the very first upload for a tab) still takes the original full-recreation path. The main frame's `bind_group` only gets rebuilt alongside a genuinely new texture (it references the texture's view directly, so there's no way around that when the resource itself changes) -- the thumbnail's own `egui_id`, once registered, is instead just re-pointed at a replacement texture via the existing `update_egui_texture_from_wgpu_texture` call on a real resize, so nothing holding that id ever sees it change. Explicitly added `wgpu::TextureUsages::COPY_DST` to both textures' usage flags, required for `write_texture` to target them at all. `upload_favicon`/`upload_image` (one-shot registrations, never re-uploaded after their first call) needed the same struct fields threaded through to keep compiling, but intentionally weren't given the same reuse branch -- there's nothing to reuse when a resource is only ever created once. Build-verified with zero warnings in both debug and release on the first attempt. Live-verified two ways: confirmed via temporary logging that the reuse branch actually fires on every frame after the first for a real tab (identical width/height each time, as expected for a static page); confirmed via a headless screenshot that tab content and its thumbnail still render correctly with no visual regression. **Honestly reported, not oversold:** re-running the same idle frame-timing test this fix was motivated by showed no measurable change in either debug (~80ms/frame, unchanged) or release (~18ms/frame, unchanged) -- the debug-mode cost turns out to be dominated by the *separate*, still-unaddressed CPU-side pixel format-conversion/downscale feeding the thumbnail (untouched by this fix, since that cost exists whether or not the destination texture is reused), and the release-mode idle test doesn't call `upload_frame` often enough once a page settles to show a texture-allocation cost either way. The fix is real and correct on its own terms -- it eliminates genuine, unnecessary GPU resource churn, confirmed structurally via the reuse-branch logging -- but its actual frame-time benefit would need a scenario with continuous frame delivery (a video, a CSS animation, active scrolling) to actually demonstrate, which this verification pass didn't attempt.
- **Fixed: the CPU-side pixel work the previous entry's own investigation left unaddressed -- `to_thumbnail_rgba`'s channel swap and `downscale_rgba`'s box filter -- turned out to be the actual dominant idle cost, and both are now GPU-side.** Confirmed before writing any code: re-timing the exact same idle test with only the persistent-texture fix applied still showed ~80ms/frame in debug, unchanged, so the two CPU functions (not texture allocation) were the real target. Surveyed two approaches first: an external SIMD-optimized resize crate (`fast_image_resize`) would have been the lower-risk pick, but only reliably fixes the *release*-mode cost (already fast) without an additional per-package `opt-level` override for debug; a GPU downsample fixes the debug-mode cost at the root, since it removes the work from the CPU entirely regardless of how `manifold` itself was compiled -- chosen for exactly that reason, accepting the larger implementation surface. New `fs_downsample` fragment shader (a fixed 4x4 grid of bilinear-filtered taps per output texel, spread across that texel's own footprint in source UV space) replaces the CPU box filter -- a single bilinear tap per output texel would alias badly at the 4-8x downscale ratios a full-resolution tab frame needs, so this emulates a true box average via supersampling instead, cheap because the tap count is a compile-time constant with no dynamic branching. Needed its own render pipeline/bind group layout (a third binding for a small `box_size` uniform `fs_main` doesn't have, and a fixed `Rgba8Unorm` target format instead of the surface's own `config.format`) and a 16-byte uniform buffer written fresh via `queue.write_buffer` before every draw. `render_thumbnail`'s own doc comment spells out the one correctness trap this class of change warned about going in: the main texture is `Bgra8UnormSrgb`/`Rgba8UnormSrgb`, and sampling an sRGB texture auto-linearizes the color -- writing that straight into the thumbnail's plain `Rgba8Unorm` target would silently shift brightness/contrast versus the real tab content. Sampled instead through a *reinterpreting* view in the non-sRGB counterpart format (new `non_srgb_view_format` helper), which needed the main texture's own `view_formats` (declared once, at creation) to list that counterpart -- wgpu requires every format a texture will ever be viewed as to be named up front, not just the format it's created with. Channel order (BGRA vs RGBA) needed no handling at all, unlike the removed CPU swap: `textureSample` already returns a consistent `(r,g,b,a)` regardless of the bound texture's own declared layout, that remapping is what a texture *format* means to the GPU. Deliberately reproduces, not fixes, the CPU path's own previously-documented "slightly washed out" cosmetic gap (bypassing sRGB decode on the way in rather than decoding-then-re-encoding) -- this pass changes where those bytes get produced, not what they are. `to_thumbnail_rgba`/`downscale_rgba` deleted outright; nothing else referenced them. Build-verified with zero warnings in both debug and release, on the first attempt despite the size of the change. Live-verified thoroughly given the sRGB risk: a headless screenshot of a real `example.com` tab showed the thumbnail with correct, uncorrupted colors (white background, dark text, red top bar, blue link -- no channel swap, no severe gamma shift) both at the card's normal size and zoomed to 300%; a live window resize (forcing the "no same-size texture to reuse" branch, the one case that still allocates a fresh destination texture) re-rendered correctly with the same color fidelity, confirming the fresh-creation path is equally correct, not just the common reuse path. Performance verified, not just assumed: re-running the exact idle frame-timing test from the previous entry showed debug-mode frame time drop from ~80ms/frame (~13fps) to ~16.7ms/frame (~60fps) -- matching release mode (~16.7ms, unchanged, no regression) almost exactly. Between this and the persistent-texture fix, a debug build now performs indistinguishably from release for this specific workload, though the general "release is unoptimized-by-default for everything else" caveat in Running above still applies to any other CPU-bound path this investigation didn't specifically target.
- **Added concurrency safety for `session.json`/`settings.json`: a save now checks whether the file changed on disk since this process last loaded or saved it, and redirects to a conflict file instead of silently overwriting.** Motivated by a real incident, not a hypothetical: two of this project's own concurrent development/testing sessions -- an interactive one and a spawned background task -- shared the same `%APPDATA%\manifold\` profile with no isolation, and one's `save_session()` call silently clobbered the other's `session.json` mid-test, with zero warning either way. A spike first surveyed the actual solution space this pointed at -- plain concurrent-write safety, shared live tab-graph metadata (real multi-instance collaboration), and full co-browsing of shared live tab content -- and scoped down to just the first: the other two would need a genuine architectural shift (an authoritative shared store every instance is a live client of, or a captured-frame relay/multi-viewer subsystem for content itself) that's a real feature decision on its own, not a safety patch. New `session::fingerprint()`/`settings::fingerprint()` (a process-local `DefaultHasher` over the raw file contents -- not cryptographic or cross-run-stable, just good enough to notice "this file's bytes changed since I last looked") are recorded on `App` at load time and after every successful save. `save_session`/a new `save_settings` wrapper (replacing all 7 direct `self.settings.save()` call sites) check the current on-disk fingerprint against that recorded value immediately before writing; a mismatch redirects the write to `session.conflict-<unix-seconds>.json`/`settings.conflict-<unix-seconds>.json` instead (settings adds `-<n>` after the seconds when that name is taken, so a second conflict in one second keeps the first) (the same collision-avoidance naming convention this project's own screenshot/crash-log paths already use -- a fixed conflict filename would let a *second* conflict later in the same run silently overwrite the first one's still-unreviewed data, exactly the failure mode being fixed) and shows a toast naming the exact path. The very first save of a run always proceeds normally (`session_fingerprint`/`settings_fingerprint` still `None` -- nothing was loaded yet, so there's nothing to conflict with). `Settings` gained its own `save_to(path)`, mirroring `Session`'s existing one, so a conflicted settings save has somewhere else to go. Build-verified with zero warnings. Live-verified by reproducing the original incident on purpose: launched headless, externally overwrote `session.json` with different content while it was still running (simulating the second process), then triggered a save (`NEWTAB`) -- confirmed the real `session.json` still held the external process's own content untouched, and a new `session.conflict-<ts>.json` correctly held this process's own state (its original tab plus the new one), nothing lost on either side. Repeated the identical test for `settings.json` (external `home_page` edit, then `ZOOMIN` to trigger a save) with the same result. **Disclosed honestly: this fix is forward-looking, not retroactive** -- the real incident that motivated it happened before this existed, and the session data actually lost in it was not recovered by building this afterward.
- **One close path and one node menu**: every node card's hover ✕ and context-menu Delete now go through the same `close_node`, so hover-closing a Note/Image/Pdf/Json also lands in Recently Closed (before, only menu Delete did). The seven per-kind right-click menus share one `node_menu` state and one `build_node_menu` dispatcher; Escape now dismisses any of them (before, only the group menu). Verified live: hover-closing a Pdf card, and separately an Image card, each produced "Recently Closed (1)" where before only menu Delete did; right-clicking Group, Pdf, and Tab cards reproduced their pre-change menus (matching baseline screenshots pixel-for-pixel outside an unrelated JSON-thumbnail decoration); right-clicking a Json card and then, without dismissing it, an Image card switched the menu instantly with only ever one on screen, confirming the single shared `node_menu`/fade design; a Note card's own menu opened (Copy Text/Delete) and closed itself after Copy Text; click-outside dismiss and the Tab menu's Close both worked as before. Escape's new any-menu-dismiss behavior and Image's "Remove from Group" reparenting were confirmed by code inspection rather than reproduced live. Two follow-ups landed on the same branch. First, a Note card was never hovered at all (no hover border, no hover ✕, with a real mouse too, ever since Notes were added): its hover-only interact was registered under its own click/drag interact, which egui never reports as hovered. It's now registered after the card's content, and hover ✕ → Recently Closed → reopen was checked live. Second, closing a blank (empty or whitespace-only) note no longer fills a Recently Closed slot with an entry that restores nothing.
- **One graph card frame for Download/Note/Image/Pdf/Json**: `App::draw_graph_card` plus `App::paint_card_body` now replace five separate per-kind render loops in `build_graph_ui` that used to hand-copy the same drag/hover/close-button/context-menu/filter-dim scaffolding once per kind, differing only in each kind's own body paint and click action. `node_card_size` is now the one size source feeding all 4 sites that need a card's dimensions (bounds computation, LOD-count thresholding, the right-click hit test, and the frame's own layout) -- the LOD count in particular now uses Image/Pdf's *exact* scaled size instead of an approximation. Because every kind's hover/drag interact is registered by the shared frame after the body is painted, the Note-hover bug fixed just above is now structurally impossible for any of the five kinds, not something each one has to remember to get right. The right-click hit test picks whichever card is topmost in draw order, so overlapping cards no longer risk showing the wrong kind's menu. Download now has full parity with the other four: previously a download could only ever enter a group by being spawned from an already-grouped tab, and could never leave one; it now also joins a group by dragging onto it, and its right-click menu offers "Remove from Group" once grouped, so it can leave one too. It gets the same sticky hover as every other card -- a card keeps its hover (and ✕) until another card takes the hover, nothing toggles on click -- and it lost the hover fill-brightening no other kind had. Verified live via before/after screenshots of the same nine scripted scenes (scene, close-note, drag-json, filter, and all four hover variants, plus a right-click) -- every pair matched except a pre-existing Group-thumbnail timing flake (the "Try grouping" card's mini preview sometimes rendering blank independent of this change; see `task-3-report.md`), confirming the shared frame reproduces each kind's existing look and behavior exactly. Beyond those baselines: clicking an Image card correctly maximized it into Item view; two back-to-back clicks on a Json card (no gap) opened its JSON viewer popup; dragging a Json card fully onto a Note and right-clicking the overlap showed the Json menu ("Expand as Graph"), not the Note's, confirming the topmost-wins hit test; clicking a Note's body and then its header strip left it still rendered, not reverted to non-editing (its `TextEdit` itself never visibly gains focus in headless runs -- no `request_focus()` call on this path, unchanged since before this work and not something this task touched). Download's own live checks (group drop, then "Remove from Group" on the grouped download) weren't possible at first: there was no headless way to create a Download node. A follow-up added one, below, and ran them.
- **Headless `ADDDOWNLOAD <path>` debug command**: creates a Download node as if the focused tab had just started downloading `path` (nothing is fetched or written). Before this, downloads only ever came from a real engine download, so no headless run could produce one. `App::insert_download_node` now holds the insert, shared by the engine's `DownloadStarted` event and this command, so the debug path runs the real code. Verified live on the demo scene: dragging the download onto "Try grouping" put it in the group (and off the top level). Inside the group, its right-click menu showed "Remove from Group", and clicking that moved it back to the top level. Two headless-driver lessons, now written up on `DebugCommand`:
  - Open a right-click menu with a spaced `MOUSEDOWN`/`MOUSEUP ... right`, not `CLICK ... right`. A menu's first frame is egui's sizing pass, which assumes a default ~400 px tall area, so lower on screen the menu is placed above the pointer that frame. A release in that same frame counts as a click outside the menu and closes it. A real mouse release comes a frame or more later, after the sizing pass.
  - Demo tabs can take 30 s or more to spawn headless, and `ADDDOWNLOAD` does nothing until the focused tab exists.
- **Headless `WAITTABS [secs]`, and one frame per headless command**: `WAITTABS` holds the commands after it until no tab spawn is in flight (at most `secs`, default 60), replacing a guessed `sleep 45` before commands that need the demo tabs. The app counts spawns in flight (`tab_spawns_in_flight`) and the stdin reader blocks on a channel until that count reaches zero. Separately, headless drags sometimes turned into a canvas pan. The cause was the test driver, not the app: the off-screen headless window got `RedrawRequested` only sporadically (measured about 40 frames in 20 s, some a second apart, under load; see the slow-frame entry below), so a `MOUSEDOWN` and the `MOUSEMOVE`s after it could land in one egui frame. That frame hit-tested the press at the pointer's final position, off the card, so the background `Scene` took it and panned. A real on-screen window repaints normally, and a real mouse spreads a drag over many frames, so real input isn't affected. In headless mode the app now renders a frame right after each debug command. With that, a drag with no `sleep`s between commands landed on the card 3 runs out of 3; with the frame disabled, 0 out of 3 (every command landed before the first frame). The full drop → drill in → right-click → Remove from Group flow also passes with no input sleeps. The `DebugCommand` notes are updated: mouse commands no longer need pacing, but a card still needs a `MOUSEMOVE` onto it before a `MOUSEDOWN`.
- **Node menus fade out as they were drawn, and ignore clicks while fading**: a node's right-click menu used to redraw its fade-out from the node's new state, so after "Remove from Group" the row vanished mid-fade, and "Pin" flipped to "Unpin". The menu now reads a `NodeMenuView` snapshot each frame while open, and the fade-out draws the frozen copy. A menu whose action removed its node (Delete, Close, Dissolve) now fades as well, instead of vanishing. While fading, node menus and every other clickable popup (toolbar lists, engine pickers, tab actions, quick switcher, link menu) are click-through, so the second click of a double-click can't run an item twice (e.g. Duplicate twice). Checked headless with the fade slowed to 3 s: the fading menu kept its row, and a second click passed through to the card behind.
- **Investigated, no change: slow headless frames (0.05-0.9 s in `GpuState::render`)**: temporary per-stage timings showed the app's own work (atlas sync, recording, submit) stays under ~5 ms in the big stalls. The time is spent waiting in `get_current_texture` or `present`, often in steps of about 200 ms, as if Windows presents the off-screen (-32000, -32000) headless window at ~5 Hz. Seen on both Vulkan (what wgpu picks here, RX 9060 XT) and DX12. With `PresentMode::Immediate` the 200 ms steps went away, and 50-90 ms stalls remained, spread across every stage including CPU-only recording. Those look like CPU contention while tab engines start up. Follow-up on-screen check (`--debug-commands`, same script, four runs each way, same trace): the visible window and the headless one both held ~60 fps with no frame over 50 ms, except two ~54 ms frames in one headless run. By then a `java` process that had been on a GPU during the stalled runs was gone. So the stalls weren't reproduced on-screen or headless under a quiet machine. The likely cause is contention (the GPU shared with another process, or engine startup load), not the app or the off-screen window as such. The same probably explains the sparse `RedrawRequested` frames (about 40 in 20 s) behind the drag-pan flake. The frame-per-command fix still guarantees each command its own frame either way. `Fifo` stays, since `ControlFlow::Poll` would present uncapped otherwise. Seen again 2026-09-24 in a visible `--debug-commands` window: `get_current_texture` returned `Timeout`, then no `RedrawRequested` arrived for ~3 s (which is what dropped `SCREENSHOT`s before they blocked the reader). Four other processes (long-running jobs from other sessions) were using the RX 9060 XT's 3D engine at the time, so it was left unmeasured; re-check on an idle GPU before treating it as the app's problem.
- **Fixed: an unreadable `session.json` was silently ignored and then overwritten**: `Session::load_from` dropped parse errors, so a file missing a required field (`next_tab_id` had no default) started the app fresh with no message, and the first save replaced the file. Now the error is toasted at startup, Open Session and Import, and an unreadable `session.json` is first renamed to `session.unreadable-<unix-seconds>.json`. `next_tab_id` defaults like the other counters, and `apply_session` raises every id counter past the ids it restores, so a missing or stale counter can't reuse an id. Toasts are now drawn in Graph view too (they were only drawn in Tab and Image view). `settings.json` and `bookmarks.json` get the same move-aside-and-toast handling through the shared `json_file.rs`, group import errors are toasted too, and a partial `settings.json` keeps the fields it has instead of resetting all of them. A toast's 4 s now starts at its first draw, so one queued during startup isn't used up before the window appears. Load-error toasts for the JSON files name only the file (full paths go to stderr), and an image or JSON node whose file is missing or unreadable now toasts instead of silently vanishing on restore, add-file or reopen. On restore it also stays on the canvas as a placeholder card (a `FILE_X` glyph, the error as its tooltip) and stays in `session.json`, so the next save doesn't forget it. Clicking it tries the file again, and once the file is back the real card takes its place. A PDF card that fails to render now does the same: it was already kept, but failed silently with PDFium's nine-line debug dump as its tooltip. Now it toasts "Couldn't open PDF <name>: <reason>" (`pdf_render::pdfium_error` turns an I/O error into its plain message), and a click renders it again. This was the "about:blank tab never appears" follow-up in the node-menu spec: the tab URL never mattered.
- **Fixed: a Download/Note/Image/Pdf/Json card stayed "hovered" after the pointer left it**: its border stayed bright and its ✕ stayed up until another card was hovered. `draw_graph_card` passed `is_hovered`, which includes last frame's `self.hovered`, to `finish_draggable_node`, which writes `self.hovered` back from it, so it could never clear. That term is what kept the ✕ drawn while the pointer is on it (the ✕ is registered last, so it takes the hover from both card interacts). Now `self.hovered` is written from this frame's hover of the card or its ✕, the way the tab and group loops already did. Found while checking the placeholder cards' tooltips. Checked headless: after leaving a card for empty canvas, the ✕ and highlight are gone; on the ✕ itself it stays drawn (red) with the card highlighted.
- **Fixed: `SCREENSHOT` could be silently lost.** `pending_screenshot` holds one path. When frames stalled (surface acquire timing out headless, or a visible window that wasn't being painted), the next `SCREENSHOT` replaced the waiting one, and `QUIT` exited after one redraw even if that redraw never got a surface. Of four screenshots 3 s apart, often only the first and last were written; a trace showed each request arriving while the previous one was still pending. Now the stdin reader blocks on `SCREENSHOT` until the app has saved it (up to 30 s, then it warns and moves on), the same way `WAITTABS` waits for tab spawns, so later commands, `QUIT` included, can't overtake it. A blocked reader exposed an older problem: the driver scripts ran `sleep` in bash while writing the pipe, so during a `WAITTABS` or `SCREENSHOT` wait they kept going, and the lines they buffered then arrived back to back with no gaps. New `SLEEP secs` pauses the reader itself, so pauses stay in order; the drivers now send `sleep N` lines as `SLEEP`, and run a line starting with `!` in bash (not in step with the app). Checked: the same four-screenshot script now writes all four in a visible window, and a six-screenshot headless run that used to lose one writes all six.
- **A group's close is the one close (2026-10-14).** The group card's ✕, Del and middle-click now run `close_node` like every other card: the group and its members are hidden together and reopen from Recently Closed. They used to dissolve it (members moved up, the group hard-deleted, no history), while Ctrl+W, Close Selected and "Close Group and Contents" closed it. `CardControl::action` no longer takes a kind (Close is `Delete` everywhere), so `key_hint` no longer does either, and Del hints "close" on a group card and on the "Close Group and Contents" row; "Dissolve Group" (menu) and Ungroup All (toolbar) are the only dissolves, unhinted. Also: Close Selected closes the outermost selected cards first and skips one a group close already hid; a drop or "Move into" onto a group another replica closed (and a merge into one) is a no-op instead of hiding the node with it. Undo of Dissolve stays parked.
- **The unknown-type card (2026-10-14, homogeneity).** A node whose type this build doesn't know (a newer client's, or a retired one such as the old JSON leaf) is now a live card instead of a hidden node. It shows a question mark and its type string, or its stored name with the type under it, and it carries the generic fields only (pos, color, name, opened-from, group); every other field stays raw in the document, untouched by a move, rename or regroup. It links and sits in groups, but opens nothing and takes no jar. Duplicate and JSON import make a shell of its type (Close and Reopen keep the node whole) (the type string and generic fields, never the raw extras). One function, `card_kind::canonical_type`, maps every stored type to its caps row, with the sentinel row `application/vnd.manifold.unknown` (restorable, drawn with notes) in place of the old `*/*` row; `canonical_type("")` is "" (no row), so `worth_reopening("")` stays false where it used to hit the `*/*` row. `CLOSENODE` replaced `CLOSEGROUP`, EXPORTSTATE cards gained `stored_type`, and `gate unknown-type` checks it end to end. Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-unknown-type-card-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-unknown-type-card.md`.
- **Pin on every card (2026-10-12, homogeneity).** `toggle_pin` takes any node (it was tabs only), every card has the pin dot (`card_controls`), and the Pin/Unpin row moved from the two tab menus into `node_menu_config`'s shared tail, just above Colors, so every kind gets it from one place (a tab's row is no longer first). The layout already skipped any pinned node. "Pin All" and Ctrl+P stay tabs-only (a Tab-view button; the focused tab). Still open from the 2026-10-04 decisions: jars as graph nodes, `Kind` to type strings, JsonLeaf/FROM_JSON retirement.
- **Selection on every card (2026-10-12, homogeneity).** The rectangle selection, Space and Ctrl-click now take any card (they were tabs only), so Group Selected, Close Selected and a selection drag work on notes, groups, files and downloads too. The marquee picks from `level_items()` (the cards actually drawn, by their real rects). `Interaction::selected_tabs` is now `selected`, and so is the `EXPORTSTATE` key. A drop's unit leaves out the card dropped on (`take_drag_unit(id, target)`): a selection holding two groups dropped on one of them would otherwise put that group inside itself. Ctrl+P now pins the whole selection (all of it unpinned when every card is pinned), or the focused card with no selection (`App::pin_shortcut`); "Pin All" stays a Tab-view button for open tabs, since that view shows only tabs. The Close Selected button counts what a selected group takes with it: "Close Selected (2 + 5 inside)" (`unselected_members_of_selection`).
- **JSON leaf retired (2026-10-12, homogeneity).** `Kind::JsonLeaf`, `TYPE_JSON_LEAF`, `JsonPayload.leaf`, the leaf card, its caps row and the fields `from_json` (9), `key` (12) and `value` (13) are gone, with `Mirror::{from_json, key, value}` and `json_summary`. No migration: an old document's leaf has an unknown type, so it loads as a `Kind::Unknown` node nothing shows (kept in the document, as any unknown type is), and a group's stored `from_json` stays a raw field. Field numbers 9, 12 and 13 are reserved (comment in `store/model.rs`). `LINKABLE` went with it: it equalled `CARD_TYPES`.
- *(Superseded 2026-10-24: a card has no colour of its own; labels give it one, see **Labels**.)* **Shorter card context menu (2026-10-12, homogeneity).** The Jar section and the Color swatches no longer sit in the menu: a "Jar..." row (a group's is "Default jar...") and a "Color..." row open a page that replaces the menu's items in the same popup, with a "Back" row on top (`MenuPage`, `NodeMenuItem::Page`, `NodeMenuConfig::jar_page`; the open page is `MenuPopups::node_menu_page`, keyed by the menu's node and position so a new menu starts on the items, and kept through the fade-out). Picking a swatch or a jar closes the menu as before; "New Jar..." still swaps the page for the name field. A page in the same popup needs no second `Area`, no edge clamping and no egui `SubMenu` (which expects a `Popup`, ours is a hand-built `Area`). The accordion and the standalone-picker options were weighed and not built. A right-click on a card's swatch (the color dot) opens the menu on its Color page (`paint_card_control`'s caller sets `node_menu_page`); one on the jar chip opens it on the Jar page (the right-press handler in `graph_ui.rs` asks `card::on_jar_chip`; the chip's deferred paint records its scrim rect in egui temp data per card, valid for the frame it was painted and the next). `gate layout` has `tab-menu-jar`, `tab-menu-color` and `group-menu-jar` (`Open::CardMenu(type, page)` right-clicks, then sends the debug command `MENUPAGE jar|color|none`, which `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` also uses for `graph-menu-jars`, `graph-menu-color` and `graph-menu-group-jars`). Tests: the swatch click is driven through `build_graph_ui`; the chip's rect record and expiry are a unit test; the chip click through a whole frame is the `graph-menu-chip` view of `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` (it clicks 18 points right of and 33 above the card's bottom-left corner, where the chip sits at any zoom), checked by eye, not a gate scenario.
- **Closed as unreproduced: the smoke test's `arrow_nav` screenshot often isn't taken within 30 s** (2026-09-24; parked until the GPU is idle). It failed in 4 of 5 runs, then in 2 of 2 diagnostic runs, and the other three screenshots were fine every time. Temporary logging showed every retry of that screenshot's frame getting `CurrentSurfaceTexture::Timeout` from `get_current_texture`. Once the reader's 30 s wait ran out, the next command's frame took it at once. About 40 acquire timeouts per run happen elsewhere too, and most frames recover on the next try. During these runs, two jobs from another session (`conv_pc2_isolated_8x8_fra…`) were saturating the same adapter manifold renders on. So GPU contention, together with the off-screen window's slow presentation (see the slow-headless-frames entry above), is the leading suspect. It's unconfirmed: why the stall always hits the frame after Enter loads a results page is unexplained. Update 2026-10-06: it passed on an idle machine (CPU 12%, Defender real-time off, no other process on the eGPU, no live leases), and in two more full smoke runs the same day under CPU load of 40-80% (48 checks, 0 failures each). That supports contention as the cause; the unexplained "frame after Enter" pattern is still unexamined. Update 2026-10-12: smoke passed 54/54 in three gate runs (CPU 12-62%, Defender real-time off) with the `arrow_nav` shot taken each time; no recurrence since 2026-10-06, so it is closed as unreproduced. Next step if it recurs: log how long each `get_current_texture` blocks.
- **Replacing the session drops tab spawns still in flight**: importing or loading a session while tabs were still starting (e.g. the first-launch demo's) let those late tabs land in the new scene. When one's id matched an imported tab's (both count from `tab-0`), it replaced that tab and the tab crash-looped. `replace_running_session` now bumps a `session_generation`, and a spawn result from an older generation is dropped, which shuts its engine host down. Headless repro: before the fix, 1 of 2 runs crash-looped; after it, 4 of 4 runs were clean, with no engine processes left over.
- **Content dispatch: one MIME table for every way content arrives** (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-content-dispatch-design.md`). Before this, a picked/dropped/navigated file was routed by its own extension or a single hardcoded `image/*` check, JSON conversion was an opt-in "Save as Node" button, and there was no OS drag-and-drop at all. New `content.rs` owns one table from MIME type to node kind (`image/*` → Image, `application/pdf` → Pdf, `application/json`/`*/*+json` → Json, `text/plain` → Note, `text/html`/`application/xhtml+xml` → Tab, anything else → the engine's own download or a finished Download card), plus the one check each kind's bytes must pass (an image decodes, a PDF starts `%PDF-`, JSON parses, a note is UTF-8 under 1 MB) and one `sniff` for local files, which have no Content-Type header of their own. Every arrival path — a tab's finished load, the file picker, a real OS file drop, and the headless `ADDFILE <path> [x y]` (replacing the old per-kind `ADDIMAGE`/`ADDPDF`/`ADDJSON`) — funnels through the same `insert_content`, so a dropped `.pdf` and a navigated PDF response become the identical node. JSON, PDF and plain-text pages now convert automatically, the same one-way, destructive redirect Image pages already had; "Save as Node" is gone, superseded. Image/Pdf/Json/Note nodes keep the source `url` (the store's existing field 5; `path` is made `explicit` so a replica that refetches under its own profile root doesn't overwrite another replica's path), which gives any such node two new menu actions: **Refresh** (Image/Pdf/Json — refetch, re-check, replace the file in place) and **Open as tab** (any node with a URL — a live tab beside it, e.g. to read a PDF past its first page). Fetched files move out of `%TEMP%\manifold-redirects` into `profile::content_dir()` (`%APPDATA%\manifold\content`, named `<node id>.<ext>`); a missing file with a known URL refetches instead of erroring, and a background sweep at launch prunes content-dir files untouched for 7 days that no live node, Recently Closed entry or named session still names (never after the store was recovered). Live-verified via `scripts/drive.sh` seeding local `file://` tabs: WebView2 and CEF both convert JSON/PDF/PNG/text correctly and keep the source url; on WebView2, Refresh swapped a Json node's stored bytes in place and Open as Tab added an unconverted tab beside the original without disturbing it; a deleted content file with a known url refetched byte-identical on restart while an unrelated junk file in the same directory was pruned. Headless `ADDFILE` lands a node at the drop point; the real Explorer drop is pending the user's own check; the smoke test now covers every kind (Image, Pdf, Json, Note, a `.md` note, a `.html` page at its `file://` url, and an unrecognized `.bin` as a Download card), plus a tab navigated to a local JSON and PDF converting into its node. Left open: Servo and WebKit weren't live-tested (their engine-hosts didn't spawn in this checkout); `skip_convert` (which backs "Open as tab") isn't persisted, so that tab converts back to a card on its first load after a restart; and the real Explorer drop still needs the user's own check.
- **JSON node: a navigable level, replacing the popup viewer and "Expand as Graph"** (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-27-json-node-rework-design.md`). Double-clicking a Json card no longer opens the read-only tree popup or explodes the file into real graph nodes -- it enters the node's own JSON level (`current_group` becomes the node itself; the toolbar's back button, or the crumb bar, leaves it). Each non-empty object or array gets its own record card at that value's own tree depth, connected by an edge to the card holding the key/index it came from (an empty `{}`/`[]` renders inline as a row instead); a card shows up to 20 entries and pages the rest 20 at a time (its last row, `+ N more`); a reveal (selecting an outline row or a search match) more than 5 pages past a card's rows moves the card to that page alone, and only then does a `+ N earlier` row above its rows page back up 20 at a time (Wave 2, H1b; `headless-smoke-test.sh` clicks it for real: `JSONSELECT /items/900` on a 1000-entry array, then a `CLICK` on `json_level`'s `earlier` point, and the window grows up from skip 900 to 880); collapsing a card hides its whole subtree, down to the header; the layout is a left-to-right tree by depth, and "Relayout" forgets every card a user has dragged and lays the whole level out fresh again. A right-hand outline panel (shown or hidden by the bar's list button; the choice and the panel's width are remembered per profile) lists the open tree with a search box that narrows it to matching keys/scalar values (not array indices) plus their ancestors, all opened; clicking an outline row or a card row selects it and pans it into view if it isn't already fully on screen. The bar above the graph shows the selected pointer, "Copy value" (Ctrl+C) and "Copy path" (Ctrl+Shift+C) buttons, and a menu to pick the copied path's form: a JSON Pointer (`/users/0/name`) or a JS-style accessor (`users[0].name`). Card layout, per-pointer collapse/page state and color are never written back to the source file — they're stored as compact JSON on the node's own `view` field (store field 14, an ordinary diffed `Str`), keyed by pointer, so they survive a restart and sync to other replicas like any other field. The field is last-writer-wins as a whole: two replicas changing the view at once keep only one change. A failed Refresh, or a missing file still being refetched, keeps the view untouched (the placeholder can't be entered); only a successfully reloaded value drops overrides whose pointer no longer resolves. A JSON level is read-only: the creation shortcuts (`Ctrl+Shift+G`, headless `NEWGROUP`/`NEWNOTE`) do nothing there, and a dropped or added file, an import or a Recently Closed reopen first leaves to the Json node's group, then lands there in view. A session saved by an older build that used "Expand as Graph" still loads: its exploded `Group`/`JsonLeaf` nodes render exactly as they did before (`GroupPayload::from_json`, `JsonPayload::key`), just with no way to create more of them — "Add File..." on the same source is the way back to a plain Json node with a level. Headless: `JSONSELECT`/`JSONTOGGLE`/`JSONMORE`/`JSONRELAYOUT`/`JSONSEARCH` drive a level exactly like the mouse does, and `EXPORTSTATE`'s `json_level` reports it (see "Headless mode" above). Build-verified with zero warnings; live-verified by `gate smoke` (its json-level section): entering a JSON file, selecting and searching a nested match, a far reveal and the earlier row, paging, collapsing and expanding a card, relayout, and leaving, checked at each step through `EXPORTSTATE`.

- **Compact menus and tighter button groups (2026-10-01).** Menus and dropdowns were rows of stock buttons under the airy global spacing (13x7 padding, 7 px gaps, ~38 px rows with a resting fill), and `toolbar_chip` added 5 px at each end plus 6 px between buttons on top of each button's own padding. `theme::menu_item_style` now applies inside the context menus, the toolbar list popups, Tab actions and the address-bar suggestions: no gap between rows, 10x4 padding (~26 px rows), no resting fill, hover and highlight fill the row; `popup_frame`'s margin is 4. `toolbar_chip` pads 1 px per end with 2 px between buttons. Dialogs and the settings page keep the airy spacing. Compared with `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` before and after.
- **Hover no longer resizes buttons (2026-10-01).** egui pads a button for its state's stroke (padding minus stroke width), draws the stroke only if the frame is drawn, and applies `Button::stroke(..)` after the padding is chosen. So a button with a `.stroke(NONE)` override, or one drawn only when hovered (`selectable_label`, `toggle_value`, `Button::selectable`), was 2 px smaller at rest than hovered, and everything after it in the row (or column) shifted: "+ New Tab", the toolbar icon buttons, the JSON outline toggle, and the settings page's Mute row. `theme::add_borderless` (no stroke in any state) and `theme::rest_borderless` / `selectable_label` (no resting stroke) fix it at the source; `theme::tests::hovering_a_widget_does_not_resize_it` lays each kind out at rest and hovered and compares sizes. Found with `scripts/chrome-shots.sh` views `hover-graph`, `hover-json`, `hover-tabview`, `hover-find`, `hover-settings` (they sweep the mouse along the bar or down the settings column and `scripts/hover_diff.py` reports hovers that change more than one widget) and `focus-graph` (clicks into fields).
