Also scanned, with no layout shift: the tab context menu, the quick switcher and the address-bar dropdown (`hover-menu-tab`, `hover-quickswitch`, `hover-dropdown`). `tab-graph`, `tab-tabview` and `tab-settings` press Tab through the Graph toolbar, the Tab-view toolbar and the settings page (the new `TABKEY [shift]` debug command) and found no visible keyboard focus on buttons whose fill is overridden (egui shows focus only as the "active" look) and black, unreadable text on the zoom readout when focused; `theme::focus_ring` now draws a 1.5 px inset outline (no layout change). The Tab-view toolbar and settings page show a visible focus on every stop (the settings scan's extra changed rows are the "Last saved Ns ago" timer). Also clean: the JSON bar with a card selected, so the Copy buttons are enabled (`hover-json-sel`), and Tab past the Graph toolbar (10 stops; cards are not Tab-focusable, so stops 11+ change nothing). The settings page's Bookmarks section is blank in these shots because Export/Import are hidden when headless. Popups under Tab focus (`tab-find`, `tab-quickswitch`, `tab-popup`): the find bar and the Bookmarks list are clean; in the quick switcher Tab walked focus out to the toolbar behind it, fixed with `lock_focus(true)` on its search field (arrows and Enter pick rows). The History and Downloads lists and the address-bar dropdown (`tab-popup-history`, `tab-popup-downloads`, `tab-dropdown`) are clean: Tab moves a visible highlight and nothing shifts (the extra changed rows in the list scans are the "Last saved" timer). The search-engine combo (`tab-combo`) and engine picker (`tab-engine`): no layout shift; Tab highlights combo options, but the engine picker was not keyboard-operable: a probe (open, Tab x3, Enter) closed it without picking, because the cards (`Sense::click` interacts) never took focus and Tab walked on to the widgets behind it. Fixed for all three pickers (Default starting engine, Current Tab, Split): the selected card is focused on open, Up/Down move between cards (the focused card holds a focus lock so Tab and the arrows stay in the popup), Enter picks, Escape closes the picker only (the settings page and Tab view skip their own Escape while one is open, `App::engine_picker_open`), and `focus_ring` marks the focused card. Probe afterwards: Down + Enter changed the default engine to Servo; Escape closed the picker, a second Escape left the page. On a keyboard close (pick or Escape) focus returns to the opening button (`App::engine_opener_button` / `engine_opener_clicked` remember it); a focused settings engine button draws its label dark, since the engine color was unreadable on the gold focus fill. The Split picker was probed the same way (`tabview-split-key`: open, Down, Enter opens the Split pane). Tab scans now cover every view.

- **Toolbar matches the approved mockup (2026-10-01).** Option A of `docs/superpowers/mockups/toolbar_redesign_sketch.html` was only partly built. Now `+ New Tab` is the solid-gold `primary_button`, `toolbar_chip` draws a hairline between a chip and whatever precedes it, and `toolbar_panel` makes fields soft-filled (`SURFACE_RAISED`) and idle buttons borderless. The mockups are saved in `docs/superpowers/mockups/`.

- **Popup rows, Escape and graph cards are keyboard-operable (2026-10-01).** List popup rows (Recently Closed, Bookmarks, History, Downloads) are `popups::list_row`: focus lands on the first row on open, Up/Down wrap, Enter clicks, a focus ring shows. Any open anchored popup takes Escape (`build_ui`; the settings page and Tab view skip theirs while `popup_open()`), and focus returns to the opener button on a keyboard close. Graph cards take Tab in creation order (`node_order`): a card is a Tab stop only as the first card or while it holds focus (`draw_graph_card`), and `card_tab_step` moves focus between them (Tab/Shift+Tab, panning to the target; past either end egui takes over). Card corner controls are mouse-only; Delete on a focused card does what its close button does (closes it, a group with its members; removes a download from the list) and Shift+F10 opens its node menu (first item focused, Escape returns focus to the card; `SHIFTF10` debug command, `graph-menu-key` view). While any card or control has focus, each card shows its stop number in a corner badge (`CardPass::show_order`) and the focused one a white ring; the camera pans to a card that gains focus. Enter on a Tab-focused card acts like its click/double-click (a Group is entered); the global arrow/Enter focus model (`move_graph_focus`, `open_focused_graph_node`) is untouched, but its Enter is skipped while a card has egui focus (`card_nav_active`). Verified with `VIEWS="tab-graph graph-enter-group"` (Tab ×21 reaches the Group card, Enter enters it; `GROUP_STOP` overrides the count). Badges are clamped on screen. JSON levels take keys when no text field has focus (`handle_json_keys`): Up/Down select the previous/next outline row (revealing and panning as a click does), Enter expands/collapses the selected card, Right/Left open/close its outline row (Left on a closed one selects the parent); covered by unit tests and the `json-keys` view. The first card and the focused card are never culled, so Tab entry and focus survive a pan. Entering a JSON level with Enter on a Tab-focused card focuses its root card at once (`json_focus_root_key`; a double-click doesn't), sparing the toolbar's ~17 stops (`graph-enter-json` view). JSON cards are ordinary Tab stops (after the toolbar and outline) with a white focus ring; focusing one selects it, and Enter then toggles it. `ARROWRIGHT`/`ARROWLEFT` debug commands; `json-keys` shows keys and the Tab scan (18 stops, `JSON_TAB_STOPS`). Keyboard drag-to-group: every node menu lists "Move into <group>" for each sibling group at its level (on a group card it nests the group inside the other, while dragging a group onto a group merges the two) (`NodeMenuItem::MoveTo`, `NodeAction::MoveToGroup`, applied as `DropEffect::Join` through `apply_drop`, so a rectangle selection moves together); no cycle is possible since targets are siblings. "Remove from Group" is the way out. Cards announce themselves to screen readers: `draw_graph_card` calls `widget_info` (a labeled Button) with `Node::accessible_name` ("Group: Reading", "Note: first line", ...), so egui reports it on focus. AccessKit is wired in (`egui-winit` `accesskit` feature, `UserEvent::Accessibility`, adapter in `resumed`; the real window is created hidden and shown after the adapter exists, headless has none): a screen reader connecting calls `enable_accesskit`. `card_widget_info_reaches_the_accesskit_tree` checks the Button/name reach egui's tree; a visible window was smoke-tested. Not yet heard through Narrator. Keyboard multi-select: Space on a Tab-focused tab card (live or sleeping) toggles it in `selected_tabs`, as the marquee would; the toolbar's Group Selected/Close Selected (Tab stops) then act on it. Space is taken before the card's interact, since egui would read it as a click and open the tab. `SPACEKEY` debug command, `graph-select-key` view (`SELECT_STOP`, default 12 = first card; 13 is a tab in the seeded set). `VIEWS=mouse-regress` asserts the mouse side of the Tab-focus model (drag by exactly the drag distance, swatch click tags, middle-click closes) and prints PASS/FAIL. The F1 shortcut popup (`build_shortcuts_help`) now has sections: Everywhere, Graph cards, JSON level (a `#` key row is a heading); it scrolls, so the card and JSON sections sit below the fold at 800 px. Keys are shown where they apply: the focused card gets a strip of keycaps (`build_card_hint`, from `App::card_hint`; Tab/Enter/Space/Del/Shift+F10 by kind; hidden while its menu is open), the F1 popup draws keys as keycaps (`theme::kbd`/`kbd_keys`; egui has no keycap widget, only `Button::shortcut_text`, which the node menu uses for `NodeAction::key_hint`, i.e. the row doing what Del does on the card shows Del: Close, Delete or Remove from List, and Close Group and Contents on a group, whose Dissolve Group is unhinted), and the tab-view reload tooltip names Ctrl+R. Key-hint survey (2026-10-01, from every `consume_key`/`key_pressed` site): hinted in context: Ctrl+T (+ New Tab tooltip), Ctrl+Shift+T (Recently Closed tooltip), Ctrl+R (reload tooltip), Ctrl+G/Esc (Graph button tooltip), F1 (help button), Del/Enter/Space/Tab/Shift+F10 (focused-card strip, Del on the menu row it matches: Close, Delete, Remove from List, or Close Group and Contents), JSON arrows/Enter and Ctrl+C (bottom strip while a row or card is selected; Copy value/path tooltips). In the F1 popup only, with no hint where they apply: Ctrl+W, Ctrl+Shift+W, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+P, Ctrl+L, Ctrl+F, Ctrl+=/-/0, F12, F2, Ctrl+K, Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+Shift+G (no toolbar button), graph arrows/Enter/Esc, the lists' Up/Down/Enter. Later the same day: the address bar's hint text names Ctrl+L, the quick switcher shows an Up/Down/Enter/Esc keycap line, the find field's tooltip names Enter/Shift+Enter/Esc, and the F1 popup documents the address-bar, find-bar and quick-switcher keys. The Tab actions popup now leads with the focused tab's Pin/Unpin (Ctrl+P), Duplicate (Ctrl+D), Find in Page (Ctrl+F) and Developer Tools (F12), each with its key as `Button::shortcut_text`, calling the same `*_shortcut` methods as the keys. Later: the Tab actions popup also has Close Tab (Ctrl+W) and the zoom chip's tooltips name Ctrl+-, Ctrl+0 and Ctrl+=. The breadcrumb's current-group tooltip names F2. Still F1-only: Ctrl+Shift+W, Ctrl+Tab. `VIEWS=tabview-actions-click` asserts the popup's Pin, Duplicate and Close Tab rows work (PASS/FAIL). Enter on a Tab-focused Note card starts editing and focuses its `TextEdit` (id `("note_edit", id)`); Escape ends it and returns focus to the card (a click elsewhere keeps the focus it took); the card strip shows Enter edit. `VIEWS=note-key` asserts all of it (the state export now has `editing` per card). JSON cards are walked by `draw_json_level`'s own Tab handling (`json_tab_target`), like `card_tab_step`, so a card culled off screen is still reached: the target is drawn that frame, takes focus, and its `gained_focus` selects and pans. A focused JSON card is never culled either (panning away used to drop focus). `VIEWS=json-tab-offscreen` pans all three sample cards off screen (zoom, then a canvas drag) and asserts Tab still selects each in turn; it caught the dropped-focus bug. The graph card strip no longer shows over a JSON level. Plain digit keys in the Graph view jump to the card with that badge number (`handle_card_digits`): they focus and pan, Enter then opens; two digits within 0.6 s make 10+ (a digit that no longer number could extend jumps at once, `digits_ready`); ignored while a text field, popup, menu, JSON level or Tab view has the keyboard. Numbers are creation order, so closing a card renumbers later ones. `DIGITKEY n` debug command, `digit-jump` view (asserted), F1 line "1 - 9". Order badges are keycaps now (the number is the key), and holding Ctrl alone for 0.35 s (`ctrl_reveal_step`; another key press, Shift or Alt cancels it until release) reveals them on every card plus a bottom strip of the Graph view's Ctrl chords (`build_ctrl_hint`), to cut always-on clutter; menu rows, the focused-card strip and F1 stay always visible. Only the timing is unit-tested: a held modifier can't be injected, so the strip itself has not been seen on screen. Nothing in the keyboard audit is mouse-only now except the card corner controls.
- **Toolbars and menus use Familjen Grotesk (2026-10-01).** Chosen from the `docs/superpowers/mockups/toolbar_grotesque.html` shortlist: controls (toolbar buttons and fields, menu rows, the primary button's SemiBold) are Familjen Grotesk at 13 px; body text stays Literata and headings Newsreader; chrome around them is too: the settings page body, dropdown lists, dialogs (quick switcher, shortcuts, import), engine picker, toast and key-hint strip all call `control_font`; cards and page content stay Literata. Two static instances (`FamiljenGrotesk-Regular.ttf`, `-SemiBold.ttf`, OFL, from Google's variable file via `fonttools varLib.instancer`) are bundled and bound as the named families `control_family()` / `control_bold_family()`. egui 0.36 buttons take their font from `TextStyle::Body`, not `TextStyle::Button` (`Style::widget_style`), so setting `text_styles[Button]` does nothing; `theme::control_font(ui)` sets `override_font_id` on a `Ui` instead, and `toolbar_panel` and `menu_item_style` call it. The control families are built after `egui_phosphor::add_to_fonts` so icon glyphs resolve in them (built before, every icon button drew a tofu box); `bound_family` falls back to Literata where fonts aren't applied yet (headless tests would otherwise panic on an unbound family). With it came the toolbar polish: bar 32 -> 38 px, inset `toolbar_field` for the address and filter fields, chips with 8 px corners. Menu rows got a little tighter (Tab actions rows 27 -> 24 px, so `chrome-shots.sh`'s Close Tab click moved to y=140). `control_font_switches_button_family` checks the family; The Tab toolbar's Graph, Split and Tab actions buttons are icon-only now (Phosphor `GRAPH`, `COLUMNS`, `DOTS_THREE`, each with a tooltip; an active split shows only through its Close Split X, no longer "Split ▾"), so `chrome-shots.sh` clicks moved (Tab actions at W-95, Split at W-126). Vertical centring (2026-10-01): the Tab toolbar is top-aligned, so its panel margin is 5 px (items are 28 px in a 38 px bar), and Familjen is lifted 0.06 em (`FontTweak::y_offset_factor`) because its caps sit ~0.075 em below its line-box centre; measured on the headless shots before the sizing pass: button box centre 19.5, "New Tab" ink centre 20.0. Sizes follow the mockup (2026-10-02): `toolbar_panel` sets `interact_size.y` 28 for buttons and fields (`TextEdit::min_size` 28 on the address and filter fields, filter 150 px wide), `toolbar_chip` is 30 px (2 px padding around 26 px buttons; it restores the row's spacing and `interact_size` afterwards), icon buttons are at least 32 px wide, the divider is 1x18 and the primary button has 11 px side padding. Measured: primary 5..33, chips 4..34, both fields 5..33 on both bars. Tab toolbar restructure (2026-10-02): the Graph button sits first in the nav chip (Graph, Back, Forward, Reload, Home; other kinds keep it standalone), Bookmark is a standalone 32 px button, and the address field holds the favicon or spinner at its left and Ctrl L keycaps (`theme::paint_keycaps`, hidden while focused) at its right via a 32/56 px text margin. Icon buttons are named for screen readers: `NamedTip::named_tip` sets the tooltip and the accesskit label (`named_tip_labels_the_button_for_screen_readers`); before, they announced their glyph. Not checked with a real Narrator. Menus (2026-10-02): rows are 26 px with 5 px hover corners and the popup frame has 8 px corners, as in the mockup's menu (Tab actions rows 24 -> 26 px, so `chrome-shots.sh`'s Close Tab click is y=155). Key hints are right-aligned (rows take the menu's width so far: `min_size(ui.min_rect().width())`; `available_width` in a popup is the screen's, which made the menus screen-wide; Tab actions is 200 px). Settings page (2026-10-02): `theme::toggle` (30x17 switch, eased knob, Space/click, focus ring, screen-reader name) and `theme::setting_row` (label and hint left, control right) now carry Mute (Current Tab) and "Sleep background tabs"; with sleep on, an "Idle time" select (10/30/60 min) appears under it. Turning sleep off keeps no duration, so turning it on again starts at 10 min. Zoom stays a stepper and "Reduce motion" was not added (no such setting). Not looked at in the real app, and the sleep-on state (select visible) is not in a headless shot.
- **2026-10-22: the serif is gone; Familjen Grotesk is the only family (supersedes the entry above where it says body stays Literata and headings Newsreader).** Regular is the proportional family, SemiBold is `bold_family()` (titles via `heading_text`, the primary button). Literata and Newsreader and their files, `control_family`, `heading_family` and `control_font` are deleted: egui's Body size is already 13, so the per-`Ui` override did nothing once the family was shared. The `manifold://` pages use a sans stack (`--sans` in `base.css`). Three tests had leaned on the old metrics and were loosened: the bookmark folder name is longer and matched by prefix, and the trail-strip show-through check ignores a one-pixel line-box overlap.
- **Tab cards use design F (2026-10-02).** A Tab card is a 40 screen-point header (favicon or engine dot, title, url dimmed) over the page thumbnail; `tab_thumb_rect` gives the thumbnail rect to both `paint_tab_body` and the Tab-view transition (`transition_content_rect_points`). Cards under 80 screen points tall fall back to the old strip over the thumbnail. Other card kinds are unchanged; see `docs/superpowers/mockups/README.md` for the rollout plan and the deferred Download source URL. Checked headless (`chrome-shots.sh graph`); not yet checked in the real app at your DPI, with a real favicon, or mid-transition.
- **Image and PDF cards use the overlay header (2026-10-02).** `paint_file_header` puts a scrim with glyph, file name and source (url, else path elided at the start) over the top 40 screen points of the picture; the card keeps the picture's size. Cards under 80 screen points tall get a name-only strip at the bottom. The Tab card's thumbnail is now cropped to its rect (`cover_uv`) rather than stretched. Checked headless for an Image card (`chrome-shots.sh graph`); PDF, small cards and the Tab-view transition are unchecked.
- **The Tab-view transition lands on the cropped thumbnail (2026-10-02).** A content pane is now a `gpu::PanePx { viewport, clip }`: the page is stretched over `viewport` (which may extend past the window) and a scissor rect shows only `clip`. `transition_content_rects_points` eases the clip between the content area and the card's thumbnail area, and the viewport between the content area and the page scaled to cover that area (`cover_uv`), so the quad ends on what the card shows. Ordinary panes have `viewport == clip`. Checked: tests, clippy, and the tabview, split, image-view and mouse-regress shots; the animation itself was not captured mid-flight (about:blank would show no crop anyway), so look at it with a real page.
- **Sleeping, Waking and Failed tab cards and Download cards use header F (2026-10-02).** One `App::paint_card_header` (glyph or favicon, name, source line; strip fallback on small cards) now serves Image, PDF, Unloaded and Download; Image/PDF use its scrim form, the rest sit on the card fill. Note, JSON and Group keep their old bodies. Covered by unit tests and clippy only; not looked at on screen (no seeded card of these kinds).
- **Group cards use header F, and downloads remember their source URL (2026-10-02).** A group card is a tab card's size (`GROUP_NODE_SIZE`, `DOWNLOAD_NODE_SIZE` are `GRAPH_NODE_SIZE`) with a folder header ("N items") over a 2×2 member grid. `EngineToHost::DownloadStarted` gained `url`; it is stored on `DownloadPayload.url` and saved under `field::URL` for downloads (older sessions load it as `None`). Header F's Download source line is that URL, else the path. `chrome-shots.sh cards-f` shoots a Download card; a sleeping card could not be seeded (`SLEEPNODE` on the seeded about:blank tab did not sleep it). The CEF and WebKit URL reads were not run (WebKit not even compiled).

- **Foreign imports drop every `path`; restore still touches own paths on the UI thread (2026-10-17).** `import` under `Trust::Foreign` now drops a `path` of any kind (`2030121`), not just a Download's, so a crafted Import Session/Group or `--import` file can't make restore open a UNC share (an SMB connection leaks the user's NTLM hash and stalls the UI). Own-trust restore keeps its paths, and refusing UNC there would break users' own network files. Restore still calls `exists` (`session_sync.rs:702`, now only when the node has a URL, `c4b18fc`), `fs::metadata` for a Download's byte count (`:754`, `fetched.rs:487`) and reads json inline, all on the UI thread. **Parked, by decision (`PARKED.md`): moving those off the UI thread.** The restore code needs the answer at once to choose between a placeholder and a real card, so it means a "checking" card state plus a result message from a worker, and the gate stages that assume settled cards after restore would have to wait for it. It only matters for a slow own-network share. If done, start with the Download byte count (show 0, a worker fills in the size; no new card state), then image/pdf/json.
- **Parked work lives in `PARKED.md` (2026-10-17).** One list with a trigger and evidence per item; the older "parked" mentions in this file and in `docs/superpowers/plans/` are copies.
- **View latency, measured (2026-10-17; runs `viewlat-poll2`, `viewlat-idle3` in `docs/runs.md`).** A throwaway probe (reverted) stamped an input sent to a WebView2 tab, its next frame received by the reader thread, and that frame uploaded; debug build, headless, CPU 6-13%, 20 keystrokes. While the loop polls: input to upload is 34-36 ms (about 20 to the frame arriving, about 14 from arrival to upload, the drain cadence), with outliers at 51-69 ms. Once the loop is idle (`IDLE_FRAME`), a frame the page produced on its own (a timer) waited 62-97 ms (mean about 83) between arriving and being uploaded, against about 13 ms when active: the reader's `FrameReady` arm does not call `wake()`. A keypress is a window event, so it leaves idle at once; the delay hits page-initiated change after a second of quiet (animation start, load finish, video). Not measured: present (`Fifo`) time, a release build, real-window input. **Fixed (591a48d, run `viewlat-fixed`):** the reader wakes the loop for a frame stored into an empty slot (frames piling up behind it do not), and `note_view_frames` makes the next idle tick due, so an idle loop uploads and draws a page's own frame at once: 0.5-1.5 ms from arrival to upload, was 62-97. Typing is unchanged (34-36 ms). Idle CPU, same page (a frame every 1.7 s), 30 s, two runs each: 8.1 and 8.2% of a core before, 7.9 and 7.5% after (`idlecpu-*`; debug build, idle path forced in headless, machine at 45% CPU), so no regression; a burst of `BUSY_FRAMES` still ends idle as before. **Typing's drain wait, measured (run `viewlat-cadence`):** the 14 ms from arrival to upload is the vsync-paced redraw, not a missing wake. The active loop redraws every 16.0 ms (median, p90 17.4) and a frame waits 11.2 ms on average for the next drain (p90 15.5, n=84; debug build, CPU 44%, probe reverted). A wake can't shorten it, because the loop already redraws as fast as `Fifo` presents; only a shallower present queue (`desired_maximum_frame_latency`, now 1, see below) or a different present mode could. **Present queue depth, measured in a real window (runs `keylat-ab-11` and `-13`):** a script sends a real key to a visible window and polls a screen pixel for the page's colour flip (GDI, one reading per 16.7 ms frame, so the numbers are quantized to a frame and the monitor's own scanout is left out; the difference between builds is what counts). Debug build, CPU 9-10% before, 50 keys per build per run, two runs with the build order reversed. `desired_maximum_frame_latency` 1: median 99.2 and 99.8 ms. At 2: median 116.5 and 115.9 ms. A launch of the latency-2 build came out at either 116.5 or 100-101 ms (so one launch in two sat a frame behind), while the latency-1 build gave 99.2-99.8 ms in all four launches: never slower, and sometimes one frame (16.7 ms) faster. Not measured: a release build, other monitors, power use or tearing (`Fifo` stays, only the queue is shallower). The harness is `scripts/keylat.ps1` (`-Build a=<exe>,b=<exe>`, order A B B A; it takes the foreground for about 40 s per launch, so launch it through `run.ps1` with `-Priority Normal`). **Applied (`gpu.rs`, runs `keylat-real-change`, `gate-light-latency1b`):** `desired_maximum_frame_latency` is 1. The same probe on HEAD against the previous build: median 99.4 ms against 101.2, mean 93.5 against 110 (the old build gave 115.7 in one launch and 100.7 in the other), never slower. Gate `light` (the tier `gate tier` names for `gpu.rs`): 9 stages exit 0, CPU 13-19%, Defender real-time off. **Mailbox, tried (run `keylat-mailbox`):** not available on this machine. The surface reports `[Immediate, Fifo, FifoRelaxed]` on both Vulkan and DX12, and wgpu rejects a `Mailbox` configure, so the comparison cannot be made here. `FifoRelaxed` (run `keylat-relaxed`, both at depth 1, n=50 each): median 83.5 ms against 83.4 for `Fifo`, mean 90.7 against 89.2, so no gain, and it can tear; `Fifo` stays. **Release build** (run `keylat-release`, n=50 each, A B B A): latency 2 median 116.7 ms, latency 1 100.0, every launch of each the same, so the gain holds in release (-16.7 ms). Idle CPU in a real window on the Welcome page (`idlewin-release`, 30 s, manifold and its direct children): 6.1 and 5.6% of a core at latency 2, 4.8 and 4.9% at latency 1, so no idle cost from the shallower queue. **`Immediate`, tried (release, `keylat-immediate`, `idlewin-immediate`):** median 33.7 ms against 100.2 for `Fifo` at latency 1 (-66.5 ms, far more than the one frame the queue explained), but idle CPU is 12.1 and 13.8% against 5.0 and 4.8%, and the active loop would present uncapped (the `present_mode` comment in `gpu.rs`); tearing was not inspected. **Capped `Immediate`, prototyped (release, runs `keylat-cap3`, `idlecap`, `redrawcount*`):** the active loop under `Immediate` redraws 1-3 ms apart (about 400 a second) at startup, so it needs a cap: `about_to_wait` waits until `last_present` plus the monitor refresh interval (`interval_from_millihertz`), and a page frame (`on_view_wake`) redraws at once. Median key to screen 33.5 ms with both, 49.8 with the cap alone, 100.0 for `Fifo`; manifold idle CPU 4.3-4.5% against 3.5%. **On (2026-10-18):** `gpu.rs` asks for `PREFERRED_PRESENT_MODE` (now `Immediate`; the surface falls back to `Fifo` when it lacks the mode) and the loop asks `GpuState::present_blocks` whether it needs the cap, so switching is one constant. A first try failed the gate `layout` stage (`history-popup: nothing shows through it`): `list_row_nav` focused a popup's first row only if a non-interactable pass had been drawn since it closed, which depends on frame cadence. It now compares the pass number (`cumulative_pass_nr`), so any gap counts as a reopen; `layout` then passes under `Immediate` (228 checks, run `layout-imm-focusfix`). Final tree, release, re-measured (run `keylat-final`, n=50 each, A B B A): `Immediate` median 34.0 ms against 100.1 for `Fifo` (-66.1 ms), the machine at 60-67% CPU. **Tearing, checked (2026-10-17):** by eye on a sweeping bar, none seen (not a confident test); PresentMon 2.6.0 (scoop `presentmon`; `presentmon --process_name X.exe --timed 8 --terminate_after_timed --output_file f.csv`) reports `Composed: Flip` with `AllowsTearing=0` for every present of the `Immediate` build (466 in 8 s, so the cap holds about 58 per second) and of the `Fifo` build (475), so the compositor sits between the window and the screen and there is nothing to tear in a window on this monitor. Not checked: another monitor, a fullscreen window. Launches land at either ~83 or ~98 ms for both modes (the phase against the display), so only same-session A/B pairs compare.
